# AC Arthritis

*Shoulder & Elbow · 1 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — **most common cause of AC joint pain**

**Demographics** — ↑ with age; can start by **2nd decade**

**Classic patient** — **weight-lifters** + overhead throwing athletes (constant heavy overhead activity)

**Risk factors** — trauma · post-traumatic (**clavicle fracture, AC instability**) · **distal clavicle osteolysis** · inflammatory (RA) · post-infectious (septic arthritis)

### Pathophysiology

**Mechanism** — **large axial loads through a small contact area** → **repetitive microtrauma** → OA (**same mechanism as distal clavicle osteolysis**)

**Aggravators** — articular disc degeneration + oblique joint surface orientation → ↑ contact forces

### Anatomy – AC joint & ligaments

**AC joint** — **diarthrodial**; scapula ↔ clavicle; **fibrocartilaginous intra-articular disc** (analogous to knee meniscus); small surface, high axial + rotational loads

| Ligament | Stability provided | Injury → |
|---|---|---|
| **AC ligaments** | **Anterior–posterior (horizontal)** | AP instability |
| **Posterior + superior AC ligaments** | **Most important** AC stabilisers | **Posterior instability** of distal clavicle |
| Anterior + superior AC ligaments | Anterior | **Anterior instability** (one OB explanation) · **no instability** (another OB explanation) — read the options |
| **CC ligaments (conoid + trapezoid)** | **Superior–inferior (vertical)** | **Superior instability** |

![Axial view – AC joint ligaments; posterior + superior AC ligaments highlighted](../../MCQ/attachments/q211920-a-ac-joint.jpg)

**Not AC stabilisers** — **coracohumeral ligament** (rotator interval) · **coracoacromial ligament** (anterior acromion; released in subacromial decompression) · deltotrapezial fascia (only elevated in open DCE)

**Rare instabilities** — **inferior** (CC ligaments resist superior displacement) · **rotational** (rotation is normal baseline load)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — activity-related **superior shoulder pain**; overhead activity, **pressing (bench press, push-up)**, lying on affected side

**Palpation** — **direct AC joint tenderness**; prominent distal clavicle (osteophytes)

**Provocative** — **cross-body adduction test** (most classic) · **O'Brien active compression** (90° forward flexion) → AC pain

![Cross-body adduction test](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3049-crossarm.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Finding |
|---|---|
| **X-ray – Zanca view (15° cephalic tilt; 10–15° in TO/OB)** | **Best view**; osteophytes, **joint space narrowing**, **distal clavicle osteolysis** |
| X-ray caveat | Changes often present **without symptoms** (poor clinical correlation) |
| **MRI** | ↑ signal + **oedema** in AC joint; shows associated cuff / LHB pathology |

![Zanca view – AC osteophytes and joint space narrowing](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3049-zanca.jpg)

### Diagnostic injection

**AC joint injection (corticosteroid / local anaesthetic, e.g. bupivacaine)** — **diagnostic + therapeutic**; relief confirms AC source

**Accuracy** — joint hard to access, **often missed** → **ultrasound ↑ accuracy**; most get **no long-term relief**

## 5 Management

```
Symptomatic AC arthritis
├─ First line → activity modification (avoid pressing) + PT (shoulder girdle) + NSAIDs
├─ Persistent → corticosteroid injection (US-guided)
└─ Failed nonop, severe symptoms → distal clavicle excision (Mumford)
       ├─ Arthroscopic → resect 0.5–1 cm, evaluate + treat GH pathology
       └─ Open → meticulous deltotrapezial fascia repair
```

### Distal clavicle excision – preserving posterosuperior capsule

**Mumford procedure** — distal clavicle resection, arthroscopic (burr) or open; indication = **severe symptoms, failed nonop**

| Resection amount | Source |
|---|---|
| **0.5–1.0 cm** (lateral clavicle) | Orthobullets, OB |
| **4–8 mm** may suffice arthroscopically (preserves superior AC ligaments → less bone than open) | OB (Nuber) |
| **>1–1.5 cm** = aggressive → **instability** | Orthobullets, OB |

**Structure to preserve** — **posterosuperior joint capsule / posterior + superior AC ligaments** → prevents **AP (posterior) instability**

**Over-resection** — AC or CC ligament damage → instability; even correct length + posterosuperior damage → AP instability

**Under-resection** — persistent pain

![Arthroscopic distal clavicle excision](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3049-dcr.jpg)

### Arthroscopic vs open distal clavicle excision

**Outcomes** — similar; choice = surgeon preference

**Only proven arthroscopic advantage** — **evaluation of the glenohumeral joint** + treat associated pathology (**rotator cuff, LHB, labrum / SLAP**)

**Missed pathology with open** — **15 missed SLAP lesions** in failed open DCE (Berg) → do **diagnostic arthroscopy even with open DCE**

**Not proven arthroscopic advantages** — lower complication rate · lower infection rate · ↓ surgical time · preservation of CC or inferior AC ligaments

**Other arthroscopic pluses (OB)** — direct view of clavicle end, less soft-tissue violation, shorter rehab, quicker return

**Open DCE** — requires **meticulous deltotrapezial fascia repair**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Cause |
|---|---|
| **AP instability** | **Over-resection >1–1.5 cm** · sacrificing **posterior + superior AC ligaments** |
| **Superior–inferior instability** | Iatrogenic; resection compromising **CC ligaments** |
| **Persistent pain** | **Incomplete resection** – most commonly **posterior-superior distal clavicle** |
| Heterotopic ossification | |
| Stiffness, infection | Causes of failed DCE (OB) |
| **Deltoid dehiscence** | Inadequate **deltotrapezial fascia repair** after **open** DCE |

**Failed DCE management** — conservative, revision, or **ligament reconstruction**

## X vs Y

| | **AC ligaments** | **CC ligaments** |
|---|---|---|
| Components | Superior, posterior, anterior, inferior | **Conoid + trapezoid** |
| Plane | **Anterior–posterior** | **Superior–inferior** |
| Injured by | Aggressive DCE / debridement | Over-aggressive resection (iatrogenic) |
| Instability | **Posterior** (post + sup injured) | **Superior** |

| | **Arthroscopic DCE** | **Open DCE** |
|---|---|---|
| GH evaluation | **Yes** | No (add scope) |
| Key closure step | — | **Deltotrapezial fascia repair** |
| Specific risk | Under-resection (visualisation) | **Deltoid dehiscence**, missed SLAP |

## How it's asked

**"Weightlifter, superior shoulder pain with bench press — best X-ray view?"** → **Zanca (15° cephalic tilt)**

**"Weightlifter, persistent popping after arthroscopic DCE, posterosuperior ligaments damaged — instability direction?"** → **posterior**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior · inferior · rotational · superior = CC ligaments

**"Structure most important to preserve during arthroscopic DCE?"** → **posterosuperior joint capsule**\
✗ Wrong options: coracohumeral · coracoacromial · anterosuperior capsule · deltotrapezial insertion

**"Only documented benefit of arthroscopic over open DCE?"** → **ability to evaluate the glenohumeral joint**\
✗ Wrong options: lower complications · ↓ surgical time · CC / inferior AC ligament preservation

**"Persistent pain after DCE — commonest cause?"** → **incomplete resection of posterior-superior distal clavicle**

**"Deltoid weakness after open DCE?"** → **inadequate deltotrapezial fascia repair**

**"How much clavicle to resect arthroscopically?"** → **0.5–1 cm** (4–8 mm may suffice; >1–1.5 cm → instability)

# Acromioclavicular Joint

*Shoulder & Elbow · 2 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Osteology & articular disc

**Joint type** — **diarthrodial**; capsule + intra-articular synovium + articular cartilage interface

**Intra-articular disc** — **fibrocartilaginous**; usually **meniscoid-like** · complete disc **very rare** (TO); variable size/shape

**Cartilage (TO)** — hyaline → **fibrocartilage**: **acromial side by age 17** · **clavicular side by age 24**

### Motion

**Clavicle** — rotates **40–50° posteriorly** with shoulder elevation

**Through AC joint** — only **8°** of rotation; remainder = **scapular rotation + SC joint motion**

**Joint itself** — **gliding motions only**; most motion from the bones, not through the joint

### Stability – AC vs CC ligaments

| Restraint | Direction | Key facts |
|---|---|---|
| **AC ligaments / capsule** (superior, inferior, anterior, posterior) | **Horizontal (AP)** | **Superior strongest**, then **posterior**; constrain posterior clavicular rotation; small-displacement vertical stability (TO) |
| **Trapezoid** (CC; **anterolateral**) | Horizontal + vertical loads | Inserts **3 cm** from clavicle end; **>3× thicker at clavicular end** (TO); origin **posterior half of coracoid dorsum** (TO) |
| **Conoid** (CC; **posteromedial**) | **Vertical** | Inserts **4.5 cm** from clavicle end, **posterior border**; **more important** CC ligament; clavicular insertion **~2× wider/thicker** than coracoid → **inverted cone** (TO); origin more posterior on **coracoid base** (TO) |
| Deltotrapezial fascia, capsule, deltoid, trapezius | Additional | Secondary stabilisers |

![Coracoclavicular ligaments – trapezoid and conoid](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3033-cc-ligaments-v4.jpg)

**Normal CC distance** — **11–13 mm** (superior coracoid → inferior clavicle)

### Biomechanics of the AC ligaments (TO)

**AP vs vertical (TO)** — AC capsule + ligaments give **3× more control in AP** than vertical direction

**Thickness (TO)** — **1–5 mm**; inner osseous insertion **3–4 mm from joint line**, peripheral fibres farther

**Superior ligament** — **most robust**; principal horizontal stabiliser; **~50%** of AP strength (OB); **thickest posteriorly**

**Posterior ligament** — adds **~25%** of AP strength (OB)

**Two-bundle model (TO)** — **superoposterior bundle** resists **posterior** clavicular translation · thin **anteroinferior bundle** limits **anterior** displacement

## 5 Management

### Distal clavicle excision – posterosuperior AC ligaments

**Mumford procedure** — (arthroscopic) distal clavicle resection for **AC arthrosis**; resection length **debated**

**Must preserve** — **superior + posterior AC ligaments / capsule** → prevents post-op **AP (posterior) instability**

**Over-resection sign** — popping + pain + **mild posterior instability**, no deformity on X-ray (e.g. 8 mm excised in weightlifter) → **posterior + superior AC ligaments compromised**

**✗ Wrong option: anterior + superior AC** — opposite answer; these resist **anterior** translation

**✗ Conoid / trapezoid** — **vertical** restraints; injured in **grade III or higher** separations → superior clavicular migration

**✗ Wrong option: coracohumeral ligament** — not an AC stabiliser

### AC separation – reduction & stabilisation

| AC separation | Feature | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Type III** | — | **Controversial** |
| **Type V** | Clavicle elevated **100–300%** + **extensive soft-tissue stripping** | **Surgical AC reduction + stabilisation** |

![Type V AC separation – marked clavicular prominence](../../MCQ/attachments/q8711-a-14a.jpg)

**✗ Type V distractors** — sling · NSAIDs + rapid return · arthroscopic partial claviculectomy · acromionectomy

## X vs Y

| | **AC ligaments** | **CC ligaments (conoid + trapezoid)** |
|---|---|---|
| Plane | **Horizontal / AP** | **Vertical** |
| Strongest component | **Superior** (then posterior) | **Conoid** (more important) |
| Threatened by | **Distal clavicle excision** (too much) | **Grade ≥III** AC separation |
| Failure → | **Posterior instability**, popping | **Superior clavicle migration** |

| | **Trapezoid** | **Conoid** |
|---|---|---|
| Position | **Anterolateral** | **Posteromedial** |
| Distance from clavicle end | **3 cm** | **4.5 cm** |
| Shape (TO) | >3× thicker at clavicular end | **Inverted cone** |
| Role | Horizontal + vertical | **Vertical** (primary) |

## How it's asked

**"Structure responsible for AP stability of AC joint?"** → **posterosuperior part of capsule** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: anteroinferior capsule · trapezoid · conoid

**"Arthroscopic distal clavicle excision — preserve what to prevent AP instability?"** → **superior + posterior AC capsule**\
✗ Wrong options: trapezoid · conoid · coracohumeral

**"Popping + posterior clavicle instability after Mumford — what was compromised?"** → **posterior + superior AC ligaments**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior + superior

**"Quarterback, clavicle tenting under skin (type V) — management?"** → **AC reduction + stabilisation**\
✗ Wrong options: sling · NSAIDs · claviculectomy · acromionectomy

**"Primary vertical stabiliser of AC joint?"** → **conoid ligament**

**"Strongest AC ligament?"** → **superior** (~50% AP strength), then posterior (~25%)

# Acromioclavicular Joint Injury

*Shoulder & Elbow · 3 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — **9% of shoulder girdle injuries** · **40–50% of shoulder injuries in sports** (OB)

**Demographics** — **♂ > ♀**, **athletes**, young

**AKA** — "shoulder separation" = disruption of AC ± CC ligaments

### Anatomy

**AC joint** — **diarthrodial**; medial acromion + lateral clavicle; oblique surface

**Intra-articular disc** — **fibrocartilaginous** (≈ knee meniscus); involutes, **gone by age 40**

**Motion** — mainly **gliding**; clavicle rotates **40–50° posteriorly** with elevation but only **~8° through AC joint** (synchronous scapuloclavicular motion)

**Dynamic stabilisers** — **anterior deltoid + trapezius** (deltotrapezial fascia)

### AC vs CC ligament function (horizontal vs vertical stability)

| Ligament | Controls | Key fact |
|---|---|---|
| **AC ligaments** (sup, inf, ant, post) | **Horizontal = anterior–posterior** stability | **Posterior + superior (posterosuperior) most important** → preserve in distal clavicle resection |
| **Conoid** (CC) | **Vertical = superior–inferior** | **Most important for vertical stability**; **conoid tubercle, posteromedial**; inserts **4.5 cm** medial to lateral clavicle edge |
| **Trapezoid** (CC) | Vertical | **Trapezoid tubercle, anterolateral**; inserts **3 cm** medial to lateral edge |
| Coracoacromial (CA) | — | **Spared** in AC injuries |

**✗ Ligament traps** — ✗ Wrong options: anterosuperior AC = vertical · conoid = horizontal · **inferior AC** = no significant horizontal role · trapezoid "most posteromedial" (that is conoid) · **anterior + inferior AC** most important · trapezoid at 4.5 cm

![AC, CA and CC (conoid, trapezoid) ligaments](../../MCQ/attachments/q214098-illa-ligaments-labeled.jpg)

**Normal CC distance** — **11–13 mm** (top of coracoid → undersurface of clavicle)

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — **direct blow to the shoulder** (fall onto **adducted** shoulder); bike falls, contact sport

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain over AC joint ± referred to **trapezius**; visible step-off

**Exam** — AC/lateral clavicle tenderness; abnormal contour vs other side

**Stability testing** — **horizontal (AP) = AC ligaments** → cross-body adduction; **vertical (sup–inf) = CC ligaments**

**Horizontal instability (ISAKOS IIIB)** — may need more aggressive treatment

**Type IV clue** — **skin tenting + posterior fullness**

**Type V clue** — **severe shoulder droop, not corrected by shrug**

**Type VI clue** — rare, associated injuries, **paraesthesias**

### Provocative tests

| Test | Technique | Positive = |
|---|---|---|
| **O'Brien (active compression)** | **Resisted flexion at 90° flexion, 10° adduction, full IR** | **Superficial pain → AC joint** · **deep pain → SLAP** |
| **Cross-body adduction** | Arm adducted across chest | AC joint pain |
| ✗ Wrong option: Jobe / empty can | Resisted abduction 90° scapular plane, pronated | Supraspinatus tear |
| ✗ Wrong option: apprehension | Supine abduction + ER | Anterior instability |
| ✗ Wrong option: belly press | Palm on abdomen, resisted IR | Subscapularis tear |
| ✗ Wrong option: Neer | Passive forward elevation in IR | Subacromial impingement |

**Persistent AC pain after nonop type III (6 wk)** — **O'Brien / cross-body adduction** → if pain + instability → **AC + CC reconstruction**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Technique | Use |
|---|---|---|
| **Bilateral AP AC joints** | **1/3 penetration** of standard shoulder AP | Compare **CC distance** with contralateral (III vs V) |
| **Axillary lateral** | — | **Required to diagnose type IV (posterior)** |
| **Zanca** | **10–15° cephalad tilt, 50% penetrance** | **Most accurate view of AC joint** |
| **Cross-body adduction (Basmania)** | Scapular Y with cross-body adduction stress | Horizontal stress view |
| **Alexander** | Scapular Y centred on AC joint, **15° cranial tilt** | **Posterior displacement** (also superior) |
| Weighted stress views | — | Rarely used; II vs III |
| CT | — | Degree/direction of displacement; often unnecessary |
| ✗ Wrong option: **Serendipity** | **40° cephalic tilt** | **SC joint** |
| ✗ Wrong option: Stryker notch · West Point · supraspinatus outlet · Velpeau | — | Not for AC joint |

**Penetration** — AC joint needs **1/3–1/2** of glenohumeral exposure → standard AP **over-penetrates (dark)** AC joint

**Zanca tilt** — **10–15°** (Orthobullets, OB) · **15°** (one OB explanation)

![Zanca view technique](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3047-shows-zanca-view.jpg)

### Rockwood classification & ligament injury pattern

| Type | AC lig | CC lig | Exam | Radiograph | Reducible | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | Sprain | Normal | Tender, **no instability** | Normal | Yes | Sling |
| **II** | **Torn** | **Sprain** | **Horizontal instability** | AC disrupted; **CC ↑ < 25%** | Yes | Sling |
| **III** | Torn | **Torn** | — | **CC ↑ 25–100%** | Yes | **Controversial** |
| **IIIA** (ISAKOS) | Torn | Torn | **Vertical instability, no horizontal** | CC ↑ 25–100% | Yes | — |
| **IIIB** (ISAKOS) | Torn | Torn | **Vertical + horizontal instability** | CC ↑ 25–100% | Yes | **May need more aggressive** |
| **IV** | Torn | Torn | **Skin tenting, posterior fullness** | Clavicle **posterior through trapezius** on **axillary** | **No** | Surgery |
| **V** | Torn | Torn | **Severe droop**, not improved by shrug | **CC ↑ > 100%** | **No** | Surgery |
| **VI** | Torn | Torn | Rare; associated injuries; paraesthesias | **Inferior** — **subacromial or subcoracoid**, tethered by **conjoint tendon** | **No** | Surgery |

![Rockwood classification types I–VI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3047-illustration-rockwood-classification-2-moved.jpg)

**Type III CC threshold** — **25–100%** (Orthobullets, OB) · **20–100%** (one OB explanation) — read the options

**Type I trap** — written in one OB explanation as "AC sprain + CC sprain, no instability" (Orthobullets: CC normal)

**Types III–VI** — **both AC + CC torn**; IV–VI = **irreducible**

**Type V irreducible** — clavicle **buttonholed through deltotrapezial fascia** (most common culprit)

**✗ Interposed-structure traps** — conjoint tendon (coracoid tip) · CC ligaments (ruptured, not incarcerated) · CA ligament

**Type IV and trapezius** — clavicle **pierces trapezial fascia** → muscle innervated by **CN XI (spinal accessory)**

**✗ Nerve traps** — axillary (deltoid, teres minor) · CN VII (face, platysma) · pectoral nerves · suprascapular (SS, IS)

**ISAKOS consensus** — Rockwood misses nuances → added **biomechanical stability (IIIA vs IIIB)**

### Differential (mimics)

| Mimic | Distinguishing feature |
|---|---|
| **Base of coracoid fracture** | Superiorly displaced clavicle but **normal CC distance** (CC ligs intact) |
| **Distal clavicle fracture Neer 2A** | Ligaments stay on distal fragment; medial fragment displaces |
| **Distal clavicle fracture IIB** (OB) | Fracture **between conoid + trapezoid** → **only conoid** disrupted; distal fragment CC distance intact |
| Paediatric distal / medial clavicle physeal injury | Physis |

**AC separation vs IIB clavicle fracture** — AC separation = **conoid + trapezoid** both injured (CC ↑ 25–100%)

## 5 Management

```
AC joint injury (Rockwood)
├─ Type I–II → sling, ice, early ROM + PT
├─ Type III, most patients → nonop (good if displacement < 2 cm)
│      └─ laborer / elite athlete / cosmetic concern / failed nonop → surgery
└─ Type IV, V, VI (acute) → ORIF / CC reconstruction
```

### Low-grade injuries – sling & early motion

**Nonop regimen** — brief sling (**2–4 wk** for I–III, OB), ice, activity modification, **early ROM**, PT; ± steroid injection

**Timeline (nonop)** — functional motion by **6 wk** · normal activity at **12 wk**

**20% ↑ CC distance** — = **type II** → **sling + early PT**\
✗ Wrong options: CC reconstruction · AC capsule repair · retrograde pinning · distal clavicle excision

**58-yo accountant, non-dominant, II vs III, no AP translation** — **sling + early ROM**\
✗ Wrong options: Weaver-Dunn · CC recon

**Nonop complications** — AC arthritis, chronic subluxation/instability

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Acute type IV, V, VI** | Recent studies: no functional difference op vs nonop even for high grade (IV–V may be treated nonop, OB) |
| **Acute type III** | **Laborers, elite athletes, cosmetic concerns** |
| **Chronic type III** | **Failed nonop** |
| Contraindications | Poor rehab compliance; skin problems over approach |

**Timing** — acute stabilisation usually **within first 2 wk** (OB) · surgery **after 6 wk failed nonop = same outcome as immediate** (Orthobullets)

**Old teaching** — acute → ORIF, chronic → CC reconstruction (no longer supported)

### Type III injuries – operative vs nonoperative

| Outcome | Operative | Nonoperative |
|---|---|---|
| **Complications** | **Higher** (hardware commonest) | Lower |
| Return to work/sport | **Slower** | **Faster** |
| **Early function (6 wk, 3 mo)** | Worse | **Better** |
| Function / PROs / QoL at 1 yr | **Equal** | **Equal** |
| Return to athletics at 1 yr | Equal | Equal |
| **Cosmesis** | **Better** (only consistent benefit) | Poorer |
| **Radiographic alignment** | **Better** (hook plate, COTS RCT) | Worse |
| Disability at 2 yr | **Both improve significantly** | **Both improve significantly** |
| ROM / strength / endurance | No difference | No difference |
| Secondary surgery | Higher (hardware removal) | Lower |
| Cost | Higher | Lower |

**Early-score wording** — nonop "higher DASH at 6 wk & 3 mo" (Orthobullets; ↑ DASH = worse) · nonop **better** early scores/function at 6 wk (OB) — read the options

**Spencer systematic review** — surgery **not better**, **higher complication rate**, **longer recovery**

**Canadian (COTS) RCT** — hook plate: **better radiographic alignment**, **not clinically superior**; nonop better early scores

### Surgical techniques

| Technique | Pros | Cons / complications |
|---|---|---|
| **CC screw (Bosworth)** — clavicle → coracoid, sup → inf | Rigid | Out of favour; screw too long → critical structures; **routine removal 8–12 wk** (breakage from clavicle–scapula motion); **failure at coracoid thread purchase** |
| **CC suture fixation** — around/through clavicle + around coracoid base (or anchors) | **No hardware failure/migration** | Weaker than screw; **careful passage under coracoid** (NV structures); **suture erosion → distal clavicle fracture** |
| **AC pin (Phemister)** — wire across AC joint | Percutaneous | **High pin migration** (catastrophic); rarely done |
| **Hook plate** | Rigid | **2nd surgery** for removal; **acromial erosion**, hook pullout |
| **Modified Weaver-Dunn** — **distal clavicle excision + CA ligament transfer** to clavicle | — | **CA ligament only 20% as strong** as CC; failure without internal fixation |
| **Free tendon graft CC reconstruction** — figure-of-8 around coracoid, clavicle tunnels | **Closest to native CC strength** | Graft morbidity; needs internal fixation backup |
| Cortical flip button (Dog Bone) ± arthroscopy | — | — |

**Grafts** — autograft **palmaris longus, semitendinosus** · allograft **tibialis anterior**

![Hook plate fixation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3047-hook-plate.jpg)

**Post-op rehab** — sling **6 wk, no ROM** · full activity at **6 months**

### AC joint cyst (Geyser sign) (TO)

**Entity (TO)** — **synovial cyst of AC joint**; uncommon; elderly; chronic shoulder pain, ↓ ROM, large lump, cosmetic concern

| Type (TO) | Cause | Treatment (TO) |
|---|---|---|
| **Type 1** | **AC joint chronic arthropathy** | **Excision + lateral clavicle resection** |
| **Type 2** (**most common**) | **Chronic rotator cuff tear** | Mild/moderate pain + reparable cuff → **distal clavicle resection + cuff repair** · severe pain + irreparable cuff (elderly) → **shoulder arthroplasty (RSA > hemi)** |

**Mechanism (TO)** — chronic cuff tear → **superior humeral head migration** → erodes **inferior AC capsule** → fluid escapes into AC joint + subcutaneous tissue

**Geyser sign (TO)** — fluid jet through AC joint on **MRI / arthrogram / USG**; MRI reliably shows cyst type

(✗ Wrong options: **Distractors (TO)** — AC exostosis) · SLAP type 4 · biceps sheath GCT

![Geyser sign on MRI](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17119518-2.jpg)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Residual AC pain** | **30–50%** |
| **AC arthritis** | **More common after surgery** than nonop (nonop: no articular contact → no arthritic symptoms; surgery: cartilage injury + incongruity) |
| **Hardware failure** | CC screw breakage/pullout **at coracoid** |
| **Pin migration** | AC pinning (Phemister) |
| **Coracoid fracture** | **Coracoid tunnel drilling** |
| **Distal clavicle fracture** | Suture erosion |
| Acromial erosion | Hook plate |
| Neurological injury | **Musculocutaneous nerve** closest to coracoid (✗ Wrong options: axillary (runs at inferior subscapularis, distant)) |

### Surgical complications (Weaver-Dunn AP translation, AC arthritis)

**Failed modified Weaver-Dunn** — persistent symptoms from **anterior–posterior (horizontal) translation**\
✗ Wrong options: sup–inf translation · inadequate resection · over-reduction · SC instability

**Prevention** — **anatomic** repair/reconstruction **preserving distal clavicle** → less horizontal instability

**CC screw statement traps** — ✗ Wrong options: "more migration than AC pinning" · "fails at clavicle" (coracoid) · "avoid removal" (remove at 8–12 wk) · "axillary nerve"

## X vs Y

| | **Type III** | **Type V** |
|---|---|---|
| CC distance ↑ | **25–100%** | **> 100%** |
| Reducible | Yes | **No** (deltotrapezial fascia) |
| Shrug | Corrects | **Does not correct droop** |
| Treatment | Controversial / mostly nonop | Surgery |

| | **Type IV** | **Type VI** |
|---|---|---|
| Direction | **Posterior** (through trapezius) | **Inferior** (subacromial/subcoracoid) |
| View | **Axillary lateral** (Alexander) | AP |
| Clue | Posterior fullness, skin tenting | Rare, paraesthesias, associated injuries |

| | **Zanca** | **Serendipity** |
|---|---|---|
| Joint | **AC** | **SC** |
| Tilt | **10–15° cephalad** | **40° cephalic** |

## How it's asked

**"Type V on X-ray — ligaments torn?"** → **AC + CC**\
✗ Wrong options: SC · CA

**"CC distance 13 mm contralateral, type III — which labelled ligaments torn?"** → **AC + conoid + trapezoid**\
✗ Wrong options: CA ligament (spared)

**"Type II AC injury?"** → **AC torn, CC sprain, horizontal instability**\
✗ Wrong options: no instability = type I · CC torn = III

**"IIB distal clavicle fracture vs AC separation?"** → **fracture = conoid only; AC separation = conoid + trapezoid**

**"Posterior clavicle displacement after fall over handlebars?"** → **type IV**

**"Type V, irreducible, skin tenting — interposed structure?"** → **deltotrapezial fascia**\
✗ Wrong options: conjoint · CC · CA

**"Type IV, posterior fullness — nerve to muscle involved?"** → **CN XI** (trapezius)

**"Most important for horizontal stability?"** → **posterosuperior AC ligament**

**"Most important for vertical stability?"** → **conoid**

**"Trapezoid inserts how far from lateral clavicle?"** → **3 cm** (conoid 4.5 cm)

**"Best extra view for AC joint?"** → **Zanca**\
✗ Wrong options: Stryker · West Point · outlet · Velpeau · Serendipity = SC

**"Type III — surgery vs nonop?"** → **higher complication rate** with surgery

**"Type III non-athlete — only benefit of fixation?"** → **improved cosmesis**

**"Type III, 19-yo wants surgery — true?"** → **surgery ↑ complications + nonop faster return**\
✗ Wrong options: better function

**"Type III nonop vs op?"** → **nonop better function at 6 wk**; equal at 1 yr

**"Hook plate RCT — true?"** → **both groups improve significantly by 2 yr**\
✗ Wrong options: op faster return · op worse cosmesis · equal alignment

**"Type III nonop, still painful at 6 wk — exam?"** → **O'Brien: resisted flexion at 90° flexion, 10° adduction, full IR**

**"20% ↑ CC distance, symptomatic — treatment?"** → **sling + early PT**

**"58-yo accountant, non-dominant AC separation, no AP translation?"** → **sling + early ROM**

**"Persistent symptoms after modified Weaver-Dunn?"** → **anterior–posterior translation**

**"CC screw fixation — true post-op?"** → **AC arthritis more likely than nonop**

**"Elderly, large shoulder lump, MRI Geyser sign?"** → **AC joint cyst** (TO)

**"Commonest AC cyst type?"** → **type 2 — chronic cuff tear** (TO)

**"Geyser sign, severe pain, irreparable cuff, elderly?"** → **arthroplasty (RSA > hemi)** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Adhesive Capsulitis (Frozen Shoulder)

*Shoulder & Elbow · 4 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Prevalence (TO)** — **2–5%** of population · incidence in **diabetics 10–36%**

**Demographics** — **♀ > ♂**, **40–60 y**; **nondominant** arm most often; **minorities > Caucasians**\
✗ Wrong options: Caucasian ↑ risk

**Bilateral** — **age <50** and **diabetes** → ↑ risk of bilateral disease; **sequential, not simultaneous**

### Risk factors & associations (diabetes, thyroid disease)

**Risk factors (TO)** — **female**, **age >40**, **preceding trauma**, **HLA-B27 positivity**, **prolonged immobilisation** of GH joint

**Associations (TO)** — **diabetes**, **thyroid disease**, **cerebrovascular disease**, **coronary artery disease**, **autoimmune disease**, **Dupuytren's**

**Other associations** — atherosclerotic disease, **cervical disc disease**, **history of MI**, **Crohn's**, **breast cancer treatment / axillary dissection**, previous surgery

**Diabetes (both types)** — **most common association**; stiffness may be **first manifestation of DM** → work up · often **bilateral** · **worse outcomes regardless of treatment** (nonop + surgical)

**Insulin-dependent (TO)** — **more severe** limitation + **more resistant** to nonop than non-insulin diabetics

**Risk ↑ within diabetics** — **older age (>50)** · **duration of DM** · **autonomic neuropathy** · **history of MI**

**Not risk factors in diabetics** — **type of diabetes** (↑ in **both type I and II**) · diabetic regimen (insulin vs non-insulin) · glycaemic control

**Thyroid** — autoimmune aetiology; **hypothyroidism** (↑ TSH) classic

**✗ Distractors** — **menopause** (no association) · **steroid use** (steroids treat it) · epilepsy · renal disease · prior dislocations

### Genes & lab markers

| Test | Finding in AC | ✗ Distractor meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **TSH** | **↑** (hypothyroidism) | ↑ **free T4** = hyperthyroidism (wrong option) |
| **HbA1c** (hemoglobin A1C) | **↑** (diabetes) | — |
| **HLA-B27 (TO)** | Positivity = risk factor | — |
| Uric acid | Normal | ↑ = gout |
| ESR / CRP | Normal | ↑ = infection / inflammatory arthropathy |
| RF / anti-CCP | Normal | ↑ = RA |
| ANA | Normal | + = lupus |

**Workup** — metabolic panel + endocrine labs (**TSH, HbA1c**) before choosing treatment

### Pathophysiology – fibroblastic proliferation & reduced capsular volume

**Forms** — **primary/idiopathic** (most) · **post-traumatic** (proximal humerus fracture, immobilisation) · **post-surgical** (RCR, axillary dissection)

**Pathoanatomy** — inflammation (subsynovial layer, TO) → **fibroblastic proliferation** of capsule → thickening, fibrosis, adherence of capsule to itself + humerus → **mechanical block** · **↓ intracapsular volume**

**Cell type** — **fibroblasts / myofibroblasts**\
✗ Wrong options: adipocytes · granulocytes · lymphocytes · monocytes

**Collagen** — abundant **type III collagen**

**Essential lesion** — **coracohumeral ligament (CHL)** + **rotator interval capsule**

**Why ER lost first (TO)** — changes start in **subsynovial layer of capsule in rotator interval**

**✗ Wrong options (other processes)** — isolated posterior capsular tightness = **GIRD** · ↓ blood supply = **AVN** · degenerative tendon tear = **cuff tear** (active loss only)

### Rotator interval anatomy

| Boundary | Structure |
|---|---|
| **Superior** | **Anterior edge of supraspinatus** |
| **Inferior** | **Superior edge of subscapularis** |
| Medial (base) | **Coracoid process** |
| Lateral (apex) | **Transverse humeral ligament** |
| **Contents** | **LHB tendon, SGHL, CHL, RI capsule** |

**✗ RI traps** — LHB = content · AIGHL + **axillary pouch** lie **inferior** to RI · MGHL is not the inferior border (subscapularis is)\
✗ Wrong options: superior border

**RI clinical** — **closed** for instability · **contracted** in AC → **released**

![Rotator interval borders and contents](../../MCQ/attachments/q2556-illa-rotatorinterval.jpg)

### Capsuloligamentous restraints

| Structure | Restraint | Release effect |
|---|---|---|
| **RI (SGHL + CHL + capsule)** | **Inferior translation + ER in adduction** | ↑ **ER at side** + inferior translation |
| **SGHL** | **Inferior translation at 0° abduction** | — |
| **MGHL** | **Anterior (± posterior) translation at ~45° abduction** + ER | ↑ anterior translation at 45° |
| **AB-IGHL** | **Anterior + inferior translation at 90° abduction + max ER** | ↑ anteroinferior translation at 90° |
| **Posterior capsule** | IR + **cross-body adduction** | ↑ **IR + cross-body adduction** |

**Normal role** — check reins at extremes of motion; IGHL complex = anterior band + axillary fold + posterior band

![Capsular structures from glenoid side](../../MCQ/attachments/q210158-illa-labeled.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

### Clinical diagnosis – loss of active and passive motion

**Symptoms** — **insidious** diffuse pain **preceding** loss of motion · **night pain / difficulty sleeping** · ADL difficulty (reaching overhead)

**Exam** — **symmetric loss of both active AND passive ROM** · **tethered endpoint** · pain throughout arc or at terminal motion (stage-dependent)

**First movement lost (TO)** — **external rotation**\
✗ Wrong options: IR · flexion · abduction

**Most common + most sensitive** — **passive ER deficit vs contralateral** shoulder

**Provocative tests** — impingement, biceps, SLAP often **positive** (false); cuff testing limited by stiffness

**Diagnosis** — **clinical** (normal X-ray + painful active + passive loss); MRI not necessary

| ✗ Wrong option: exam pattern | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| Active < passive (e.g. active FF 100°, passive 150°) | **Weakness** — cuff tear |
| Pain 0–30° active abduction, full passive | Supraspinatus tendinosis/tear |
| Empty can + pain/weakness, full passive | Rotator cuff tear |
| Speed's +, full ROM | Biceps tendinopathy |
| Isolated IR loss (posterior capsule) | GIRD |

### Clinical stages

| Stage | Features | Duration |
|---|---|---|
| **1 Freezing / painful** | Gradual diffuse pain at rest + motion, night pain | **6 wk – 9 mo** |
| **2 Frozen / stiff** | Pain lessens; ↓ ROM affecting ADLs | **4 – 9 mo or more** |
| **3 Thawing / resolution** | Pain gone; gradual return of motion (less than normal) | **5 – 26 mo** |

**Total duration** — self-limited; average **3–6 mo**, up to **18 mo – 2 y** (OB) — read the options

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray views** — **AP in neutral rotation**, **scapular-Y**, **axillary lateral** (± AP in IR/ER) · **mandatory** to exclude **OA, posterior dislocation, hardware**

**X-ray finding** — normal or **disuse osteopenia**; no high-riding head

**MRI (± arthrography)** — not needed for diagnosis; excludes other pathology · **loss of axillary recess** (capsular contracture) · thickened CHL

**MR arthrogram** — **↓ intracapsular volume**

| MRI sign of AC (TO) | Value |
|---|---|
| Axillary recess thickening | **≥1.3 cm** (TO) |
| **Anterior capsule thickening** | **>3.5 mm** + hyperintensity |
| Synovial thickness | **>4 mm** |
| RI | Soft tissue thickening + signal change; scarring |
| Biceps anchor | Encased by abnormal soft tissue |
| **CHL** | **Thickened** |
| **Subcoracoid triangle sign** | **Obliteration of fat** in subcoracoid space |
| Inferior capsular fold | **Obliterated**; capsule hypervascular |

**NOT seen (TO)** — **widening** of axillary pouch (pouch is contracted/obliterated)

**CHL on MRI** — thickened (Orthobullets) · **not better visualised** than normal (OB wrong option) — read the options

**Incidental partial cuff tear** — common in **asymptomatic >60 y** → still treat AC with stretching

![Coronal MRI, loss of axillary recess](../../MCQ/attachments/q4266-illa-coronalmrit2frozen.jpg)

| ✗ MRI distractor | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| Fluid extravasation down humerus | **HAGL** |
| Hypertrophied synovium, papillary projections | **PVNS** |
| Massive retracted tear | Cuff tear |

### Arthroscopic stages

| Stage | Finding |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Patchy, fibrinous synovitis** |
| 2 | **Capsular contraction + fibrinous adhesions** |
| 3 | Increasing contraction, **synovitis resolving** |
| 4 | **Severe contraction** |

## 5 Management

```
Adhesive capsulitis
├─ Early (freezing) → reassurance + gentle stretching ± IA steroid + NSAIDs
│      (treat diabetes / thyroid)
├─ Failed nonop (3–6 mo) → MUA and/or arthroscopic capsular release
│      └─ diabetic / post cuff or labral repair → release (MUA 50% fail)
└─ Residual IR / cross-body loss → add posterior capsular release
```

### Nonoperative management – stretching, injection & natural history

**First-line** — **PT + NSAIDs ± IA steroid injection** ± heat/cryotherapy; often effective, **majority** succeed

**PT program** — **gentle, pain-free stretching + moist heat**; supervised; **3–6 mo**; stress **motion, not strengthening**

**Home exercise program** — **as helpful as organised therapy**

**IA steroid** — ↓ inflammation → allows stretching; **earlier** return of ROM, **no long-term difference** (OB, TO) · stage II → **IA steroid + stretching**

**PT detail (TO)** — stretching then strengthening = mainstay **regardless of stage**; **avoid abduction initially** (impingement)

(✗ Wrong options: **Wrong early** — **sling** (↑ contracture, prolonged recovery)) · **immediate aggressive therapy** (counterproductive unless pain controlled) · **PRP** (no evidence) · MUA / arthroscopy before conservative trial · cuff strengthening

**Distension arthrography** — rarely performed

**Natural history** — **self-limited**; most resolve without surgery

**Most common outcome after nonop stretching** — **decreased range of motion vs contralateral shoulder**\
✗ Wrong options: limiting pain · recurrence (low) · surgery (rare, **>90% satisfied**)

### Stretching exercise mechanics

**ER wand exercise (arm adducted, elbow flexed)** — stretches **anterior-superior capsule, SGHL, CHL, IGHL** · **LEAST stretch on posterior capsule**

**Most commonly contracted** — **anterior-superior capsule (RI)** → limits **ER in adduction**

### Operative criteria

| Factor | Threshold / rule |
|---|---|
| Failed therapy before release | **3 mo** (Orthobullets) · **3–6 mo** (OB) · **≥6 mo** (OB) · **4–6 mo** compliant therapy (TO) — read the options |
| Indication | **Progressive loss of motion** despite prolonged PT |
| MUA contraindication | **Diabetics (50% failure)** · **after RCR or labral repair** · freezing phase (controversial) |
| Arthroscopy (TO) | Reserve for **recalcitrant** cases (✗ Wrong options: RCT superiority over MUA) |

### Manipulation under anaesthesia

**Technique** — **document ROM** first · **full muscle paralysis** · indwelling regional catheter · **steady force** · **early PT**

**FEAR sequence (TO)** — **F**lexion, **E**xtension, **A**bduction, adduction, external + internal **R**otation

**Post-MUA (TO)** — **20 ml 0.5% bupivacaine + corticosteroid** IA

**Results (TO)** — **up to 94%** subjective relief, sustained long term

### Arthroscopic capsular release – structures & outcomes

**Portals** — slightly **higher** than normal (contracted capsule)

**Rotator interval release** — from **anterior biceps to superior subscapularis** → CHL visualised + released → restores **ER at side**

**Released** — **SGHL, MGHL, IGHL, CHL** (circumferential) · **not coracoacromial ligament** (= landmark for complete RI release)

**Posterior capsular release** — ↑ **IR + cross-body adduction**

**Subacromial** — **bursectomy + adhesion release**; **no acromioplasty**

**Sequence** — MUA before or after release; TO: release before manipulation allows inspection + precise capsulotomy; **anterior release always ± posterior** (posterior: early benefit, similar outcome)

**Advantage over open** — **spares subscapularis**; releases intra-articular + subacromial adhesions

**Not done** — completion + repair of small partial tear (that is for **>6 mm or 50%**) · **capsular plication** (instability) · **distal clavicle resection** (AC arthritis)

**Outcome** — gains in ROM + function **maintained at long-term follow-up**

![Arthroscopic anterior capsular release](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3059-anterior-release.jpg)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Detail / prevention |
|---|---|
| **Residual / recurrent stiffness** | **Most common** after release → **immediate ROM + PT**, **no sling**, ± CPM, regional analgesia |
| **Axillary nerve injury** | **Inferior capsular release** → stay **close to glenoid rim**; rare |
| Infection, instability, impingement | Less common |
| **MUA (overzealous, osteoporotic bone)** | **Proximal humerus fracture**, dislocation, **cuff + labral tears**, **brachial plexus palsy** |

**Prognosis** — self-limited · **worse in diabetics**

## X vs Y

| | **Adhesive capsulitis** | **Cuff tear** | **GIRD** | **GH OA / posterior dislocation** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Active ROM | ↓ | ↓ | ↓ IR | ↓ |
| Passive ROM | **↓ (= active)** | **Normal** | ↓ IR only | ↓ |
| First / key loss | **ER** | Strength (weakness) | **IR** (posterior capsule) | — |
| X-ray | Normal / osteopenia | ± high-riding head | Normal | **Diagnostic** (why X-ray is mandatory) |

| ✗ Fibroblastic distractor | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| >50 y, giant cell arteritis, bilateral shoulder stiffness | **PMR** |
| Podagra, acute shoulder pain | **Gout** (crystal) |
| Ulnar drift | **RA** |
| +ANA | **Lupus** |

## How it's asked

**"Known risk factor for adhesive capsulitis?"** → **diabetes mellitus**\
✗ Wrong options: menopause · steroid use · renal disease · epilepsy

**"Risk factor in diabetics EXCEPT"** → **type of diabetes**\
✗ Wrong options: age >50, duration, MI, autonomic neuropathy

**"55 F, stiff shoulder, no PMH — lab most likely abnormal?"** → **↑ TSH**\
✗ Wrong options: uric acid, ESR, anti-CCP, dislocations

**"Basic blood work — which elevated?"** → **HbA1c**\
✗ Wrong options: free T4, RF, CRP, lactic acid

**"True statement about AC?"** → **age <50 + diabetes → bilateral disease**\
✗ Wrong options: Caucasian · CHL better seen · release usually needed · flexion lost first

**"NOT seen in adhesive capsulitis (MRI)?"** → **widening and thickening of axillary pouch** (TO)

**"MR arthrogram in diabetic stiff shoulder?"** → **↓ intracapsular volume**\
✗ Wrong options: PVNS · HAGL extravasation

**"Fibroblastic proliferative process — which patient?"** → **50 F hypothyroid, loss of active + passive motion**\
✗ Wrong options: PMR, gout, RA, lupus

**"Pathophysiology + other finding?"** → **fibroblastic proliferation of capsule; HbA1c 11.7%**\
✗ Wrong options: posterior capsule = GIRD

**"Cell line responsible?"** → **fibroblasts**

**"First movement hampered?"** → **ER** (TO)

**"Uncontrolled diabetic, 3 mo shoulder pain — exam?"** → **symmetric loss of active + passive ROM**

**"Most common outcome after stretching program?"** → **↓ ROM vs contralateral**

**"Early stiffening stage, 1 mo — treatment?"** → **reassurance + gentle stretching**\
✗ Wrong options: aggressive PT, PRP, MUA, arthroscopy

**"Diabetic, 10 wk, greatest ER loss — treatment?"** → **PT + medical management**\
✗ Wrong options: cuff repair · posterior release · RSA

**"Recent-onset AC, 2 wk — next step?"** → **home exercise program**\
✗ Wrong options: sling

**"4 mo diffuse pain, stage II — management?"** → **IA corticosteroid + stretching**\
✗ Wrong options: sling · aggressive stretching

**"Global stiffness + MRI partial supraspinatus tear, >60 y?"** → **stretching regimen**\
✗ Wrong options: strengthening · acromioplasty / repair

**"Mitigate most common complication of arthroscopic release?"** → **immediate ROM + PT** (stiffness)

**"Release increases inferior translation + ER in adduction?"** → **rotator interval (A)**\
✗ Wrong options: MGHL · AIGHL

**"Release improves cross-body adduction?"** → **posterior capsule (H)**

**"Failed nonop, 2 mm articular tear — surgery includes?"** → **rotator interval release**\
✗ Wrong options: tear completion, plication, SAD, DCR

**"NOT released in AC arthroscopic release?"** → **coracoacromial ligament**

**"Expectation counselling before release?"** → **improvements maintained long term**\
✗ Wrong options: axillary nerve most common · posterior capsule most common stiffness · stiffness rare

**"Arthroscopic release structure (figure)?"** → **rotator interval (D)**\
✗ Wrong options: subscapularis, supraspinatus, biceps, infraspinatus

**"Rotator interval superior border?"** → **anterior edge of supraspinatus**\
✗ Wrong options: LHB · AIGHL · MGHL · axillary pouch

**"ER wand exercise — LEAST stretch?"** → **posterior capsule**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Avascular Necrosis of the Shoulder

*Shoulder & Elbow · 5 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Pathogenesis & diagnosis (corticosteroid risk)

**Pathogenesis** — interrupted blood supply → **cellular death of subchondral bone** → resorption + remodelling → **subchondral collapse** → incongruity → arthritis

**✗ Pathogenesis distractors** — cell-mediated synovial hypertrophy = **RA** · humoral response after infection, HLA-B27 = **Reiter's** · hyperuricaemic crystals = **gout** · bacterial PMN cartilage destruction = **septic arthritis**

**Most common cause** — **corticosteroids** (asthma, Crohn's flares, SLE, leukaemia chemotherapy, graft-versus-host disease); also **sickle cell**, chronic **alcohol**

**Young patients** — high-dose steroids in chemotherapy regimens → ↑ demand for arthroplasty in the young

**vs hip** — humeral head AVN **less common than hip**; same pathophysiology (genetic predisposition + risk factors such as steroids); treatment similar to hip, based on **subchondral collapse**

**Workup** — proximal humerus AVN on X-ray → also get **hip radiographs**

| ASEPTIC mnemonic | Cause |
|---|---|
| **A** | Alcohol, AIDS |
| **S** | **Steroids (most common)**, Sickle cell, SLE |
| **E** | Erlenmeyer flask (**Gaucher**) |
| **P** | Pancreatitis |
| **T** | Trauma |
| **I** | Idiopathic / Infection |
| **C** | Caisson's (the bends) |

**Gaucher (wrong option)** — **large bony infarcts** + severe pain; less common cause than chronic steroids

### Post-traumatic AVN

| Proximal humerus injury | AVN rate |
|---|---|
| **4-part fracture-dislocation** | **~100%** |
| Displaced 4-part | ~45% |
| 3-part | ~14% |
| **Valgus-impacted 4-part** | **~11%** |

### Anatomy – humeral head blood supply

**Anterior humeral circumflex** — **ascending branch** runs **lateral to LHB tendon in bicipital groove** → **arcuate artery** = intraosseous continuation, penetrates head · **35%**\
✗ Wrong options: injure when plating

**Posterior humeral circumflex** — **main supply** per most current literature · **65%**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **insidious** shoulder pain, often no inciting event · ↓ motion · crepitus · weakness

**Exam** — limited ROM (pain throughout arc) · crepitus · cuff + deltoid weakness (cuff strength may be normal)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — 5 views; best seen on **neutral rotation AP** · **normal at onset** · osteolytic lesion first at **superior-middle humeral head** (most common initial site)

**Stage signs (X-ray)** — **"snowcap" sign** = sclerosis, contour preserved (II) · **crescent sign** = subchondral fracture (III) · flattening/collapse, loss of sphericity (IV) · glenoid changes (V)

**MRI** — **preferred** modality · **~100% sensitive** · early = **↓ subchondral signal on T1 and T2** (oedema) · variegated pattern · metaphyseal oedema common/normal

![Cruess stage II: snowcap sclerosis with preserved contour (X-ray, T1, T2 fat-sat)](../../MCQ/attachments/q4809-a-fig-a-2nd-revision.jpg)

### Cruess classification

| Stage | Radiographic findings | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Normal X-ray, MRI changes only** | Core decompression |
| **II** | **Sclerosis** (wedged, mottled), osteopenia; snowcap | Core decompression |
| **III** | **Crescent sign** = subchondral fracture | Resurfacing or hemiarthroplasty |
| **IV** | **Flattening and collapse** (glenoid spared) | Hemiarthroplasty (or resurfacing) |
| **V** | Degenerative changes **extend to glenoid** | **TSA** |

![Cruess stages I–V](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3060-cruess-classification.jpg)

**Ficat-Arlet** — hip staging adapted to the shoulder (used in core decompression series)

## 5 Management

```
Humeral head AVN
├─ First line → analgesia, activity modification (no overhead/manual labour), PT
├─ Precollapse (Cruess I–II) → core decompression ± arthroscopy
├─ Collapse, glenoid + cuff intact (III–IV) → hemiarthroplasty
│      └─ focal III defect + enough epiphyseal bone → resurfacing
├─ Glenoid involved (V) → TSA
└─ Older, cuff pathology → reverse TSA
```

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication | Not when |
|---|---|---|
| **Core decompression** (+ arthroscopy to confirm cartilage) | **Precollapse Cruess I–II** | Subchondral fracture/flattening (post-collapse = **contraindicated**) |
| **Resurfacing hemiarthroplasty** | **Stage III focal chondral defect + sufficient epiphyseal bone**; least bone removal | Large defect · global head involvement |
| **Hemiarthroplasty** | **III–IV**: collapse, **glenoid intact, cuff intact**; young → preserves glenoid bone stock for later TSA | **Irreparable cuff tear** · glenoid changes |
| **TSA** | **Stage V** (glenoid involved) | Young + intact glenoid (glenoid loosening) |
| **Reverse TSA** | Older + cuff pathology | **Intact cuff**, young |

### Treatment by stage – core decompression vs resurfacing/hemiarthroplasty

**Core decompression** — ↓ subchondral marrow pressure → **neovascularisation** → slows progression · ± bone graft into void · percutaneous small-diameter technique (Harreld: no arthroplasty at 32 mo, 95% improved)

| Core decompression (LaPorte, ~10 y; Ficat stage) | Success |
|---|---|
| I | **94%** |
| II | **88%** |
| III | 70% |
| IV | 14% |

**Stage I–II outcome** — good/excellent **88–100%** at 5–10 y → **~90% successful long term** · better in **non-steroid** than steroid cases\
✗ Wrong options: 50% satisfied · most need arthroplasty by 10 y · unpredictable · not indicated

**Conflict on core decompression** — effective for I–II (Mont, LaPorte; LaPorte recommends I–III; Harreld tries it with arthroscopy in III) · role in precollapse "**highly debated**" and drilling did **not** alter progression (L'insalata) · Sarris: I–II **conservative**, surgery when persistently symptomatic + collapse — read the options

**Arthroscopic debridement / release** — no sustained benefit; AVN keeps progressing; role unknown

**Most reliable, durable treatment after collapse** — **prosthetic arthroplasty** → **hemiarthroplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopic debridement · arthrodesis · resection arthroplasty · cortisone injection

**Arthrodesis indications** — brachial plexus injury · infantile paralysis · deltoid + cuff insufficiency · chronic infection · failed revision arthroplasty · refractory instability · tumour resection bone loss

**Stemless resurfacing** — preserves metaphyseal bone · Levy & Copeland 285 cementless · Hawi (Eclipse) 9 y, no loosening

**"Ream and run"** — HA + glenoid reaming → unnecessary with intact glenoid cartilage

**Arthroplasty outcomes** — HA + TSA both safe/effective · 10-y survivorship **HA 88.9%, TSA 80%**; **TSA better internal rotation** (Wang) · RSA ≈ TSA when cuff compromised · AVN arthroplasty has **↑ complications** vs other diagnoses (bone quality, comorbidity)

**Prognosis** — related to **stage**; stage + lesion size predict progression

## 6 Complications

**Hemiarthroplasty** — **glenoid cartilage wear** over time → 45% unsatisfactory (Smith, steroid AVN); still used in young active III–IV

**Glenoid component in young** — **loosening** risk → avoid TSA/RTSA when glenoid spared

## X vs Y

| | **AVN** | **Calcific tendinitis** | **OA** | **Gaucher** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Onset | Insidious, steroid user | **Severe acute pain** | Long-standing symptoms | Severe pain |
| Imaging | **Subchondral** ↓ T1/T2 signal, collapse, fragmentation | **Hypointense deposit in cuff soft tissue** | No subchondral collapse/fragmentation | **Large bony infarcts** |

| Stage | **Core decompression** | **Hemiarthroplasty** | **TSA** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cruess | I–II (precollapse) | III–IV (collapse, glenoid spared) | V (glenoid) |
| Cuff | — | Must be intact | Intact |
| Key trap | Not after collapse | Not with irreparable cuff or glenoid cysts | Not in young with intact glenoid |

## How it's asked

**"Asthmatic, 20 y steroids, humeral head collapse — pathogenesis?"** → **cellular death of subchondral bone after vascular interruption**\
✗ Wrong options: RA · Reiter's · gout · septic

**"Crohn's on prednisone, atraumatic pain, MRI subchondral lesion — diagnosis?"** → **osteonecrosis**\
✗ Wrong options: Gaucher · OA · cuff tendinopathy · calcific tendinitis

**"Steroid user, snowcap sclerosis, contour preserved — surgery?"** → **core decompression**\
✗ Wrong options: HA · RTSA with intact cuff · resurfacing · TSA

**"Leukaemia + high-dose cortisone, collapse — most reliable pain relief?"** → **hemiarthroplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: debridement · arthrodesis · resection · injection

**"39F, collapse + loss of sphericity, glenoid spared, failed PT — procedure?"** → **resurfacing hemiarthroplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopic release · core decompression post-collapse · TSA/RTSA glenoid loosening

**"GVHD steroid AVN — best case for hemiarthroplasty?"** → **subchondral fracture + intact cuff**\
✗ Wrong options: sclerosis = decompression · irreparable cuff · glenoid cysts = TSA

**"SLE patient had HA — preop imaging?"** → **isolated humeral head collapse** (Cruess IV)\
✗ Wrong options: glenoid changes = V · osteopenia / MRI-only / mottled sclerosis = I–II

**"Leukaemia, flattening + collapse, glenoid intact, cuff normal?"** → **hemiarthroplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: TSA · core decompression · ream and run · RTSA

**"Alcoholic, Cruess II — core decompression outcome?"** → **~90% successful long term**

**"Humeral head AVN on X-ray — what else to image?"** → **hips**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Biceps Subluxation

*Shoulder & Elbow · 6 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Association with subscapularis tears

**Definition** — LHBT subluxes/dislocates **medially** out of the bicipital groove → recognised cause of **anterior shoulder pain**

**Most common associated pathology** — **subscapularis tear** (even **partial**); biceps subluxation is **almost always** associated with a subscapularis tear

**Most important medial restraint** — **subscapularis tendon** = most important restraint to medial subluxation/dislocation of LHBT

**Walch series** — dislocated LHBT associated with partial/complete **subscapularis tear in 96%**

**Other cause** — **disruption of the biceps sling (pulley)**

**Not the cause** — **subacromial impingement** does not cause LHBT instability · isolated degenerative tendinosis (instability is from the subscapularis tear) · SLAP

### Biceps pulley stabilisers (coracohumeral & transverse humeral ligaments)

**Origin** — LHBT arises from **supraglenoid tubercle + superior labrum** → intra-articular → bicipital groove

**Function** — **dynamic stabiliser**; shoulder flexion, abduction

| Biceps sling / pulley (intra-articular) | Rotator interval tissue? |
|---|---|
| **Coracohumeral ligament (CHL)** | **Yes** |
| **Superior glenohumeral ligament (SGHL)** | **Yes** |
| **Subscapularis** fibres | Contributes to sling |
| **Supraspinatus** (anterior fibres) | Contributes to sling |

![Biceps sling: CHL, SGHL, subscapularis, supraspinatus over LHBT](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3046-screen-shot-2019-11-24-at-9-07-02-pm.jpg)

**Transverse humeral ligament (THL)** — stabilises LHBT in the groove **at the level of the greater + lesser tuberosities** (distal to pulley); **not rotator interval tissue**

**Bicipital groove** — length 81–87 mm · width 7.7–11.6 mm · depth 3.7–6 mm · **bottleneck ~2/3 down** from top → THL may constrain groove → anterior pain; tenodesis **below the groove** may be preferred

| ✗ Distractor | Why wrong |
|---|---|
| **Coracoacromial ligament (CAL)** | Not rotator interval; no LHBT stability at tuberosities |
| **IGHL** | Not rotator interval; not an LHBT stabiliser |
| **AIGHL** | Not associated with biceps subluxation (injured in anterior dislocation) |
| Superior labrum / infraspinatus / posterior labrum | Not associated with **medial** biceps subluxation |

**Imaging finding = injured structure** — MRI medial biceps subluxation → **CHL disruption** (sling)

**Anterosuperior cuff tear** — sling disrupted → LHBT subluxes medially → **signals subscapularis tear** (often missed on MRI)

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **anterior** shoulder pain ± **clicking**

**Yergason test** — anterior pain with **resisted supination**, arm at side, elbow flexed 90°

**Speed test** — anterior pain with **resisted forward flexion**, shoulder 90° flexed, elbow extended, palm up

**Palpable click** — with **abduction + external rotation** as tendon subluxes/dislocates out of groove

**Always test subscapularis strength** — **lift-off** (Gerber), belly press, push-off

**Lift-off test (Gerber)** — back of hand held off mid-lumbar spine in IR; hand falls or patient extends elbow/shoulder to compensate → **subscapularis tear**

| ✗ Exam distractor | Tests |
|---|---|
| Drop arm | **Supraspinatus** |
| External rotation lag | **Infraspinatus** |
| Hornblower's | **Teres minor** |
| Kim test | **Posteroinferior labral tear** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**MRI** — ↑ **T2 signal** + LHBT **displaced out of bicipital groove** (axial: on lesser tuberosity → fully medial); coincides with **subscapularis tear**

**MRI limitation** — misses subscapularis tears unless tear extends **≥½ cranio-caudal** length → small tears missed → test lift-off

**LHBT subluxation on MRI as predictor of full-thickness subscapularis tear (Shi)** — sensitivity **82%** · specificity **80%** · PPV **35%** · **NPV 97%** → value lies in **NPV** (no subluxation → full-thickness subscapularis tear unlikely); correlates with tear severity

**Ultrasound** — **dynamic test** of biceps instability

**Arthroscopy** — ↓ **biceps–glenoid angle** associated with subluxation (**87°** vs **90°**; Koh) → statistically significant but **not clinically relevant**

![Axial MRI: LHBT dislocated medial to empty groove](../../MCQ/attachments/q218102-illa-screenshot-2023-07-22-at-5-30-29-pm.jpg)

![LHBT position relative to subscapularis and tuberosities](../../MCQ/attachments/q211117-illa-screen-shot-2019-11-26-at-4-40-13-am.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Biceps subluxation
├─ Initial → NSAIDs + PT + steroid injection
│      (injection near, not into, the tendon)
└─ Refractory → arthroscopic/open tenotomy or tenodesis
       └─ subscapularis tear → + subscapularis repair
```

### Nonoperative

**First line** — **NSAIDs, PT strengthening, activity modification, steroid injection** (adjacent to tendon, **not into it**)

### Operative

**Indication** — **refractory** symptoms

**Options** — arthroscopic vs open **biceps tenotomy vs tenodesis** ± **subscapularis repair**; instability can be tested intra-op

**Acute traumatic medial LHBT instability (Maier)** — open subscapularis repair + LHBT stabilisation = equal function to tenotomy/tenodesis, **better cosmesis, less cramping**

**THL release** — routine release at tenotomy/tenodesis may ↓ post-op anterior pain

### Biceps tenotomy for irreparable cuff tear

**Indication** — large/massive **irreparable** cuff tear, **pain** main complaint, **good active elevation** (e.g. 120°), **normal articular surfaces** → **biceps tenotomy**

| Alternative | When |
|---|---|
| **Latissimus dorsi transfer** | Severe **ER deficit** + deficient **teres minor** |
| **Reverse TSA** | **Cuff tear arthropathy + pseudoparalysis** |
| ✗ Wrong option: SLAP repair | Contraindicated in this age group |

## X vs Y

| | **Biceps sling (pulley)** | **Transverse humeral ligament** |
|---|---|---|
| Level | Intra-articular, rotator interval | Bicipital groove at **tuberosities** |
| Components | CHL, SGHL + subscapularis, supraspinatus | Single ligament, superficial roof of groove |
| Rotator interval tissue | **CHL + SGHL = yes** | **No** |

| | **Tenotomy for massive cuff tear** | **Latissimus transfer** | **Reverse TSA** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Key feature | Pain, elevation preserved, no arthritis | ER deficit, teres minor deficient | Arthropathy + pseudoparalysis |

## How it's asked

**"62F, anterior pain, + Yergason/Speed, MRI LHBT on lesser tuberosity then dislocated — true statement?"** → **biceps pathology due to partial subscapularis tear**\
✗ Wrong options: SLAP repair · isolated tendinosis · impingement

**"MR arthrogram, biceps out of groove — arthroscopy will show?"** → **subscapularis tear + biceps subluxation**\
✗ Wrong options: THL disruption · absent CHL · short head rupture

**"Skier, MRI dislocated biceps tendon — most important exam?"** → **lift-off test**\
✗ Wrong options: drop arm · ER lag · Hornblower · Kim

**"Post-dislocation, MRI medial biceps subluxation — injured structure?"** → **coracohumeral ligament**\
✗ Wrong options: AIGHL · superior labrum · infraspinatus · posterior labrum

**"Stabilises LHBT at tuberosities, not rotator interval?"** → **transverse humeral ligament**\
✗ Wrong options: CHL · SGHL = interval · CAL · IGHL

**"Main diagnostic value of LHBT subluxation on MRI?"** → **negative predictive value (97%)**

**"Massive irreparable cuff tear, FE 120°, normal joint, MRI biceps subluxed?"** → **biceps tenotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: latissimus transfer · reverse TSA

**"Most important medial restraint of LHBT?"** → **subscapularis tendon**
