# Biceps Tendonitis

*Shoulder & Elbow · 7 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Pathoanatomy

**Nature** — **tendinosis** (degenerative) > true inflammation; inflammatory, traumatic or degenerative causes

**Associations** — usually **secondary** to other shoulder pathology: **subacromial impingement**, **bicipital groove stenosis**, **rotator cuff tears — esp. subscapularis**

**Hourglass biceps** — chronic inflammation → **hypertrophied intra-articular tendon** → **incarcerated in joint** during motion, esp. **elevation**

![Hourglass biceps incarcerated in the joint](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3045-hourglassbiceps.jpg)

### Long head of biceps anatomy – labral attachment, rotator interval & pulley

**Origin (TO)** — **supraglenoid tubercle + superior labrum**; most commonly **50% labrum / 50% tubercle**

**Insertion** — with short head → **radial tuberosity** · action: **elbow flexion + forearm supination** · **no shoulder ROM/strength contribution**

**Synovial status (TO)** — **intra-articular but extrasynovial**; synovial sheath communicates with GH joint → extends into groove

### Tuoheti classification – LHB attachment on labrum (TO)

| Type | Attachment | Frequency |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Completely posterior | — |
| **2** | **Predominantly posterior** | **Most common** |
| 3 | Anterior + posterior | — |
| 4 | Anterior | **Least common** |

### Rotator interval (TO)

| Feature | Structure |
|---|---|
| **Superior border** | **Supraspinatus** |
| **Inferior border** | **Subscapularis** |
| **Medial edge (base)** | **Coracoid process** |
| Ceiling | Joint capsule |
| Floor | Articular surface of humeral head |
| Reinforced | **CHL laterally**, **SGHL medially** |
| Contents | **LHB** (on the base, covered by capsule), CHL, SGHL, RI capsule |

**✗ Boundary trap (TO)** — **CHL is NOT a boundary** (content/reinforcement)

**Shape (TO)** — triangular **space** (3-D), not 2-D area

**Jost 4 layers (TO)** — 1 superficial CHL fibres (coracoid → greater + lesser tuberosities) · 2 CHL blended with cuff tendons · 3 deep CHL fibres · 4 **SGHL + capsule** mesh

**CHL (TO)** — arises **outside joint**, lateral base of **coracoid** → inserts **greater + lesser tuberosities** → **spans bicipital groove** · superficial layer covers articular surface of supra/infraspinatus · **deep layer thicker** → greater tuberosity

### Biceps pulley & stabilisers (TO)

**Biceps pulley (TO)** — **SGHL + CHL** fuse along lateral half of RI with **subscapularis + supraspinatus** fibres → cradle intra-articular LHB

| LHB portion | Stabilisers (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Intra-articular** | **Labrum + capsuloligamentous structures**, **CHL**, **SGHL**, **supraspinatus**, **subscapularis** |
| **Extra-articular** | **Transverse humeral ligament**, **pectoralis major** muscle/tendon, **bicipital groove** |

**✗ Stabiliser traps (TO)** — **transverse humeral ligament = extra-articular** (✗ Wrong options: intra-articular) · CHL/SGHL = **intra-articular** stabilisers (✗ Wrong options: extra-articular)

### Rotator cable vs crescent (TO)

| | **Rotator cable** | **Rotator crescent** |
|---|---|---|
| Structure | **Thick bundle** of fibres | **Thin, crescent-shaped sheet** of cuff |
| Location | Avascular zone of CHL; **perpendicular to supraspinatus fibres** | **Distal** supraspinatus + infraspinatus insertions |
| Relationship | Proximal margin of crescent | Bounded proximally by cable |

**"Suspension bridge" (TO)** — cable + crescent span supraspinatus + infraspinatus insertions · swapping the two definitions = classic trap

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **anterior shoulder pain** ± radiating down biceps; mimics cuff/impingement pain

**Groove tenderness** — over **bicipital groove**; worse with arm **internally rotated 10°**

| Test | Manoeuvre | Positive |
|---|---|---|
| **Speed** | Forward elevate against resistance, **elbow extended + forearm supinated** | Pain in bicipital groove; **also + in SLAP** |
| **Yergason** | Active **supination against resistance**, **elbow 90°**, forearm **pronated** | Pain in bicipital groove |
| Popeye | Distal bulge of biceps muscle | **Rupture** |

![Popeye deformity](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3045-popeye.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**US** — **thickened tendon** within bicipital groove

**MRI** — evaluates **concurrent pathology** (subscapularis, cuff) · **thickening + tenosynovitis** of proximal LHB · **↑ T2 signal around tendon** · longitudinal split tears

![Axial MRI, fluid around LHB in groove](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3045-8-1-2012-8-08-59-pm.jpg)

**Diagnostic US-guided injection** — groove steroid → temporary relief supports LHB as pain generator (e.g. 90% relief × 4 wk)

## 5 Management

```
Biceps tendonitis
├─ First line → NSAIDs + activity modification + PT + steroid injection
│      (injection near, not into, tendon)
└─ Refractory to nonop → arthroscopic / open tenotomy or tenodesis
       ├─ faster, simpler, less rehab; ↑ Popeye → tenotomy
       └─ natural contour; drilling / retraction nerve risk → tenodesis (supra- or subpectoral)
```

### Nonoperative

**Regimen** — NSAIDs, **PT strengthening**, activity modification, **steroid injection** (in proximity, **not into tendon**)

### Tenodesis vs tenotomy

**Only proven difference** — **Popeye (cosmetic) deformity ↑ with tenotomy**

**No difference** — **strength**, **pain (VAS)**, **ASES / patient-reported outcomes**, **shoulder + elbow ROM**, **supination** (level 1 evidence)

**Cramping** — tenodesis **possibly ↓ arm cramping** (surgeon belief) · **not shown in high-level studies** — read the options

**Tenotomy pros** — faster, simpler, **less rehab**, **faster return** to activity

**Tenodesis pros** — natural muscle **contour**; but extra risks: nerve injury (**bicortical drilling**, **musculocutaneous retraction**)

**Bite study (OB)** — tenotomy vs suprapectoral tenodesis with cuff repair: differences small; non-inferiority **statistically inconclusive** on Constant-Murley at 1 y

### Tenodesis technique – suprapectoral vs subpectoral, arthroscopic vs open

| | **Arthroscopic suprapectoral** | **Open subpectoral** |
|---|---|---|
| Fixation site | **Proximal** in groove | **Distal** groove, at **pectoralis major insertion** |
| Rationale | — | Removes tendon from groove (eliminates **groove pain**) |
| Residual / groove pain | **Similar** | **Similar** |
| Popeye, ROM, strength, PROMs, revision | **Similar** (revision very low) | **Similar** |
| **Nerve injury** | Lower (**unicortical** drilling) | **↑ transient neuropraxia** · **axillary n.** (bicortical drilling) · **medial NV** structures (retraction) |

**Complications** — overall complication rates **similar** (OB) · **subpectoral ↑ transient neuropraxia** (OB) — read the options

**Groove pain** — Orthobullets lists tenodesis → possible "**groove pain**"; recent prospective studies → **no difference** supra vs subpectoral

![Subpectoral tenodesis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3045-tenodesis-moved.jpg)

### Postoperative function after tenodesis

**Rehab** — full active shoulder ROM; **avoid active supination with elbow 90°** + **resisted elbow flexion** until healed

**Long-term** — **no change in shoulder or elbow strength**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ elbow flexion · ↓ supination · ↓ shoulder abduction/elevation

## 6 Complications

| Procedure | Complication |
|---|---|
| **Tenotomy** | **Popeye deformity** · ? cramping |
| Tenodesis | Groove pain · **nerve injury** (axillary, musculocutaneous, medial NV) · fixation failure |

## X vs Y

| | **Speed** | **Yergason** |
|---|---|---|
| Elbow | **Extended** | **Flexed 90°** |
| Forearm | Supinated | Pronated → **resisted supination** |
| Resisted motion | **Forward elevation** | **Supination** |
| Also + in | **SLAP** | — |

| | **Tenotomy** | **Tenodesis** |
|---|---|---|
| Popeye | **↑** | ↓ |
| Strength / pain / PROMs / ROM | Same | Same |
| Rehab / return | **Faster** | Slower |
| Nerve risk | — | Drilling / retraction |

## How it's asked

**"Tenodesis vs tenotomy — possibly lower incidence of?"** → **arm cramping**\
✗ Wrong options: elbow flexion weakness · stiffness · shoulder weakness

**"True comparing tenodesis to tenotomy?"** → **↓ Popeye deformity with tenodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: strength, pain, supination, ROM differences

**"Primary outcome difference tenodesis vs tenotomy?"** → **cosmetic deformity**\
✗ Wrong options: ASES, ROM, strength, VAS

**"Photographer worried about strength + deformity (figure D)?"** → **tenotomy ↑ cosmetic deformity, not inferior in PROMs**\
✗ Wrong options: tenodesis superior

**"Suprapectoral vs subpectoral — true?"** → **similar residual anterior shoulder pain**\
✗ Wrong options: ROM, cosmesis, nerve, PROMs differences

**"Subpectoral differs from suprapectoral in?"** → **higher rate of nerve injury**\
✗ Wrong options: PROMs, strength, revision, ROM

**"Arthroscopic suprapectoral vs prior open subpectoral — counselling?"** → **equivalent patient-reported outcomes**

**"Post-op avoid active supination at 90° — long-term function?"** → **no change in shoulder or elbow strength**

**"Rotator interval boundaries EXCEPT"** → **CHL** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: coracoid, subscapularis, supraspinatus

**"True about rotator interval structures?"** → **intra-articular LHB is extrasynovial** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: cable/crescent swapped · CHL/SGHL extra-articular

**"NOT a stabiliser of intra-articular LHB?"** → **transverse humeral ligament** (TO)

**"Tuoheti — most common LHB attachment?"** → **predominantly posterior** (TO)

**"Speed test also positive in?"** → **SLAP lesion**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Brachial Neuritis (Parsonage-Turner Syndrome)

*Shoulder & Elbow · 8 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **1.6–3/100,000/y** (Orthobullets text) · **1–30/100,000/y** (Orthobullets INA vs HNA table); underdiagnosed

**Demographics** — **♂ > ♀** (1.5:1 to 11.5:1) · **middle age (4th decade)**, 20–60 y, mean 41 (range 3–81)

**Side** — unilateral · **bilateral 10–30%** (16% simultaneous)

**Synonyms** — **neuralgic amyotrophy** (NA) · idiopathic neuralgic amyotrophy (**INA**) · Parsonage-Turner syndrome (**PTS**) · acute brachial neuritis/plexitis · idiopathic brachial plexus neuropathy

### Nerves involved

**Distribution** — any brachial plexus nerve/branch · **outside plexus in 17%** · usually **upper plexus + >1 nerve**

**Most common nerves** — **long thoracic** (→ **medial scapular winging**) · **suprascapular** · **axillary** · **musculocutaneous** · radial (Orthobullets, OB)

**Others** — cervical roots, **AIN**, PIN, LABC

**Muscles** — deltoid, supraspinatus, infraspinatus, **serratus anterior**, biceps

![Medial scapular winging (long thoracic nerve) in brachial neuritis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3065-36-moved.jpg)

### Pathophysiology

**Aetiology** — unknown; multifactorial → **inflammatory response in brachial plexus**

**Autoimmune** — ↑ blastogenic lymphocyte activity vs plexus tissue · ↑ **complement-fixing antibodies to peripheral nerve myelin**

**Biomechanical** — **mobile upper trunk** → wear of **blood–nerve barrier** → immune factors reach nerve (strenuous exercise, manual labour)

**Operative finding** — **hourglass-like constrictions** of nerves, **no external compression**

### Risk factors / antecedent events

| Trigger | Frequency | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Viral infection** (most common) | **25–55%** | EBV, VZV, Coxsackie B, **parvovirus B19**, CMV, mumps, smallpox, HIV; "flu-like illness", URTI |
| Bacterial infection | — | Leptospira, TB, Yersinia, Salmonella, Borrelia |
| **Immunisation** | **15%** | Tetanus, hepatitis B; **influenza vaccine** (OB stem) |
| **Perioperative / peripartum** | **14%** | Elective surgery |
| **Strenuous exercise** | **8%** | Cross-training, heavy upper-body work |
| Drugs | — | Abacavir, streptokinase, **heroin**, infliximab, **interferon** |
| Iatrogenic | — | **Interscalene block**, surgery, LP, irradiation; burns |

**Viral illness figure** — **25–55%** (Orthobullets, OB) · **25–50%** (one OB explanation) — read the options

### Genes & lab markers

**HNA gene** — **autosomal dominant** · **SEPT9 (septin 9)** on **chromosome 17q24** (cytoskeletal protein in glial cells) · ~200 families worldwide

**Routine labs** — **CBC + ESR usually normal**

**Inconsistent labs** — ↑ liver enzymes · **+ antiganglioside antibodies** · + ANA

**CSF** — usually normal; ± mildly ↑ protein, slight pleocytosis, oligoclonal bands

## 3 Presentation

### Diagnosis – acute pain followed by weakness

| Phase | Features | Duration |
|---|---|---|
| **I – Pain** | **Sudden, severe, unrelenting shoulder pain** (90%); radiates to proximal arm/neck; **wakes from sleep**; burning; often atraumatic | **Days–weeks** (♂ 45 d vs ♀ 23 d) |
| **II – Painless flaccid paralysis** | Weakness as pain subsides: within **24 h (33%) to 4 wk (80%)**; **patchy paresis** (muscles of same nerve unequally affected) | — |
| **III – Recovery** | Slow, steady motor return; length ∝ length of pain phase | **6–18 months** |

**Key exam pattern** — **inability to actively abduct** with **full passive motion** · **normal cervical spine exam** (–ve Spurling)

**Pain behaviour** — **not worsened by motion or palpation** · **not relieved by subacromial lidocaine** (neuropathic)

**Motor** — **severe weakness of ER + abduction** (SS, IS, deltoid) · hypotonia, **areflexia** (LMN) · **atrophy**, fasciculations · **medial winging** (serratus anterior)

**Sensory** — changes in **78%**: paraesthesia/hypoaesthesia over deltoid, lateral arm, radial forearm; often unnoticed · sensation may be **normal** (OB stems)

**Autonomic (15%)** — trophic skin, temperature dysregulation, ↑ sweating, altered nail/hair

**Upper trunk pattern** — pain → weakness + atrophy + scapular winging after viral illness = **upper trunk neuralgic amyotrophy**\
✗ Wrong options: isolated LTN palsy (doesn't explain whole picture)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**; often a **diagnosis of exclusion**; tests rule out mimics; **EMG confirms**

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray shoulder / C-spine** — **normal**; rule out **calcific tendinitis** (also severe incapacitating pain)

**Chest X-ray** — baseline to exclude compressive **Pancoast tumour** (OB)

| MRI timing | Sequence | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| **Early** | **T2 / STIR** | **Diffuse ↑ signal (oedema)** in muscles of plexus (SS, IS, deltoid, teres minor most common) |
| **Late** | **T1** | **Fatty infiltration + atrophy** |

**MRI also excludes** — cuff tear (intact cuff) · SSN notch/spinoglenoid **cyst** · brain MS plaques · cervical disc

![T2 MRI – diffuse muscle oedema in brachial neuritis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3065-mri.jpg)

### EMG findings

**EMG** — compatible findings in **96%**; confirms diagnosis + **monitors recovery** (serial)

| Timing | EMG |
|---|---|
| **Early** — **3–4 wk** (Orthobullets) · **first 3 wk** (OB) | **Acute denervation**: **positive sharp waves + fibrillation potentials** in **both peripheral nerve AND nerve root distributions** |
| **Late** — **3–4 months** (Orthobullets) · **as early as 6 wk** (OB) | Chronic denervation + **early reinnervation**: **polyphasic MUPs** (= **resolving** PTS) |

**Sensory NCS** — less useful than EMG · **↓ amplitude (axonal loss)**, **preserved conduction velocity + distal latency** · may be normal (doesn't exclude)

| ✗ EMG distractor | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| Normal EMG | PTS shows denervation |
| Fibrillations from **spinoglenoid notch** compression | SSN compression (cyst on MRI) |
| Sharp waves + fibs **deltoid + biceps only** | **C5 root** compression |
| Polyphasic MUPs, early reinnervation | **Resolving** PTS (not early) |

### Classification – INA vs HNA

| Factor | **INA** (idiopathic) | **HNA** (hereditary) |
|---|---|---|
| Incidence | 1–30/100,000/y | Very rare (~200 families) |
| Gene | — | **Septin 9 (chr 17q24)**, **AD** |
| Age at onset | Middle age (20–60) | **Young** (childhood/adolescence; mean 28) |
| Recurrence | **Rare** (~1.5 episodes) | **Frequent** (3.5 episodes) |
| Appearance | Normal | **Dysmorphic**: short stature, **hypotelorism**, cleft palate, facial asymmetry, skin folds |
| Nerves outside plexus | Uncommon (**17%**) | Common (**56%**): **lower cranial nerves** (VII, IX, X, XI, XII), lumbosacral plexus, phrenic, recurrent laryngeal |
| Severity / outcome | Better | More severe paresis, worse outcome |

### Differential diagnosis

| Mimic | Distinguishing feature |
|---|---|
| **Cervical radiculopathy** (C5/C6–7, disc) | Single root dermatome/myotome; pain from neck; **↑ with movement**; abnormal neck exam/Spurling (normal neck exam here makes it unlikely) |
| **Rotator cuff tear / impingement** | Pain persists with weakness; impingement signs; **relieved by subacromial lidocaine**; MRI shows tear; does not explain winging |
| **Calcific tendinitis** | Calcification on X-ray; no acute severe weakness |
| **Adhesive capsulitis (frozen shoulder)** | **Loss of passive + active** motion |
| **Entrapment neuropathy** (SSN) | **Single nerve** distribution on EMG (SS + IS only) |
| **Poliomyelitis** | No (excluded by acute severe weakness pattern, OB) |
| **ALS** | **UMN + LMN** signs, **painless, no sensory loss**, cranial nerve signs |
| **Idiopathic hypertrophic brachial neuritis** | **Painless**; plexus hypertrophy on MRI; **demyelinating** NCS (slow velocity, prolonged latency) |
| Multiple sclerosis | Brain MRI demyelination |

**Exclusion logic (OB)** — loss of **active** motion excludes radiculopathy + impingement · **normal MRI** excludes cuff tear · **full passive motion** excludes adhesive capsulitis · normal C-spine exam excludes disc disease

## 5 Management

```
Brachial neuritis
├─ Pain phase → NSAIDs, judicious opioids, immobilisation
│      └─ severe pain → oral prednisone 1 mg/kg/d × 2 wk + 2-wk taper
├─ Weakness phase → physical therapy (ROM + shoulder girdle strengthening)
│      └─ follow monthly: exam + serial EMG
└─ No clinical/EMG recovery by 6–9 mo (9–12 mo in summary) → exploration
       └─ hourglass constriction → neurolysis
```

### Nonoperative

**Mainstay** — **observation + pain control**; self-limiting

**Corticosteroids** — early, severe pain; **2 wk prednisone 1 mg/kg/d + 2 wk taper** · faster pain resolution, **no change in progression/prognosis** · OB: may shorten symptoms/accelerate recovery

**Physical therapy** — once pain abated; ROM + strengthening; reduce traction on nerves · reverses atrophy · **observation alone = observation + PT at 1 y**

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Threshold |
|---|---|
| Exploration / neurolysis / graft / transfer | **No regeneration on exam + EMG by 6–9 months** (Orthobullets treatment) · **9–12 months** (Orthobullets summary) — read the options |

| Procedure | Note |
|---|---|
| **Neurolysis** | **Superior** to neurorrhaphy + grafting; LTN microneurolysis, ulnar transposition, Guyon/radial tunnel/carpal tunnel release |
| Neurorrhaphy / grafting | Excise diseased segment |
| Nerve transfer | — |
| **Split pectoralis major transfer** | **Serratus anterior paralysis** (winging) |

**✗ Wrong options: decompression / arthroscopic repair** — not indicated (no compressive lesion, cuff intact)

### Prognosis

**Overall** — **good**; **gradual return of strength without long-term functional deficit**; slow · residual weakness may last months–years

| Time | Recovery |
|---|---|
| 1 month | **66%** some motor recovery |
| 1 year | **35%** recovered (excellent 36%) |
| 2 years | Excellent **75%** |
| 3 years | **~90%** full strength, no pain (89%) |
| Up to 8 years | Full strength in some |

**Poor prognosis** — **female** · **lower trunk** involvement (upper trunk = best) · **persistent pain + no motor recovery by 3 months** · **hereditary** cases

**No effect** — **age**

**Recurrence** — **rare** in idiopathic (common in HNA)

## X vs Y

| Prognosis statement (OB distractor) | Disease |
|---|---|
| **Gradual return of strength, no long-term deficit** | ✓ **PTS** |
| Decompression improves strength | **SSN notch cyst** |
| Immunomodulators ↓ relapses | **Multiple sclerosis** |
| Arthroscopic repair best for active patient | **Rotator cuff tear** |
| Progressive weakness → **respiratory failure** | **ALS** (pure motor; no ↓ sensory amplitudes) |

| | **Brachial neuritis** | **Cervical radiculopathy** | **Rotator cuff tear** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pain onset | Acute severe → resolves before weakness | Neck → arm | Persists with weakness |
| Pain with motion | **No** | **Yes** | Yes |
| Subacromial lidocaine | No relief | — | Relief |
| Distribution | **Multiple roots + peripheral nerves**, patchy | One root | Cuff only, no winging |
| EMG | **Root + peripheral nerve** denervation | Root | Normal |

## How it's asked

**"Acute atraumatic burning shoulder pain, can't abduct, full passive ROM, normal C-spine + MRI — diagnosis?"** → **brachial neuritis**\
✗ Wrong options: C6–7 radiculopathy · impingement · cuff tear · adhesive capsulitis

**"Flu-like illness 1 wk earlier, then burning pain → weakness, normal X-rays?"** → **brachial neuritis**\
✗ Wrong options: calcific tendinitis · poliomyelitis · cervical disc disease · impingement

**"URTI, 3 mo pain + weakness, atrophy, medial winging, flaccid — diagnosis?"** → **upper trunk neuralgic amyotrophy**\
✗ Wrong options: isolated LTN palsy · ALS · frozen shoulder · cuff tear

**"Post-influenza-vaccine pain → weakness, T2 oedema SS/IS — expected EMG?"** → **acute denervation in both peripheral nerve + nerve root distributions with sharp waves + fibrillations**

**"EMG showing polyphasic MUPs / early reinnervation?"** → **resolving PTS** (late)

**"Sharp waves + fibs in deltoid + biceps only?"** → **C5 root compression**

**"Cross-training athlete, viral illness, pain → weakness, ↓ sensory amplitudes, normal brain/C-spine MRI — prognosis?"** → **gradual return of strength without long-term deficits**\
✗ Wrong options: decompression · immunomodulators · arthroscopic repair · respiratory failure

**"Most common antecedent event?"** → **viral illness (25–55%)**

**"Most commonly involved nerve / cause of winging?"** → **long thoracic nerve**

**"Gene in hereditary neuralgic amyotrophy?"** → **SEPT9, chromosome 17q24, AD**

**"Poor prognostic factors?"** → **female, lower trunk, no recovery by 3 months, hereditary**\
✗ Wrong options: age

**"Indication for surgery?"** → **no EMG/clinical recovery by 6–9 (9–12) months** → **neurolysis**

# Calcific Tendonitis

*Shoulder & Elbow · 9 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — calcification + tendon degeneration **near rotator cuff insertion** → pain + ↓ ROM; **unknown etiology**

**Age** — **30–60 y**

**Sex** — **♀ > ♂**

**Location** — **supraspinatus** most common · supraspinatus and/or **infraspinatus** (OB)

**Risk factors** — endocrine: **diabetes**, **hypothyroidism**

### Genes & lab markers

**Crystal** — **calcium hydroxyapatite** (Orthobullets, OB) · "calcium carbonate apatite" (one OB explanation) — read the options

**Histology** — hydroxyapatite deposits surrounded by **fibroblasts, macrophages, giant cells, lymphocytes**

**Molecular pathways** — **osteopontin** · **cathepsin K** · **transglutaminase 2**

**✗ Wrong option: calcium pyrophosphate** — **positively birefringent rhomboid/rod** crystals → CPPD / **pseudogout**

**✗ Wrong option: calcium oxalate** — **kidney stones**, urine; excess in **ethylene glycol (antifreeze)** poisoning → renal failure

**Process** — **active, cell-mediated**\
✗ Wrong options: purely degenerative; Uhthoff & Loehr

### Phases of disease (resorptive phase pain)

| Stage (Uhthoff) | Phase | Pathology | Pain |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Precalcific** | — | **Fibrocartilaginous metaplasia**; **tenocytes → chondrocytes** | **Pain-free** |
| **Calcific** | **Formative** | **Cell-mediated** calcific deposits | ± |
| | **Resting** | **No inflammation / vascular infiltration** | ± |
| | **Resorptive** | **Phagocytic resorption + vascular infiltration** | **Most painful** |
| **Postcalcific** | — | Calcium resorbed + **tissue remodelling** | — |

**✗ Resorptive distractors** — "vascular infiltration, painless" (it is painful) · lowest inflammation (= resting) · cell-mediated deposits (= formative) · fibrocartilaginous metaplasia (= precalcific)

**Fibrocartilaginous metaplasia** — only in **calcific tendonitis** among shoulder pathologies\
✗ Wrong options: cuff arthropathy · biceps tenosynovitis · chronic cuff tear · neuropathic arthropathy

**Surgery timing** — avoid surgery in **resorptive** phase (Uhthoff & Loehr) · surgery for refractory cases in **formative** phase

## 3 Presentation

**History** — similar to **subacromial impingement**

**Symptoms** — **atraumatic pain** (worst in resorptive phase) · **catching**, crepitus · mechanical block · pain with overhead activity / active elevation

**Exam** — supraspinatus fossa **atrophy** · **↓ active ROM** · scapular dyskinesia · ± ↓ cuff strength · positive **impingement signs**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Radiographs** | **Gold standard / mainstay**; monitor progression; location, density, extent, delineation |
| **AP, supraspinatus outlet, axillary** | **Supraspinatus** calcification |
| **Internal rotation view** | **Infraspinatus + teres minor** |
| **External rotation view** | **Subscapularis** |
| Deposit site | **1–1.5 cm** from supraspinatus insertion (Orthobullets) · **1–2 cm**, zone of ↓ vascularity, can be multifocal (OB) — read the options |
| CT | Rarely required; 3D anatomy |
| **MRI** | **Low signal on all sequences** (globular, T1 + STIR/T2 hypointense); limited diagnostic use; refractory pain → concomitant cuff tear |
| **Ultrasound** | Deposits **hyperechoic**; quantify extent; **guides needle lavage** |
| MR arthrography alone | Poor sensitivity + inter-rater agreement (Zubler) |

![Calcific deposit over supraspinatus insertion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3042-calcific-tendinitis.jpg)

**✗ Wrong options (next tests after failed barbotage)** — bone scan · MR arthrogram · EMG (no added value)

**Imaging ✗ distractors** — no acromial spur (✗ Wrong options: impingement) · no tear (✗ Wrong options: partial/full-thickness tear) · **adhesive capsulitis = clinical diagnosis**, no consistent X-ray findings

### Gartner & Heyer classification

| Type | Appearance | Phase | Image |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Well circumscribed, dense** | **Formative** | ![Gartner and Heyer types](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3042-gartner-and-heyer-classification.jpg) |
| **II** | Soft contour/dense **or** sharp/transparent | — | |
| **III** | **Translucent, cloudy**, no clear circumscription | **Resorptive** | |

**Radiographic types (one OB explanation)** — Type I = **fluffy, fleecy, poorly defined** → acute pain, formative phase · Type II = **discrete homogeneous, well defined** → subacute/chronic — conflicts with Gartner (Orthobullets: type I well circumscribed) — read the options

### Mole et al. classification

| Type | Appearance |
|---|---|
| **A** | Dense, **homogeneous**, sharp contours |
| **B** | Dense, **segmented**, sharp contours |
| **C** | **Heterogeneous, soft** contours |
| **D** | **Dystrophic** calcification at cuff **insertion** |

## 5 Management

```
Calcific tendonitis
├─ All phases, first line → NSAIDs + PT (stretch/strengthen, moist heat) ± steroid injection
├─ Refractory, formative/resting phase → adjunct ESWT
├─ Persistent, resorptive phase → US-guided needle lavage / barbotage
└─ Progressive, refractory, ↓ ADLs → arthroscopic / mini-open debridement of deposit
       └─ remaining cuff defect → ± rotator cuff repair (controversial)
```

### Treatment – conservative, barbotage & arthroscopic debridement

**Nonop outcome** — symptoms resolve **60–70% at 6 months**; most improve spontaneously

**Failure more likely** — **bilateral or large** deposits · deposit under **anterior third of acromion** · deposit **medial to acromion**

**Steroid injection** — common but **controversial**; variable relief

**Formative phase, 4-month mild pain** — **moist heat + stretching** first \
✗ Wrong options: aspiration (deposit **granular** → hard to aspirate) · ESWT (role poorly defined, OB) · **low-dose radiation** (malignant transformation risk) · arthroscopic debridement (only after failed nonop)

**ESWT** — adjunct; refractory **formative + resting** phases; **dose-dependent**: high-energy > low-energy for outcome scores + resorption, but also > procedural pain + local reaction (ecchymosis)

**Needle lavage** — **2 needles** (inflow saline ± anaesthetic + aspiration outflow); indicated for **resorptive phase**; better outcomes **Gartner II/III vs I**

**Barbotage** — needle breaks up deposit → **corticosteroid injection**

![US-guided two-needle lavage](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3042-usevan.jpg)

### Operative criteria

| Indication for surgical decompression of deposit |
|---|
| **Progression of symptoms** |
| **Refractory to nonoperative treatment** (incl. failed barbotage) |
| **Interference with ADLs** / work |

**Surgery** — arthroscopic or mini-open removal (shaver or spinal needle) ± SAD ± RCR; **paste-like ("toothpaste") calcium** expressed from cuff

![Arthroscopic paste-like calcium expressed from cuff](../../MCQ/attachments/q8975-d-calcium.jpg)

**After failed barbotage** — **arthroscopic debridement**\
✗ Wrong options: isolated RCR without debridement (won't resolve symptoms)

**RCR after debridement** — **controversial**; depends on remaining tissue integrity + defect size · debridement without RCR safe: **3.7%** cuff tears at 7 y (El Shewy)

**Deposit with osseous involvement** — same results as tendon-only (Seyahi)

### Debridement ± subacromial decompression

| SAD + debridement vs debridement alone | Result |
|---|---|
| **Time to pain-free activity** | **↑ (longer)** with SAD (Marder) |
| Pain at midterm | No difference |
| ROM | No difference |
| Recurrence | No difference |
| Cuff tears | **↑ in operated shoulder vs contralateral** regardless (Balke, 6 y) |

**Debridement + SAD** — 91% good/satisfactory at 26 mo (Ark); SAD gives **no apparent benefit**

**Return to work** — longer with SAD and/or RCR

## 6 Complications

**List** — **recurrence** · **persistent pain** · **stiffness** (operative risk) · **iatrogenic cuff injury** · ↑ cuff tears after surgery

## X vs Y

| | **Formative phase** | **Resting phase** | **Resorptive phase** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Process | Cell-mediated deposition | No inflammation / vascular infiltration | Phagocytosis + vascular infiltration |
| Pain | ± | ± | **Most severe** |
| Deposit (Gartner) | **Type I** dense, well circumscribed | — | **Type III** cloudy, translucent |
| Treatment niche | **ESWT**; conservative; aspiration difficult (granular) | **ESWT** | **Needle lavage**; avoid surgery |

| | **Hydroxyapatite** | **CPP** | **Calcium oxalate** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Disease | **Calcific tendonitis** | **Pseudogout / CPPD** | Kidney stones, ethylene glycol |
| Crystal | Deposits ringed by fibroblasts, macrophages, giant cells | **Positively birefringent rhomboid/rod** | Urine crystals |
| Colchicine | **no role** | — | — |

## How it's asked

**"Calcific deposit on X-ray — structure abnormal at arthroscopy?"** → **supraspinatus**\
✗ Wrong options: infraspinatus · labrum · biceps · GH cartilage

**"35-y ♀, insidious pain, low-signal globule in supraspinatus on MRI?"** → **calcific tendinitis**\
✗ Wrong options: impingement · adhesive capsulitis · cuff tear

**"Fibrocartilaginous metaplasia — which image?"** → **AP X-ray with supraspinatus calcification**\
✗ Wrong options: cuff arthropathy · biceps tenosynovitis · neuropathic arthropathy

**"Best description of resorptive phase?"** → **highest pain levels**\
✗ Wrong options: painless vascular infiltration

**"Formative-phase deposit, mild 4-month pain — initial treatment?"** → **moist heat + stretching**\
✗ Wrong options: aspiration · ESWT · radiation · arthroscopy

**"Debrided material pathology + next step?"** → **calcium hydroxyapatite; debridement ± cuff repair**\
✗ Wrong options: CPP · oxalate · IV colchicine

**"Failed PT, NSAIDs and US barbotage — next?"** → **arthroscopic debridement**\
✗ Wrong options: isolated RCR · bone scan · MR arthrogram · EMG

**"Adding SAD to debridement — effect?"** → **longer time to pain-free activity**\
✗ Wrong options: better pain · ROM · fewer tears · ↓ recurrence

**"Most painful phase?"** → **resorptive**

**"View for subscapularis calcification?"** → **external rotation AP**

# Distal Biceps Avulsion

*Shoulder & Elbow · 10 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — rare; **~10% of all biceps ruptures**

**Demographics** — **dominant elbow (86%)** of **men (93%)** in their **40s** (30–50 y)

**Risk factors** — **anabolic steroids** · **smoking (7.5× risk)** · hypovascularity · intrinsic degeneration · mechanical impingement in space available for tendon

**Patterns** — complete avulsion · **partial** (on **radial side** of tuberosity footprint) · **intersubstance muscle transection** (rope wrapped round arm, **tug-of-war**)

**Rare association** — **median nerve compression**

### Footprint anatomy

**Insertion** — **radial (bicipital) tuberosity**; innervation **musculocutaneous only**\
✗ Wrong options: dual musculocutaneous + radial

| | **Short head** | **Long head** |
|---|---|---|
| Origin | **Coracoid** | Supraglenoid tubercle / superior labrum |
| Footprint | **Distal + anterior** (thin sliver) | **Proximal + posterior** (oval; **apex** of tuberosity) |
| Better at | **Elbow flexion** | **Supination** (furthest from axis of rotation) |

![Long head inserts proximal, short head distal on radial tuberosity](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3081-biceps-footprint.jpg)

**Biceps function** — **primary supinator**; lesser elbow flexor · **brachialis = primary elbow flexor** (inserts **coronoid + ulnar tuberosity**, not radial tuberosity)

**Annular ligament** — anterior radial head, PRUJ stability; does not attach to radial tuberosity

**Lacertus fibrosus (bicipital aponeurosis)** — from **medial side of distal short head**, distal to elbow crease → obliquely distal + medial across cubital fossa → protects **brachial artery + median nerve** → continuous with flexor-origin fascia; **may be mistaken for intact tendon**; intact lacertus → ↓ deficit in low-demand patient

### Antecubital fossa

**Medial → lateral** — **median nerve** (most medial) · **brachial artery** · **biceps tendon** · **radial nerve** (most lateral)

**Borders** — **pronator teres** ulnarly · **brachioradialis** radially

**Radial recurrent artery** — **superficial** to biceps tendon, crosses near tuberosity

**Superficial radial nerve** — **lateral (radial) + superficial** to biceps tendon; radial nerve bifurcates (PIN + SRN) **at level of antecubital fossa / radiocapitellar joint**

**LABCN** — terminal sensory branch of **musculocutaneous (lateral cord)** → exits **between biceps + brachialis** lateral to musculotendinous junction → pierces fascia, **lateral to biceps tendon, deep to cephalic vein** → superficial along **brachioradialis** → volar-lateral forearm sensation

![Antecubital fossa: radial recurrent artery, SRN, LABCN](../../MCQ/attachments/q219839-illa-ac-fossa-1.jpg)

| Nerve course (✗ Wrong options: distractors for LABCN) | Nerve |
|---|---|
| Branches 5–8 cm distal to lateral epicondyle, **between 2 heads of PT**, along volar FDP → pronator quadratus | **AIN** |
| From radial n. at radiocapitellar joint, **through supinator (arcade of Frohse)**, around **radial neck** → dorsal wrist capsule | **PIN** |
| **Deep to brachioradialis**, lateral to radial artery, pierces fascia **7–9 cm proximal to wrist** → snuffbox, dorsoradial hand | **SRN** |
| With brachial artery between **PT + biceps tendon**, between **FDS + FDP**, emerges between FDS + FPL → carpal tunnel | **Median** |

**Radial nerve motor order (Abrams)** — BR → ECRL → supinator → ECRB → EDC → ECU → EDQ → APL → EPL → EPB → **EIP last**

## 2 Mechanism

### Mechanism – eccentric contraction

**Eccentric contraction** — **muscle lengthens during contraction**; flexed elbow forced into extension ("**flexed elbow unacceptably challenged**")

**Contributors** — **vascular watershed** · mechanical attrition (abrasion during pronosupination)

**Partial tear mechanism** — pronation → **sharp radial margin of tuberosity impinges tendon** → released in supination; rupture through pre-existing degeneration on **radial aspect**

| ✗ Wrong option: contraction type | Definition |
|---|---|
| Isometric | Constant **length** |
| Isokinetic | Constant **speed** |
| Isotonic | Constant **force** |
| Concentric | Muscle **shortens** (biceps curl) |

## 3 Presentation

**History** — painful **"pop"** as elbow eccentrically loaded (catching falling object, lifting furniture/engine, deadlift, curls)

**Hallmark** — **weakness + pain in supination** > flexion

**Inspection** — **"reverse Popeye"** (proximal retraction of muscle belly), contour change, **antecubital / medial ecchymosis**, **palpable defect**

**Complete vs partial** — tendon **absent** in complete · **palpable but painful** in partial (otherwise similar picture)

### Diagnosis – hook test, MRI, partial tears

**Hook test (O'Driscoll) (TO)** — patient looks at palm, **shoulder elevated, elbow 90°, forearm supinated** → intact tendon = cord-like structure finger hooks around; torn → **brachialis is flat** → nothing to hook

**Hook technique** — index finger hooks **lateral edge** of tendon, **lateral → medial**; intact/partial → finger 1 cm beneath tendon

**Accuracy** — **sensitivity + specificity 100%** → **most sensitive exam** for complete rupture

**False "normal" hook** — **partial tear** · **intact lacertus fibrosus** (hooking **medial → lateral** catches lacertus) · underlying **brachialis**

**Partial tears** — hook test **normal but painful**; hook test not useful for partial tears

![Hook test: finger hooks lateral edge of tendon](../../MCQ/attachments/q212062-illb-hook-test.jpg)

**Ruland biceps squeeze test** — elbow **60–80° flexion**, forearm slightly **pronated**, squeeze biceps belly → **no supination = positive** (like Thompson); **sensitivity 96%**

**Other exam (OB)** — **biceps crease interval test**, **passive forearm pronation** test

**✗ Wrong option: milking manoeuvre** — tests **UCL**, not biceps

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — usually **normal**; occasionally **fleck/avulsion** from radial tuberosity

**MRI** — distinguishes **complete vs partial**, muscle vs tendon, **degree of retraction**; **FABS position** (elbow **flexion**, shoulder **abduction**, forearm **supination**) ↑ sensitivity

**MRI when?** — **needed for partial tear** (palpable but painful tendon) · not always needed for complete tear with conclusive exam

**Partial tear MRI** — ↑ signal + ↑ **diameter** of tendon · **avulsive marrow oedema** in tuberosity · **bicipitoradial bursa distension**

**Ultrasound** — can confirm diagnosis in office

| Clinical finding | Next step |
|---|---|
| Abnormal hook, retraction, ecchymosis | Clinical diagnosis → repair |
| **Palpable tendon, painful hook** | **MRI** (✗ Wrong options: CT · immobilise 3 wk · exploration · early PT) |
| Partial tear, failed ≥6 mo nonop | **Detachment + repair** of biceps tendon |

## 5 Management

```
Distal biceps rupture
├─ Older, low-demand, sedentary → nonop: support + PT
│      (accepts supination/flexion loss)
├─ Young, active, complete tear → anatomic repair within a few weeks
│      └─ chronic, retracted → primary repair in hyperflexion
└─ Partial tear → trial nonop
       └─ persistent pain/weakness → detach + repair
```

### Nonoperative outcome – loss of supination strength

**Greatest loss** — **forearm supination**\
✗ Wrong options: elbow flexion (brachialis compensates; flexion returns near normal)

| Nonop deficit | Orthobullets | OB explanations |
|---|---|---|
| **Sustained supination (endurance)** | **50%** | 50% |
| **Supination strength** | **40%** | 40% (up to 40%) |
| **Flexion strength** | **30%** | **30%** · **10%** (one OB) · **~40% sustained flexion** (one OB) |
| Grip | 15% | — |

**Flexion loss value** — **30%** (Orthobullets, most OB) · **10%** (one OB) · **40% sustained** (one OB) — read the options

**Also with nonop** — **delayed return to activity**\
✗ Wrong options: chronic elbow pain · instability (terrible triad / anteromedial coronoid) · lateral pain on resisted wrist extension (lateral epicondylitis)

**Activity hardest** — **using a screwdriver** (supination)

| ✗ Wrong option: activity affected | Injury |
|---|---|
| Pushing up from chair | **LUCL** tear |
| Bench press | **Pectoralis major** tear |
| Wrist extension | ECRB/ECRL (common extensor) |
| Elbow extension | **Triceps** tear |

**Nonop EXCEPT** — **LABCN neuritis** (exclusively surgical)

**Nonop indications** — **older, low-demand, sedentary**, willing to sacrifice function · not healthy middle-aged with dominant-arm tear

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Young, healthy, active** | Don't want to sacrifice function (**main aim = restore supination strength**) |
| **Partial tear failing nonop** | Persistent pain + weakness |
| **Subacute / chronic** | **Direct repair** (no allograft) usually possible; may need **hyperflexion** |
| Timing | **Within a few weeks**; delay → harder primary repair, **more extensile approach** |

**Not surgery indications** — cosmesis · preventing elbow degeneration

**Repair outcome** — anatomic repairs **high success**; **loss of terminal extension** common after repair\
✗ Wrong options: high re-rupture

### Surgical anatomy – BR/PT interval & recurrent radial artery

| | **Anterior single-incision** | **Dual-incision (Boyd-Anderson)** |
|---|---|---|
| Rationale | Developed to ↓ **HO + synostosis** of 2-incision | Developed to avoid **radial nerve / PIN** injury |
| Interval | **Brachioradialis (radial n.) / pronator teres (median n.)** — not PT/FCR | Same anterior + **posterolateral: ECU / EDC** |
| ✗ Wrong interval | — | **Kocher (ECU/anconeus)**, **anconeus/ulna** → expose ulna → HO/synostosis |
| Forearm | **Maximal supination** protects PIN (✗ Wrong options: pronation) | Hemostat through IO space along **medial border of tuberosity** → pierces anconeus → tents skin |
| **LABCN injury** | **Higher** | Lower |
| **HO / synostosis** | Lower (↓ periosteal stripping) — **not eliminated** | **Higher** |
| Repair | — | **More anatomic** (better footprint exposure; ulnar placement) |
| Supination strength | Similar | Similar or slightly better |

![Avoid intervals exposing the ulna (ECU/anconeus, Kocher)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3081-wrong-intervals.jpg)

**Single-incision steps** — limited antecubital incision → BR lateral, PT medial → **identify LABCN** (exits between biceps + brachialis) and retract laterally → **recurrent radial vessels** coagulated/ligated or retracted → protect PIN\
✗ Wrong options: forceful lateral retraction, keep supinated

**Recurrent radial artery** — **crosses field immediately deep to BR/PT interval, near tuberosity**; large, may bleed profusely → **ligate** if cannot retract → "flash of bright red blood" with deep retractor

| ✗ Artery distractor | Location |
|---|---|
| Anterior interosseous a. | From ulnar a., along **IOM** |
| Anterior recurrent ulnar a. | From ulnar a., proximal + superficial to brachialis insertion |
| Recurrent interosseous a. | From posterior interosseous a., **posterior forearm** |
| Inferior ulnar collateral a. | From brachial a., **medial elbow** |
| Ulnar a. | Far medial, between **FDS + FDP** |

**Approach vs effectiveness** — single vs two-incision: **no proven difference** in effectiveness

**Post-op** — immobilise **110° flexion + moderate supination**

### Fixation techniques

| Technique | Approach | Key step |
|---|---|---|
| **Bone tunnel** | 2-incision | Acorn reamer slot; **2–3 × 2-mm holes 1 cm apart** through far (lateral) radius; sutures tied over bone bridge |
| **Suture anchors** | Single | 2 anchors; **distal anchor tied first** (brings tendon out to length) → maximises contact |
| **Intraosseous (interference) screw** | Single | Whip-stitch sutures through bioabsorbable tenodesis screw |
| **Suspensory cortical button** | Single | Ream anterior cortex, smaller hole far cortex, **flip button** on far cortex, tension-slide tendon in |

| Load | Force |
|---|---|
| Elbow 90°, no load | **50 N** (repair must withstand) |
| Elbow 90°, 1 kg load | 112 N |
| Tendon rupture | 200 N |

**Strength order** — **suture button (400 N) > suture anchor (380) > bone tunnel (310) > interference screw (230)**

**Strongest** — **button + interference screw combination** · **weakest** — **interference screw alone** \
✗ Wrong options: bone tunnels weakest · button alone strongest

### Repair technique – anatomic (ulnar) footprint & chronic retracted tears

**Insertion point** — repair to **ulnar aspect of tuberosity** → ↑ **supination lever arm (windlass effect)**; not more distal (off tuberosity); no flexion advantage

**Chronic retracted (e.g. 5 wk, 6 cm)** — **primary fixation in hyperflexion is well tolerated**: **no flexion contracture / ROM loss**

**Allograft augmentation** — only if tendon **cannot reach tuberosity even in hyperflexion**; less consistent results; not "necessary" for retraction

**Chronic traps** — ✗ Wrong options: two-incision required (may need extensile approach) · add interference screw to button if tendon reaches · nonop equivalent

## 6 Complications

### LABCN injury – most common complication (single-incision)

**LABCN neuropraxia** — **most common complication overall** and **most common nerve** injured, either approach; **more common with single-incision**

**Cause** — **aggressive / prolonged retraction**; not related to fixation type (anchor/screw/button) or timing\
✗ Wrong options: direct laceration

**Deficit** — numbness **volar-lateral (radial) forearm**

**Origin** — **lateral cord → musculocutaneous → LABCN** (labelled musculocutaneous on plexus diagrams)

| LABCN incidence | Source |
|---|---|
| **9%** | Orthobullets, OB |
| **5–7%** | OB (Cohen) |
| **Nearly one-quarter** | OB |

**Resolution** — **3–6 mo** (Orthobullets, most OB) · **by 3 mo** (one OB) · by 6 mo, **up to 2 y** (one OB) — read the options; **observe → gradual resolution**; permanent deficit/neuroma rare (neuroma excision + tension-free repair, traction resection, transposition)

### PIN injury – two-incision technique & forearm positioning

**PIN** — **most commonly injured motor nerve** (**1–2%**; 5% Cohen); resolves 3–6 mo

**Mechanism** — **radially based retractors** around radial neck · prolonged traction · **Beath pin out dorsal forearm** · **entrapment by cortical button** on posterior radius

**Protect PIN** — **maximal supination** + limit forceful lateral retraction

**PIN palsy** — **weak wrist extension with radial deviation** (ECRL intact — radial n. proximal; ECU out — PIN) · weak thumb / **index finger extension**

**PIN risk by approach** — **single-incision higher** (Orthobullets, OB) · **two-incision higher** (other OB explanations) · **similar** (recent meta-analysis, OB) — read the options

### Complication rates (OB)

| Minor (~20%) | Rate | Major (~4%) | Rate |
|---|---|---|---|
| **LABCN neuropraxia** | **9%** | **PIN neuropraxia** | **1–2%** |
| HO | 3–4% | Re-rupture | 1–2% |
| **SRN palsy** | **2–3%** | Deep infection | 1% |
| Superficial infection | 1% | **Radioulnar synostosis** | 1% |
| Stiffness | 1% | Ulnar / AIN injury | **<0.1%** |

**Nerve order** — LABCN > SRN > PIN > median > AIN (OB)

**2nd most common nerve** — **SRN** = 2nd most common **cutaneous/sensory** (Orthobullets, OB) · **PIN** = 2nd most common nerve (one OB) — read the options

**SRN injury** — numbness **dorsoradial hand / dorsal thumb**; lateral + superficial to tendon

**Re-rupture** — **<4–5%** (OB) · **1–2%** (OB) → weak supination > flexion

**HO** — when **IOM + ulnar periosteum disrupted**; risk of **2-incision**; often asymptomatic; some advise prophylaxis

**Synostosis** — loss of pronation/supination; avoid exposing **ulnar periosteum** and dissection between radius + ulna; **more with 2-incision**; ↓ with intermuscular (ECU/EDC) interval

**Proximal radius fracture** — large tunnels or tunnels **proximal to tuberosity**

**Suture rupture** — bone tunnel method

**Expanding hematoma** — brachial artery laceration / radial recurrent bleeding — very uncommon

| Post-op deficit | Nerve |
|---|---|
| **Lateral volar forearm numbness** | **LABCN** |
| Dorsal thumb / dorsoradial hand numbness | **SRN** |
| Weak wrist / thumb / index extension | **PIN** |
| Medial volar forearm numbness | MABCN |
| Can't flex middle finger | Median |
| Can't flex thumb IP | AIN |
| Weak finger abduction/adduction | Ulnar |

## X vs Y

### Proximal long-head biceps rupture

| | **Proximal LHB rupture** | **Distal biceps rupture** |
|---|---|---|
| Deformity | **Popeye** deformity | **Reverse Popeye** (belly retracts proximally) |
| Hook test | — | **Abnormal** (no cord) |
| Main deficit | — | **Supination** |

**✗ Photo distractors** — brachial plexus palsy · pectoralis major tear · normal biceps

| | **Complete tear** | **Partial tear** |
|---|---|---|
| Hook test | Abnormal | **Normal but painful** |
| Tendon | Absent | Palpable |
| Location | Off tuberosity | **Radial side** of footprint |
| MRI | Not always needed | **Needed** |
| Treatment | Repair (active) | Trial nonop → repair if fails |

| | **LABCN** | **PIN** |
|---|---|---|
| Type | Sensory (most common overall) | **Motor** (most common motor) |
| Approach | **Single-incision** | Disputed (see above) |
| Protection | Gentle retraction, retract laterally | **Supination**, no radial retractors |

## How it's asked

**"Nonop distal biceps rupture — greatest loss?"** → **forearm supination**\
✗ Wrong options: elbow flexion (brachialis)

**"Main indication for repair in a 45-year-old coach?"** → **restore supination strength**\
✗ Wrong options: cosmesis · prevent degeneration

**"Nonop management results in all EXCEPT"** → **lateral antebrachial neuritis**

**"Partial tear MRI, nonop — outcome?"** → **decreased supination strength**\
✗ Wrong options: instability · chronic pain

**"Activity hardest after nonop?"** → **using a screwdriver**\
✗ Wrong options: bench press = pec · chair push-up = LUCL

**"Hook test diagnoses?"** → **biceps tear** (TO)

**"Partial tear, normal hook, failed 6 mo nonop — next?"** → **detach + repair biceps**\
✗ Wrong options: radial tunnel release · EMG

**"Palpable tendon, painful hook — next step?"** → **MRI scan**\
✗ Wrong options: CT · immobilise + re-examine

**"Partial tears typically occur in which part of the footprint?"** → **radial** side

**"True of the injured structure (MRI distal biceps)?"** → **inability to hook a cord-like structure is most sensitive**\
✗ Wrong options: LH distal · dual innervation · primary flexor · high re-rupture

**"Orange area: distal + anterior on tuberosity?"** → **short head of biceps**\
✗ Wrong options: long head = proximal/posterior · lacertus = medial

**"Healthy 44-year-old, positive hook, weak supination — management?"** → **distal biceps repair**\
✗ Wrong options: sling + PT · allograft · UCL reconstruction

**"Contraction causing rupture?"** → **muscle lengthens during contraction** (eccentric)

**"Nerve injured in superficial dissection, single anterior incision?"** → **LABCN**

**"Most likely deficit after two-incision repair?"** → **numbness lateral volar forearm** (LABCN)

**"Most common complication after button repair?"** → **LABCN irritation**\
✗ Wrong options: re-rupture · synostosis · PIN

**"True about surgical treatment of distal biceps rupture?"** → **most common complication = injury to terminal branch of musculocutaneous nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: button alone strongest · bone tunnels weakest

**"Course of nerve in commonest neurologic complication?"** → **pierces biceps fascia, lateral to tendon, deep to cephalic vein, superficial along BR**

**"Burning radial forearm pain 3 mo after single-incision reconstruction — sequela?"** → **gradual resolution**

**"True of LABCN injury?"** → **highest risk after single-incision**\
✗ Wrong options: laceration · permanent · less common than PIN

**"Sensory nerve from lateral cord injured — true?"** → **more common single- than double-incision**\
✗ Wrong options: resolves 9–12 mo · second most common

**"Commonest nerve injured — brachial plexus origin?"** → **lateral cord**

**"Labelled plexus: branch of which nerve most at risk, single incision?"** → **musculocutaneous (A)**

**"Altered lateral forearm sensation after repair — contributing factor?"** → **single-incision approach**\
✗ Wrong options: fixation type · timing

**"Dorsoradial hand numbness after single-incision repair — nerve relation to tendon?"** → **lateral (radial)** (SRN)

**"True of complications of distal biceps repair?"** → **LABCN = most common complication of single-incision**\
✗ Wrong options: ECU/EDC ↑ HO · screw highest load · synostosis eliminated

**"Most common sensory + motor nerve deficits?"** → **lateral forearm numbness + weak wrist extension** (LABCN + PIN)

**"Arm kept maximally supinated during footprint prep protects?"** → **PIN → wrist extension weakness**

**"Most common complication, findings?"** → **volar/radial forearm numbness**

**"True of single-incision technique?"** → **higher rate of sensory nerve injury**\
✗ Wrong options: PT/FCR interval · pronation · more anatomic

**"True of management (positive hook)?"** → **single-incision uses BR/PT interval**\
✗ Wrong options: anconeus/ulna interval · more flexion than supination loss

**"Vessel most at risk in volar approach?"** → **recurrent radial artery**

**"Deep retractor in BR/PT interval, bright red blood?"** → **recurrent radial artery**\
✗ Wrong options: inferior ulnar collateral · recurrent interosseous

**"5 weeks, 6 cm retraction — true?"** → **primary fixation in hyperflexion well tolerated**\
✗ Wrong options: allograft necessary · two-incision required

**"Resident's plan: button on radial side of tuberosity — improve by?"** → **move insertion more ulnar**\
✗ Wrong options: more distal · anchors

**"More ulnar insertion improves?"** → **forearm supination**

**"Photo best showing long head biceps rupture?"** → **Popeye deformity of upper arm**\
✗ Wrong options: abnormal hook = distal

# Distal Clavicle Osteolysis

*Shoulder & Elbow · 11 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Demographics** — **20s**, mostly **♂**; **weightlifters** (bench press, push-ups), overhead athletes, wrestlers

**Risk factor** — history of **trauma** (acute injury) or **repetitive microtrauma**

**Adolescents (OB)** — mean age 15.9 y in one series; overhead sports / weightlifting

### Anatomy

**Clavicle** — S-shaped; **last bone to fuse** (medial physis fuses early 20s)

**AC joint** — **diarthrodial** with **fibrocartilage meniscus**

| Ligament | Stability |
|---|---|
| **AC ligaments** (esp. posterosuperior capsule) | **Horizontal** |
| **CC ligaments** | **Vertical** |

## 2 Mechanism

**Pathoanatomy** — **repetitive stress + micro-fractures** of distal clavicle → **osteopenia + bone resorption**

**Load** — bench press / manoeuvres with **elbows posterior to torso** → traction through AC joint

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — **distal clavicle / anterosuperior shoulder**; **insidious**; ↑ with repetitive loading (bench press, push-ups); mimics AC arthritis

**Exam** — **point tenderness at AC joint / distal clavicle** · **pain with cross-body adduction** · pain without weakness on resisted forward flexion + adduction · **full ROM**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **AP clavicle** + **Zanca view (15° cephalad tilt)** (Orthobullets) · **10–15°** (TO, OB) — read the options

![Zanca view technique](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3048-zanca-view-method.jpg)

**X-ray (distal clavicle only, not acromion)** — **cysts**, **osteopenia**, **resorption + erosion**, **tapering** of distal clavicle, **AC joint widening**

![Distal clavicle osteolysis, AP](../../MCQ/attachments/q636-a-distal-clavicle-osteolysis.jpg)

**MRI** — **↑ T2 signal + bone marrow oedema** in distal clavicle / AC joint (fluid-sensitive)

**Bone scan** — **↑ uptake** distal clavicle; **earlier than X-ray changes**

**Follow-up MRI (OB)** — mean **5 mm AC widening**

## 5 Management

```
Distal clavicle osteolysis
├─ First line → rest + NSAIDs + PT + modify lifting technique
│      └─ persistent → corticosteroid injection (diagnostic + therapeutic; US-guided)
└─ Failed nonop → distal clavicle excision (open or arthroscopic)
       └─ address cuff + LHB pathology at same time
```

### Nonoperative

**Activity modification** — avoid aggravating lifts; **narrow hand grip**; **stop bar descent 4–6 cm above chest**; avoid elbows behind torso

**Steroid injection** — **diagnostic + therapeutic**; **more accurate with US**

**Success (OB)** — **93%** respond to conservative treatment (adolescent series)

### Distal clavicle excision

**Indication** — persistent symptoms after **failed nonop** (rest, PT, cortisone)

**Mumford (arthroscopic)** — resect **only 0.5–1 cm**; **too much → AC instability**

**Open vs arthroscopic** — surgeon preference; arthroscopic **comparable pain relief**, ↓ morbidity, fewer restrictions, **quicker return**, better cosmesis, **evaluates GH joint** + treats concomitant pathology

**Open** — meticulous **deltoid-trapezial fascia** repair

**Results (OB)** — Auge: **10 weightlifters**, limited arthroscopic resection → **return to sport 3.2 d**, weight training **9.1 d** · Zawadsky: 22 excellent, 16 good, **3 failures — all traumatic aetiology**

![After arthroscopic distal clavicle excision](../../MCQ/attachments/q210103-illb-postop.jpg)

## 6 Complications

**Horizontal instability** — **violating posterosuperior capsule / AC ligaments** during excision

**AC instability** — **over-resection** (beyond 0.5–1 cm)

## X vs Y

| | **Distal clavicle osteolysis** | **AC separation** | **SLAP / biceps (wrestler)** | **Cuff tear** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Key clue | AC tenderness, cross-body pain, **no weakness**, full ROM | **Instability** | Common in wrestlers, but exam ≠ AC | **Weakness** |
| Imaging | Lysis, cysts, tapering; marrow oedema | — | Labral pathology | Tendon tear |
| Surgery | **Distal clavicle excision** | **CC ligament reconstruction ± DCE** | SLAP repair / tenodesis | Cuff repair |

## How it's asked

**"Bodybuilder, cross-body pain, AC tenderness, X-ray lysis — most successful treatment?"** → **arthroscopic resection of the distal clavicle**\
✗ Wrong options: GH injection · periscapular strengthening · labral repair · capsular release

**"Wrestler, failed rest + PT + cortisone, AC tenderness, MRI marrow oedema — next step?"** → **distal clavicle excision**\
✗ Wrong options: SLAP repair · subpectoral tenodesis · CC reconstruction

**"Best early imaging before X-ray changes?"** → **bone scan**

**"Complication of excessive / posterosuperior capsular resection?"** → **horizontal AC instability**

**"How much clavicle to resect?"** → **0.5–1 cm**

# Elbow Anatomy & Biomechanics

*Shoulder & Elbow · 12 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Joints & function

**3 articulations** — **ulnohumeral** (hinge) · **radiocapitellar / radiohumeral** (pivot) · **proximal radioulnar**

**Function** — lever arm positioning the hand · fulcrum for forearm lever

### Range of motion & load sharing

| Parameter | Value |
|---|---|
| Total flexion–extension | **0–150°** |
| **Functional flexion–extension** | **30–130°** |
| **Functional pronation/supination** | **50° each** |
| Carrying angle (valgus) | **♂ 5–10° · ♀ 10–15°** (anatomy note) · ~11° ♂ / ~13° ♀ (exam note) · 6–11° (UCL note); ↓ with flexion |
| Axial load, extended elbow | **40% ulnohumeral · 60% radiohumeral** |

### Osteology

**Spiral groove** — posterior humeral shaft; **radial nerve**; **~13 cm** proximal to trochlear articular surface

**Distal flare** — medial + lateral epicondyles; flare = half of elbow joint · **medial + lateral columns**

**Trochlea** — spool-shaped, **medial** · **capitellum** — lateral

**Sublime tubercle** — medial **coronoid** process; distal attachment of **anterior bundle of MCL**

### Arthrology

| Feature | Value |
|---|---|
| Articular surface **anterior tilt** | **~30°** relative to humeral shaft |
| Valgus | **6°** |
| Rotation | **5° internal** |
| **Axis of flexion** | Centre of **trochlea + capitellum**; passes through **anteroinferior medial epicondyle** |
| **Radial head cartilage** | Covers **~240°**; **lateral 120° bare** (safe zone for radial head fixation) |
| **Capsule** | **Max distension 70–80° flexion**; anterior capsule inserts **6 mm distal to coronoid tip** → **coronoid intra-articular** |
| Coronoid fossa | Receives coronoid tip in deep flexion |
| **Coronoid tip** | **Buttress against posterior dislocation** |

### Muscles of the elbow

**Flexors** — **biceps** (radial tuberosity) · **brachialis** (inserts **11 mm distal to coronoid tip**) · brachioradialis

**Extensor** — triceps

### Collateral ligaments – MCL anterior bundle & LCL complex

| Stabiliser tier | Structures |
|---|---|
| **Primary static** (3) | **Ulnohumeral joint (coronoid)** · **MCL (anterior bundle)** · **LCL complex** |
| **Secondary static** | **Radiocapitellar joint** · **capsule** · **flexor + extensor origins** |
| **Dynamic** | Muscles crossing elbow: **anconeus, brachialis, triceps, biceps** → compression |

**Coronoid** — loss of **≥50% height** → instability

**MCL** — anterior, posterior, transverse bundles; resists **valgus + distraction**; origin **anteroinferior medial epicondyle** → insertion **sublime tubercle**

| MCL bundle | Role |
|---|---|
| **Anterior** | **Most important valgus restraint** (prime medial stabiliser); esp. in flexion (Morrey) |
| **Posterior** (posterior oblique) | **Floor of cubital tunnel** · **primary valgus restraint in maximal flexion** · secondary medial stabiliser · tension ↑ **60–120° flexion** → contracture **limits flexion** |
| **Transverse** | **No role** in valgus stability or ROM |

**2nd most important valgus restraint** — **radial head**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnotrochlear articulation

![MCL – A anterior bundle, B posterior oblique band, C transverse, D annular, E oblique cord](../../MCQ/attachments/q5863-a-medial.jpg)

**LCL complex** — **RCL** · **LUCL** · **accessory collateral ligament** · **annular ligament**; whole complex = **capsular thickening**; arises from **isometric point on lateral capitellum**

| LCL component | Key fact |
|---|---|
| **RCL** | **Originates at axis of rotation for flexion** |
| **LUCL** | **Primary restraint to varus + external rotation** through full arc; origin **posterior lateral epicondyle** → insertion **crista supinatoris** (✗ Wrong options: sublime tubercle) |
| Accessory collateral | ± substantial lateral stability (with RCL) |
| **Annular** | **PRUJ** stability |

**LCL repair** — optimal stability with **appropriately tensioned** repair

![LCL complex – A RCL, B annular, C LUCL](../../MCQ/attachments/q6194-a-21.jpg)

**Not LCL complex** — ulnohumeral joint (primary but not LCL) · radiocapitellar joint (secondary) · anterior bundle UCL · **transverse ligament (MCL complex)**

**LUCL injury** — abnormal **supination of ulna** on humerus → radial head subluxes **posteriorly** → **PLRI** (external rotatory subluxation of ulna + valgus on trochlea)

**LCL disruption** — **uniform** in dislocations + fracture-dislocations; commonest pattern = **avulsion from distal humerus** (McKee)

**Radiocapitellar joint** — important valgus constraint; radial head = **~30% of valgus stability**, most at **0–30° flexion / pronation**

**Capsule** — greatest stability contribution **in extension** · anterior capsule resists varus-valgus **in extension, not flexion** (Morrey)

### Nerves of the elbow

| Nerve | Origin | At the elbow |
|---|---|---|
| **Musculocutaneous** | Lateral cord | Between **biceps + brachialis** (supplies both); exits laterally distal to biceps tendon → **LABC deep to cephalic vein** |
| **Radial** | Posterior cord | **Triangular interval** (teres major, long head triceps, humerus) → spiral groove **13 cm** above trochlea → pierces **lateral IM septum 7.5 cm** above trochlea (middle/distal third) → between **brachialis + brachioradialis** → superficial to capsule at radiocapitellar joint |
| **Median** | Medial + lateral cords | With brachial artery (lateral → medial); superficial to brachialis; **no branches in arm** |
| **Ulnar** | Medial cord | Medial to brachial artery → pierces medial IM septum (**arcade of Struthers**) → cubital tunnel; **no branches in arm**; first FCU branch **distal to elbow** |

### Blood supply

**Brachial artery** — medial in arm → enters cubital fossa → splits into **radial + ulnar** arteries

**Cubital fossa** — contents lateral → medial: **biceps tendon, brachial artery, median nerve** · borders: **brachioradialis** (lateral), **pronator teres** (medial), distal humerus (proximal)

**Branches** — superior/inferior ulnar collateral · nutrient/muscular · supratrochlear

## 2 Mechanism

### Axis of forearm rotation

**Flexion–extension axis** — centre of trochlea; line through isometric points of capitellum + trochlea

**Pronation–supination axis** — **centre of radial head → fovea of ulnar head** (capitellum → radial head → distal ulna); forms a **cone**

**✗ Axis distractors** — radial head–radial styloid · radial head–ulnar styloid · coronoid–sigmoid notch · coronoid–radial styloid

**Forearm rotation** — radiocapitellar + PRUJ + DRUJ + IOM + forearm muscles · **pronators**: pronator teres + quadratus · **supinators**: biceps + supinator

### Biomechanics calculation – moment arms & anchor strength

**Joint reaction force** — large; **short, inefficient lever arms** (biceps inserts near COR) → degenerative change · static loads ≈ body weight · **dynamic loads > body weight**

**Free body** — ΣM = 0 → **B × 5 cm = W × 15 cm → B = 3W**

![Free body diagram – biceps 5 cm, weight 15 cm](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3078-elbow-moved.jpg)

**Anchor problem** — arm 20 N at 15 cm, biceps 5 cm → biceps force **60 N** × safety factor 3 = **180 N** minimum pullout

## 3 Presentation

### Olecranon bursitis (student's elbow)

**Stem (TO)** — direct blow posterior elbow + **olecranon spur** → continued sport → **painless swelling with effusion** over elbow

**Answer (TO)** — key **not provided by source**; module label = **olecranon bursitis (student's elbow)** · options also list miner's elbow, little leaguer's elbow, golfer's elbow — read the options

## 5 Management

### Posterior bundle of MCL – release for flexion

**Release for flexion loss** — **posterior (oblique) band of MCL + posterior capsule**; flexion 89° → **127°** (Wada; medial incision, ulnar nerve freed)

**Limited extension** — **anterior capsulectomy**

**✗ Release distractors** — **anterior band MCL** (→ valgus instability) · transverse band (no ROM gain) · **annular ligament** (never) · oblique cord of IOM

### Arthrodesis position

| Situation | Position |
|---|---|
| **Unilateral** | **90° flexion**, **0–7° valgus** |
| **Bilateral** | One at **110°** (feeding) · other at **65°** (perineal hygiene) |

### Diagnostic injection

**Soft spot** — triangle of **lateral epicondyle + olecranon + radial head** → intra-articular injection

### Complex instability (OB)

**Simple dislocation** — closed reduction + short immobilisation; recurrent instability **uncommon**

**Complex / persistent instability** — fix or replace bony elements + ligament repair ± **hinged fixation**

## X vs Y

| | **MCL (anterior bundle)** | **LCL complex (LUCL)** |
|---|---|---|
| Resists | **Valgus** + distraction | **Varus + external rotation** |
| Origin | **Anteroinferior medial epicondyle** | **Posterior lateral epicondyle** / isometric point capitellum |
| Insertion | **Sublime tubercle** | **Crista supinatoris** |
| Failure | Valgus instability | **PLRI** |
| Backup | **Radial head** (2nd valgus restraint) | — |

| | **Posterior bundle MCL** | **Anterior bundle MCL** |
|---|---|---|
| Valgus role | Primary only in **max flexion** | **Most important overall** |
| Release for stiffness? | **Yes — restores flexion** | **No —** valgus instability |
| Cubital tunnel | **Floor** | — |

## How it's asked

**"Primary stabiliser against valgus (labelled figure)?"** → **anterior bundle MCL**\
✗ Wrong options: posterior bundle = secondary · transverse · annular · biceps

**"Structure A on lateral ligament figure?"** → **radial collateral ligament** (B annular, C LUCL; transverse = MCL complex)

**"Sublime tubercle is the insertion of?"** → **anterior bundle MCL**

**"True statement about elbow stability?"** → **RCL originates at axis of flexion**\
✗ Wrong options: LUCL on sublime tubercle · anterior bundle primary in max flexion · ulnotrochlear 2nd valgus restraint

**"Varus + external rotation instability — which primary stabilisers incompetent?"** → **LUCL, accessory collateral, annular, RCL**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnohumeral · radiocapitellar · transverse ligament

**"Release to restore flexion?"** → **posterior oblique band MCL (B)**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior band · annular

**"Axis of forearm rotation?"** → **radial head → ulnar head**

**"Anchor pullout, 20 N at 15 cm, biceps 5 cm, safety 3?"** → **180N**

**"Posterior elbow blow, olecranon spur, painless effusion?"** → **student's elbow** (olecranon bursitis; key not provided, TO)

**"Optimal unilateral arthrodesis?"** → **90° flexion**

**"Safe zone for radial head fixation?"** → **lateral 120° non-articular**

# Elbow Arthritis

*Shoulder & Elbow · 13 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Types & epidemiology

**3 types** — **osteoarthritis (primary)** · **post-traumatic** · **inflammatory**

| | **Primary OA** | **Post-traumatic** | **Inflammatory (RA)** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Frequency | **Rare** (symptomatic ~**2%**) | **2nd most common** | **Most common / most prevalent** elbow arthritis (historically) |
| Patient | **Middle-aged ♂ laborer**, **♂:♀ 4:1**, 20–70 y (**avg 50**), **dominant arm** | **Younger** than other types | Older ♀; elbow involved in **20–50%** of RA |
| Cause | Strenuous manual labour | Non-op radial head fracture, (fracture-)dislocation, traumatic instability | RA, psoriatic, SLE, PVNS |
| Joint | **Radiocapitellar** preferentially; **ulnohumeral relatively spared** | Whole joint or focal ulnohumeral/radiocapitellar | Global; symmetric narrowing |
| Hallmark | **Osteophytes, capsular contracture, loose bodies** | Incongruity → altered load; stiffness, instability, malunion/nonunion | Synovitis, **osteopenia**, ligament attenuation, instability |

**Secondary OA causes** — post-traumatic · **OCD** · **synovial osteochondromatosis** · **MUCL insufficiency / valgus extension overload**

### Genes & lab markers

**RA pathology** — **pannus** → **hyperplastic synovium** → **collagen + proteoglycan degradation** → chronic synovitis, ligament attenuation, **periarticular osteopenia**, contracture

**RA trap** — synovial proliferation from pannus, **not rheumatoid factor mutation**

**RA pathoanatomy** — fixed flexion contracture · instability · **ulnar** (or less often radial) neuropathy · erosions · cysts · deformity · progressive bone resorption

### Stabilisers (relevance to surgery)

**Primary** — **anterior band MCL** (**anterior oblique fibres** most important; valgus + distraction) · **LCL** · olecranon–coronoid–trochlea congruity

**Secondary** — **radial head** (most important secondary; **30%** valgus stability; **0–30° flexion + pronation**) · capsule (**distraction in full extension**) · anconeus + lateral capsule (varus)

## 3 Presentation

**OA symptoms** — pain at **end range** · **loss of terminal extension** · painful **locking/catching** (loose bodies) · night pain unusual\
✗ Wrong options: mid-range

**OA exam** — forearm rotation preserved early · **ulnar neuropathy in up to 50%**

**RA** — **hand + wrist precede elbow** · fixed flexion contracture · ligamentous incompetence · ± ulnar neuropathy · rheumatoid nodule · **evaluate C-spine in all RA**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** | **AP + lateral**; **C-spine X-rays for RA before surgery** |
| OA osteophytes | **Coronoid process + fossa** · **radial head + fossa** · **olecranon tip + posteromedial olecranon fossa** |
| Joint space | Narrowing; **ulnohumeral relatively preserved** in OA |
| **Loose bodies** | **Underestimated on plain X-ray** |
| RA | **Periarticular erosions + cysts**, osteopenia; **osteophytes not prominent** (✗ Wrong options: characteristic) |
| **CT** | Surgical planning; better defines **osteophytes + loose bodies** |

![Olecranon osteophyte impinging in olecranon fossa](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3084-olecranon-osteophyte.jpg)

### Larsen grading (RA)

| Stage | Radiographs |
|---|---|
| 1 | Soft tissues involved; **near-normal** radiographs |
| 2 | **Periarticular erosions + mild cartilage loss**; soft-tissue swelling, osteopenia |
| 3 | **Marked joint space narrowing** |
| 4 | Advanced erosions **penetrating subchondral plate** |
| 5 | Advanced damage, **loss of articular contour** |

**TEA threshold** — **Larsen 3–5** in patients >65 y

## 5 Management

```
Elbow arthritis – failed NSAIDs, injection, splint, activity modification
├─ Mild–moderate OA, arc >90°, painful end-range, mechanical symptoms → arthroscopic OCA
├─ Severe contracture (e.g. 40–90°), prior ulnar transposition → open: O-K or column procedure
├─ Young (<65), high demand, END-STAGE (OA/RA/PTA), stable → interposition arthroplasty
│      └─ isolated radiocapitellar → anconeus interposition / radial head replacement
└─ >65, low demand, severe (Larsen 3–5) → semiconstrained TEA
```

### Primary OA – osteocapsular arthroplasty (arthroscopic vs open) & patient selection

**Nonop** — NSAIDs · cortisone injection · resting splints · activity modification → **mild–moderate** symptoms

**OCA** — removal of osteophytes (coronoid, olecranon, fossae) + loose bodies + **capsular release**

**Best OCA candidate** — **<65 y**, active, **arc >90°**, **mild–moderate** OA, **painless mid-arc + painful end-range**

| Arthroscopic debridement / release | |
|---|---|
| **Indications** | **Mechanical symptoms (loose bodies)** · capsular contracture · **bony block** to motion · **>90° motion** preferred |
| **Contraindications (relative)** | **Prior ulnar nerve transposition** (nerve location unknown) · **severe contracture / arthrofibrosis** · prior extensive open procedures |
| Not a CI | Prior **olecranon osteotomy** · ulnar nerve **subluxation** (✗ Wrong options: absolute CI; identify pre-op, esp. prior submuscular/intramuscular transposition) |
| Complications | **Neurologic** (**ulnar** palsy commonest, **radial** 2nd — anterolateral portal) · **synovial fistula** · recurrent stiffness |

| Arthroscopic vs open OCA | Arthroscopic |
|---|---|
| **Post-op pain** | **↓** |
| HO / ankylosis | **↓** (✗ Wrong options: equal) |
| Access | **Easy anterior + posterior**, better cartilage view |
| Reliability | **Pain relief** more reliable (open → more predictable **ROM** gain) |

**O'Driscoll & Blonna 8 steps** — posterior view → remove debris + fat pad → osteophytes → posterior capsule release → anterior view → strip anterior capsule → coronoid osteophytes/reshape → excise anterior capsule; **4.8% transient nerve injury** (502 pts)

**Elbow arthroscopy indications** — septic I&D · synovectomy (inflammatory) · debridement + loose body removal (OA) · contracture release · OCD capitellum · lateral epicondylitis release

**Advanced radiocapitellar OA** — consider **radial head replacement**

**Avoid radial head resection in laborer** — ↑ load on arthritic ulnohumeral joint; doesn't fix ROM or end-range impingement

**No PT / dynamic splints** — no role initially in advanced painful arthritis with **fixed** contracture · no static splinting when arc functional + complaint is pain

**Fusion** — severe functional loss; rare (unmanageable **sepsis**, **Charcot**)

![Outerbridge–Kashiwagi olecranon fossa fenestration](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3084-ob-k1.jpg)

**Outerbridge–Kashiwagi (ulnohumeral arthroplasty)** — open **posterior** approach; **burr fenestration of olecranon fossa** → release posterior impingement + anterior access; younger pts with ↓ ROM; **decompress ulnar nerve if pre-op flexion contracture**; failure = missed anterior / peripheral (medial + lateral olecranon) osteophytes

**Column procedure** — medial or lateral open capsular release + bony resection for extrinsic contracture; **lateral most common** (access to all 3 joints) via lateral supracondylar ridge · anterior arthrotomy **ECRL/common extensor interval, stay anterior to LUCL** · posterior: elevate triceps · **go medial to gain flexion** (excise **posterior band MCL**)

**Open release** — elderly regain motion **more reliably than <20 y** (Strubel & Cohen)

### Ulnar nerve – neuritis & decompression when gaining flexion

**Risk** — severely limited pre-op motion: **flexion <100°** · **extensor lag / max extension >60°** → **post-op ulnar nerve dysfunction**

**Rule** — any motion-restoring procedure (OCA, O-K, column, interposition) in such patients → **concomitant ulnar nerve decompression ± transposition**

**Not radial neuritis** — radial tension **↓** with gained extension

**Not column procedure add-ons** — release anterior band MUCL (primary valgus) · release LUCL (primary varus) · radial head resection (rotation full + painless) · **TFL interposition** (reserved **<50 y**)

### Young post-traumatic arthritis – interposition arthroplasty

**Indication** — **young, high-demand, END-STAGE** OA / RA / **post-traumatic** who would otherwise get TEA if older; **no lifting restrictions**; needs **preserved bone stock + inherent stability**

**Technique** — burr distal humerus + proximal ulna congruent → graft over distal humerus (**Achilles allograft**, **autogenous TFL**, dermal/fascial) → ligament repair → **hinged distracting ex-fix** (offloads graft, early ROM, protects repairs)

**Advantage** — can **convert to TEA** later (successful; Blaine)

| Interposition contraindication | Type |
|---|---|
| **Elbow instability** / chronic instability without bone stock for ligament reconstruction | **Absolute** |
| **Active infection** | **Absolute** |
| Elderly **>60 y** (TEA candidates) | Relative |
| Bone loss: **destructive JRA**, **distal humerus osteonecrosis** | → prosthetic replacement |
| Not CI | Young age · post-traumatic · inflammatory · previous debridement |

**Anconeus interposition** — arthritis **isolated to radiocapitellar** joint

**Failed interposition (68 y, pain + instability)** — **convert to TEA**\
✗ Wrong options: revision interposition · hemiarthroplasty (ulnar side arthritic) · arthrodesis

### Rheumatoid elbow – TEA & synovectomy

**Synovectomy** — open or arthroscopic; **young RA, joint space preserved**; most improve

**Radial head resection + synovectomy** — only if synovitis **encircles radial head/neck** + painful crepitus on rotation + **PIN signs** (can't extend thumb)

**Complication of radial head resection** — **rapid progression of ulnohumeral arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: loss of pronation/supination · longitudinal instability with intact IOM · ulnar palsy (less likely)

**End-stage RA** — **semiconstrained TEA** (concomitant ligamentous instability) \
✗ Wrong options: constrained (**high early loosening**) · arthroscopy (incomplete, unpredictable)

**TEA outcome RA vs OA** — **equal or better** for RA\
✗ Wrong options: poorer

### Operative criteria – TEA
