| TEA | |
|---|---|
| **Indications** | **>65 y**, severe arthritis (**Larsen 3–5**) · complex distal humerus fracture, elderly, poor bone · distal humerus non/malunion, elderly low demand · post-traumatic arthritis (lower demand) · **failed interposition** |
| **Contraindications** | **Active <65 y** · **infection** · **Charcot joint** |
| **Restriction** | **Lifelong 5–10 lb lifting** limit |

| TEA design | Feature |
|---|---|
| **Unconstrained** | Needs **competent collaterals**, soft-tissue envelope, good bone |
| **Semiconstrained** | **"Sloppy hinge"** → some varus-valgus + rotational laxity → ↓ bone-cement stress → ↓ loosening; **best longevity + function** |
| **Constrained** | **High early loosening** |

### Elbow arthrodesis position (TO)

| Arthrodesis | Position |
|---|---|
| **Unilateral (TO)** | **90° flexion + neutral to slight valgus** (✗ Wrong options: varus · 110°) |
| **Bilateral (TO)** | One **110°** (feeding) · other **65°** (perineal hygiene) |

## 6 Complications

### Total elbow arthroplasty complications

**Rate** — up to **43%** (Orthobullets) · up to **25%** (OB) — read the options

**Bushing wear** — **most common mode of failure of semiconstrained TEA** at midterm, esp. **post-traumatic indication** (younger, non-adherence to restrictions, unstable injury, deformity, contracture) \
✗ Wrong options: gender · smoking · cement · age

![Bushing wear in semiconstrained TEA](../../MCQ/attachments/q216807-a-bushing-wear.jpg)

**Infection / wound risk factors** — prior elbow surgery · prior infection (**S. epidermidis**) · psychiatric comorbidity · **RA** · wound drainage · reoperation · poor skin (**long-term steroids**); two-stage revision → poor survival

**Aseptic loosening** — **linked** implants · **post-traumatic** OA

**Instability** — **unlinked** implants

**Others** — **ulnar neuritis** (iatrogenic or after motion restored without decompression) · **triceps avulsion/insufficiency** · fracture · mechanical failure

## X vs Y

| | **Arthroscopic OCA** | **Interposition arthroplasty** | **TEA** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Stage | **Mild–moderate** | **End-stage** | **End-stage** |
| Age / demand | Active, <65 | **Young, high demand** | **>65, low demand** |
| Motion | Arc **>90°** | Any; needs stability | Any |
| Key CI | **Prior ulnar transposition**, severe contracture | **Instability**, infection | **Active <65**, infection, **Charcot** |
| Restriction | — | **None** | **5–10 lb lifetime** |

| | **Elbow OA** | **Elbow RA** |
|---|---|---|
| X-ray | **Osteophytes**, loose bodies, ulnohumeral spared | **Erosions, cysts, osteopenia**, symmetric narrowing |
| Symptom | **End-range** pain | Diffuse pain, effusion |
| Instability | No | **Ligament attenuation** |
| End-stage op | OCA / interposition / TEA | **Semiconstrained TEA**; synovectomy early |

## How it's asked

**"Plumber, advanced OA, lacks 45° extension, flexes to 110° — initial treatment?"** → **osteophyte resection + capsular release**\
✗ Wrong options: PT/dynamic splints · TEA · fusion · radial head resection

**"Carpenter, mid-range locking, arc 35–130°, failed injection?"** → **arthroscopic capsular release + loose body removal + osteophyte decompression**\
✗ Wrong options: interposition · radial head arthroplasty · TEA

**"Firefighter 58, arc 30–130°, pain — most predictable pain relief?"** → **arthroscopic debridement**\
✗ Wrong options: open debridement = ROM · static splint · interposition

**"True of arthroscopic vs open OCA?"** → **less post-op pain**\
✗ Wrong options: equal HO · limited access · radial palsy commonest · subluxation absolute CI

**"Relative CI to arthroscopic debridement in post-traumatic OA?"** → **prior ulnar nerve transposition**\
✗ Wrong options: loose bodies · contracture · bony block · olecranon osteotomy

**"Who benefits most from arthroscopic OCA?"** → **50-y ♂, painful limited end-range**\
✗ Wrong options: RA destruction · end-stage · elderly pain throughout

**"Best OCA candidate?"** → **51-y mailman, mild OA, arc 30–135°**\
✗ Wrong options: 40–90° contracture → open · prior transposition · end-stage → interposition

**"OCA for 50–90° arc — specific complication?"** → **ulnar neuritis**\
✗ Wrong options: radial neuritis · implant wear

**"Lateral column procedure, pre-op arc 30–90° — add?"** → **ulnar nerve decompression ± transposition**\
✗ Wrong options: release anterior MUCL · LUCL · radial head resection · TFL

**"Distraction interposition arthroplasty best for?"** → **41-y laborer, post-traumatic arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: destructive JRA · osteonecrosis · 70-y RA

**"Failed interposition at 68 y with pain + instability?"** → **convert to TEA**

**"Contraindication to interposition arthroplasty?"** → **elbow instability**\
✗ Wrong options: young age · PTA · inflammatory · prior debridement

**"40-y inflammatory end-stage, stable, arc 75°?"** → **Achilles interposition**\
✗ Wrong options: anconeus = isolated radiocapitellar · TEA

**"Burred surfaces + Achilles allograft + hinged ex-fix — best candidate?"** → **20-y severe post-traumatic arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: active infection · chronic instability · elderly → TEA

**"61-y RA, end-stage destruction?"** → **semiconstrained TEA**\
✗ Wrong options: constrained (early loosening)

**"RA elbow pathology — true?"** → **proteoglycan + collagen destruction**\
✗ Wrong options: osteophytes · worse TEA results · unconstrained · RF mutation

**"RA synovectomy + radial head resection — likely complication?"** → **rapid ulnohumeral arthritis**

**"Bushing wear at 6 y — risk factor?"** → **indication for TEA (post-traumatic)**\
✗ Wrong options: gender · smoking · cement · age

**"Optimal unilateral elbow arthrodesis?"** → **90° flexion + slight valgus** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Elbow Arthroscopy: Indications & Approach

*Shoulder & Elbow · 14 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Indications

| Source | Indications |
|---|---|
| **Orthobullets** | **Loose body removal**, osteophyte debridement, **synovectomy**, **capsular release (stiffness)**, **OCD capitellum**, lateral epicondylitis, **septic arthritis** debridement |
| **TO** | Loose bodies · **OCD capitellum** · osteochondral lesions radial head · osteophytes humerus/olecranon · **RA (partial synovectomy)** · stiff elbow (debridement, lysis of adhesions) · **tennis elbow release** · evaluation of painful elbow · **inflamed olecranon bursa** · **intra-articular fractures** |

**Osteocapsular arthroplasty** — young patient with degenerative elbow where **TEA inappropriate**, or significant contracture

**Advantages** — better articular visualisation, ↓ post-op pain, **faster recovery**

**Disadvantages** — technically demanding; **high risk of NV injury** (proximity to joint)

### Contraindications (prior ulnar nerve transposition)

| Source | Contraindications |
|---|---|
| **Orthobullets** | Prior trauma, surgical scarring, **previous ulnar nerve transposition** |
| **TO** | **Bony ankylosis** · **severe fibrous ankylosis** (scope can't enter safely) · **active infection** · surgery altering anatomy e.g. **anterior ulnar nerve transposition (relative)** |
| **OB** | **Ulnar neuropathy with prior submuscular transposition** = contraindication |

**Not contraindications (OB)** — **Panner disease** · loose body ulnohumeral joint · **s/p radial head ORIF** · elbow stiffness · **osteoarthritis**

**Ulnar nerve subluxation** — **not an absolute contraindication**; identify pre-op (esp. prior submuscular/intramuscular transposition)

### Capsular anatomy & distension (TO)

**Capsule (TO)** — attached to articular margins; fibres blend with **annular ligament**; anterior → above coronoid + radial fossae; posterior → above olecranon fossa

**Tension (TO)** — taut **anteriorly in extension**, **posteriorly in flexion**

**Maximum distension (TO)** — **70–80° flexion**; capacity **25–30 mL**\
✗ Wrong options: 90–120° · 10° · 120–150°

## 5 Management

### Positioning & joint distension (TO)

**Positions** — **supine** (suspended), **prone**, **lateral decubitus**

**Most commonly used (TO)** — **lateral**, elbow hanging freely on support (**flexed**)

**All positions = elbow flexed (TO)** — **elbow extended, prone** = not a position

**Anaesthesia** — **general** (muscle relaxation, tolerates prone/lateral) · regional: **no immediate post-op nerve check**, poor tolerance of position

### Portal technique

**Distend first** — **fully distend joint through lateral soft spot** → NV structures move away from trocar

**Nick and spread** — skin only with blade, **hemostat spread** to capsule, **blunt trocar**

**Elbow position** — **anterior portals at 90° flexion** · posterior portals in some extension

**Kit** — standard **30° scope** · tourniquet · **solid cannulas** → maintain distension, ↓ extravasation\
✗ Wrong options: trephinated

**Landmarks** — olecranon, both epicondyles, radiocapitellar joint, **ulnar nerve**; **mark before insufflation** (distension distorts)

**Order** — distend → viewing portal (medial or lateral) → working portal under **direct vision + needle localisation**

**First portal** — **anterolateral** after distension (TO, OB) · establishing a **medial portal first** (before swelling) may be advantageous (Orthobullets) — read the options

**Viewing vs working (TO)** — **anterolateral = viewing**; **proximal anteromedial = most common working portal** + most commonly used medial portal

**Posteromedial portal** — **avoided** (lies over **ulnar nerve**)

### Factors that ↓ NV injury (TO)

| ✓ Manoeuvre (TO) | Protects |
|---|---|
| **90° flexion + joint distension** | Radial nerve moves anteriorly |
| **Pronation** | **PIN** |
| **Keep lateral portal more proximal** | **Radial nerve / PIN** |
| Avoid deep knife; hemostat + blunt trocar | Lateral + posterior antebrachial cutaneous |
| Draw landmarks, **palpate ulnar nerve** | Ulnar |
| **Low-flow infusion 40–45 mL/min or gravity** (✗ Wrong options: pressurised) | Compartment syndrome |
| Instrument tips always in view; no suction near nerves | All |

**NOT a protective manoeuvre (TO)** — **more distal lateral portal** (risk ↑ with distal placement)

### Portals & nerves at risk

| Portal | Location | Use | Nerves at risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Proximal anterolateral** | **1–2 cm proximal, 1 cm anterior** to lateral epicondyle | Views anteromedial joint; **safest anterolateral** (TO) | **Radial** (↓ risk more proximally) |
| Mid anterolateral (TO) | Just proximal, ~1 cm anterior to radiocapitellar joint | — | — |
| **Distal anterolateral** | **1–3 cm distal, 1 cm anterior** to lateral epicondyle; anterior to radial head/neck | 1st portal supine; radial head, medial elbow, coronoid, trochlea, brachialis insertion, coronoid fossa | **PIN** + **lateral antebrachial cutaneous**; **radial at maximum risk** (TO) → rarely used |
| **Direct lateral (soft spot)** | Triangle **olecranon – radial head – lateral epicondyle** | **Initial distension**; posterior compartment (capitellum, radial head, radioulnar joint) | **Relatively safe**; LABC |
| **Anteromedial** (direct medial) | **2 cm anterior + 2 cm distal** to medial epicondyle; through flexor-pronator | Augments proximal AM; medial recess; place under direct vision | **MABC + median** (+ basilic vein, TO) |
| **Proximal anteromedial (superomedial)** | **2 cm proximal** to medial epicondyle, **anterior to IM septum** | Entire anterior compartment, radial head, capitellum, coronoid, trochlea; **most common working portal** (TO) | **MABC**; ulnar (7 mm, posterior to septum) + median (OB, TO) |
| **Straight posterior (transtriceps)** | **3 cm proximal** to olecranon, midline through triceps | Partial extension; **olecranon osteophytes**, posteromedial loose bodies | **Posterior antebrachial cutaneous**; ulnar **15–25 mm** away (OB) |
| **Posterolateral** | **2–3 cm proximal** to olecranon, **just lateral to triceps** (centre of anconeus triangle) | **20–30° flexion** (relax triceps); **best posterior compartment**, radiocapitellar (**OCD capitellum**), olecranon fossa | PABC, medial brachial cutaneous, ulnar (Orthobullets) · PABC + **lateral brachial cutaneous** (TO) |
| ✗ Wrong option: **posteromedial** | Medial to triceps | **Not used** | **Ulnar nerve** — unacceptably high |

![Standard elbow arthroscopy portals marked](../../MCQ/attachments/q215632-illa-standard-portals-for-elbow-arthroscopy.jpg)

**Proximal anterolateral location** — **1–2 cm proximal** (Orthobullets) · **2 cm proximal** (TO, OB) · **3 cm proximal** (one OB explanation) — read the options

**Distal anterolateral location** — **1–3 cm distal** (Orthobullets) · **2–3 / 3 cm distal** (TO) · **1–2 cm distal** (OB)

**Straight posterior location** — **3 cm proximal** (Orthobullets, OB) · **2–3 cm proximal, 2 cm medial to posterolateral** (TO)

**Posterior compartment workhorses** — **straight posterior + posterolateral**, both **3 cm proximal to olecranon tip**\
✗ Wrong options: any posteromedial portal

### Nerve distances (OB)

| Portal | Nerve – distance |
|---|---|
| Anterolateral (joint line) | **Radial 4.8 mm** · PABC 12.6 mm |
| **Proximal anterolateral** | **Radial 4–7 mm** · PIN 1–14 mm |
| Anteromedial | **MABC 1 mm · median 7 mm** (one OB) · MABC 8.9 · median 12.9 · ulnar 22.1 mm (another OB) |
| Proximal anteromedial | MABC 1 mm · **median 12 mm** · ulnar 7 mm (posterior to septum) |
| Posterolateral | Radial 4.8 mm (3–8) (OB) |
| Straight posterior | Ulnar **~20 mm (15–25)** |

![Nerves at risk from medial (A) and lateral (B) portals](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3088-structures-around.jpg)

### Radial nerve & anterior capsulectomy

**Radial nerve course** — on the **anterior capsule at mid-capitellum** (OB), runs longitudinally **medial to capitellum** → at risk in capsular excision; seen near probe when viewing radiocapitellar joint from **anteromedial** portal

**Protective layer** — **brachialis (~4 mm)** between capsule and radial nerve **proximal to radial neck**\
✗ Wrong options: anconeus · brachioradialis · biceps · pronator teres

**Danger zone** — distal capsule **over radial head/neck** → transect capsule **≥2–3 cm proximal to radial head**

## 6 Complications

### Nerve injury

**Rate** — **nerve palsy 1–5%**; neurologic = **most common** complication, mostly **transient**

**Greatest risk** — **rheumatoid arthritis**, **elbow contracture**

| Ranking | Answer |
|---|---|
| **Most common complication / nerve (TO, OB)** | **Transient (sensory) ulnar nerve palsy** |
| 2nd | **Radial nerve palsy** (Orthobullets, OB) · **persistent wound drainage**, then transient sensory radial palsy (TO) |
| **Most common permanent nerve palsy (TO)** | **PIN**, then ulnar |
| **Least involved nerve (TO)** | **Median** |

**Mechanism** — **direct** (trocars, instruments, no blunt dissection → neuromas) · **indirect** (**compartment syndrome** from aggressive distension/extravasation; **local anaesthetic extravasation** — transient)

### Deficit → nerve → portal

| Deficit | Nerve | Portal |
|---|---|---|
| **Wrist drop: no wrist + MCP extension** | **Radial nerve proper** | **Proximal anterolateral** |
| Weak wrist extension + **ulnar deviation** (lost ECRL) | Radial nerve proper | Proximal anterolateral |
| **No finger (MCP) extension, partial wrist extension; can't extend thumb IP**; wrist extends in **radial deviation**; no paraesthesia | **PIN** (ECRL spared) | **(Distal) anterolateral** |
| **Paraesthesia dorsal 5th digit**, intrinsic weakness, ↓ finger ab/adduction | **Ulnar** | **Posteromedial** |
| Paraesthesia **medial volar forearm** | MABC | Anteromedial |
| Can't flex wrist | Median | Anteromedial |
| Sensory deficit only | Superficial radial | — |

**PIN palsy** — EPL (thumb IP extension) + finger extensors; **ECRL** innervated **proximal to bifurcation** → spared

**✗ PIN traps** — index abduction = **1st DI (ulnar)** · APB = **median** (APL = PIN) · "wrist extension + radial deviation lost" = radial nerve proper

![PIN palsy after anterolateral portal: finger drop, partial wrist extension](../../MCQ/attachments/q3258-a-pin-clinical-pic-palsy.jpg)

### Other complications

**HO / ankylosis** — **less than open**; minimise bleeding

**Infection** — **sinus tract (posterolateral portal)**; ↑ risk with **intra-articular steroid at time of arthroscopy**; possibly ↑ with **inflammatory arthritis**

### Infection prevention – avoid intra-articular steroid

**✓ Prevent infection** — **avoid intra-op steroid injection**

**✗ Traps** — avoid posterior midline portals · avoid scope in OA · absorbable subcuticular suture only (no difference) · restrict ROM 3–4 wk (↑ stiffness)

## X vs Y

| | **Proximal anterolateral** | **Distal anterolateral** |
|---|---|---|
| Location | 1–2 cm **proximal**, 1 cm anterior to LE | 1–3 cm **distal**, 1 cm anterior to LE |
| Nerve | **Radial nerve proper** (wrist drop) | **PIN** (finger + thumb IP extension), LABC |
| Safety | **Safer** → more commonly used | Riskier → rarely used |

| | **Anteromedial** | **Proximal anteromedial** |
|---|---|---|
| Location | 2 cm anterior + 2 cm distal to ME | 2 cm proximal to ME, anterior to septum |
| Nerves | **MABC (1 mm) + median (7 mm)** | MABC (1 mm), ulnar (7 mm), median (12 mm) |
| Role | Augment PAMP, medial recess | **Most common working portal** |

## How it's asked

**"First portal in elbow arthroscopy?"** → **anterolateral** (TO)

**"Most common nerve involved?"** → **ulnar** (TO)

**"Most common complication?"** → **transient sensory ulnar nerve palsy** (TO)

**"Most common permanent nerve palsy?"** → **PIN** (TO)

**"Least involved nerve?"** → **median** (TO)

**"NOT a manoeuvre to avoid nerve injury?"** → **more distal lateral portal** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: pronation + 90° · distension · low flow

**"Most commonly used working portal?"** → **proximal anteromedial** (TO)

**"NOT a position for elbow arthroscopy?"** → **elbow extended, prone** (TO)

**"Maximum elbow distension at?"** → **70–80° flexion** (TO)

**"Portal generally not used?"** → **posteromedial** (ulnar nerve)

**"Shaver through anterolateral portal, finger drop — structure?"** → **PIN**

**"Structure nearest probe, radiocapitellar joint from anteromedial portal?"** → **radial nerve**

**"Greatest risk with proximal anterolateral portal?"** → **radial nerve**

**"Two safest posterior compartment portals?"** → **straight posterior + posterolateral, 3 cm proximal to olecranon**

**"Portal 2 cm proximal, 1 cm anterior to LE — deficit if nerve injured?"** → **loss of wrist + MCP extension**

**"Can't extend thumb IP post-op — portal?"** → **anterolateral**\
✗ Wrong options: direct lateral = LABC · posterolateral = PABC

**"Portal 2 cm anterior + 2 cm distal to ME — risk?"** → **MABC + median**

**"AL, AM and posteromedial portals used — likely deficit?"** → **paraesthesia dorsal 5th digit** (ulnar)

**"Protects radial nerve in anterior capsulectomy?"** → **brachialis**

**"Prior radial head ORIF, AL portal — iatrogenic finding?"** → **can't extend thumb IP**\
✗ Wrong options: wrist extension + radial deviation = radial proper

**"Contraindication to elbow arthroscopy?"** → **prior submuscular ulnar nerve transposition**\
✗ Wrong options: Panner · loose body · stiffness · s/p radial head ORIF

**"Prevent infection after elbow arthroscopy?"** → **avoid intra-op steroid injection**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Elbow Physical Exam

*Shoulder & Elbow · 15 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Exam sequence & normal values

**Sequence** — inspection → palpation → ROM → stability → motor → sensory → vascular → provocative tests

| Parameter | Normal | Functional |
|---|---|---|
| **Carrying angle** (full extension) | **~11° ♂ · ~13° ♀** (exam note) · ♂ 5–10° / ♀ 10–15° (anatomy note); ↑ in throwers | — |
| **Flexion–extension** | 0–140° | **30–130°** |
| **Pronation** | 75° | **50°** |
| **Supination** | 85° | **50°** |

**Pronation–supination** — test with shoulders fully adducted + elbow at 90°; compare both sides, active + passive

**End point** — **soft = effusion / capsular tightness** · **firm = mechanical block** (loose body, fracture, osteophyte)

**Throwers** — loss of full extension even **without pathology**

### Motor – muscle & root

| Movement | Muscle | Root |
|---|---|---|
| Elbow flexion, **full supination** | Brachialis + biceps | C5–C6 |
| Elbow flexion, **neutral** (thumb to ceiling) | **Brachioradialis** | C6 |
| Elbow extension | Triceps | C7 |
| Pronation | Flexor-pronator mass | C7–C8 |
| Supination | Biceps (primarily) | C6 |
| Wrist extension | ECRL, ECRB, ECU | C6–C8 |
| Wrist flexion | FCR, FCU | C6–C8 |
| Finger/thumb extension | EDC, EPL | C7–C8 |
| Finger/thumb flexion | FDS, FDP, FPL | C7–C8 |
| Intrinsics | Lumbricals, interossei | T1 |

**Brachial artery** — anterior elbow, **medial to biceps tendon**; also check radial + ulnar pulses

## 3 Presentation

### Inspection

**Effusion** — elbow held at **70–80° flexion** (= max capsular distension) · fullness of **soft spot** (radial head + lateral epicondyle + olecranon)

**Cubital tunnel** — **1st dorsal interosseous / 1st web atrophy** · ring/small clawing (more with **Guyon's** — unopposed FDP)

**Distal biceps rupture** — **medial ecchymosis** + swelling · proximal retraction of belly = **reverse Popeye sign**

### Palpation – point of maximal tenderness

| Condition | Tender point |
|---|---|
| **MCL injury** | **MCL origin, just inferior to medial epicondyle**; palpate at **50–70° flexion** (flexor-pronator mass moves anterior) |
| **Valgus extension overload** | **Posteromedial olecranon** |
| **Lateral epicondylitis** | ECRB insertion, **few mm distal to tip of lateral epicondyle** |
| **Radial tunnel syndrome** | **3–5 cm distal to lateral epicondyle** (more distal than tennis elbow) |
| **Medial epicondylitis** | **5–10 mm distal + anterior** to medial epicondyle; swelling/warmth if inflamed |

**Radial head** — best palpated while rotating forearm pronation → supination

**Ulnar nerve subluxation** — over medial epicondyle, extension → flexion · hypermobility in **33% of adults**\
✗ Wrong options: necessarily CuTS

**Snapping medial triceps** — snaps over medial epicondyle on **resisted extension from full flexion** (differentiate from ulnar nerve)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Stability – MCL (valgus)

**Valgus stress test** — flex **20–30°** (**unlocks olecranon**) + **externally rotate humerus** + valgus → tests **MCL**

| Test | Technique | Positive | Accuracy |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Milking maneuver** | **Pull patient's thumb**, forearm **supinated**, elbow **90°**; supine or seated/standing | Apprehension, instability or **pain at MCL origin** | Sens **87.5%**, **NPV 100%** |
| **Moving valgus stress** | Same position + valgus while ranging full arc; shoulder **fully ER** | Pain at MCL origin **between 70° and 120°** (early acceleration 70° · late cocking 120°) | **Sens 100%, spec 75%** |

![Milking maneuver](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob322131-milking-maneuver.jpg)

**Valgus extension overload** — **pain with forced elbow extension** · valgus in terminal extension reproduces pain · **varus loading ↓ pain**

### Stability – LCL / posterolateral rotatory instability

| Test | Technique | Positive / accuracy |
|---|---|---|
| **Lateral pivot-shift** | Supine, arm overhead, shoulder fully ER, **forearm supinated + valgus**, extension → flexion | Pain/apprehension or **clunk at 40°** (radiocapitellar dislocated); 2nd clunk with ↑ flexion (triceps reduces radial head); **more reliable under anaesthesia** |
| **PLR drawer** | Supine, elbow **40°**, forearm supinated, AP force over lateral proximal forearm | Apprehension or **skin dimple** (radial head posterior subluxation) |
| **Chair push-up** | Push up from chair, forearm **supinated** | Unable / apprehension; sens **87.5%** (**100% with prone push-up**) |
| **Prone push-up** | Push-up, forearm supinated | Unable; sens 87.5% (100% with chair test) |
| **Table-top relocation** | Press-up on table edge, elbow lateral, forearm supinated; 3 parts | Pain in parts 1 + 3, **relief with thumb on radial head (part 2)** = PLRI |

**Table-top (wrong option)** — pain in **all 3 parts** = intra-articular **radial head fracture**

### Provocative tests – nerve

| Condition | Test / sign | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| Cubital tunnel | **Froment** | Thumb **IP flexion by FPL (AIN)** on key pinch → compensates adductor pollicis (ulnar) |
| Cubital tunnel | **Jeanne** | Thumb **MCP hyperextension** + adduction by **EPL (radial)** on key pinch |
| Cubital tunnel | **Wartenberg** | Persistent **small finger abduction** + extension; weak intrinsics + **unopposed EDM** |
| Cubital tunnel | **Masse** | Flat palmar arch, lost ulnar hand elevation (weak **ODQ**, ↓ small MCP flexion) |
| Cubital tunnel | **Tinel** | Over cubital tunnel, **elbow extended** |
| Cubital tunnel | **Elbow flexion test** | Symptoms with flexion **>60 s** |
| Radial tunnel | **Resisted long finger extension** · **resisted supination** (elbow + wrist extended) · **passive pronation + wrist flexion** | Pain at radial tunnel (weakness from pain); supinator stretch → **250 mmHg** (normal 50) |
| PIN syndrome | Resisted supination ↑ pain · **normal tenodesis** (vs extensor tendon rupture) | Weak finger MCP extension; **wrist extends in radial deviation** (ECRL intact, ECU absent) |
| AIN syndrome | **OK sign** (FDP index + FPL) | **Can't make OK**; weak **PQ** = weak resisted pronation with **elbow maximally flexed** |
| AIN vs FPL rupture (RA) | **Tenodesis** | Intact tendons: passive wrist extension flexes thumb IP + index DIP |

**Cubital tunnel motor** — **weak pinch** (up to **70%** lost; adductor pollicis)

### Pronator syndrome – site-specific tests

**Pronator syndrome** — **Tinel proximal anterior forearm**, not at wrist · wrist-flexion provocation as in CTS

| Resisted test | Compression site |
|---|---|
| **Elbow flexion + forearm supination** | **Bicipital aponeurosis (lacertus)** |
| **Forearm pronation, elbow extended** | **Two heads of pronator teres** |
| **FDS contraction to middle finger** | **FDS fibrous arch** |

### Sensory

**Cutaneous nerves** — medial, lateral, posterior antebrachial cutaneous · ulnar · median · superficial radial

**Cubital tunnel** — ↓ 2PD **small + ulnar ring** · **↓ dorsal ulnar hand** = proximal to wrist (dorsal branch arises **5 cm proximal** to wrist)

**Pronator syndrome** — ↓ sensation in **palmar cutaneous branch** territory (arises **4–5 cm proximal** to carpal tunnel) · not in CTS

### Provocative tests – tendon

| Condition | Test | Key fact |
|---|---|---|
| **Triceps rupture** | **Modified Thompson squeeze** | Prone, forearm hanging; squeeze triceps → **no elbow extension** = complete tear (triceps + lateral expansion) |
| **Distal biceps** | **Hook test** | Elbow 90°, **full supination**, hook **lateral** edge; intact = finger **1 cm beneath tendon**; **sens + spec 100%** |
| Distal biceps | **Ruland biceps squeeze** | Elbow **60–80°**, slight pronation; squeeze belly → **no supination** = positive; **sens 96%** |
| Distal biceps | **Biceps crease interval (BCI)** | Crease → cusp distance **>6 cm or 1.2× contralateral**; sens 92%, spec 100% |
| Distal biceps | **Passive forearm pronation** | Belly moves proximal on supination, distal on pronation (intact) |
| Distal biceps | Strength | **Loss of supination > flexion** |

**Hook test false positives** — **partial tear** · **intact lacertus fibrosus** · **underlying brachialis**

**Biceps triad** — hook + passive pronation + BCI in sequence → **100% sens + 100% spec** for complete rupture

![Hook test](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob322131-hook-test.jpg)

![Biceps crease interval](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob322131-biceps-crease-test.jpg)

**Lateral epicondylitis** — pain on resisted wrist extension (elbow extended, pronated) · **Maudsley = resisted middle finger extension** (ECRB) · maximal wrist flexion · passive wrist flexion in pronation

**Chair test** — elbow extended, forearm pronated, shoulder flexed, lift chair → lateral elbow pain = **lateral epicondylitis**

**Medial epicondylitis** — pain on **resisted pronation + wrist flexion** · **no instability/apprehension** on valgus stress or milking (vs MCL)

## 5 Management

### Olecranon bursitis – aspiration & compression

**Cause** — **repeated minor trauma** (leaning on elbow, desk work) > single injury → inflamed lining secretes fluid

![Olecranon bursitis](../../MCQ/attachments/q217371-a-bursa.jpg)

```
Aseptic olecranon bursitis
├─ First line → nonoperative (more effective + safer than surgery)
└─ Aspiration chosen → posterolateral needle, aspirate alone
       └─ then → compressive wrap (bursal walls adhere)
```

**Aspiration ✓** — **alone** (no steroid) + **compressive dressing** → walls adhere, prevents reaccumulation

**Approach** — **posterolateral**\
✗ Wrong options: posteromedial → ulnar nerve

**Avoid steroid** — **↑ septic bursitis** (local immunosuppression); no outcome benefit · **recurrence occurs with or without steroid**\
✗ Wrong options: "steroid ↑ recurrence"

**LUCL rupture** — from repeated steroid injections for **lateral epicondylitis**, not bursa aspiration

| Finding | Septic | Aseptic |
|---|---|---|
| Tenderness | 88% | 36% |
| Erythema/cellulitis | 83% | 27% |
| Warmth | 84% | 56% |
| Trauma / skin lesion | 50% | 25% |
| Fever | 38% | **0%** |

**Septic vs aseptic** — clinical + lab data **overlap** → hard to distinguish

## X vs Y

| | **Lateral epicondylitis** | **Radial tunnel syndrome** |
|---|---|---|
| Tender point | **Few mm distal** to lateral epicondyle (ECRB) | **3–5 cm distal** |
| Provocation | Maudsley, chair test, resisted wrist extension | Resisted long finger extension, resisted supination |

| | **MCL injury** | **Medial epicondylitis** |
|---|---|---|
| Tender point | **Just inferior** to medial epicondyle | **5–10 mm distal + anterior** |
| Milking / valgus stress | **Pain, apprehension, instability** | **No** instability or apprehension |
| Provocation | Moving valgus stress 70–120° | Resisted pronation + wrist flexion |

| | **Pronator syndrome** | **Carpal tunnel syndrome** |
|---|---|---|
| Tinel | **Proximal forearm** | Wrist |
| Palmar cutaneous branch sensation | **↓** | **Spared** |

| | **Cubital tunnel** | **Guyon's canal** |
|---|---|---|
| Clawing | Less | **More** (unopposed FDP) |
| Dorsal ulnar hand | **↓** | Spared |

## How it's asked

**"Olecranon bursa aspiration — true statement?"** → **compressive wrap after aspiration alone promotes bursal wall adherence**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid ↑ recurrence · posteromedial · similar infection risk with steroid · LUCL rupture

**"Steroid injected into olecranon bursa — risk?"** → **↑ septic bursitis**

**"Needle approach for olecranon bursa?"** → **posterolateral** (avoid ulnar nerve)

**"Position to palpate MCL origin?"** → **50–70° flexion**, just inferior to medial epicondyle

**"Elbow position to test valgus stability?"** → **20–30° flexion** (unlocks olecranon), humerus ER

**"Moving valgus stress test positive arc?"** → **70–120°**

**"Clunk at 40° on pivot-shift?"** → **dislocated radiocapitellar joint** (PLRI)

**"Table-top test, pain in all 3 parts?"** → **radial head fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: PLRI

**"Hook test false positive?"** → **partial tear / intact lacertus / brachialis**

**"Tenderness 3–5 cm distal to lateral epicondyle?"** → **radial tunnel syndrome**

**"Elbow held at 70–80° flexion?"** → **effusion** (max capsular volume)

**"Wrist extends into radial deviation?"** → **PIN palsy** (ECRL intact, ECU lost)

**"Weak resisted pronation with elbow fully flexed?"** → **pronator quadratus (AIN)**

**"Resisted pronation, elbow extended reproduces median symptoms?"** → **pronator teres heads**

**"Numbness thenar eminence + forearm Tinel?"** → **pronator syndrome**\
✗ Wrong options: CTS (palmar cutaneous spared)

**"Firm end point to flexion?"** → **mechanical block**\
✗ Wrong options: effusion = soft

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Elbow Stiffness and Contractures

*Shoulder & Elbow · 16 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Functional range of motion

| Motion | Functional range |
|---|---|
| **Flexion–extension** | **30°–130°** = **100° arc** (Orthobullets, OB) · **90° arc** (one OB explanation) — read the options |
| **Pronation–supination** | **50°–50°** |

**Tolerance** — **30° loss of extension well tolerated** · **flexion loss causes more dysfunction than extension loss**

### Causes

**Aetiology** — **OA**, **trauma (fractures)**, surgery, **CP**, **TBI**, **burns**, prolonged immobilisation, recurrent haemarthroses, congenital (**arthrogryposis**, **congenital radial head dislocation**)

| Type | Causes |
|---|---|
| **Intrinsic** (intra-articular) | Joint incongruity, synovitis, **loose bodies**, intra-articular fracture, malunion, OCD, post-traumatic arthritis (**coronoid + olecranon tip osteophytes**, radiocapitellar narrowing) |
| **Extrinsic** (extra-articular) | Burn **eschar**, **HO**, **capsular adhesions/contracture**, ligament contracture (**posterior oblique MCL**) |
| **Mixed** | Late effect of intrinsic → extrinsic stiffness |

### Elbow ligaments

| Ligament | Key fact |
|---|---|
| **Anterior bundle MUCL** (anterior oblique) | **Isometric**; **primary valgus + distraction stabiliser**; highest tensile strength; **Tommy John** reconstruction · do not release → **valgus instability** |
| **Posterior bundle MUCL** (posterior oblique) | **Dorsal to axis of rotation**; lengthens **9 mm between 60° and 120°** flexion · contracture → **loss of flexion** · **release → ↑ flexion without valgus instability** |
| Transverse band MUCL | Release **does not improve ROM** |
| Radial collateral ligament | Do not release |
| **LUCL** | Do not release → **PLRI** (pivot shift) |
| Annular ligament | — |

**Anterior capsule contracture** — **loss of extension**

![Medial ligaments: A anterior band, B posterior band, C transverse](../../MCQ/attachments/q4701-a-fig-a-medial.jpg)

**Ulnar nerve** — close to joint → at risk when contracted / during flexion gain

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — **mid-arc pain → intra-articular** pathology · **pain at extremes** → extrinsic contracture (bony impingement + soft-tissue stretch)

**Function** — ↓ motion limits **ADLs**

**Exam** — **scars**, skin quality, inflammation · flexion/extension + pronation/supination vs contralateral · **median, radial, ulnar nerve** function

**Flexion <90–100°** — **posterior band of MCL contracted** → release (+ consider ulnar nerve decompression/transposition)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **AP, lateral, oblique**; **serial X-rays if HO**

![Heterotopic ossification, elbow](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3087-xrhoelbow.jpg)

**CT** — **loose bodies**, nonunion, joint incongruity, abnormal bony anatomy; HO planning

**MRI** — rarely indicated

**HO maturity** — **well-corticated margins + no change on serial X-rays** · **labs not needed** · matures **12–18 mo** (OB) → **no bone scan** years after injury

## 5 Management

```
Elbow stiffness
├─ Most / contracture <40° → NSAIDs + supervised PT (active + passive ROM)
│      └─ failed PT + flexion contracture >30° or flexion <130° → static progressive splint
├─ Plateau 4–6 mo, extrinsic, congruent joint → capsular release ± posterior band MCL
└─ Intrinsic arthritis → choose by extent + demand
       ├─ confined to olecranon fossa → osteophyte excision + capsular release
       ├─ diffuse, young high-demand → distraction interposition arthroplasty
       └─ diffuse, elderly low-demand → TEA
```

### Static progressive splinting

**Indications** — after **failed PT** with **flexion contracture >30°** or **flexion <130°**

**Best initial option (OB)** — **supervised therapy + static progressive (turnbuckle) splinting** over **6 mo** (6–12 mo) → greatest ↑ **DASH** + functional ROM

**Not indicated** — **mechanical block** (esp. pronation/supination) · **ankylosis** (extensive HO) · functional arc already (e.g. 10–140°) · routinely after arthroscopic loose body removal

| ✗ Alternative | Why wrong |
|---|---|
| **Self-directed exercise** | **Poor results** |
| Supervised therapy alone | First line, but response **variable, often unsatisfactory** |
| **CPM** | Limited role in **established** contractures; no end-range gain |
| **Closed manipulation (MUA)** | **Worsens stiffness**: tearing, **haemarthrosis**, more fibrosis |

![Turnbuckle static progressive splint](../../MCQ/attachments/q3026-illa-turnbuckle.jpg)

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Capsular release ± posterior band MCL** | **Extrinsic** contracture + **normal joint congruency** (most predictable) · arthritis (less predictable) · ankylosis → less predictable |
| **Osteophyte excision** (+ capsular release) | **Intrinsic** contracture, **arthritis confined to olecranon fossa**; bony block to terminal motion → remove **coronoid, coronoid fossa, olecranon, olecranon fossa** |
| **Distraction interposition arthroplasty** | Intrinsic, **diffuse arthritis**, **young high-demand** |
| **TEA** | Intrinsic, diffuse arthritis, **elderly low-demand** · high failure in young · **permanent 5-lb lifting limit** |
| **Musculocutaneous neurectomy** | **Neurogenic** contracture, **flexion deformity <90°** |
| **Timing of release** | **4–6 mo** post-injury/surgery once ROM **plateaued** despite splinting/therapy |
| **HO excision** | At **maturity** |

**Contraindications to release** — **Charcot elbow**, **neurologic elbow disorder** · **poor skin** (relative → rotational flap)

### Release – posterior bundle of MCL for flexion

**Flexion gain** — capsular release + **posterior oblique band of MUCL**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior band · transverse · RCL · LUCL

**Flexion <100° preop** — consider **ulnar nerve transposition/decompression** (↓ nerve injury)

### Arthroscopic vs open release (prior ulnar transposition, HO)

**Arthroscopic** — technically demanding · posterior: olecranon fossa/osteophyte debridement + posterior capsular release (**no suction medially** — ulnar nerve) · anterior: coronoid fossa debridement + anterior capsulotomy/capsulectomy

**Nerves at risk with portals** — **radial (most)** > ulnar > median (stiffness note) · overall most common = **transient ulnar**, most common permanent = **PIN** (TO, arthroscopy MCQs) — read the options

**Relative contraindications to arthroscopic release** — **prior ulnar nerve transposition** (variable nerve position) · **HO** · obesity · severe loss of pronation–supination · muscular contracture (**CP**)

**Not contraindications** — age >40, gender, **osteophytes / arthritis** (unpredictable outcome only), heavy labour

| Open approach | Key points |
|---|---|
| **Lateral column (Morrey)** | Lateral or posterior skin incision · elevate **ECRL + BR** off anterior supracondylar ridge, triceps posteriorly · brachialis off anterior capsule · **robust anterior access** → capsular release + **HO excision**; coronoid + olecranon tip/fossa, radiocapitellar joint |
| **Medial "over the top" (Hotchkiss)** | Extrinsic contracture, **MCL calcification**, **baseline ulnar nerve symptoms** · with ulnar nerve decompression/transposition · **release posterior band MCL** · work anterior to flexor-pronator mass |
| **Combined medial + lateral** | Single **posterior incision** → both columns |

### Rehabilitation & outcomes

**Post-op** — **regional block**, **CPM through full ROM**, compressive dressing, active + active-assist ROM, extension / dynamic / static progressive splinting as needed

**Compliance** — critical

**Outcome** — variable; **retain ~two-thirds of motion gained** at surgery

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Heterotopic ossification** | Prophylaxis **low-dose radiation** or **indomethacin** · no radiation with **acute fracture** (nonunion risk) |
| **Transient ulnar neuropraxia** | After release (nerve close to contracted joint) |
| **Ulnar nerve damage** | Transpose if **preop flexion <100°** |
| **Recurrent contracture** | — |

## X vs Y

| | **Posterior band MUCL** | **Anterior band MUCL** |
|---|---|---|
| Length change | **+9 mm (60°→120°)** | **Isometric** |
| Contracture → | **Loss of flexion** | — |
| Release → | **↑ flexion**, stable | **Valgus instability** |
| Other | — | Valgus restraint in throwing; Tommy John; strongest |

| | **Arthroscopic release** | **Open release** |
|---|---|---|
| Best for | Osteophytes, capsular contracture, loose bodies | **Extensive HO**, **prior ulnar transposition**, severe contracture |
| Risk | **Radial nerve** (portals) > ulnar > median | Ulnar neuropraxia / damage |

## How it's asked

**"Static progressive turnbuckle splint most appropriate?"** → **3 mo post distal humerus ORIF, arc 45–100°, plateaued**\
✗ Wrong options: radial head block to supination · 10–140° arc · ankylosis with HO · after arthroscopic loose body removal

**"2.5 mo after fracture-dislocation fixation, arc 75°, DASH 45 — best initial?"** → **supervised therapy + static progressive splinting**\
✗ Wrong options: self-directed · CPM · MUA

**"True about posterior oblique MCL?"** → **released to gain flexion in post-traumatic contracture**\
✗ Wrong options: strongest · Tommy John · valgus restraint = anterior bundle · pivot shift = LUCL

**"Lacks 40° extension, flexion to 80° — release capsule plus?"** → **posterior band MCL (B)**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior band → valgus instability · LUCL → PLRI

**"Relative contraindication to arthroscopic release?"** → **prior ulnar nerve transposition**\
✗ Wrong options: age, gender, osteophytes, labour

**"Terrible triad 5 y ago, arc 60–95°, extensive HO — treatment?"** → **open capsular release + HO excision via lateral column approach**\
✗ Wrong options: bone scan · arthroscopy · downsize radial head · interposition arthroplasty

**"Nerve most at risk with elbow arthroscopy portals?"** → **radial**

**"Functional elbow ROM?"** → **30–130° (100° arc)**; pronation/supination **50–50°**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Glenohumeral Arthritis (Shoulder Arthritis)

*Shoulder & Elbow · 17 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — ↑ with age; mostly **>60 y** · **♀ > ♂**

**Dislocation as risk** — **56%** of primary anterior dislocators have arthrosis at **25 y**

### Aetiology

| Type | Key point |
|---|---|
| **Primary OA** | No known cause · irreversible cartilage loss + **hypertrophic subchondral bone** · flattened head, osteophytes, cysts, **posterior subluxation + posterior glenoid wear** |
| Post-traumatic | Healed articular incongruity (humeral fractures, chronic dislocations) |
| **Dislocation arthropathy** | ↑ with **age >25 y** at dislocation + **posterior** dislocation · **not related to number of dislocations** |
| **Post-capsulorrhaphy** | Over-tightening (e.g. **Putti-Platt**) pushes head one way → eccentric wear; presents 5th decade |
| **Cuff tear arthropathy** | Torn cuff → head migrates up → abrasion against acromion |
| **RA** | Affects **90%** of RA patients · **central glenoid wear + medialisation** · cuff tear 25–50% |
| CPPD / gout | Pyrophosphate (pseudogout) / urate crystals |
| **AVN** | Proximal humerus fracture: **35% 3-part, 90% 4-part** (this topic's Orthobullets note) · chronic dislocation · cuff repair · steroid, EtOH, haemoglobinopathy, Gaucher |
| **Chondrolysis** | After arthroscopy: **radiofrequency**, **continuous intra-articular anaesthetic infusion**, bioabsorbable anchors, contrast · **fewer osteophytes** than OA |
| Neuropathic | Charcot |

**Cuff tears with OA** — **5–10%** (Orthobullets) · full-thickness supraspinatus **<10%** in primary OA (OB) · CTA excluded by definition\
✗ Wrong options: 30–55% = asymptomatic >60 y population

### Anatomy

**Version** — glenoid **~3° retroverted** · humerus **20–30° retroverted**

**Stabilisers** — static: GH ligaments, labrum · dynamic: cuff, rotator interval, biceps

## 2 Mechanism

**Posterior wear + subluxation** — posterior capsule stretched · **anterior capsule + subscapularis contract** → **loss of ER**

**Walch B vs C** — **B**: posterior subluxation **causes** posterior wear · **C**: glenoid retroversion causes wear → subluxation later

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain with motion, often none at rest · difficulty sleeping · **loss of ER** (anterior capsule contracture)

**Exam** — ↓ ER, FF, IR · crepitus · catching/squeaking · examine cuff carefully

**Subscapularis tear** — **weak belly-press** + **↑ passive ER** (90° at side)

**Outcome scores** — ASES · Constant · Oxford

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — **true AP (Grashey)** + AP + **axillary lateral** (axillary/CT to judge posterior glenoid wear)

| Cause | X-ray hallmark |
|---|---|
| **Primary OA** | JSN, sclerosis, cysts, **inferior "goat's beard" osteophyte**, **posterior glenoid wear**, fixed posterior subluxation |
| Dislocation arthropathy | Large osteophytes, hardware |
| **RA** | **Osteopenia**, marginal erosions, **few osteophytes**, **central wear + medialisation**, ↓ acromiohumeral distance |
| CPPD | **Chondrocalcinosis** |
| Gout | Usually unremarkable; erosions after repeat attacks |
| AVN | Normal early → central head resorption → **crescent sign** → collapse |
| **CTA** | **Superior migration**, ↓ acromiohumeral interval, acromial erosion, superior glenoid loss, **"acetabularisation"** of CA arch, osteopenia |

![Primary OA with inferior goat's beard osteophyte](../../MCQ/attachments/q213974-a-osteophyte-shoulder.jpg)

**CT** — **glenoid morphology** + templating · underestimates full-thickness RCT and fatty infiltration vs MRI

**MRI** — cuff assessment · **less accurate than CT** for glenoid type · **Goutallier IV** fatty infiltration = irreparable cuff

### Glenoid wear patterns (Walch, posterior wear, medialisation in RA)

**Most common OA wear** — **posterior** (or central)\
✗ Wrong options: anterior · superior · inferior

**Inflammatory (RA/JRA) vs OA** — **concentric erosion + medialisation of joint line** · severe erosion → glenoid resurfacing not always feasible → some favour HA\
✗ Wrong options: inferior osteophyte · posterior subluxation · sclerotic glenoid · posterior wear = OA

| Walch | Description |
|---|---|
| **A** (59%) | **Concentric**, head centred · **A1** minor central erosion · **A2** deep central erosion (rim line transects head) |
| **B** | **Biconcave**, asymmetric wear, **posterior subluxation** · **B0** pre-arthritic subluxation · **B1** (17%) posterior JSN, osteophytes, sclerosis, no bone loss · **B2** (15%) posterior rim erosion, **retroversion <25°** · **B3** **monoconcave**, posterior wear, subluxation **≥70%** or retroversion **>15°** |
| **C** (9%) | **Dysplastic**, **retroversion >25°** · **C1** >25° regardless of erosion · **C2** biconcave + posterior bone loss + posterior translation · subtypes **"Lazy J"** + **"Delta"** |
| **D** | **Anteversion** or **anterior** subluxation (<40%) |

![Walch classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3058-walch-classification.jpg)

**B2 trap** — biconcave + posterior subluxation + **10°** retroversion = B2 · same with 25° = **C2** · deep concentric = A2 · monoconcave + 70% subluxation = B3 · anteversion = D

**B2 outcomes** — poorer after arthroplasty · anatomic TSA → instability/early glenoid loosening if retroversion **>27°** or **80%** posterior subluxation → **reverse** often better in older/severe deformity

## 5 Management

```
Glenohumeral arthritis
├─ First line → NSAIDs, PT (capsular stretching), activity modification
├─ Second line → intra-articular corticosteroid (HA/PRP/stem cell: limited evidence)
├─ Young, high demand → arthroscopic debridement / CAM (temporising)
└─ Failed nonop, end stage → arthroplasty by cuff status
       ├─ cuff intact (incl. partial tear) → anatomic TSA
       └─ cuff deficient / >80% subluxation / Walch D → RSA
```

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indications | Contraindications / cautions |
|---|---|---|
| **Anatomic TSA** | **Intact cuff** (partial tear OK), glenoid wear, posterior subluxation, failed nonop | **Deficient deltoid or cuff** · active infection · **Charcot** · young high-demand (glenoid loosening) |
| **Hemiarthroplasty** | Young · **RA with irreparable cuff / poor glenoid bone stock** · **AVN without glenoid involvement** | Early failure, poor pain/function in OA (not routine) |
| **Ream-and-run** | Young, **intact cuff, no inflammatory arthropathy** | — |
| **RSA** | **Irreparable/large cuff tear** · CTA · OA/RA with major glenoid pathology · age · **failed arthroplasty** · complex fracture | Higher complication rate than TSA |
| **Arthroscopic debridement** | Mild–moderate OA without structural change · loose bodies · small AVN lesions · synovial chondromatosis | Advanced degeneration |
| **Arthrodesis** | Paralysis · recurrent infection · poor deltoid/soft tissue · brachial plexus palsy · failed repair of instability | — |

### Anatomic TSA vs hemiarthroplasty – indications & cuff status

**TSA vs HA** — TSA: **better pain relief + function** · HA: **↑ revision rate**\
✗ Wrong options: better pain · ↑ blood loss · ↑ instability · ↑ infection

**End-stage OA, cuff strong** — **TSA** = most predictable pain relief\
✗ Wrong options: RSA unnecessary · arthroscopic osteophyte removal · interposition · HA

**Partial articular cuff tear (35%)** — **good candidate for anatomic TSA** (± repair)

**Not anatomic TSA candidates** — 52 y powerlifter (glenoid loosening → HA) · prior aTSA with high-riding humerus (cuff tear → **convert to RSA**) · Walch B **85%** subluxation (RSA if **>80%**) · elderly **Walch D** (RSA)

**Tight anterior structures** — ER **<40–45°** under EUA → **subscapularis lengthening + anterior capsule release**

**TSA outcomes** — 10-y survival **92–95%** · complications: **glenoid/humeral loosening**, infection, fracture, nerve injury, cuff tear · **↑ PJI if within 3 months of arthroscopy** · vs RSA: **↑ IR** + **↑ glenoid loosening**

### Reverse shoulder arthroplasty – cuff deficiency, post-instability arthropathy, RA

**Irreparable cuff + OA (Goutallier IV)** — NOT appropriate = **arthroscopic cuff repair** · appropriate = NSAIDs/injection, PT, activity modification, then HA or RSA after failed nonop

**Elderly, can't elevate arm (CTA), failed nonop** — **RSA** most reliable (Delta III: Constant +42, +65° elevation, **96%** minimal/no pain) · HA = historical standard, non-uniform results

**OA + complete subscapularis tear** — **RSA**\
✗ Wrong options: anatomic TSA: subscap repair fails

**Post-Bankart arthropathy + supraspinatus fatty atrophy + posterior subluxation/erosion** — **RSA**\
✗ Wrong options: HA: glenoid pain persists · TSA: incompetent cuff · arthroscopic release

**RA + cuff tear** — **RSA good outcomes**, even with **reparable** tear · options = TSA + cuff repair or RSA · **TSA > HA** in RA (pain, elevation, ROM) · **etanercept/DMARDs/prednisone: no effect** on RSA outcome · RSA complications in RA **≈ CTA** (not more) · no interposition arthroplasty in elderly

**RSA outcomes** — 10-y survival **~90–95%** · complications: **scapular notching**, infection, instability, nerve injury

### Young patients – nonoperative care & arthroscopic debridement (CAM procedure)

**Young OA first step** — **NSAIDs, cortisone injection, PT / ROM stretching** before any surgery\
✗ Wrong options: TSA · fusion · resurfacing · arthroscopy as first step

**Arthroscopic debridement** — **temporising** · improves pain + ROM · better with **age <50**, little JSN, concentric joint, added subacromial procedure · **~20% convert to arthroplasty within 1 y**\
✗ Wrong options: 45%

| Arthroscopic debridement predictor | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Nonconcentric joint space** / moderate–severe degeneration | **Poor** |
| **Posterior glenoid wear**, ↑ retroversion | **Poor** (↑ failure) |
| **Bipolar** (head + glenoid) OA | Poor |
| Preoperative stiffness, loose bodies | **Good** |
| Prior infection / instability without degeneration | No effect |

**Technique** — treat **both glenoid and humeral head aggressively**\
✗ Wrong options: ream glenoid + spare humerus

**CAM procedure** — young patient · GH debridement, chondroplasty, synovectomy, loose body removal, **humeral osteoplasty (goat's beard excision)**, capsular release, subacromial + subcoracoid decompression, **axillary nerve decompression**, biceps tenodesis

**Inferior humeral osteophyte** — **axillary nerve** entrapment → **teres minor** fatty infiltration\
✗ Wrong options: infraspinatus/supraspinatus = suprascapular · subscapularis = upper + lower subscapular · teres major = lower subscapular ± thoracodorsal

## 6 Complications

| Procedure | Typical complications |
|---|---|
| TSA | **Glenoid / humeral loosening**, infection, fracture, nerve injury, cuff tear (most common group) |
| RSA | **Scapular notching**, infection, instability, nerve; overall > TSA |
| HA | Glenoid wear/pain, **↑ revision** |
| Arthroscopy (pain pump, RF) | **Chondrolysis** |

## X vs Y

| | **Primary OA** | **RA** | **CTA** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Glenoid wear | **Posterior** | **Central, medialisation** | **Superior** |
| Osteophytes | **Large, inferior** | **Few** | — |
| Head position | **Posterior subluxation** | Medialised | **Superior migration** |
| Bone | Sclerosis | **Osteopenia**, erosions | Osteopenia, acetabularisation |
| Cuff | Tear 5–10% | Tear 25–50% | Torn by definition |
| Arthroplasty | **TSA** | TSA (+ repair) or RSA; HA if no glenoid | **RSA** |

![RA: osteopenia, central wear, few osteophytes](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3058-ra.jpg)

| | **TSA** | **RSA** |
|---|---|---|
| Design | Concave glenoid + convex humerus | **Convex glenoid (ball) + concave humerus** |
| Cuff | Must be intact | Deficient OK |
| 10-y survival | 92–95% | ~90–95% |
| Distinct | ↑ IR, ↑ glenoid loosening | Scapular notching, ↑ complications |

## How it's asked

**"72 y, OA X-ray, neutral ER — where is glenoid wear?"** → **posterior**

**"Inflammatory vs osteoarthritis at arthroplasty — more likely?"** → **medialisation of joint line**

**"Walch B2 description?"** → **biconcave, posterior subluxation + erosion, 10° retroversion**\
✗ Wrong options: 25° = C2 · anteversion = D · deep central = A2 · monoconcave 70% = B3

**"Chance of full-thickness supraspinatus tear at TSA for primary OA?"** → **<10%**

**"TSA vs HA for OA with intact cuff — HA gives?"** → **↑ revision surgery**

**"74 y, end-stage OA, posterior erosion, good cuff strength?"** → **TSA**

**"Best candidate for anatomic TSA?"** → **60 y with 35% partial articular cuff tear**

**"80 y, drop arm, Goutallier IV, GH arthritis — NOT appropriate?"** → **arthroscopic cuff repair**

**"75F, can't elevate arm, cuff arthropathy, most reliable surgery?"** → **RSA**\
✗ Wrong options: HA · resurfacing · debridement · fusion

**"Old cuff repair, weak belly-press, ER 90°, end-stage OA?"** → **RSA**

**"Post-Bankart arthropathy, supraspinatus fatty atrophy, posterior erosion?"** → **RSA**

**"RA on etanercept, 2 cm cuff tear — true statement?"** → **RSA has good outcomes in this cohort**

**"Predictor of poor outcome after arthroscopic debridement?"** → **nonconcentric joint space**

**"40 y labourer, OA, no prior treatment?"** → **NSAIDs, cortisone injection, PT**

**"35 y weightlifter, new pain + stiffness, OA X-ray — next step?"** → **anti-inflammatory drugs + range-of-motion stretching program**\
✗ Wrong options: TSA · HA + interposition · resurfacing · debridement first

**"Inferior humeral osteophyte neuropraxia — fatty infiltration of?"** → **teres minor** (axillary nerve)

**"True about arthroscopy for OA?"** → **↑ failure with posterior glenoid wear**\
✗ Wrong options: 45% conversion at 1 y · >50 y better · bipolar no effect · spare humerus

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Glenohumeral Internal Rotation Deficit (GIRD)

*Shoulder & Elbow · 18 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Who** — **overhead athletes**, esp. **baseball pitchers**; also javelin

**Definition** — loss of glenohumeral **IR of throwing shoulder vs contralateral** · ↑ ER · ↓ total arc (symptomatic)

**Course** — deficit worsens with ↑ repetitions over a season + a career; may be **painless**

### Definition & thrower adaptations (IR loss, total arc, posterior capsule)

**Characteristic motion change** — **↓ IR** in throwing arm · pitchers: **↓ IR + ↑ ER**\
✗ Wrong options: ER, abduction, adduction, forward elevation

| Adaptation (dominant throwing shoulder) | Direction |
|---|---|
| **Humeral head retroversion** | **↑** (✗ Wrong options: anteversion) |
| **Glenoid retroversion** | **↑** (✗ Wrong options: anteversion) |
| **Posterior capsule / PIGHL** | **Tight + thickened** |
| Anterior capsule / AIGHL | **Lengthened, attenuated** (✗ Wrong options: stretch it) |
| ER at 90° + scapular plane | ↑ |
| IR | ↓ |

**Retroversion mechanism** — humerus retroverted in utero → **de-rotates until maturity** → throwing stress on physis **halts de-rotation** → ↑ retroversion → more ER, less IR · bony changes in those who **began pitching before skeletal maturity**

**Crockett CT** — athletes: ↑ humeral head retroversion, ↑ glenoid retroversion, ↑ ER at 90° + scapular plane, ↓ IR · not in non-athletes

**Why ER gain** — **arc of acceleration ↑ → velocity ↑** · short IR arc + tight posterior capsule → **deceleration harder** → overuse injury

**Posterior capsule thickening** — from **repetitive eccentric stress in follow-through**

### Total arc of motion concept

| Situation | Total arc (IR + ER) |
|---|---|
| **Adaptive, asymptomatic** thrower (ERG ≥ GIRD) | **Normal** vs other side; normal kinematics |
| Thrower vs **average population** | **↑ overall arc** (ER gain > IR loss) |
| **Pathological GIRD** (GIRD > ERG) | **↓** vs contralateral → deranged kinematics |

**Total arc** — should be **equal between shoulders** · ~**180°** (total motion concept)

**Source nuance** — "tight posterior capsule with **normal** total arc" (OB) · "**↑** total arc vs average population" (OB) · "**↓** total arc" in symptomatic GIRD (Orthobullets, OB) — read the stem: adaptation vs pathology

**Injury risk ✓** — **↓ ER vs non-dominant arm** independently linked to injury (esp. with pre-existing IR deficit)\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ ER (compensatory) · ↑ IR (uncommon) · ↑ horizontal adduction · ↓ flexion

**Newer view** — loss of total arc + **failure to gain ER** may matter more than IR loss alone

## 2 Mechanism

**Cause** — **repetitive throwing**; during **late cocking + early acceleration**

**Capsular constraint mechanism** — tight capsule → humeral head translates **OPPOSITE** to tight region

| Tight region | Position | Head translation |
|---|---|---|
| **Posterior capsule** | Flexion | **Anterosuperior** |
| **Posteroinferior capsule (PIGHL)** | **ABER / cocking** | **Posterosuperior** ✓ |

**Posterosuperior shift** — ↑ clearance for greater tuberosity but → internal impingement + **peel-back** of biceps anchor (biceps vector shifts posterior)

![Throwing phases](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3055-throwing-phases.jpg)

**Throwing phases** — wind-up → early cocking → **late cocking (max ER)** → acceleration → deceleration → follow-through · "5 distinct phases" (OB)

**Pathologic cascade** — ↓ velocity + command → posterior stiffness, **slow to loosen up** → posterior pain in late cocking / early acceleration (no mechanical symptoms) → **"SLAP event"** (posterosuperior labrum + biceps anchor fail in tension) → **surgery after SLAP event**; stretching works before it

### Internal impingement & associated lesions (SLAP, articular-sided cuff tears)

**Most closely linked pathology** — **internal impingement**\
✗ Wrong options: subacromial · HAGL · Hill-Sachs · biceps tendinitis

**Internal impingement** — in **ABER (late cocking)** greater tuberosity + **posterosuperior glenoid** pinch **articular side of posterior supraspinatus + infraspinatus** and posterosuperior labrum

![Internal impingement](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3055-illustration-moved.jpg)

**Cuff tear in throwers** — **articular-sided partial**, **SS–IS junction** (posterior SS + anterior IS) · (✗ Wrong options: bursal-sided · full-thickness (not described)) · general population = anterior supraspinatus

**Tear mechanism** — **compressive** (GT vs glenoid) + **tensile** (repetitive eccentric load, hyper-ER twisting)

**Lesion most associated** — **SLAP tear** (type II, posterior extension) \
✗ Wrong options: Bankart · HAGL · anterior capsular contracture

**SLAP risk** — throwers with GIRD **25% more likely** to have SLAP · **peel-back** in late cocking

![Peel-back mechanism](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3055-peel-back.jpg)

**Cause of internal impingement** — **posterior capsular contracture (GIRD)** or **anterior capsular laxity**

**Chondral injury** — **posterosuperior**\
✗ Wrong options: anteroinferior = anterior instability

**Associated conditions** — GH instability · internal impingement · articular-sided partial cuff tears · SLAP · **ipsilateral elbow pain** (altered throwing)

### SICK scapula

| Letter | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **S** | Scapular malposition (hangs lower) |
| **I** | **Inferior medial border prominence** |
| **C** | **Coracoid pain** + malposition (pec minor contracture → tendinitis) |
| **K** | Dys**K**inesis of scapular movement |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — vague / **posterior** shoulder pain · may be painless · **↓ velocity + command** · "dead arm" · arm heavy in later innings · slow to loosen up

**Exam** — **stabilise scapula** for true GH rotation · ↓ IR + ↑ ER at 90° abduction · **↑ sulcus sign** (stretched CHL + rotator interval)

**Internal impingement exam** — deep **posterior** pain at **90° abduction + max ER**; negative apprehension

**Subacromial impingement (wrong option)** — **anterolateral** pain with IR in abduction

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria

| Criterion | Value |
|---|---|
| **Clinically relevant GIRD** | **IR loss >25°** vs contralateral (Orthobullets, OB) · **20–25°** (OB, Little Leaguer's) — read the options |
| Range reported | up to **50°** |
| Pathological | **GIRD > ERG** (↓ total arc) |
| No | **<15°** loss |

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — AP + lateral; **usually normal**

**CT** — **↑ glenoid retroversion**

**MRI** — **ABER view** shows associated lesions (articular-sided cuff tear, labrum) · often normal · not needed initially for isolated posterior tightness

### Little Leaguer's shoulder (proximal humeral epiphysiolysis)

![Little Leaguer's shoulder — widened physis](../../MCQ/attachments/q218674-a-19a-moved.jpg)

**X-ray** — **widened proximal humeral physis** + metaphyseal changes · **17% normal**

**Risk** — pitchers throwing **breaking pitches**, ignoring pitch counts/rest, year-round pitching

**GIRD link** — GIRD exam findings at diagnosis → **increased likelihood of recurrence (3×)**, mean **6.2–7.6 months** · **pain after 2 months PT** = independent predictor of recurrence

**Treatment** — stop throwing (**~3 months** no pitching) → PT (restore motion, kinetic chain) → return-to-throw · **no breaking balls until skeletal maturity**

## 5 Management

```
GIRD in thrower
├─ First line → rest from throwing + posterior capsular stretching
│      (sleeper + cross-body + periscapular/pec minor + cuff)
├─ Fails extensive PT → arthroscopic posteroinferior capsular release
│      (some advocate anterior stabilisation)
└─ After "SLAP event" → surgery (SLAP repair)
```

### Treatment – posterior capsular stretching (sleeper/cross-body) & scapular work

**Initial ✓** — **PT directed at posteroinferior capsule stretching** + periscapular strengthening, **regardless of pathology** · then cuff + scapular stabilisation

**Regimen** — rest from throwing + PT **6 months** (Orthobullets)

**Emphasise** — **sleeper + cross-body stretches + periscapular strengthening**

**Sleeper stretch** — lying on affected side, shoulder **90° abduction** (Orthobullets, OB) · **90° forward flexion** (OB), elbow 90°, push forearm to table, **scapula stabilised** → stretches **posterior capsule**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior · anteroinferior · rotator interval · MGHL

![Sleeper stretch](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3055-sleeper-stretch-moved.jpg)

| Stretch / exercise | Target | Restores IR? |
|---|---|---|
| **Sleeper stretch** | Posteroinferior capsule | **Yes** |
| Roll-over sleeper | Arm flexed **60°**, body rolled forward **30°** | Yes |
| **Cross-body adduction** | Posterior capsule | Yes |
| Doorway stretch | — | — |
| **Pec minor stretch** (rolled towel, push shoulders back) | SICK scapula, coracoid pain | No (adjunct) |
| Rows (retraction) · punches (protraction) | Scapula | No |
| Humeral head depression, ball on wall | Closed-chain cuff | No |

**Coracoid pain + GIRD** — **pec minor stretching + sleeper stretches**\
✗ Wrong options: weighted ball program · subacromial steroid · arthroscopy

**Outcomes** — **~90%** respond (young throwers) · ~10% older throwers fail → arthroscopic release

**Internal impingement + partial posterior SS tear** — **NSAIDs + posterior capsular stretching + cuff strengthening** · refractory → SLAP repair / cuff debridement \
✗ Wrong options: cuff repair

| ✗ Wrong initial option | Why |
|---|---|
| Arthroscopic posteroinferior release | Only after failed PT |
| **Anterior(-inferior) capsular stretch / release** | Anterior capsule already **lax/lengthened** |
| **Selective ER stretching** | ER already normal / ↑ |
| Posterior labral repair + capsulorrhaphy | **Posterior instability** (linemen, weightlifters) |
| Capsulolabral imbrication / plication | **MDI** |
| Subacromial steroid | Pathology is **intra-articular** |
| Sling | Comfort only |
| Cessation 3–6 months alone | Does not address contracture; deconditioning |
| ↑ deltoid / latissimus weights | ↑ stiffness |
| Change pitch mix | Pitch selection not a risk factor for GIRD |
| MRI arthrogram first | Not needed for isolated tightness |

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Arthroscopic posteroinferior capsule release** (± PIGHL) | **Failure of extensive PT** only |
| Anterior stabilisation | Alternative (controversial) |
| Repair thinned cuff | Significant thinning (transcuff or takedown) |

### Posterior capsule release vs anterior stabilization

**Technique** — electrocautery via **posterior portal**, camera **anterior** · release posteroinferior capsule + **posterior band IGHL** from **9 to 6 o'clock** at glenoid rim until cuff fibres seen · **shaver widens gap** (prevents recurrence) · gentle manipulation

**Result** — immediate gain **~65° IR**

## X vs Y

| | **GIRD / internal impingement** | **Subacromial impingement** | **Adhesive capsulitis** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pain | **Deep posterior**, ABER | **Anterolateral**, IR in abduction | Global |
| Motion | ↓ IR, ↑ ER | Normal | **Global active + passive ↓** |
| Cuff tear side | **Articular** | Bursal | — |

| | **GIRD thrower** | **Anterior instability** |
|---|---|---|
| Anterior capsule | Lax (microtrauma) | Torn / Bankart |
| Lesion | **SLAP**, articular cuff | **Bankart, HAGL** |
| Chondral injury | **Posterosuperior** | Anteroinferior |

## How it's asked

**"Motion decreased in thrower's dominant arm?"** → **internal rotation**

**"Dominant thrower adaptations?"** → **tight posterior capsule with normal total arc**\
✗ Wrong options: humeral anteversion

**"Pitcher, ↓ velocity, posterior pain, slow to loosen up — exam?"** → **IR loss >25°**

**"Side-lying stretch, arm 90°, push forearm to table — diagnosis + finding?"** → **GIRD; >25° IR loss**\
✗ Wrong options: adhesive capsulitis · <15°

**"ROM finding most associated with injury risk?"** → **decreased ER** vs non-dominant

**"12-year-old, GIRD + widened physis — clinical course?"** → **↑ recurrence**\
✗ Wrong options: elbow pain unlikely · non-breaking pitches

**"Pitcher, 30° IR loss vs average population?"** → **thickened posterior capsule**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ total arc · humeral anteversion · anteroinferior chondral injury

**"Sleeper + prone IR exercises — diagnosis?"** → **GIRD**

**"Pitcher, 30° IR loss, positive O'Brien, normal MRI — emphasise?"** → **sleeper + cross-body + periscapular strengthening**

**"Total arc 150°, scapula hangs low — initial management?"** → **aggressive PT, posterior capsular stretching + scapular strengthening**

**"IR stretch at 90° flexion, scapula stabilised — stretches?"** → **posterior capsule**

**"Pitching staff, mean GIRD 45° — intervention?"** → **posterior capsular stretching**

**"IR deficit 25°, negative O'Brien/Neer/Hawkins — next step?"** → **posterior capsular stretching program**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · arthroscopy

**"ER 110° vs 100°, IR 30° vs 70° — best treatment?"** → **posterior capsular stretching**\
✗ Wrong options: selective ER stretching · thermal shift

**"Late-cocking posterior pain, IR 25° vs 60°, O'Brien painful — next?"** → **posterior capsular stretching**\
✗ Wrong options: SLAP repair · subacromial injection

**"Home exercise to improve IR deficit?"** → **sleeper stretch**\
✗ Wrong options: rows · punches · towel pec stretch

**"↓ IR + inferomedial scapular prominence + partial posterior SS tear — treatment?"** → **NSAIDs + posterior capsular stretching + cuff strengthening**

**"Pitcher, figure of GIRD — initial treatment?"** → **PT for posteroinferior capsule stretching**\
✗ Wrong options: anteroinferior stretch/release

**"GIRD + coracoid tenderness, normal MRA — next?"** → **pec minor + sleeper stretches**

**"IR 5° vs 15°, ER 115° vs 90° — next?"** → **posterior capsular stretches**\
✗ Wrong options: 3–6 months rest · anterior stretches

**"Humeral translation in cocking with posterior contracture?"** → **posterosuperior**

**"GIRD linked most closely to?"** → **internal impingement**

**"Range of motion in pitchers with internal impingement?"** → **↓ IR, ↑ ER**

**"Javelin thrower, posterior pain at 90° abduction + max ER?"** → **internal impingement**

**"Arthroscopy: posterior SS + posterosuperior labral lesion — throwing phase?"** → **late cocking**

**"Lesion most associated with internal impingement?"** → **SLAP tear**\
✗ Wrong options: Bankart · HAGL · full-thickness tear

**"Sleeper + cross-body stretches — associated EXCEPT?"** → **bursal-sided partial cuff tear**

**"Deep posterior pain at max ER, 30° less total arc — MRI?"** → **articular-sided SS/IS partial tear**

**"Dead arm, total arc −45° — arthroscopic finding?"** → **SLAP lesion**\
✗ Wrong options: glenoid anteversion · bursal tear

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Glenohumeral Joint Anatomy, Stabilizer, and Biomechanics

*Shoulder & Elbow · 19 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Scapulohumeral rhythm

**GH : ST ratio (TO)** — **2:1**\
✗ Wrong options: 1:2 · 2.5:1 · 3:1

**First 30° elevation (TO)** — largely **glenohumeral**; scapulothoracic small + inconsistent → after 30° both move **simultaneously**

**180° abduction** — **120° GH (⅔) + 60° scapulothoracic (⅓)**

**Scapular plane** — **30° anterior** to coronal plane

**Abduction needs ER** — clears greater tuberosity from acromion → **IR contracture → cannot abduct >120°**

### Osteology

| Structure | Key numbers |
|---|---|
| **Humeral head** | Spheroidal in 90% · diameter **43 mm** · **inclination 130°** to shaft · tuberosities = cuff attachments |
| **Glenoid** | **Pear-shaped** · upward tilt **5°** · covers only **25–30% of head** (TO) |
| **Coracoid** | **"Lighthouse"** of deltopectoral approach · coracobrachialis, pec minor, short head biceps |
| **Acromion** | 3 ossification centres: **pre- (tip), meso- (mid), meta-acromion (base)** |
| **Acromiohumeral interval** | **7–8 mm** |

### Glenoid & humeral version

| | **Glenoid** | **Humeral head** |
|---|---|---|
| Orthobullets | **5° retroversion** avg (scapular body axis); **7° retro → 10° ante** | **~20° retroversion** (transepicondylar axis) |
| TO | **Near 0°**, more often **slight retroversion**; <10° either way | **Retroverted 26° ± 11°** |

**Humeral retroversion** — **20°** (Orthobullets) · **26° ± 11°** (TO) — read the options

**False (TO)** — **"both glenoid and humeral head anteverted"**

### Humeral head blood supply

**Main supply** — **posterior humeral circumflex artery** (current literature) → injury most likely → **AVN**

**Why PHCA?** — **AHCA disrupted in ~80%** of proximal humerus fractures yet AVN infrequent

**Ascending branch of AHCA** — runs **parallel to lateral aspect of LHB** in bicipital groove → **arcuate artery** = intraosseous continuation into head; do not injure when plating

![Arteriogram: 4 = posterior humeral circumflex artery](../../MCQ/attachments/q4422-illa-mhc-illustration-2.jpg)

| ✗ Arteriogram distractor | Label |
|---|---|
| Deltoid branch thoracoacromial | 1 |
| Axillary | 2 |
| Anterior humeral circumflex | 3 |
| Profunda brachii | 5 |

### Labral anatomy & blood supply

**Composition** — **fibrocartilage** (TO)

**Function** — **50% of socket depth** · cavity-compression · ↑ depth, ↑ articular surface area, **centralises head**, **seal → intra-articular pressure** (TO)

**Embryology (TO)** — labrum forms **~8th week**; LHB + capsule insertion defined by **9th week**

**Blood supply** — **suprascapular** · **circumflex scapular** (TO) / anterior humeral circumflex (Orthobullets) · **posterior humeral circumflex**; from **capsule + periosteal vessels**, not underlying bone

**Poorest blood supply** — **anterosuperior labrum** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: posterosuperior

**Biceps attachment (TO)** — to labrum, **mainly posterior**

**Weak links** — anterior labrum anchors **IGHL** → **Bankart** · superior labrum anchors **biceps** → **SLAP**

## 2 Mechanism

### Static vs dynamic stabilisers

| **Static** | **Dynamic** |
|---|---|
| **Glenohumeral ligaments** | **Rotator cuff** (main) |
| **Glenoid labrum** | Rotator interval |
| Articular congruity + version | **Long head biceps** (humeral head depressor) |
| **Negative intra-articular pressure** (release → head subluxes **inferiorly**) | Periscapular muscles |

**Bony articulation** — **inherently unstable**

**Posterior capsule** — thin (**<1 mm**), **no ligaments**

### Glenohumeral ligaments – function by arm position (IGHL, MGHL, SGHL, CHL)

| Arm position | Anterior restraint | Inferior restraint | Posterior restraint |
|---|---|---|---|
| **0° (side) + adduction** | | **SGHL / CHL** | |
| **45° abduction + ER** | **MGHL** | | **MGHL** |
| **90° abduction + ER** | **Anterior band IGHL** | **Anterior band IGHL** | **Posterior band IGHL** |
| **90° flexion, adduction, IR** | Anterior band IGHL | | **SGHL / CHL** |

![Arthroscopic view: 1 SGHL, 2 subscapularis, 3 MGHL, 4 IGHL, 5 biceps](../../MCQ/attachments/q4514-illa-arthroscopy-question-3.jpg)

**Anterior static stability (TO)** — **anterior band IGHL = main restraint** (90° abduction + ER) · **MGHL** (45° abduction + ER) · **SGHL** (arm at side)

**Hammock (TO)** — at **90° abduction** IGHL = hammock: **anteroinferior band = anterior** support, **posteroinferior band = posterior** support

**Abduction + ER main stabiliser (TO)** — **IGHL**

**>45° abduction, anterior + posterior stress (TO)** — **anterior inferior GHL**\
✗ Wrong options: SGHL · CHL · anterosuperior labrum

**SGHL** — anterosuperior labrum → humerus · **inferior translation at 0° abduction / adduction** (neutral rotation) → **greatest stress = inferiorly translated, arm adducted** · restrains **ER of adducted arm** · prevents **anteroinferior translation of LHB** (biceps pulley)

**MGHL** — **anterior (and posterior) translation in midrange ~45° (45–60°) abduction + ER** · from labrum, crosses **subscapularis obliquely (~45°)** → **just medial to lesser tuberosity** · do not confuse with subscapularis

**Anterior band IGHL** — **anterior/inferior translation at 90° abduction + maximal ER** (**late cocking**) · anchors anterior labrum → **Bankart weak link**

**Posterior band IGHL** — **posterior subluxation at 90° flexion + IR** (forward flexed, adducted) · restraint in **posterior instability** (Kim test) → must repair · **tightness → internal impingement** + ↑ shear on superior labrum (SLAP)

**Superior band IGHL** — **most important static stabiliser** (Orthobullets); **100% ↑ strain with SLAP lesion**

**CHL** — coracoid → **rotator cable** · limits **posterior translation in flexion, adduction, IR** · limits **inferior translation + ER in adduction**

**Primary restraint to inferior translation** — **CHL** (OB; Bigliani: inferior capsule + IGHL also contribute) · **SGHL/CHL** at 0° (Orthobullets, OB) — read the options

### Ligament anatomy (TO)

| Ligament | Origin → insertion (TO) | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **CHL** | **Proximal ⅓ of horizontal coracoid** → variable: superficial fibres **greater tuberosity**; only **15–50%** to lesser tuberosity | Blends with supraspinatus + subscapularis |
| **SGHL (Flood ligament)** | **Superior glenoid tubercle** → **fovea capitis** just above lesser tuberosity | Key **stabiliser of LHB** in groove |
| **MGHL** | Just beneath/medial to SGHL → **just medial to lesser tuberosity** | Blends with subscapularis |
| **IGHL** | Anterior + posterior bands + **axillary pouch** (reinforced by **fasciculus obliquus**) → humeral neck like a **collar** under articular margin | Hammock |

**Foramina (TO)** — **Weitbrecht** = between SGHL + MGHL · **Rouvière** = between MGHL + IGHL

**IGHL anterior band origin** — **2–4 o'clock** (one TO) · **3–5 o'clock** (another TO); posterior band **7–9 o'clock** — read the options

![Glenohumeral ligaments (Netter)](../../MCQ/attachments/q5623-illa-ghligs.jpg)

### Stabilisers by abduction, neutral rotation (TO)

| Abduction | Anterior | Posterior | Inferior |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Rest** | Supraspinatus | Posterior capsule | **Superior capsule, CHL, SGHL**, supraspinatus, biceps |
| **0–45°** | Subscapularis, **MGHL**, IGHL | Posterior capsule, IGHL | IGHL, MGHL |
| **45–90°** | **IGHL**, posteroinferior capsule, biceps (IR), cuff, deltoid | Posterior capsule, IGHL, biceps (ER) | IGHL, MGHL |
| **>90°** | **IGHL, axillary pouch**, subscapularis, infraspinatus | Posterior capsule, IGHL | — |

**Glenoid inclination** — also listed among stabilisers in the TO table

### Rotator interval contents

**Definition** — triangular space between **upper border of subscapularis** + **anterior margin of supraspinatus** (TO); bridged by capsule

| Boundary | Structure |
|---|---|
| **Base (medial)** | **Coracoid** (lateral base) |
| Superior | Anterior edge **supraspinatus** |
| Inferior | Superior border **subscapularis** |
| **Apex (lateral)** | **Transverse humeral ligament** |

**Contents** — **CHL + SGHL** + LHB + thin capsule; CHL + SGHL = most constant structures of fibrous capsule\
✗ Wrong options: MGHL

**Layers (TO)** — 4 layers incl. **superficial CHL** · supraspinatus + subscapularis fibres · **deep CHL**

**Arthroscopy** — area between **biceps + subscapularis tendon** = **rotator interval**; biceps deep to interval

**Contracture** — **adhesive capsulitis** → release · **laxity** → **sulcus sign** (inferior laxity in adduction) → **RI closure / CHL imbrication** improves inferior stability (Harryman)

### Dynamic stabilisers – concavity compression & rotator cuff

**Rotator cuff** — **main dynamic stabiliser**; **compresses humeral head into glenoid**; works most in **midrange**, not extremes

**Concavity compression** — **most important midrange stabilising mechanism**; depends on **depth of concavity + magnitude of compressive force**\
✗ Wrong options: isometric ligaments · capsule tension · deltoid

| ✗ Distractor | Actual role |
|---|---|
| SGHL, MGHL | **Static** stabilisers |
| Teres major | Adducts + IR; not a significant stabiliser |
| Deltoid | Abducts; not main stabiliser |

### Free body analysis of deltoid

**Arm 90° abduction** — A (deltoid lever) = 3 cm, B = 30 cm → (A × D) − (B × 0.5W) = 0 → **D = 5W**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification
