### Normal variants (Buford complex, cord-like MGHL) – do not repair

| Variant | Frequency | Description |
|---|---|---|
| **Normal** (labrum on rim + flat/broad MGHL) | Most common | Cord-like MGHL quoted in **86%** (Orthobullets) |
| **Sublabral recess (sulcus)** (TO) | **Most common** variant | **11–1 o'clock** at biceps–labral complex; smooth, **<2 mm**; follows rim **medially**; **does not extend posterior to biceps anchor** |
| **Sublabral foramen (hole)** (TO) | **~12%** (Orthobullets) · **11%** (TO) | Anterosuperior labrum detached **1–3 o'clock**, anterior to biceps anchor; smooth, **<1.5 mm** |
| Sublabral foramen + cord-like MGHL | | |
| **Buford complex** | **~1.5%** | **Cord-like MGHL + absent anterosuperior labrum** (MGHL attaches to base of biceps anchor) |
| Meniscoid labrum | 1% | |

![Anterosuperior labral variants: (a) normal, (b) sublabral foramen, (c) foramen + cord-like MGHL, (d) Buford](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3032-variants-in-anterosuperior-labrum-anatomy-a-normal-b-sublabr.jpg)

**Buford (TO)** — **normal variant**, 1.5% · cord-like **MGHL** (✗ Wrong options: SGHL) + absent **anterosuperior** (✗ Wrong options: anteroinferior) labrum · not abnormal · **should not be repaired** → ↓ ROM

**Repairing Buford to glenoid** — **painful loss of ER** (and elevation) · **loss of ER with arm at side** > at 90° abduction (MGHL tighter at side)

**Sublabral foramen vs tear (TO)** — true anterosuperior tear **propagates superiorly into biceps anchor or inferiorly into IGHL** attachment

**Tubercle of Assaki (TO)** — ridge of subchondral bone at **centre of glenoid** with focal **cartilage thinning** → do not mistake for cartilage defect

**Subacromial pseudospur (TO)** — prominence of acromial angle at **CA ligament** attachment → mimics caudal osteophyte; also at deltoid tendon attachment → mimics enthesophyte

### Key radiology findings

**Sublabral recess** — **best seen with arthrography** (TO)

**AHI** — **7–8 mm**; normal on X-ray but ↓ on supine MRI (arm weight removed) → **multiple-tendon cuff tear**

**HAGL on MRI** — tear of **humeral attachment of IGHL**

### Acromial morphology

| Type | Shape |
|---|---|
| I | **Flat** |
| II | **Curved** |
| III | **Hooked** |

### HAGL lesion

**Mechanism** — **abduction + hyperextension + ER** (e.g. 15-year-old wrestler)

**HAGL** — humeral avulsion of IGHL; **much less common than Bankart** (anteroinferior labral tear)

| ✗ Distractor | Why |
|---|---|
| Lesser tuberosity avulsion | Very rare; **12–13-year-olds** |
| Subscapularis tear | Same mechanism, **older** patients |

## 5 Management

### Arthroscopic vs open repair – external rotation preservation

**Advantage of arthroscopic** — **preservation of ER** (spares subscapularis; no anterior capsular incision)

**Not advantages** — ↓ healing time · outpatient (both) · faster return (recovery identical) · ↓ recurrence (arthroscopic **slightly higher or equal**)

### Shoulder arthrodesis position

| Plane | Angle |
|---|---|
| Abduction | **15–20°** |
| Forward flexion | **20–25°** |
| Internal rotation | **40–50°** |

**Other sources** — **30-30-30** (Orthobullets arthrodesis note) · ~40° abd / 35° FF / 20° IR (OB) — read the options

## X vs Y

| Ligament | Position tested | Restrains |
|---|---|---|
| **SGHL** | **Arm at side (0°/adducted)** | **Inferior** translation, ER |
| **CHL** | Adducted · flexion + adduction + IR | **Inferior** + ER · **posterior** |
| **MGHL** | **45° abduction + ER** | **Anterior** (± posterior) |
| **AIGHL** | **90° abduction + max ER** | **Anterior / inferior** |
| **PIGHL** | **90° flexion + IR** (adducted) | **Posterior** |

| | **Sublabral recess** | **Sublabral foramen** | **Buford complex** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Position | **11–1 o'clock** (biceps anchor) | **1–3 o'clock** (anterosuperior) | Anterosuperior |
| Labrum | Superior labrum not attached to cartilage | Detached from rim | **Absent** |
| MGHL | Normal | Normal / cord-like | **Cord-like** |
| Width | <2 mm | <1.5 mm | — |
| Repair? | No | No | **No (→ loss of ER)** |

## How it's asked

**"Most important static stabiliser at 45° abduction + ER?"** → **MGHL** (TO)

**"Main stabiliser in abduction + ER?"** → **IGHL** (TO)

**"At 90° abduction, main stabiliser against anterior dislocation?"** → **anterior-inferior GHL** (TO)

**">45° abduction, restraint to anterior + posterior stress?"** → **anterior inferior GHL** (TO)

**"Primary static restraint to anterior translation at 90° abduction?"** → **IGHL complex**\
✗ Wrong options: bony shape · negative pressure

**"SGHL under greatest stress?"** → **inferiorly translated, arm adducted (5°)**

**"Asterisk structure (crosses subscapularis) — function?"** → **prevents anterior translation at 45° abduction** (MGHL)

**"Labelled ligament restraining anterior translation at 45° abduction + ER?"** → **MGHL (D)**

**"Primary restraint to inferior translation?"** → **coracohumeral ligament**\
✗ Wrong options: MGHL · subscapularis · CAL

**"90° abduction, full ER — resists anterior translation?"** → **anterior band of IGHL complex**

**"Arrowed structure, posterior portal — function?"** → **resists anterior + posterior translation at 45° abduction** (MGHL)

**"Posterior instability, + Kim test — structure to repair?"** → **posterior band IGHL**\
✗ Wrong options: AIGHL · CHL

**"Besides biceps, rotator interval contains?"** → **CHL + SGHL**\
✗ Wrong options: MGHL

**"Arthroscopic area between biceps and subscapularis?"** → **rotator cuff interval**

**"Dynamic stabiliser compressing head into glenoid?"** → **rotator cuff muscles**\
✗ Wrong options: teres major · deltoid

**"Most important midrange stabilising mechanism?"** → **concavity compression**

**"Buford complex?"** → **normal variant: cord-like MGHL + absent anterosuperior labrum** (TO)

**"Repairing Buford complex causes?"** → **loss of external rotation**

**"Probe on cord-like MGHL repaired to glenoid — result?"** → **loss of ER with arm at side**

**"Asterisk, posterior portal, anterior structure crossing subscapularis?"** → **MGHL**

**"15-year-old wrestler, abduction-hyperextension-ER, MRI?"** → **tear of humeral insertion of IGHL (HAGL)**\
✗ Wrong options: lesser tuberosity avulsion · Bankart

**"AVN after proximal humerus fracture — artery?"** → **posterior humeral circumflex (4)**

**"Advantage of arthroscopic anterior stabilisation?"** → **preservation of external rotation**

**"False about glenoid labrum?"** → **posterosuperior labrum has poorest blood supply** (TO) (anterosuperior is poorest)

**"GH : ST motion ratio?"** → **2:1** (TO)

**"False about glenoid/humeral version?"** → **both anteverted** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Internal Impingement

*Shoulder & Elbow · 20 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Who** — major cause of shoulder pain in **throwing + overhead athletes**; very common in **elite baseball pitchers** · also swimming, volleyball, javelin

**Definition** — repetitive contact of **undersurface (articular side) of posterior rotator cuff** + greater tuberosity against **posterosuperior glenoid/labrum**

**Impinged structure** — **posterior rotator cuff**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior cuff · GH ligaments · biceps

**Contact** — may be **physiologic or pathologic** in throwers

### Anatomy – glenohumeral stability & cuff force couples

| Restraint | Structures |
|---|---|
| **Static** | **GH ligaments**, labrum, articular congruity + version, negative intra-articular pressure |
| **Dynamic** | Rotator cuff, biceps, periscapular muscles |

| Force couple plane | Balance |
|---|---|
| **Coronal** | **Inferior cuff (IS, TM, subscapularis)** vs superior moment of **deltoid** |
| **Transverse** | **Subscapularis (anterior)** vs **IS + TM (posterior)** |

**Cuff role** — dynamic stability, centres head → stable fulcrum; goal of cuff repair = restore equilibrium

![Glenohumeral ligaments — posterior band of IGHL](../../MCQ/attachments/q212172-illa-glenohumeral-ligs.jpg)

| Ligament | Role in internal impingement |
|---|---|
| **Posterior band IGHL (PIGHL) + posterior capsule** | **Tight + thickened** ✓ (target of sleeper stretch) |
| Anterior band IGHL | **Lax** (✗ Wrong options: tight) |
| Anterior axillary pouch, MGHL, SGHL | Not implicated |
| Superior band IGHL | Most strain in **SLAP** lesions |

## 2 Mechanism

### Pathomechanics – late cocking, abduction–external rotation & posterosuperior contact

**Position** — **abduction + maximal ER**\
✗ Wrong options: adduction/IR · abduction/IR · flexion/ER

**Phase** — **late cocking** (+ early acceleration); max ER\
✗ Wrong options: wind-up · early cocking · deceleration · follow-through

**Contact** — **posterior articular supraspinatus** (± anterior infraspinatus) between greater tuberosity + **posterosuperior glenoid**

**Peel-back** — posterosuperior labrum peeled back by biceps → SLAP

**Internal vs external** — internal = **articular side** · subacromial ("external") = **bursal side**

![Internal vs external impingement](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3054-internalvsexternal.jpg)

### Posterior capsular contracture & GIRD – stretching

**Most clearly associated cause** — **posterior capsular contracture** (✓ primary process)\
✗ Wrong options: anterior capsular laxity (variable, less direct) · CA arch stenosis (subacromial) · Bennett lesion (no causal link)

**Contracture effect** — blocks normal anteroinferior translation in late cocking → **obligatory posterosuperior translation** → posterosuperior labral + articular cuff pathology

**Etiology** — tight **posterior band IGHL** + **anterior micro-instability** · multifactorial: altered biomechanics, subtle instability, posterior contracture, scapular dyskinesia

**Primary etiology** — **posterior capsular contracture** (OB, Orthobullets) · **GH instability** commonly primary (one OB explanation) — read the options

**Anterior labrum** — may become **thinned** (obligate translation → anterior micro-instability)

**Associated conditions** — **GIRD**, SLAP tears, **SICK scapula** + dyskinesia, biceps tendinitis, anterior instability

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — diffuse **posterior shoulder** pain (posterior deltoid), deep-seated · worse with throwing, esp. **late cocking + early acceleration** · ↓ velocity

**Inspection** — proximal humeral retroversion · tender along **infraspinatus**

**ROM** — **↑ ER + ↓ IR** · IR loss **>20° at 90°** (Orthobullets) · stabilise scapula · total arc **preserved** (Orthobullets, OB) · total arc **↓** (one OB stem) — read the stem

**Strength** — rotator cuff weakness often (may be 5/5)

**Isolated finding** — ↓ IR with **no other positive provocative test**\
✗ Wrong options: sulcus sign · Speed's · ↓ ER · ↓ abduction

| Test | Technique | Positive |
|---|---|---|
| **Apprehension test** (internal impingement) | Max ER + abduction + extension | **Posterior** pain, relieved in neutral |
| **Whipple** | Forward flexion + adduction + scapular retraction, resist | Pain → **partial supraspinatus tear** |
| Sulcus sign | Downward pull, arm adducted | **Inferior instability** (superior GH laxity) |
| Speed's | 90° FF, supinated, resist downward | **Biceps tendinitis** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Classification** — **none formal**

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — complete shoulder series; usually normal · **Bennett lesion** on AP

![Bennett lesion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3054-bennet-lesion.jpg)

**Bennett lesion** — **mineralisation / exostosis of posteroinferior glenoid** = **traction spur** of posteroinferior capsule (PIGHL) · formed in **deceleration + follow-through** · **baseball pitchers** · **22%** of MLB pitchers, **none needed surgery**\
✗ Wrong options: football, swimmers, rowers

**MRI / MRA** — **PASTA** (partial articular supraspinatus–infraspinatus tendon avulsion), fraying, tear · signal at **greater tuberosity** (posterolateral head edema, **cysts**) + **posterosuperior labrum** · ↑ anterior capsular volume · thickened **PIGHL**

**ABER view** — reproduces impingement position; dynamic humeral + glenoid sides

![PASTA lesion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3054-pasta.jpg)

### Associated lesions & diagnosis (articular-sided cuff tears, posterosuperior labral fraying)

| Lesion (spectrum) | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Articular-sided partial cuff tear** | **Supraspinatus–infraspinatus interval** (✗ Wrong options: bursal = external) |
| **Posterior + superior labral lesion** | **SLAP with posterior extension**; posterosuperior fraying |
| **Bennett lesion** | Posterior capsule/glenoid hypertrophy + scarring (calcified) |
| **Chondral injury** | **Posterior glenoid** |
| Greater tuberosity | Cystic changes |
| Capsule | **Lax anterior**, **tight posterior** |

**Not part of it** — subscapularis tear · bursal-sided tear · Bankart · Hill-Sachs · tight anterior capsule · lax posterior capsule · full-thickness tear (needs significant trauma)

**Bennett + ↓ arc + deep pain + posterosuperior labral fraying** — **internal impingement**\
✗ Wrong options: SLAP alone · subacromial = Hawkins + · calcific tendinitis

**Javelin thrower, failed PT, SLAP with posterior extension** — **internal impingement**\
✗ Wrong options: EDS/MDI · traumatic anterior instability · osteonecrosis

### Asymptomatic MRI findings in pitchers

**Common asymptomatic findings** — partial cuff tear · SLAP / posterior-superior labral tear · **↑ humeral retroversion** · thick posterior capsule · posterolateral head cysts

**Asymptomatic pitcher** — **continue pitching**\
✗ Wrong options: rest · posterior release · cuff repair · rotational osteotomy

**GIRD definition** — **IR deficit 15° + total arc deficit ≥10°** (OB) · **>20° IR loss** (Orthobullets) · **>25°** (Orthobullets GIRD note) — read the options

## 5 Management

```
Internal impingement
├─ First line → stop throwing + PT (posterior capsule stretching)
├─ Failed extended PT + PASTA <50% / Bennett / peel-back → arthroscopic debridement
├─ Failed PT + partial >50% / full-thickness / unstable labrum → repair (arthroscopic or mini-open)
└─ Persistent posterior contracture / anterior instability → add posterior release or anterior plication
```

### Nonoperative

**Regimen** — break from throwing until pain-free → supervised return with proper mechanics · **sleeper + cross-body stretches**, cuff strength balancing, scapular stabilisation, kinetic chain · **6 months** stretching

**Stretch target** — **posteroinferior capsule** · **posterior band IGHL**\
✗ Wrong options: superior, anterior, anteroinferior, inferior

**Prevention ✓** — **posterior capsular stretching** ↓ shoulder + elbow injury risk\
✗ Wrong options: cuff strengthening · periscapular strengthening (less direct) · SLAP / partial cuff repair

**Outcome** — correlates with **compliance**; surgery results **unpredictable** → only after extended failed PT

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication (after failed nonop) |
|---|---|
| **Arthroscopic debridement** (cuff ± labrum) | **Partial tear <50%** · **Bennett lesion** · **peel-back labral lesion** |
| **Cuff ± labral repair** (arthroscopic / mini-open) | **Partial >50%** or full-thickness · **unstable labral tear** |
| **Posterior capsule release vs anterior stabilisation** | Persistent posterior contracture or anterior instability (adjunct) |

### Techniques

**Debridement** — EUA + diagnostic arthroscopy (intra-articular + subacromial) · shaver · **accelerated rehab**, shorter immobilisation

**Cuff repair** — arthroscopic addresses labrum too · bursectomy · **no acromioplasty** if no bursal pathology · footprint abrasion · **portal of Wilmington** · partial: **in situ trans-tendinous** (pulley; functionally shortens tendon) or complete the tear → single/double row

**Posterior release** — cautery / shaver · **axillary nerve risk** at inferior border of infraspinatus

**Anterior stabilisation** — **capsular plication** most common

## 6 Complications

**Progression to full-thickness tear** — small risk after debridement alone

**Return to play** — **delayed / worse after cuff repair** in throwers

**Axillary nerve injury** — posterior release, **inferior border of infraspinatus**

## X vs Y

| | **Internal impingement** | **Subacromial (external) impingement** |
|---|---|---|
| Cuff side | **Articular** (posterior SS/IS) | **Bursal** |
| Pain | **Posterior**, ABER, late cocking | **Anterolateral**, IR in abduction |
| Test | Posterior apprehension pain, ↓ IR | **Hawkins / Neer** + |
| Cause | Posterior capsular contracture, micro-instability | Hooked acromion, CA arch stenosis |

| | **Bennett lesion** | **Hill-Sachs** |
|---|---|---|
| Site | **Posteroinferior glenoid** | Posterosuperior humeral head |
| Nature | Traction spur (PIGHL) | Impaction fracture |
| Context | Pitchers | Anterior dislocation |

## How it's asked

**"Posterosuperior labrum impinges on which structure?"** → **posterior rotator cuff**

**"Posteroinferior glenoid mineralisation — which athletes?"** → **baseball pitchers**

**"Phase of internal impingement?"** → **late cocking**

**"Arm position?"** → **abduction + external rotation**

**"Structure pathologically thickened (figure)?"** → **posterior IGHL + posterior capsule**\
✗ Wrong options: cuff = thinned · acromion · biceps · anterior labrum = thinned

**"30° IR deficit — stretch which capsule?"** → **posteroinferior**

**"Pitcher, ↓ velocity, normal MRA, normal Hawkins — exam finding?"** → **↓ internal rotation**\
✗ Wrong options: sulcus · Speed's

**"Sleeper stretch addresses?"** → **posterior band of IGHL**

**"Proven to ↓ shoulder and elbow injury?"** → **posterior capsular stretching**

**"Most clearly associated with internal impingement?"** → **posterior capsular contracture**\
✗ Wrong options: Bennett · anterior laxity

**"25° IR deficit — tight structure (figure)?"** → **A = posterior band IGHL**\
✗ Wrong options: AIGHL, MGHL, SGHL, axillary pouch

**"16-year-old pitcher, MRI posterolateral head edema + undersurface tear?"** → **internal impingement**

**"Characteristic lesion?"** → **articular-sided rotator cuff tear**

**"Javelin, failed PT, arthroscopy SLAP with posterior extension — association?"** → **internal impingement**

**"Late-cocking pain, ER 100°/IR 45° — MRI?"** → **articular-sided supraspinatus tear**\
✗ Wrong options: bursal · Bankart · Hill-Sachs

**"Clinical + radiographic findings?"** → **↑ ER/↓ IR + articular-side posterosuperior fraying + posterior capsule/glenoid scarring**

**"Bennett lesion + ↓ arc + posterosuperior labral fraying — diagnosis?"** → **internal impingement**

**"Asymptomatic pro pitcher, MRI partial cuff + labral tear — advice?"** → **continue pitching**

# Lateral Epicondylitis (Tennis Elbow)

*Shoulder & Elbow · 21 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — **most common cause of elbow pain** · **1–3% of adults/y** · **dominant arm**

**Demographics** — **45–64 y** · **♂ = ♀** · up to **50% of tennis players** · labourers with **heavy tools**, repetitive gripping/lifting

**Risk factors (TO)** — **smoking**, **obesity**, repetitive wrist extension

**Tennis risk factors** — **poor swing technique (backhand)**, **heavy racket**, **incorrect grip size**, **high string tension**

**Association** — **radial tunnel syndrome** coexists: **5%** (Orthobullets, associated conditions) · **up to 15%** (Orthobullets, missed-diagnosis complication) — read the options

### ECRB anatomy – origin, insertion & innervation

**Most common tendon (TO)** — **ECRB**; **ECRB > ECRL** (TO); also ED, EDM, ECU, supinator can be involved\
✗ Wrong options: ECRL · supinator · ED · ECU

| Muscle | Origin | Insertion | Key fact |
|---|---|---|---|
| **ECRB** (extensor carpi radialis brevis) | **Lateral epicondyle** (anterior aspect); **13 × 7 mm diamond** superior to centre of epicondyle | **Base of 3rd MC** | **Deep branch radial n. (fibres of sixth + seventh cervical nerves, C6–C7)**; tendon just superficial to capsule; **deep + posterior to ECRL** |
| **ECRL** | **Lateral supracondylar ridge** (just below BR) | **Dorsal base 2nd MC** | **First fleshy fibres proximal to common extensor tendon** |
| **Anconeus** | **Same attachment site as ECRB** | — | — |
| ED, EDM, ECU | Lateral epicondyle (common extensor origin) | — | — |

| ✗ Distractor | Belongs to |
|---|---|
| Largest cross-sectional area of elbow flexors | **Brachialis** |
| Lateral border of cubital fossa; greatest mechanical advantage of elbow flexors | **Brachioradialis** |
| Inserts on Lister's tubercle | **No muscle** |

![Common extensor origin: ECRB, EDC, ECU, ECRL](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3082-anatomy-of-common-extensor-tendons.jpg)

**PIN** — enters **supinator just distal to radial head** → radial tunnel syndrome

**LUCL** — **lateral epicondyle → supinator crest of ulna**

## 2 Mechanism

**Overload** — **eccentric overload** at common extensor origin → **microtear of ECRB origin** (± ECRL, ECU)

**Triggers** — **repetitive wrist extension + forearm pronation** · tenodesis effect to optimise grip → ECRB overuse · **backhand** in tennis · hammering

**Underuse (TO)** — **stress shielding** / pain-inhibited **underuse** of ECRB → weakening → **TRUE cause**\
✗ Wrong options: "underuse cannot cause it"

**Shear (TO)** — shearing forces → **fibrocartilaginous ECRB enthesis** → weaker bone junction\
✗ Wrong options: tensile

### Histology – angiofibroblastic tendinosis

**Lesion** — **angiofibroblastic hyperplasia / dysplasia / tendinosis** = **fibroblast hypertrophy** (dense fibroblasts) + **disorganised collagen** + **vascular hyperplasia**

**Inflammation** — **NO inflammatory cells** → "**tendinosis**", tendonitis/epicondylitis = **misnomers**; **failed reparative process** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: macrophages · neutrophils

**Also present** — **normal tendon histology**, **hypercellularity** (TO), nerve sensitisation

**✗ Histology distractors** — abundant inflammatory cells · neutrophils around microvessels · cellular atypia · chondroblastic proliferation · primarily calcium deposition · osteoblastic rimming · reactive hyperaemia · cystic degeneration

![Angiofibroblastic hyperplasia](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3082-angiofibro-histo.jpg)

**Nirschl (OB)** — 1,213 patients, 88 operated; immature fibroblastic + vascular infiltration of ECRB origin; **97% improved** after excision + repair

### Stages of lateral epicondylitis (TO)

| Stage | Features |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Acute inflammatory** response; may resolve completely |
| **2** | **Angiofibroblastic hyperplasia** (fibroblasts, vascular hyperplasia, disorganised collagen), hypercellularity → **tendinosis**; **most common stage at presentation** |
| 3 | **Structural failure** → partial or complete **rupture** |
| 4 | Stage 2/3 + **fibrosis**, soft matrix calcification, **hard osseous calcification** |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — lateral elbow pain with **resisted wrist extension**, **gripping**; **↓ grip strength**

**Tenderness** — **point tenderness at ECRB origin**, few mm **distal to tip** of lateral epicondyle

**Neuro exam** — differentiates from **entrapment syndromes**

| Provocative test | Manoeuvre |
|---|---|
| **Cozen (TO)** | **Resisted wrist extension, elbow extended** (± forearm pronated) → lateral pain |
| **Maudsley** | **Resisted extension of 3rd (long) finger** → lateral epicondyle pain |
| Passive stretch | **Maximal wrist flexion** / passive wrist flexion in pronation |
| ✗ Wrong option: radial tunnel | **Resisted supination** (arm + wrist extended); tenderness **3–4 cm distal + anterior** to epicondyle |

**Rule out** — **LUCL insufficiency** (**chair test, push-up test**) · OCD / arthritis (↓ ROM) · **posterolateral plica** (pain/clicking on passive flexion in pronation + supination)

### Differential of lateral elbow pain in athletes

**Differential** — posterolateral plica · **PLRI** · **radial tunnel** · occult fracture · **cervical radiculopathy** · **capitellar OCD** · triceps tendinitis · radiocapitellar OA · **shingles**

**Tennis player, continuous elbow pain (TO)** — source gives no key; Orthobullets causes = **incorrect grip size**, poor (backhand) technique, heavy racket, high string tension

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical** (symptoms + exam)

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (AP + lateral)** — usually **normal**; **calcification near epicondyle (up to ½)**; rarely changes management

**MRI** — **not needed**; **↑ signal at ECRB origin (up to 90%)**, thickening/thinning, oedema · **does not correlate with severity** → does not dictate management · use when diagnosis unclear / refractory

**US** — **thickened, hypoechoic ECRB**; **most useful tool in experienced hands** (operator-dependent)

![MRI: increased signal at ECRB origin](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3082-mri-elbow.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Lateral epicondylitis
├─ First line → activity modification + eccentric PT / OT ± counterforce strap
│      (NSAIDs, ice; injections second line)
├─ Persistent → injection (PRP better at 12 mo than steroid)
└─ Failed ≥12 mo nonop + clear diagnosis → ECRB release + debridement (open = arthroscopic)
```

### Nonoperative treatment – eccentric therapy, steroid & PRP injections

**Natural history** — resolves with time; recovery **6–12 mo**; **no consistent difference between nonop treatment modalities and placebo** / observation

**Success** — **80–90%** at 1 y, only **2–4%** need surgery (Orthobullets, OB) · **up to 95%** (Orthobullets prognosis, TO) · **>95%** (OB) — read the options

**Eccentric exercise** — **first line**; **eccentric > concentric** (TO); ↓ pain (grip gain unclear); beneficial effect on **tendon biology**

**Occupational therapy (OB)** — activity modification + **eccentric strengthening** + pain management = best initial

**PT at 1 y** — **PT > steroid injection**; **steroid (± PT) = worst outcomes**

**NSAIDs** — short-term benefit only; not an inflammatory condition; ↑ **GI side effects**

| Injection | Evidence |
|---|---|
| **Corticosteroid** | **Short-term (2–6 wk)** ↓ pain + ↑ grip vs placebo (TO); **not beyond 6 wk** · RCT vs placebo: **equivalent** pain, grip, PROMs · **worst at 1 y** · **skin/muscle atrophy**, **tendon attenuation + rupture** · second line / not recommended |
| **PRP** | Orthobullets: **better than steroid** for long-term pain, healing, function · OB: **↓ pain + ↑ PROMs at 12 mo vs steroid**, **no ↑ strength/function**, no faster return to work, not better at 6 wk (steroid better short term) · TO (Cochrane): **no patient-important benefit vs placebo** — read the options |
| PRP type | **Leukocyte-rich** promising (✗ Wrong options: leukocyte-poor best); heterogeneous preparations |
| Others | Botulinum toxin, autologous blood, hyaluronate, acupuncture, ionto/phonophoresis, **nitroglycerin patch** |

**Steroid mechanism** — analgesic on **neuropeptides (CGRP, substance P)** ↑ in tendinopathy; **suppresses fibroblast collagen + ECM** production

**ESWT** — **no difference vs placebo at 6 mo**; + stretching = stretching alone (TO)

**Orthoses (TO)** — **inelastic, nonarticular, proximal forearm strap** → may ↓ pain + ↑ grip at **3 wk**; bracing ≤6 wk may help ADLs · conflicting: not better than sham; may be inferior short term to steroid + topical NSAIDs

**Tennis modifications** — slower surface, more **flexible racquet**, **lower string tension**, **larger grip**

### Operative criteria

| Criterion | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Indication** | Failed **prolonged nonop (12 mo)** · **clear diagnosis** (isolated LE) · intra-articular pathology |
| **Contraindication** | **Inadequate nonop trial** · **noncompliance** with nonop |
| **Poor-outcome / surgery predictors** | **Depression, anxiety, poor coping (greatest)** · ipsilateral **radial tunnel** · **prior injection** · **workers' compensation** |

### ECRB release & debridement

**Open** — incision over common extensor origin → **lift ECRL off ECRB** → **Nirschl scratch test** → excise degenerate tissue → **decorticate** epicondyle → **watertight capsule repair** → side-to-side closure → reattach (anchor / tunnel)\
✗ Wrong options: synovial fistula

**Arthroscopic** — visualises + treats intra-articular pathology · resect lateral capsule **anteriorly** · release ECRB where muscle begins · decorticate · **do not pass mid-radial head**

**Outcome** — **open = arthroscopic** (satisfaction, return to work, complications) · **90–100% improve**, **up to 40% persistent pain**

## 6 Complications

### Iatrogenic LUCL injury after debridement

**Cause** — debridement **posterior to equator of radial head** → **LUCL** injury → **PLRI**

**Safe vs unsafe zones** — anterior + superficial resection safe · **too distal → PIN** at risk

**Presentation** — lateral pain, **clicking/catching**, can't push up from chair / do push-ups → **chair push-up test +**

**PLRI mechanism** — instability with **elbow extension + supination + valgus**

**Chronic iatrogenic PLRI (3 mo)** — **LUCL reconstruction with palmaris longus or gracilis allograft**\
✗ Wrong options: primary repair (chronic) · revision ECRB debridement · MCL procedures (milking manoeuvre/thrower)

![Arthroscopic ECRB release zone relative to LCL](../../MCQ/attachments/q218039-illa-arthroscopic-diagram-of-ecrb.jpg)

| Complication | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Persistent pain** | **Up to 40%** |
| **LUCL injury → PLRI** | Beyond radial head equator |
| **Missed radial tunnel** | **Up to 15%** |
| Radial nerve / PIN injury | Distal dissection |
| **HO** | ↓ with **thorough irrigation after decortication** |
| Stiffness | Esp. with intra-articular procedure |
| Synovial fistula | Capsule not repaired watertight |
| Infection, missed PLRI | — |

## X vs Y

| | **Lateral epicondylitis** | **Radial tunnel syndrome** | **PLRI** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tenderness | **ECRB origin**, just distal to epicondyle | **3–4 cm distal + anterior** (supinator) | Lateral |
| Painful test | **Resisted wrist extension** (elbow extended), long-finger extension | **Resisted supination**, resisted 3rd-finger extension | **Chair / push-up test**, clicking |
| Structure | ECRB | PIN | **LUCL** |

## How it's asked

**"Most common tendon in tennis elbow?"** → **ECRB** (TO)

**"Microtear of which muscle?"** → **ECRB** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: ED, ECU, ECRL

**"Backhand pain, resisted middle-finger extension painful — attachment?"** → **ECRB** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: BR, EDC, ECRL

**"Carpenter, pain hammering — tendon?"** → **ECRB** (TO; key not provided by source)

**"Hammer pain, resisted wrist extension elbow extended — attachment?"** → **ECRB**\
✗ Wrong options: distal biceps, BR, ECRL, supinator

**"Shares origin with the angiofibroblastic tendon?"** → **anconeus**\
✗ Wrong options: BR, annular ligament, FCU, PL

**"Origin + insertion of implicated muscle?"** → **lateral epicondyle, 3rd metacarpal**\
✗ Wrong options: supracondylar ridge / 2nd MC = ECRL

**"Labelled extensor origin (figure)?"** → **B = ECRB**\
✗ Wrong options: A ECRL, C ECU, D EDM, E EDC

**"True about structure involved, golfer?"** → **innervated by C6–C7 fibres**\
✗ Wrong options: largest flexor CSA = brachialis · 2nd MC · cubital fossa border = BR · first fleshy fibres = ECRL

**"True about histopathology?"** → **inflammatory cells not seen** (TO)

**"NOT true about lateral epicondylitis?"** → **abundant macrophages** (TO) (underuse, hypercellularity, paucity of inflammatory cells = true)

**"Region with asterisks on histology?"** → **angiofibroblastic dysplasia**

**"Histology of resected tissue?"** → **angiofibroblastic tendinosis**\
✗ Wrong options: chondroblastic · inflammation · calcium · no normal tendon

**"3 mo pain, NSAIDs + band failed — next?"** → **eccentric exercises** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: PRP, steroid, ESWT

**"Squash player 3 mo — next step?"** → **eccentric exercises**\
✗ Wrong options: PRP, steroid, ultrasonic aspiration, surgery

**"Golfer, short duration, X-rays normal — next?"** → **eccentric PT program**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid · radial tunnel injection · MRI · percutaneous tenotomy

**"At 1 year, PT vs steroid?"** → **PT more improvement than steroid**

**"Steroid vs PRP vs placebo?"** → **no consistent difference from placebo**

**"PRP vs prior steroid — expected outcome?"** → **↓ pain at 12 months**\
✗ Wrong options: equal PROMs · faster return · better at 6 wk · better strength

**"Most appropriate initial management (pathophysiology-based)?"** → **OT: activity modification + eccentric strengthening + pain management**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid, NSAIDs, surgery, PRP

**"Post-arthroscopic release clicking, chair push-up pain at 3 mo — surgery?"** → **LUCL reconstruction with palmaris / gracilis allograft**\
✗ Wrong options: primary repair · MCL

**"Excessive debridement beyond which point → instability?"** → **C — equator of radial head** (posteriorly)\
✗ Wrong options: distal = PIN

**"Continuous elbow pain in tennis player?"** → key not provided; risk factors = **incorrect grip size**, poor technique, heavy racket, high string tension

# Lateral Ulnar Collateral Ligament Injury (PLRI)

*Shoulder & Elbow · 22 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Primary elbow stabilisers (coronoid)

**Primary constraint (TO)** — release alone → laxity · **secondary** — release alone insufficient, ↑ laxity only after primary divided

| Primary constraints (TO) | Secondary constraints (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Ulnohumeral articulation (coronoid)** | **Radiohumeral articulation** (radial head) |
| **Anterior MCL (AMCL)** | Common **flexor-pronator** tendon |
| **Lateral collateral ligament complex (LCLC)** | Common **extensor** tendon |
| | **Capsule** |

**Coronoid (TO)** — **main stabiliser** of elbow; transection affects stability most · **loss of ≥50% → instability**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior band MCL · LCL · radial head

**Main osseous stability (TO)** — trochlea ↔ ulnar olecranon articulation; LCL + MCL blend into capsule

### LUCL anatomy & failure site (humeral avulsion)

**LCL complex (4)** — **radial collateral (LRCL)** · **annular** · **accessory lateral collateral** · **lateral ulnar collateral (LUCL)**

**LUCL** — **primary stabiliser to varus + external rotation** (supination) stress; **most robust** of LCL complex; prevents ER subluxation of ulna on humerus

**Origin → insertion** — **lateral epicondyle** → **tubercle of supinator crest (crista supinatoris)** of ulna

**Failure site** — **humeral origin (lateral epicondyle)**, just anterior + distal to it; common extensor origin may also avulse; **radioulnar articulation intact**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnar attachment

![Lateral ligament complex: RCL, annular, accessory, LUCL](../../MCQ/attachments/q217443-illa-lateral-elbow-ligs.jpg)

| ✗ Distractor (labelled diagrams) | Role |
|---|---|
| **Annular ligament** | Stabilises radial head in pronation/supination; does not need repair; not part of LUCL recon |
| Radial collateral / accessory collateral | Less important for PLRI |
| **Orbicular** ligament | Much less important laterally |
| Anterior band MCL | **Primary valgus** restraint (medial) |
| Posterior band MCL | Disrupted in **VPMRI** |
| **Transverse ligament of Cooper** | Coronoid → olecranon; **no stability role** |
| Posterior capsule | Contributes but does not cause PLRI |
| Anconeus | Does not need repair |

| ✗ Location distractor | Site |
|---|---|
| Supinator crest | **LUCL insertion** |
| Radial tuberosity | Biceps insertion |
| Medial epicondyle | **MUCL origin** |
| **Sublime tubercle** | **MUCL insertion** |

## 2 Mechanism

**Definition** — PLRI = **posterolateral subluxation/dislocation of radiocapitellar + ulnohumeral joints**; ulna **supinates (ER) away from humerus**, radial head subluxes **posteriorly on capitellum** in **supination + extension**

**Traumatic (most common)** — **elbow dislocation**: FOOSH → **supination + axial load + valgus** (posterolateral) + extension → LCL complex fails first → **anterior capsule / coronoid** → semilunar notch externally rotates off trochlea

![Mechanism: axial compression, valgus, supination](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3129-mechanism.jpg)

**Iatrogenic** — lateral elbow surgery (**lateral epicondylitis / ECRB debridement**, arthroscopic or open) going **posterior to equator of radial head** → excessive resection → **stay anterior to equator**

**Chronic attenuation** — **cubitus varus malunion** → lateral thrust + abnormal **triceps vector** stretch LUCL

**Radial head fractures** — usually **valgus posterolateral rotatory load** → concomitant LUCL injury common (avulsed from epicondyle)

### O'Driscoll stages (circle of Horii)

| Stage | Disruption | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **LUCL** ± partial/total LCL complex | **PLRI** (pain with varus stress) |
| **2** | + **anterior capsule** | Incomplete posterolateral dislocation |
| **3a** | All soft tissue incl. **posterior MCL**, **except anterior bundle** | Dislocates posteriorly pivoting on **anterior bundle** |
| **3b** | **Complete MCL** | Full dislocation |

**Sequence** — **lateral → medial**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **pain** (vague, poorly localised) · **mechanical: clicking, catching, snapping, clunking, locking** with extension · **frank dislocation is not the most common presentation**

**Classic** — **pushing off armrests to rise from chair** (axial load + supination + valgus) · difficulty with **triceps dips**, **push-ups**

**History** — prior **elbow dislocation** ("never felt right"), or prior **tennis elbow surgery**

**Exam** — often benign; **tenderness over LUCL**, **varus instability** (no firm endpoint); full ROM

### Diagnosis – lateral pivot-shift, chair push-up & clinical signs

**Lateral pivot-shift (O'Driscoll)** — supine, arm **overhead** (shoulder fully ER), forearm **supinated**, **valgus + axial load**, elbow from **full extension → flexion** (to **40°** — Orthobullets; to 90° — OB)

**Pivot-shift findings** — subluxed in extension → **reduces with flexion (triceps tension)** with **clunk**; max rotatory/posterior displacement **~40° flexion**; **skin dimple** proximal to radial head · awake = **apprehension**, anaesthetised = **clunk** (more reliable) · analogous to ITB in ACL-deficient knee

![Lateral pivot-shift: supination, valgus, axial compression](../../MCQ/attachments/q5751-illb-illus-b.jpg)

| Test | Technique / positive |
|---|---|
| **Posterolateral rotatory drawer** | Supine, overhead, supinated; finger under radial head → **posterior force + ER → radial head subluxes** behind capitellum |
| Apprehension | Overhead, supinated, valgus while flexing |
| **Chair rise (chair push-up)** | Pain/apprehension pushing up with forearms supinated (**= positive pivot shift**) |
| **Table-top relocation** | |
| **Floor / prone push-up** | **Can't push up with forearm supinated** |

| ✗ Wrong option: valgus (MUCL) tests | Technique |
|---|---|
| Valgus stress | Valgus at **30° flexion**, medial pain |
| **Milking manoeuvre** | Elbow 90°, supinated, thumb pulled posteriorly |
| **Moving valgus stress** | Ranged **70–120°**, supinated, valgus |
| Symptom | **Pain late cocking / acceleration** = valgus instability |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (AP + lateral)** — rule out fractures, confirm **concentric reduction**; often **normal / little value** in PLRI; may show **impaction defect of posterolateral capitellum**

**Fluoroscopy / stress views** — during pivot-shift → **radial head subluxation** (radiocapitellar + ulnohumeral)

**MRI** — identifies **acute LUCL avulsion**; poor in recurrent/attenuated LUCL (**oblique course**); detects LUCL pathology in only **50%**

### Elbow instability types

| Instability | Cause / association |
|---|---|
| **PLRI** | **LUCL** — after dislocation; lateral pivot shift |
| **Posteromedial rotatory (VPMRI)** | **Varus** + **anteromedial coronoid facet** fracture |
| **Valgus** | **MUCL** — throwers, overuse |
| **Varus** | Rare; **lateral gapping** |
| **Longitudinal forearm** | **Essex-Lopresti** |

| | **VPMRI** | **VPLRI** |
|---|---|---|
| Radial head | **No** fracture | **Fracture** |
| Coronoid | **>15%, anteromedial facet** | **<15%, tip** |
| MCL | **Posterior band** ruptured; anterior band intact (on AM facet) | **Anterior band** ruptured |
| LCL | LCL complex (LUCL) avulsion | LCL complex (LUCL) avulsion |
| Exam | Valgus stress, moving valgus, milking | **Varus stress, chair rise, lateral pivot shift** |

## 5 Management

```
LUCL injury / PLRI
├─ Acute simple dislocation, stable after reduction → splint 5–7 d → early ROM
├─ Unstable after reduction / entrapment / complex → ORIF + LUCL repair
├─ Mild chronic, low-demand → brace + extensor strengthening, avoid varus
└─ Chronic PLRI, failed nonop → LUCL reconstruction with graft
```

### Acute dislocation – splinting position

**Splint** — posterior splint **5–7 days** · **90° flexion** (Orthobullets) · **within stable arc, no specific angle** (OB) — read the options

| Ligaments disrupted | Forearm position |
|---|---|
| **LCL disrupted, MCL intact** (usual) | **Full pronation** (tightens lateral structures) |
| **LCL + MCL** | **Neutral** |
| Isolated MCL | Supination — **not in PLRI** (LCL always torn) |

**Then** — early active ROM; full pronation/supination from 90° → full flexion; **↑ extension by 30°/week in full pronation**; **full supination in extension after 6 wk**

**✗ Wrong options:** sling only, no immobilisation · splint at 45°/90° in supination

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Open reduction + fracture fixation + LUCL repair** | **Osteochondral fragment / soft-tissue entrapment** blocks concentric reduction · **complex dislocation** (fractures) · **acute instability** (e.g. unstable 0–60° after reduction) |
| **LUCL reconstruction with graft** | **Chronic PLRI** (tissue **attenuated**, not amenable to repair) |
| Bracing, extensor strengthening, avoid gravity varus | **Mild chronic PLRI, low-demand** |
| MCL reconstruction / hinged ex-fix | Only if lateral restoration fails — **rare** |

**Chronic ✗ traps** — direct repair, isolated plication (less reliable) · **thermal shrinkage** (long-term effect unclear) · continued bracing after failed nonop · ECRB debridement · RCL reconstruction · MUCL (Tommy John, docking) reconstruction

**Most common lateral ligament needing repair after posterolateral dislocation** — **LUCL** (reliably torn, off humerus)

### Reconstruction & postoperative protocol (avoid varus, pronation, splinting position)

**Approach** — **posterior midline** or **Kocher**

**Graft** — **palmaris longus** (most common), gracilis, triceps fascia; auto- or allograft

**Configuration** — tunnel at **supinator crest** → **"Y" tunnel** in humerus, tied over lateral column; graft must cover **> posterior 25% of radial head** (sling) · **+ capsuloligamentous plication**

**Tensioning** — **neutral rotation + 45° flexion** (Orthobullets)

**Fixation** — **docking** (humeral Y tunnels) · interference screws both sides · overlay (sutures over bone)

![Docking LUCL reconstruction: Y tunnel humerus, supinator crest tunnel](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3129-docking.jpg)

**Coronoid** — **large fragment → screw from dorsal ulna** (ACL-type guide) · **small → excise + suture plication of anterior capsule** to tip

**Post-op** — **locked hinge brace**; **avoid varus** and **shoulder abduction** (gravity varus) **6–12 wk**; ROM with extension block → full extension + supination by **6–8 wk**

**Forearm in rehab** — **full pronation** during ROM / extension exercises until 6 wk (least stress on lateral repair); forearm most stable in pronation

**Post-op avoid** — **varus + supination**\
✗ Wrong options: valgus + pronation

| Ligament compromised | Extension exercises with forearm |
|---|---|
| **LUCL (lateral)** | **Pronation** |
| **MUCL (medial)** | **Supination** |
| **Both** | **Neutral** |

**Radial head arthroplasty + lateral repair** — extension exercises in **pronation**\
✗ Wrong options: avoid motion 6 wk → stiffness

### Iatrogenic injury after lateral epicondylitis surgery

**Presentation** — after ECRB release/debridement: ↑ lateral pain, **catching/locking**, painful rising from chair, **+ pivot shift**

**Injured** — **LUCL**\
✗ Wrong options: annular · orbicular · LRCL · anterior MCL

**Prevention** — keep debridement **anterior to equator of radial head**

## 6 Complications

**Recurrent instability** — **3–8%** (Orthobullets) · **<10%** after repair (OB)\
✗ Wrong options: >10%

**Cutaneous nerve injury** — ↓ with **posterior midline** approach

**Others** — infection · **↓ ROM**

## X vs Y

| | **PLRI (LUCL)** | **Valgus instability (MUCL)** |
|---|---|---|
| Patient | After dislocation / lateral surgery | **Thrower** |
| Symptom | **Clicking pushing off chair** | **Pain late cocking / acceleration** |
| Test | **Lateral pivot shift**, drawer, chair rise | **Milking**, moving valgus, valgus at 30° |
| Splint/rehab forearm | **Pronation** | **Supination** |
| Post-op avoid | **Varus, shoulder abduction** | Valgus |

## How it's asked

**"Ligament D injury — characteristic symptom + sign?"** → **painful clicking pushing off chair armrests; lateral pivot shift**\
✗ Wrong options: late cocking · milking

**"Volleyball player, old dislocation, catching rising from chair, clunk in supinated extension, normal imaging?"** → **posterolateral rotatory instability**\
✗ Wrong options: posteromedial · valgus · longitudinal

**"Apprehension flexing supinated elbow with axial + valgus, posterolateral capitellum defect — disrupted structure?"** → **LUCL**\
✗ Wrong options: posterior band MCL · ligament of Cooper

**"Chronic PLRI, + chair rise — manoeuvre to assess degree?"** → **radiocapitellar reduction as supinated overhead elbow flexes from extension with valgus**\
✗ Wrong options: subluxation on flexion · milking · moving valgus

**"Supine, extension → flexion with valgus, supination, axial load — tests?"** → **lateral ulnar collateral ligament**

**"Positive pivot shift after dislocation — labelled ligament?"** → **LUCL (C)**\
✗ Wrong options: annular · anterior band UCL

**"Axial load + valgus + supination in extension — injured ligament?"** → **LUCL (E)**\
✗ Wrong options: annular A

**"Primary stabiliser to varus + ER, deficient after dislocation?"** → **LUCL (E)**\
✗ Wrong options: RCL · accessory

**"Posterolateral dislocation — structure most likely needing repair?"** → **LUCL (1)**\
✗ Wrong options: annular · anconeus

✗ Wrong options: supinator crest · sublime tubercle

✗ Wrong options: Tommy John · RCL

✗ Wrong options: direct repair · thermal shrinkage

✗ Wrong options: supinated = MUCL · neutral = both

**"After tennis elbow surgery, catching + PLRI — injured ligament?"** → **lateral ulnar collateral**\

# Little League Elbow

*Shoulder & Elbow · 23 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Medial epicondyle apophysitis – pathogenesis & diagnosis

**Definition** — spectrum of **medial elbow** injuries in **adolescent pitchers**: **medial epicondyle stress fracture / apophysitis** · **UCL injury** · **flexor-pronator mass strain**

**Most common cause of medial elbow pain in adolescent pitcher** — **Little League elbow (medial apophysitis)** (TO stem; answer not given by TO source — OB: apophysitis far commoner than other throwing elbow conditions)\
✗ Wrong options: golfer's elbow · olecranon bursitis · capitellar OCD

**Age rule** — **younger / open physis → apophysitis or avulsion** > UCL sprain; medial epicondyle apophysis **fuses at 16–18 y**

**Pathogenesis** — **repetitive valgus loading** → **tension overload** of medial structures; **repetitive flexor-pronator contraction stresses chondro-osseous origin → apophysitis**

| ✗ Pathogenesis distractor | Condition |
|---|---|
| Acute fragmentation of **entire capitellar ossific nucleus** | **Panner disease** |
| Microtraumatic **vascular insufficiency of capitellum** (compression + rotation) | **Capitellar OCD** |
| Rupture anterior band of anterior bundle UCL | UCL tear |
| Repetitive triceps contraction | Olecranon (posterior) |

### MCL (UCL) anatomy & biomechanics (TO)

**MUCL components (TO)** — **anterior oblique ligament (AOL / anterior bundle)** = strongest, **primary valgus restraint** · **posterior oblique / posterior bundle** · **transverse** ligament

**Anterior bundle (TO)** — **medial epicondyle → sublime tubercle**; primary valgus stabiliser **30–120°** (when throwing valgus is highest)

**Bands of AOL (TO)** — **reciprocal**: **anterior band tight in extension**, **posterior band tight in flexion**

| Band (TO) | Primary range |
|---|---|
| **Anterior band** | Up to **90°** (one TO) · **20–120°** (another TO) — read the options |
| **Posterior band** | **>90°**, taut **90–120°** |
| **POL** | **>120°** |

**Other valgus stabilisers (TO)** — **radial head** + **flexor-pronator mass** (dynamic)

**Degeneration in pitcher's elbow (TO)** — **posterior band of anterior bundle** (max force in **acceleration at 90–100°** flexion)\
✗ Wrong options: anterior band · posterior bundle · anteromedial olecranon

**Posteromedial olecranon (TO)** — repetitive microtrauma → **osteophytes at posteromedial** olecranon\
✗ Wrong options: anteromedial

**LCL complex (TO)** — LCL, annular, LUCL

## 2 Mechanism

### Risk factors – pitch counts

| Risk factor | Threshold |
|---|---|
| **Pitches per game** | **>80** |
| **Months of competitive pitching** | **>8 months/year** |
| **Fastball speed** | **>85 mph** |
| Pitching with **arm fatigue / pain** | Yes |
| **Showcases** | Yes |
| **Breaking pitches (curveball, slider) before puberty** | Yes (✗ Wrong options: changeup) |

**Not risk factors** — playing another position after pitching · **2 games/week** (allowed) · **delayed ossification** of medial epicondyle apophysis · 65–75 mph · 6–7 months/year

| Age (y) | Max pitches / game | Max games / week |
|---|---|---|
| 8–10 | 50 | 2 |
| 11–12 | 65 | 2 |
| **13–14** | **75** | 2 |
| 15–16 | 90 | 2 |
| 17–18 | 105 | 2 |

## 3 Presentation

**Triad** — **medial elbow pain** in throwing arm · **↓ throwing speed (velocity), accuracy, distance**

**Exam** — tenderness **medial epicondyle** (inferior aspect) · **pain with valgus stress** · **valgus instability = more severe** (check through flexion → extension)

### Valgus tests for MUCL (TO)

| Test (TO) | Technique | Accuracy |
|---|---|---|
| **Valgus stress test** | Elbow **20–30° flexion** (**unlocks olecranon**) + **forearm supinated**, humerus ER, valgus | Sens **66%**, spec **60%** |
| **Milking manoeuvre** | Pull **thumb** → valgus; **forearm supinated**, **elbow 90°** | |
| **Moving valgus stress (O'Driscoll)** | Upright, shoulder **90° abduction + max ER**, elbow max flexion → extend to 30° under constant valgus; **pain 70–120°** (late cocking → early acceleration) | Sens **100%**, spec **75%** |

**AOL integrity test (TO)** — **valgus stress at 20° flexion**\
✗ Wrong options: full extension · varus · milking in pronation

![Milking manoeuvre: thumb pulled, forearm supinated, elbow 90°](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17730584-1.png)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray AP + lateral** — often normal · **physeal widening** of medial epicondyle · **fragmentation / avulsion**; **compare contralateral** elbow

![Medial epicondyle physeal widening and fragmentation](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3086-me.jpg)

**MRI** — more sensitive; **oedema of medial epicondyle apophysis**; differentiates **bony/physeal vs ligament** injury; confirms **UCL insufficiency**

![MRI: oedema of medial epicondyle apophysis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3086-mrime.jpg)

**First step** — **rule out MCL injury**: **medial widening on stress X-ray / dynamic US**, or valgus instability on exam → **no widening + tender + physeal widening/oedema = apophysitis**

| ✗ Diagnosis distractor | Clue |
|---|---|
| Displaced medial epicondyle avulsion | Displaced fragment → ORIF |
| MUCL tear | Medial widening on stress / instability |
| **Valgus extension overload** | **Olecranon (posteromedial) osteophytes** |
| Ulnar neuritis | Typical throwing condition but much less common than apophysitis |

## 5 Management

```
Adolescent thrower, medial elbow pain
├─ Apophysitis / stress fracture / strain → rest + activity mod + PT
│      (↓ innings, minimal immobilisation, coach + parent education)
├─ Displaced medial epicondyle avulsion → ORIF (cannulated screws)
└─ UCL disruption + insufficiency → UCL reconstruction (palmaris)
```

### Treatment – rest & activity modification

**Mainstay** — **rest, activity modification, ↓ innings pitched per week, PT** (also for acute medial pain + valgus pain after a pitch)

**PT aims** — strengthen **dynamic stabilisers** (flexor-pronator), shoulder + elbow ROM, **core**; gradual return with proper mechanics + safe pitching rules

**Immobilisation** — **minimal** (maintain ROM); no long-arm cast 6–8 wk → **stiffness**

**Avoid fragment excision** — preserve medial epicondyle (**ligament + soft-tissue attachments**, stability)

**No UCL reconstruction** in Little League elbow — ligament **competent**

**✗ Wrong option: arthroscopy / microfracture** — for **capitellar osteochondral lesions**, not medial apophysitis

### Capitellar OCD in young thrower

**Lateral elbow tenderness, nondisplaced capitellar OCD** — **cessation of throwing** (heals if activity curtailed)

**Cast** — only for **noncompliant**; **≤6 weeks**

**Surgery** — **loose bodies or cartilage flaps**\
✗ Wrong options: excision/drilling for intact lesion · steroid injection

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **ORIF medial epicondyle** | **Displaced medial epicondyle avulsion fracture** |
| **UCL reconstruction (palmaris longus autograft)** | **UCL disruption + insufficiency** |

### MCL (UCL) injury – reconstruction (TO)

| UCL reconstruction indications (TO) |
|---|
| **Acute complete rupture** in **competitive thrower** wishing to remain active |
| **High-grade distal tear** |
| **Chronic pain/instability** after **≥4–6 wk** supervised nonop |

**Midsubstance tear + degeneration, wants to play (TO)** — **MCL reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: repair · brace/physio · arthroscopy

**Nonop (TO)** — partial tears; **"active" rest 2–3 months** (no throwing, no valgus stress) + **flexor-pronator strengthening**; also for athletes not returning to sport and asymptomatic in daily life

**Paediatric UCL reconstruction** — similar to adult; **palmaris longus autograft**

## 6 Complications

**List** — **ulnar nerve neuropathy** · continued pain + instability · loss of motion · **inability to return to same level**

## X vs Y

| | **Little League elbow (medial)** | **Capitellar OCD (lateral)** |
|---|---|---|
| Force | **Tension** (valgus, flexor-pronator pull) | **Compression + rotation** (radiocapitellar) |
| Pathology | Apophysitis / avulsion / stress fracture | **Microtraumatic vascular insufficiency** of capitellum |
| Tenderness | **Medial epicondyle** | **Lateral** elbow |
| Treatment | Rest + activity mod + PT | **Stop throwing**; surgery for loose body / cartilage flap |

## How it's asked

**"Medial-sided elbow pain in adolescent pitcher most commonly due to?"** → **Little Leaguer's elbow** (TO; answer not given by source)

**"10-year-old pitcher: medial pain, ↓ effectiveness, ↓ distance — pathogenesis?"** → **repetitive flexor-pronator contraction stressing chondro-osseous origin → apophysitis**\
✗ Wrong options: capitellar fragmentation · UCL rupture

**"12-year-old, tender medial, no widening on dynamic US — cause?"** → **medial apophysitis**\
✗ Wrong options: MUCL tear · valgus extension overload

**"13-year-old, 85 pitches/game, 7 months/y, 75 mph — contributor?"** → **pitches per game** (max 75 at 13)

**"14-year-old, ↓ velocity, physeal widening — risk factor?"** → **averaging 85 pitches/game**\
✗ Wrong options: changeup · 65 mph · 6 months · delayed ossification

**"6-year-old pitcher, lateral tenderness, nondisplaced capitellar lesion — initial?"** → **cessation of throwing**\
✗ Wrong options: cast 3 months · excision · drilling

**"14-year-old acute medial pain after a pitch, MRI — initial treatment?"** → **rest + activity modification**\
✗ Wrong options: cast 8 wk · fragment excision · ORIF · reconstruction

**"11-year-old, 3 straight days pitching, + moving valgus — initial?"** → **rest + physical therapy**\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF screws · reconstruction · cast ≥6 wk

**"Degenerative changes in pitcher's elbow most common in?"** → **posterior band of anterior bundle of UCL** (TO)

**"Test for integrity of anterior oblique band of MUCL?"** → **valgus stress at 20° flexion** (TO)

**"Elbow position for valgus stress test?"** → **20–30° flexion + supination** (TO)

**"Athlete, MCL midsubstance tear with degeneration, wants to play?"** → **MCL reconstruction** (TO)

# Little Leaguer's Shoulder

*Shoulder & Elbow · 24 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Definition

**Little Leaguer's shoulder (LLS)** — **proximal humeral epiphysiolysis** = overuse **Salter-Harris type I** stress injury of proximal humeral physis

**✗ Wrong option: SH II** — LLS does **not** produce a Salter-Harris II fracture

### Epidemiology

**Who** — **skeletally immature overhead athletes**: **youth baseball pitchers** (dominant arm); occasionally **tennis**, football, racket sports

**Sex** — **♂ > ♀**

**Age** — **11–16 y** (Orthobullets, OB) · **11–14 y**, peak proximal physeal growth (OB)

**Throwing** — **10%** of paediatric shoulder pain is throwing-related

### Physeal zone affected

**Zone** — **hypertrophic zone** = **weakest** part of growth plate

**✗ Zone distractors** — metaphysis (not a physeal zone) · provisional zone of calcification · **proliferative** · reserve

![Physeal zones — LLS injures the hypertrophic zone](../../MCQ/attachments/q217347-illa-physis-labeled.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Forces** — repetitive **rotational (torsional) + distraction** stresses (± shear) on unossified physeal cartilage; **rotation > distraction** in importance

| Throwing phase | Physeal stress |
|---|---|
| **Late cocking** (max ER, just before acceleration) | **Extreme rotatory torque ~400% greater** than physis tolerates → **commonest cause** |
| **Deceleration** | Forward arm motion vs cuff → **excessive eccentric** physeal stress |
| ✗ Wrong option: early cocking | Not the peak |

![Pitching phases — late cocking at max ER](../../MCQ/attachments/q218568-illa-phases-of-throwing-3.jpg)

### Risk factors

**Most important** — **number of pitches thrown** + **frequency of pitching** (innings, throwing days, pitches/innings per week–month–year, **multiple teams**, year-round play)

**Breaking pitches** — **implicated** (Orthobullets) · **refuted / not worse than fastballs** (OB) — read the options; "% curveballs = most important factor" is wrong

**Mechanics / body size** — poor throwing kinematics part of accepted theory (OB) · **no clear relationship** with weight/height or mechanics (another OB)

## 3 Presentation

**History** — **↓ pitch velocity**, **↓ accuracy / control**; recent ↑ pitching (camp → season, two "select" teams)

**Pain** — diffuse arm/shoulder pain **with throwing**, **anterolateral/lateral**; worse in **late cocking / deceleration**; resolves with rest → may progress to ADL / rest pain

**Exam** — **point tenderness lateral proximal humerus over physis** · pain with rotation · **GIRD** (e.g. 30° IR difference) · ROM often full · no atrophy, no neuro deficit

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Finding |
|---|---|
| **X-ray views** | **AP in external rotation**, scapular Y, axillary |
| **Subtle cases** | **AP in ER** (changes clearest **anterolateral physis**) + **contralateral comparison** |
| **X-ray findings** | **Widened (lateral) proximal humeral physis vs contralateral** · metaphyseal sclerosis · demineralisation/lucency · osteopenia · metaphyseal calcification · **fragmentation** · cystic change |
| **Normal X-ray** | **17%** |
| **MRI** | **Metaphyseal oedema adjacent to physis**; rules out **labral tear**, **partial articular-sided cuff tear** (rare concomitant) |
| MRI necessity | **Not needed** for diagnosis when X-ray classic |

![Widened proximal humeral physis vs normal side](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3056-19a-moved.jpg)

![MRI — oedema around proximal humeral physis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3056-19b-moved.jpg)

| ✗ View distractor | Used for |
|---|---|
| Distal humeral axial | Paediatric **medial epicondyle** fracture displacement |
| **West Point axillary** | **Bony Bankart**, glenoid bone loss |
| **Zanca** | **AC joint** (AP, 15° cephalic tilt, OB) |
| Swimmer's | Lateral **C-spine** when C7 not seen |

### Diagnosis – physeal widening, imaging & throwing phase

**Classic vignette** — 11–16-yo pitcher, lateral shoulder pain with throwing, ↓ velocity, physeal tenderness, **widened physis** → **LLS**

**15-yo, pain on throwing, ↓ IR, physeal widening (TO)** — **Little League shoulder**\
✗ Wrong options: adhesive capsulitis · pectoralis major tear · subacromial impingement

| ✗ Differential distractor | Why wrong |
|---|---|
| Septic arthritis | No infection |
| SLAP tear / internal impingement | Not this picture |
| External impingement | Subacromial bursitis, cuff tear |
| Chronic retracted supraspinatus tear | Age > 40 |
| Fallen cortical fragment in empty cyst | **UBC** pathological fracture |
| Acromion tip stress fracture | LLS = **proximal humeral physeal** stress fracture |
| Deltoid strain | MRI oedema is **bony metaphyseal** |

## 5 Management

```
Little Leaguer's shoulder
├─ All → stop throwing + PT (nonoperative)
│      └─ symptom-free → progressive throwing programme
├─ Return → short low-velocity tosses → ↑ distance + velocity
└─ Surgery → never indicated
```

### Treatment – cessation of throwing & graded return

**Mainstay** — **cessation of throwing / pitching** until **completely asymptomatic** → **PT** → **progressive throwing programme**

**Rest duration** — **3 months** (Orthobullets; "minimum 3 months", OB) · **2–3 months** (OB) · **6–12 wk** (OB) · **6–8 wk** then progressive throwing (OB) · brief immobilisation + no pitching **for remainder of season** (OB) — read the options

**PT** — **rotator cuff strengthening**, **posterior capsule stretching**, core strengthening

**Throwing programme** — start **only after symptom resolution**; short, low-velocity tosses → slowly ↑ distance + velocity

| ✗ Wrong management | Why |
|---|---|
| **In situ screw fixation**, ORIF (tension band), **CRPP** | Surgery not indicated; risks physis |
| Arthroscopic debridement / Bankart / labral repair | No labral pathology |
| **Subacromial corticosteroid** | Not impingement |
| **Biopsy** | Changes classic, not neoplastic |
| CT / MRI arthrogram as next step | Diagnosis already made |
| **Continue playing** with ↓ pitch count / breaking balls | Physis must heal |
| PT + continue pitching as tolerated | Risks growth arrest |
| **Sling 4 wk + NWB** | Unnecessary; ADLs allowed |
| Avoid throwing **until skeletal maturity** | Excessive |

**Prognosis** — **large majority return to pre-injury level**\
✗ Wrong options: "unlikely to reach pre-injury level"

### Prevention

**Measures** — **proper mechanics (pitching coach)** · **discourage breaking balls until skeletal maturity** · **enforce pitch counts + rest days** · **avoid year-round pitching** · **≤ 2 games/week**

| Age (y) | Pitches/game | Max games/week |
|---|---|---|
| 8–10 | 52 | 2 |
| **11–12** | **68** | **2** |
| 13–14 | 76 | 2 |
| 15–16 | 91 | 2 |
| 17–18 | 106 | 2 |

### Recurrence risk factors (IR deficit, length of rest)

**GIRD** — **3× more likely** recurrent symptoms; ↑ physeal injury risk

**Inadequate rest** — **length of activity cessation** (e.g. only 1 month) = recurrence risk

**> 2 games/week** — recurrence risk

**Not recurrence risks** — pitch count within age limit (68 at 12 y) · avoiding breaking balls · pitching coach

## 6 Complications

**Premature physeal closure** — **growth arrest** + **angular deformity** of proximal humerus

**Cause** — **continuing to pitch** / not managed properly

## X vs Y

| | **Late cocking** | **Deceleration** |
|---|---|---|
| Arm | **Maximal external rotation** | Forward motion opposed by cuff |
| Physeal force | **Rotatory torque** (~400% > tolerance) | **Eccentric** stress |
| MCQ answer | **Commonest cause** of LLS | Also loads physis |

| | **Orthobullets** | **OB explanations** |
|---|---|---|
| Breaking balls | **Implicated**; discourage until maturity | **Refuted**; not worse than fastballs (avoidance still advised for recurrence) |
| Rest | **3 months** | 6–8 wk · 6–12 wk · 2–3 months · remainder of season |
| Age | 11–16 | 11–14 (also 11–16) |

## How it's asked

**"15-yo, throwing pain, ↓ IR, physeal widening — diagnosis?"** → **Little League shoulder** (TO)

**"12-yo pitcher, lateral tenderness, AP vs contralateral — diagnosis?"** → **Little Leaguer's shoulder**\
✗ Wrong options: SLAP · internal/external impingement · septic

**"View that helps in subtle cases?"** → **AP shoulder in external rotation**\
✗ Wrong options: West Point · Zanca · distal humeral axial · Swimmer's

**"12-yo throwing 100 pitches, anterolateral pain — imaging?"** → **widened lateral proximal humeral physis**\
✗ Wrong options: SH II · acromion stress fracture

**"Physeal zone affected?"** → **hypertrophic zone**\
✗ Wrong options: proliferative

**"Throwing phase causing LLS?"** → **late cocking**\
✗ Wrong options: early cocking

**"14-yo pitcher, ↓ velocity, normal X-ray, MRI metaphyseal oedema?"** → **proximal humeral epiphysiolysis**\
✗ Wrong options: partial articular cuff tear · SLAP

**"13-yo, MRI-confirmed physeal stress injury — treatment?"** → **brief immobilisation + no pitching for remainder of season**\
✗ Wrong options: until skeletal maturity · screw fixation

**"15-yo pitcher, 3 months pain — next step?"** → **stop throwing 6–8 wk → progressive throwing**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI arthrogram · CT · biopsy

**"12-yo on two select teams, physeal widening — management?"** → **rest from throwing**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid · biopsy · labral repair

**"Next best step?"** → **stop throwing until asymptomatic + PT**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · CRPP · continue activity

**"Lateral shoulder pain worse in late cocking — plan?"** → **no throwing 2–3 months + PT + progressive return**\
✗ Wrong options: just ↓ pitch count · sling + NWB

**"Team record for complete games — advice and outcome?"** → **stop pitching, gradual return; likely back to pre-injury level**

**"True statement about LLS?"** → **IR deficit ↑ recurrence**\
✗ Wrong options: proliferative zone · early cocking · % curveballs most important

**"Rested 1 month, now 68 pitches, 1 game/wk, coach, no breaking balls — recurrence risk?"** → **length of activity cessation**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Luxatio Erecta (Inferior Glenohumeral Joint Dislocation)

*Shoulder & Elbow · 25 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **very rare: ~0.5%** of all shoulder dislocations (anterior + posterior far more common)

**Definition** — **inferior** GH dislocation; head trapped **under coracoid + glenoid**

### Anatomy – static glenohumeral ligaments

| Ligament | Restraint |
|---|---|
| **SGHL** | **Inferior** translation at **0° abduction** (neutral rotation) |
| **MGHL** | Anterior + posterior translation in **mid-abduction (~45°)** + ER |
| **Anterior band IGHL** | **Anterior/inferior** translation at **90° abduction + max ER** (late cocking) |
| **Posterior band IGHL** | **Posterior** subluxation at **90° flexion + IR** |
| Superior band IGHL | "Most important static stabiliser" (Orthobullets) |

## 2 Mechanism

**Energy** — typically **high-energy**

**Mechanism** — **hyperabduction** → proximal humerus levered on **acromion** → tears inferior capsule/labrum → head disengages **inferiorly**

**Soft tissue** — variable tearing of static GH ligaments

## 3 Presentation

### Clinical presentation – arm locked in abduction

**Hallmark** — arm **fixed in abduction, overhead** (e.g. **140°**); **can't lower arm** → **inferior** dislocation\
✗ Wrong options: anterior · posterior · superior · lateral

**Clinical diagnosis** — possible on exam alone (unique posture); X-rays still for concomitant injury

![Luxatio erecta — arm locked overhead](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3132-luxatioclinical.jpg)

| Associated injury | Rate / note |
|---|---|
| **Neurological** | **Up to 60%** — highest NV injury of all shoulder dislocations |
| **Vascular** | **Up to 39%** (axillary artery) |
| Proximal humerus fracture | Esp. **greater tuberosity** |
| **Rotator cuff tear** | **Extremely common** (older + young) |
| Anterior capsule / labral tear | Common |

**NV exam** — **pre- AND post-reduction**: radial + brachial pulse, **axillary nerve** + distal neuro exam

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — complete shoulder series → **inferior dislocation, arm fully abducted**

![Inferior glenohumeral dislocation](../../MCQ/attachments/q3065-illa-luxatioerecta-xr.jpg)

**MRI** — **after relocation** (soft-tissue injury common) → capsulolabral pathology, **cuff tears extremely common** · **prompt in young** to avoid missed cuff tear

## 5 Management

```
Luxatio erecta
├─ All → closed reduction + NV check before and after
│      (traction–countertraction or two-step technique)
├─ Inactive elderly, no acute cuff tear → sling briefly → ROM + cuff/periscapular PT
└─ Active young → arthroscopic or open repair
       └─ acute cuff tear → prompt repair
```

### Closed reduction techniques

**Traction–countertraction** — as for anterior dislocation

**Two-step technique** — stand at head · **push humerus laterally + pull medial epicondyle superiorly** → head rotates **inferior → anterior** around glenoid (arm moves from abduction to **adduction against chest**) → then any anterior-dislocation reduction

**Post-reduction** — brief immobiliser → ROM (if cuff intact) → periscapular + cuff strengthening

### Operative criteria

| Option | Indication |
|---|---|
| Closed reduction + immobilisation | **Inactive elderly**; no acute traumatic cuff tear |
| **Arthroscopic / open repair** (repair vs reconstruction) | **Active younger** patients |
| **Prompt cuff repair** | **Acute traumatic cuff tear** (delay → retraction → irreparable) |

**Arthroscopy advantage** — addresses **capsulolabral damage + traumatic cuff tear** together

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Key fact |
|---|---|
| **Axillary nerve palsy** | **Most common nerve palsy**; may resolve with reduction · persists → **EMG at 6–12 wk** (prognosis) |
| **Brachial plexopathy** | High energy + head displacement; usually resolves after reduction + observation |
| **Axillary artery thrombosis** | **May occur late** |
| **Rotator cuff tear** | Common in old + young → prompt MRI in young |

## X vs Y

| | **Luxatio erecta (inferior)** | **Anterior / posterior dislocation** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | **0.5%** | **Much more common** |
| Arm fixed in abduction/flexion | **Yes** (overhead) | **No** |
| NV injury | **Highest** (neuro ≤60%, vascular ≤39%) | Lower |
| Reduction | Two-step → convert to anterior | Standard techniques |

## How it's asked

**"Shoulder locked at 140° abduction, can't lower arm — direction?"** → **inferior** (luxatio erecta)

**"Dislocation with highest neurovascular injury rate?"** → **luxatio erecta**

**"Most common nerve palsy?"** → **axillary nerve**

**"Persistent axillary palsy — when EMG?"** → **6–12 weeks**

**"Young patient after reduction — next?"** → **MRI** (rotator cuff tear common)

**"Late vascular complication?"** → **axillary artery thrombosis**

**"Two-step reduction converts inferior to?"** → **anterior** dislocation

# Medial Epicondylitis (Golfer's Elbow)

*Shoulder & Elbow · 26 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — **5–10× less common than lateral epicondylitis**

**Demographics** — **♂ = ♀** · **dominant arm 75%**

**Age** — 30s–60s, peak **30s–40s** (Orthobullets) · **4th–6th decades** / 40–60 y, peak working years (OB) — read the options

**Sports** — repetitive **wrist flexion + forearm pronation** at ball release: **golf**, pitching, javelin, **bowling**, weightlifting, racquet sports

**Tennis risks** — late ball strike (racquet head behind elbow) · poor forehand mechanics · no vibration dampeners

**Occupation** — lifting **>20 kg**, forceful grip, vibration (plumbers, carpenters, construction)

### Anatomy – common flexor tendon (CFT)

**CFT** — **3 cm** long · anterior medial epicondyle + anterior bundle MCL · fibres parallel to MCL · ulnar head PT confluent with anteromedial capsule

| Flexor-pronator mass | Nerve |
|---|---|
| **Pronator teres** | Median |
| **FCR** | Median |
| FDS | Median |
| Palmaris longus | Median |
| **FCU** | **Ulnar** |

![Flexor-pronator origin at the medial epicondyle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3083-common-flexors.jpg)

**Muscles affected** — classically **PT > FCR** · newer studies: **all CFT muscles except palmaris longus** · 5 tendons form one common tendon\
✗ Wrong options: number involved matters

**Valgus role** — flexor-pronators offload the **anterior band of MUCL** (primary static valgus restraint; deep to PT + FCR) · dynamic valgus support in **early acceleration** + wrist flexion at release

## 2 Mechanism

**Pathology** — repetitive **eccentric overload** → microtrauma at flexor-pronator origin

**Stages** — peritendinous inflammation → **angiofibroblastic hyperplasia** (as in lateral epicondylitis) → breakdown / fibrosis / calcification · **inflammation uncommon**

### Associated conditions

**Ulnar neuropathy** — local inflammation involves ulnar nerve

**UCL insufficiency** — rule out, esp. throwers

**Occupational cases (84%)** — CTS · lateral epicondylitis · cuff tendinitis

## 3 Presentation

**History** — insidious medial pain · worse with wrist/forearm motion + **gripping** · **late cocking / early acceleration** · ± ulnar digit paraesthesia · ↓ grip strength · rarely acute blow → CFT avulsion

**Tenderness** — **5–10 mm distal + anterior to medial epicondyle** (PT/FCR origin)

**Chronic** — flexion contracture

### Diagnosis – provocative testing

| Test | Condition |
|---|---|
| **Resisted forearm pronation + wrist flexion (fist clenched)** | **Medial epicondylitis** |
| Resisted supination + wrist extension | Lateral epicondylitis |
| **Moving (dynamic) valgus stress** | MCL injury |
| **Milking manoeuvre** | MCL origin apprehension/instability/pain |
| Pinch grip | AIN palsy |
| Elbow flexion test (max flexion, pronation, wrist extension 30–60 s), Tinel, 2PD | Ulnar neuritis |

**UCL vs flexor-pronator pain** — UCL pain **posterior + distal** to medial epicondyle

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — usually normal · **25% calcification** of CFT or UCL · posteromedial osteophytes · stress views for valgus instability

**US** — **>90%** sensitivity/specificity/PPV/NPV · dynamic · hypoechoic/anechoic degeneration

**MRI** — standard of care · **↑ T2 signal** in CFT (tendinosis/disruption) + peritendinous oedema · use for UCL/osteochondral injury, loose bodies, flexor-pronator rupture, unclear source

![MRI: T2 hyperintensity at the CFT origin](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3083-medial-epicondylitis.jpg)

**EMG/NCS** — only if ulnar nerve symptoms (not routine)

**Differential** — MCL injury · cubital tunnel · fracture · cervical radiculopathy · triceps tendinitis · **herpes zoster**

## 5 Management

```
Medial epicondylitis
├─ First line → rest, ice, NSAIDs, activity modification, PT, counterforce brace
│      └─ stop throwing 6–12 wk
├─ Refractory to above → peritendinous CFT corticosteroid injection
└─ Fails prolonged nonop, compliant, clear diagnosis → open debridement ± reattachment
       └─ ulnar symptoms → + cubital tunnel release / transposition
```

### Nonoperative treatment – injection

**Prolonged nonop** — appropriate: surgery **less predictable than lateral** epicondylitis

**Modalities** — counterforce brace / kinesiology tape · therapeutic US beneficial · **ESWT**: no definitive recommendation, equivocal (Orthobullets, OB) · ESWT "~90% pain relief" (one OB explanation) — read the options

**Next step after 2 mo brace + NSAIDs + PT + rest** — **corticosteroid into CFT peritendinous area** (short-term benefit)\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopic debridement · cubital tunnel injection · EMG without ulnar symptoms · acupuncture, not shown to help

**Steroid complications** — skin **depigmentation** (dark skin) · subcutaneous **atrophy** · tendon weakening · **ulnar nerve injury** · avoid multiple injections

### Operative criteria

| Criterion | Value |
|---|---|
| Failed nonop in **compliant** patient | **Up to 6 mo** (Orthobullets) · **4–6 mo** · **at least 6 mo** (OB) |
| Symptoms | Severe, affecting quality of life |
| Diagnosis | Clear |
| Exception | **Elite athlete with definite tendon tear** → earlier |

### Surgical outcomes & ulnar nerve involvement

**Procedure** — **open** **medial approach** · **PT–FCR interval** · excise pathologic tissue + side-to-side repair · ± epicondyle microfracture · **reattach flexor-pronator** if proximal origin involved · ± cubital tunnel release/transposition\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopic

**Reattachment** — debridement **with or without** reattachment → equivalent outcomes (Arevalo; complications 4.3%, RTS/RTW 81–100%)

**Outcome** — **pain and function both improve** · good/excellent **80%** (Orthobullets, < lateral) · **80–90%** (OB) · **87%** at 7 y (Gabel & Morrey)

**Worst prognostic factor** — **degree of ulnar neuropathy** (only parameter correlating with outcome; mild/none 24/25 good · moderate–severe 3/5 fair/poor)\
✗ Wrong options: prolonged nonop · no reattachment · number of tendons · ESWT

**Key exam finding** — **pronation weakness at 90°**

**Rehab** — sling **1–2 wk** (avoid wrist volar flexion) · ROM after 2 wk · strengthening **6–8 wk** · **RTS 3–6 mo**

## 6 Complications

**MABC neuropathy** — avulsion/transection → if seen intra-op, **transpose into brachialis**

**Others** — ulnar nerve injury · infection

**Prognosis** — harder to treat + less studied than lateral epicondylitis

## X vs Y

| | **Medial epicondylitis** | **Lateral epicondylitis** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | 5–10× less common | More common |
| Origin | Flexor-pronator (**PT, FCR**) | — |
| Provocation | **Resisted pronation + wrist flexion** | Resisted supination + wrist extension |
| Surgical success | **~80%**, less predictable than lateral | — |
| Associated nerve | **Ulnar** | — |

| | **Medial epicondylitis** | **UCL injury** |
|---|---|---|
| Tenderness | 5–10 mm distal + anterior to epicondyle | **Posterior + distal** to epicondyle |
| Test | Resisted pronation + flexion | **Milking**, moving valgus stress |

## How it's asked

**"Provocative test positive in medial epicondylitis?"** → **resisted forearm pronation + wrist flexion with clenched fist**\
✗ Wrong options: supination/extension = lateral · dynamic valgus/milking = MCL · pinch grip = AIN

**"Masters tennis player, 2 mo PT, brace, NSAIDs, rest fail — next?"** → **corticosteroid injection into CFT peritendinous area**\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopy · cubital tunnel injection · acupuncture · EMG

**"Bowler-carpenter, 7 mo failed nonop, open debridement + reattachment — 1-y outcome?"** → **pain and functional outcome scores both expected to improve**

**"Surgical golfer's elbow — factor linked to inferior outcome?"** → **degree of ulnar nerve involvement**\
✗ Wrong options: prolonged nonop · no reattachment · number of tendons · ESWT

**"Nerve injured at medial elbow surgery — intra-op fix?"** → **MABC, bury in brachialis**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Medial Ulnar Collateral Ligament Injury

*Shoulder & Elbow · 27 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — attenuation / rupture of elbow UCL → **valgus instability** in **overhead throwers**
