**Who** — originally **javelin** throwers → now mostly **baseball pitchers**; ↑ in high school + amateur pitchers; also water polo, wrestlers

**Skeletal maturity** — **uncommon in skeletally immature** → they get **little leaguer's elbow** (medial epicondyle) · isolated MUCL tear = **young skeletally mature** pitcher

**Risk factors** — **exceeding pitch count / innings limits** · short rest, no off-season · **higher pitch velocity** · poor mechanics · **kinetic chain deficits** (shoulder + core weakness, ↓ shoulder motion)

### Anatomy & biomechanics (anterior bundle, isometry, posterior bundle)

**Carrying angle** — **6–11°** valgus (UCL note) · ♂ 5–10° / ♀ 10–15° (anatomy note) · ~11° ♂ / ~13° ♀ (exam note) — read the options

**Stability** — split evenly **osseous vs soft tissue**; UCL = primary valgus restraint **30–120° flexion** (+ flexor-pronator, capsule)

| UCL component | Facts |
|---|---|
| **Anterior oblique ligament (AOL) = anterior bundle** | **Strongest, most important valgus stabiliser**; origin **anteroinferior medial epicondyle** → **sublime tubercle**, **2.8 mm distal** to ulnar articular margin; mean length **54 mm**; primary **30–120°**; greatest resistance at **~70°** |
| **Posterior oblique ligament (POL) = posterior bundle** | **Greatest change in tension** flexion → extension; **tight in flexion**; fan-like **capsular thickening**; inserts **sigmoid notch**; not subdivided into bands; secondary stabiliser at 30° |
| **Transverse ligament** | **No stability** — originates + inserts on **ulna** (same bone) |

![UCL complex – anterior oblique, posterior oblique, transverse ligament](../../MCQ/attachments/q628-illa-safran-elbow.jpg)

**Terminology trap** — AOL = "anterior bundle" = older "anterior band"

**AOL bands** — **2 bands**: anterior + posterior (Orthobullets, most OB) · **3 bands**: anterior, central, posterior (2 OB explanations) · anterior, posterior, **oblique** (1 OB) — read the options

| AOL band | Strain / tautness | Primary role |
|---|---|---|
| **Anterior band** | **Isometric** through ROM (✗ Wrong options: isokinetic); **taut 0–85°**; tightens at end-range **extension**; most vulnerable **in extension** | **Most important valgus restraint**; primary at **30°, 60°, 90°** |
| **Posterior band** | **↑ strain in flexion** (✗ Wrong options: isometric); **taut beyond 55°**; vulnerable **in flexion** | Primary at **120°** |

**Posterior bundle range** — resists from **60° to full flexion** (one OB) · beyond **90°** (others)

**Axis of rotation / isometric point** — at UCL **origin = anteroinferior medial epicondyle**\
✗ Wrong options: anterosuperior · posteroinferior · sublime tubercle = insertion

| Flexion | Main valgus restraint |
|---|---|
| **0–20°** (extension) | **Bony: olecranon in fossa + radiocapitellar articulation** |
| **~25°** | Olecranon **unlocked** → UCL takes over |
| **30–120°** | **AOL (anterior band)** |
| >90° / max flexion | **POL** |

**Radial head** — important **secondary** stabiliser in extension + flexion

**Dynamic stabilisers** — **flexor-pronator mass** (**FCU, FDS**) — dynamic, not static

## 2 Mechanism

**Acute trauma** — often with **elbow dislocation**

**Overuse** — **repetitive valgus microtrauma** → rupture of **anterior band** of AOL

**Iatrogenic** — **excessive olecranon osteophyte resection** puts MCL at risk

**Associated** — **traction ulnar neuritis** · **posteromedial olecranon impingement (VEO)** · elbow arthritis

### Throwing phase of maximal valgus stress (late cocking/early acceleration)

**Max valgus** — **late cocking → early acceleration**: forearm lags behind arm → valgus while elbow depends on **anterior band**; forces near **tensile limit** of UCL

**Single best phase** — **late cocking** (Orthobullets, several OB) · **early acceleration** (other OB: "highest in early acceleration") — choose **late cocking/early acceleration** if offered; else whichever of the two is listed

**Forces** — valgus up to **64 N·m** · **500 N** compression at radiocapitellar joint · elbow **110° → 20°** · **3000°/s**

| Phase | Key activity / pathology |
|---|---|
| **Wind-up** | Benign for elbow |
| **Early cocking** | Peak **deltoid**; cuff active |
| **Late cocking** | **Max shoulder ER ("layback")**, high torque → **max elbow valgus**; FDS + FCU protect |
| **Early acceleration** | Peak **triceps**; valgus + rapid extension |
| **Late acceleration** | Peak **pec major, latissimus, serratus anterior** |
| Ball release | Not a phase; end of acceleration |
| **Deceleration** | **Eccentric** contraction of all muscles, **highest torque**, "most harmful" → **SLAP, biceps, brachialis, teres minor** (✗ Wrong options: UCL) |
| **Follow-through** | Rebalance; elbow flexors prevent hyperextension; minimal UCL stress |

![Throwing phases – A wind-up, B early cocking, C late cocking, D late acceleration, E deceleration](../../MCQ/attachments/q217958-b-throwing-phases-copy.jpg)

**Figure labels vary** — one figure: **C = late cocking** · another: **B = late cocking** (A early cocking, D deceleration) — read the figure

**Valgus ↓ by** — **flexor-pronator contraction** · **forearm pronation** · **scapular protraction/retraction (trunk-scapular kinesis)** · **reduced velocity** · correct mechanics

**Valgus ↑ by** — **↑ glenohumeral IR torque** · poor mechanics

## 3 Presentation

**History** — acute **"pop"** + pain + can't throw · chronic: **loss of velocity ("zip")**, **loss of control/accuracy**

**Pain** — medial/posteromedial in **late cocking + acceleration** · posteromedial in **deceleration** = **valgus extension overload**

**Ulnar nerve** — paraesthesia ring + small (nerve **stretch** in late cocking) → check subluxation

**Exam** — tenderness at/near **MCL origin** (posteromedial = VEO) · flexor-pronator integrity · **palmaris longus present?** (graft) · throwers may **lack full extension** · examine shoulder + kinetic chain

**Throwing tolerance** — pain-free light throwing / several innings, no trauma → partial injury; complete rupture unlikely

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnosis – moving valgus stress, milking manoeuvre & MRI T-sign

| Test | Technique | Positive / accuracy |
|---|---|---|
| **Valgus stress test** | Elbow **20–30°** flexion (unlocks olecranon) + **humerus ER** + valgus | **50% sensitive** |
| **Milking manoeuvre** | **Pull thumb**, forearm **supinated**, elbow **90°**; supine/seated/standing | **Apprehension, instability or pain at MCL origin** |
| **Moving valgus stress test** | Milking position; valgus while ranging full arc (or valgus at full flexion → **quickly extend**) | Pain/apprehension **70–120°**; **100% sensitive, 75% specific**; **most strongly confirms** |

![Milking manoeuvre – valgus via thumb with forearm supinated](../../MCQ/attachments/q216806-a-milking.jpg)

**Pain in mid-arc (70–120°)** — **anterior bundle MCL** · **pain at terminal extension** = **posterior compartment / olecranon osteophytes**

| ✗ Exam distractor | Indicates |
|---|---|
| Pain with **resisted wrist flexion** (± pronation) | **Medial epicondylitis / flexor-pronator strain** |
| **Froment's sign** | Ulnar neuropathy |
| **Hawkins** | Shoulder impingement |
| **Lateral pivot shift**, **chair test**, **push-up sign**, PLRI test | **PLRI (LUCL)** |
| Painful **snapping** medially | **Ulnar nerve or triceps subluxation** |

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Fact |
|---|---|
| **X-ray AP + lateral** | Often **normal**; loose bodies, **UCL calcification**, **posteromedial osteophyte** (VEO) |
| Oblique views | Olecranon |
| **Stress X-rays (gravity/manual), both elbows** | **Medial opening >3 mm = diagnostic** |
| **MRI** | **Gold standard / mainstay**; thickened ligament (chronic), calcification, midsubstance tear, proximal/distal avulsion, partial **undersurface** tear |
| Plain MRI | **57% sensitive, 100% specific** |
| **MR arthrogram** | **Diagnostic**, more accurate: **92% sensitive, 100% specific** (✗ Wrong options: "lacks specificity") |
| **"Capsular T-sign"** | **Contrast extravasates** around distal (undersurface) tear at sublime tubercle |
| Dynamic US | Laxity under valgus; operator dependent |

![MR arthrogram – capsular T-sign at sublime tubercle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3079-39b-moved.jpg)

**MRI labels (water polo Q)** — 1 = **flexor-pronator origin** · 2 = **UCL** · 3 = **RCL**

### Differential diagnosis

| Diagnosis | Discriminator |
|---|---|
| **MUCL injury** | **Moving valgus +**, milking +, loss of velocity |
| **Medial epicondylitis / flexor-pronator strain** | Pain on **resisted wrist flexion/pronation** |
| **Ulnar neuropathy (isolated)** | Doesn't explain valgus instability |
| **Valgus extension overload** | **Pain at terminal extension**; posteromedial osteophyte |
| **Little leaguer's elbow** (medial epicondyle stress fracture) | **Skeletally immature** |

## 5 Management

```
MUCL injury
├─ Most patients / partial tear / non-thrower → rest 6 wk + PT + throwing program
│      └─ fails & high-level thrower → reconstruction
├─ High-level thrower, wants to continue (pro: often immediate) → reconstruction (docking, autograft)
└─ Young athlete, avulsion tear, no intraligament calcification → repair ± suture augmentation
```

### Non-thrower partial injury – nonoperative care

**First line** — most patients + **partial tears**: **rest / activity avoidance 6 wk** + NSAIDs → **flexor-pronator strengthening** (secondary dynamic stabilisers) + mechanics, peri-scapular + core → **progressive throwing program**

**Outcome** — **42%** return to pre-injury sport at avg **24 wk** · most heal without surgery in non-high-level athletes

**Adolescent acute partial (14-y basketball)** — **activity avoidance 6 wk** \
✗ Wrong options: stretching (full ROM) · arthroscopy (unless loose body) · static ex-fix (brace OK) · rheumatology work-up

**Persistent at 6–12 wk** — advanced imaging (MRI, MR arthrogram, US, stress X-rays)

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **UCL reconstruction (Tommy John)** | **High-level throwers wanting to continue** · **failed nonop partial tears** willing to rehab · **midsubstance** tears · attritional tears with **calcification** (excision leaves void) |
| **UCL repair** | **Young athletes, avulsion-type tears** (proximal/distal), no intraligament calcification; ± high-strength suture augmentation |
| ✗ Wrong option: repair | **Midsubstance tear** (reconstruction superior) |

**Repair history** — early repairs poor → replaced by reconstruction; newer augmented repair promising

### Reconstruction technique (docking vs Jobe) & return to play

**Goal** — anatomic reconstruction of **anterior bundle** (medial epicondyle → sublime tubercle); **none stronger than native**

**Approach** — **flexor-pronator muscle-splitting** (✓ ↓ morbidity)\
✗ Wrong options: **detachment** (original Jobe; flexor-pronator avulsion risk) · **medial epicondylectomy** in throwers

**Graft** — **ipsilateral palmaris longus autograft** most common/standard · gracilis autograft or allograft · PL vs gracilis both successful (PL possibly ↑ high-level return; gracilis does not ↓ laxity) · revision **autograft 1.6% vs allograft 2.6%**

| Technique | Humerus | Ulna | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Original Jobe (1986)** | Tunnels | Tunnel | **Flexor-pronator tenotomy**, **figure-of-8**, **routine submuscular ulnar nerve transposition** → **abandoned** |
| **Modified Jobe** | **2 connected tunnels, "Y"** | Single tunnel (2 angled holes, sublime tubercle) | **Muscle-splitting**, no routine transposition; graft sutured **figure-of-8** |
| **Docking** | **Single socket**; sutures tied over bony bridge | **Two converging tunnels** | **Best**: **↑ return to sport, ↓ complications**, less nerve handling, **better graft tensioning** |
| **Hybrid interference screw (DANE TJ)** | Docking socket | **Single longitudinal socket + interference screw** | ↓ iatrogenic fracture; docking + ulnar screw = **95% native strength** |
| **Cortical button (Endobutton)** | — | Suspensory | Mostly **revision** |

![Docking technique](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3079-docking-technique.jpg)

**Docking vs Jobe** — **~15% more excellent results** · **3× fewer complications** · **3–5% less ulnar neuropathy** · RTP **>90%** vs **80–85%** (figure-of-8)

**Biomechanics** — docking + cortical button **stronger** than figure-of-8 + interference screw

**Calcified attritional UCL in high-level thrower** — **reconstruction (modified Jobe)**\
✗ Wrong options: original Jobe · repair ± augmentation · arthroscopic debridement + repair

| Return to play | Rate |
|---|---|
| Modern reconstruction | **~90%** (Orthobullets) · **85–95%** (OB) — answer **80–95%** |
| All athletes (systematic review) | **86%** |
| **Collegiate** | **95%** |
| Surgery overall good results | 84–94% |
| **Revision** reconstruction | **Worse** |

**Rehab timeline** — early wrist/elbow/shoulder ROM + core/hip · strengthening **4–6 wk** · **no valgus stress until 4 mo** · throwing program **4 mo** · **competition 9–12 mo**

### Ulnar nerve management & postoperative neuritis

| Pre-op ulnar status | At UCL reconstruction |
|---|---|
| **No symptoms** | **No routine decompression/transposition** (in situ release at most) |
| **Symptoms** (cubital tunnel) | Decompression ± transposition |
| **Subluxation** | **Transposition** |

**Routine transposition** — avoid → **↑ post-op ulnar neuropathy**, worse outcomes

**Midsubstance tear + cubital tunnel symptoms + subluxation** — **autograft reconstruction + ulnar nerve transposition**\
✗ Wrong options: repair · reconstruction without nerve

**Ulnar nerve in forearm** — **between FCU and FDP**\
✗ Wrong options: medial to brachial artery / most volar in carpal tunnel = median

### Postoperative rehabilitation – forearm position

| Repair | Elbow extension exercises with forearm in |
|---|---|
| **MUCL reconstruction** | **Supination** |
| **LUCL repair** (± radial head replacement) | **Pronation** |
| Combined medial + lateral | **Neutral** |

## 6 Complications

**Ulnar neurapraxia** — **most common**; **3–26%** (Orthobullets, OB) · "<10%" (one OB) — read the options; **treat by observation** (most resolve weeks–months)

**↓ Neurapraxia by** — muscle-splitting · **more superior exiting proximal humeral tunnels** · selective transposition · docking

**MABC injury** — crosses **distal** incision

**Fracture** — ulna or medial epicondyle; **small bone bridge** → ORIF / larger fixation

**Stiffness** — **HO** → early therapy; HO excision ~**6 mo**

**Others** — failure to regain pre-injury level (esp. **revision**) · infection · flexor-pronator avulsion (detaching approach)

**Not MUCL complications** — PLRI / radial head + coronoid tip fractures (terrible triad, LCL)

## X vs Y

| | **Anterior band (AOL)** | **Posterior band (AOL)** | **POL (posterior bundle)** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Strain | **Isometric** | ↑ in flexion | **Greatest change** |
| Taut | **0–85°** | **>55°** | **>90°** / max flexion |
| Role | **Primary valgus restraint** | Primary at 120° | Secondary; max flexion; cubital tunnel floor |

| | **Original Jobe** | **Modified Jobe** | **Docking** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Flexor-pronator | **Detached (tenotomy)** | **Split** | **Split** |
| Ulnar nerve | **Routine submuscular transposition** | Not routine | Minimal handling |
| Humeral fixation | Figure-of-8 tunnels | **Y-tunnels, figure-of-8** | **Single socket, tied over bridge** |
| Outcome | Abandoned | Good | **Best RTP, fewest complications** |

## How it's asked

**"Primary valgus stabiliser in mid-flexion?"** → **AOL of MUCL**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnohumeral · radiocapitellar · POL · flexor-pronator

**"All protect elbow from valgus EXCEPT?"** → **↑ glenohumeral IR torque**\
✗ Wrong options: flexor-pronator contraction · pronation · scapular motion · ↓ velocity

**"Native MCL kinematics?"** → **posterior bundle = greatest tension change**\
✗ Wrong options: posterior bundle isometric

**"Most static valgus stability?"** → **anterior band of UCL**\
✗ Wrong options: flexor/pronator = dynamic

**"Most important static valgus stabiliser in thrower?"** → **anterior band of anterior bundle**

**"Structure B (posterior bundle) — true?"** → **most change in tension flexion → extension**\
✗ Wrong options: subdivided into bands

**"Javelin thrower — ligament, arc, insertion?"** → **AOL, 30–120°, sublime tubercle**\
✗ Wrong options: POL / sigmoid notch

**"Isometric component?"** → **anterior band of anterior bundle**\
✗ Wrong options: isokinetic

**"UCL anatomy — true (figure)?"** → **AOL greatest resistance at 70°**\
✗ Wrong options: both bands isometric · POL tightest in extension

**"Axis of rotation for repair?"** → **anteroinferior medial epicondyle**\
✗ Wrong options: sublime tubercle

**"Greatest stress on MUCL — phase?"** → **late cocking**\
✗ Wrong options: wind-up · early cocking · ball release · follow-through

**"Pitcher ruptures MUCL — phase?"** → **early acceleration** (when late cocking not offered)

**"Forces approaching tensile limit?"** → **late cocking / early acceleration**

**"Water polo, lost velocity — phase + MRI label?"** → **late cocking (C) + UCL (2)**

**"Milking-positive pitcher — phase reproducing symptoms?"** → **late cocking (figure B)**\
✗ Wrong options: deceleration = labrum/biceps

**"Highest valgus load phase (pro pitcher pre-reconstruction)?"** → **early acceleration**

**"Medial pain, positive milking, lost velocity — phase?"** → **late cocking** (figure C)

**"Valgus via thumb at 90° + moving valgus pain — phase?"** → **late cocking**

**"Pitcher lost 10 mph, normal EMG — exam that best confirms?"** → **moving valgus stress test**\
✗ Wrong options: resisted wrist flexion · Froment · Hawkins · pivot shift

**"MR arthrogram — diagnosis?"** → **MCL rupture**

**"Medial pain after 3 innings, milking test reproduces?"** → **MCL injury**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnar nerve / triceps subluxation = snapping

**"Pitcher, pain in acceleration, lost velocity — structure?"** → **anterior bundle UCL**

**"Valgus through passive ROM painful 70–120°?"** → **anterior bundle MCL**\
✗ Wrong options: olecranon osteophytes = terminal extension

**"Positive moving valgus, lateral X-ray — labelled structure?"** → **anterior oblique band (C)**

**"ER humerus + supinate + valgus, apprehension 70–120°?"** → **MUCL**\
✗ Wrong options: LUCL · annular · anterior capsule · triceps

**"Skeletally mature 17-y pitcher, overused, ulnar paraesthesia, valgus pain?"** → **attenuation of anterior band of AOL**\
✗ Wrong options: medial epicondyle stress fracture · isolated ulnar neuropathy · flexor-pronator

**"Capsular T-sign — true?"** → **evaluate by pulling thumb, forearm supinated, elbow flexed**\
✗ Wrong options: MRA lacks specificity · all isometric · 0–20° · deceleration

**"Failed rest + interval program, UCL tear on MRI?"** → **open reconstruction with ipsilateral palmaris longus**\
✗ Wrong options: ECRB release · OATS · flexor-pronator reattachment · osteophyte excision

**"Lowest complication + best outcome technique?"** → **muscle-splitting + docking**\
✗ Wrong options: routine transposition · detachment

**"Best return to sport, minor leaguer?"** → **docking + ulnar nerve in situ release**\
✗ Wrong options: classic Jobe · submuscular transposition · epicondylectomy

**"Calcified UCL, failed rest — surgery?"** → **modified Jobe reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: original Jobe · repair

**"Jobe vs docking?"** → **docking: higher RTP, fewer complications**\
✗ Wrong options: 20% neuropathy regardless

**"Pro pitcher, no ulnar symptoms — procedure?"** → **autograft docking reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: allograft · figure-of-8 · nerve decompression

**"Return to preinjury throwing after docking?"** → **80–95%**

**"Midsubstance tear + cubital tunnel symptoms + subluxation?"** → **autograft reconstruction + ulnar nerve transposition**

**"Most common complication — nerve lies where?"** → **between FCU and FDP** (ulnar)

**"Surgical management true (no ulnar symptoms)?"** → **routine ulnar transposition not indicated**\
✗ Wrong options: repair midsubstance · detachment equivalent · docking worse

**"Most likely post-op complication — management?"** → **observation** (ulnar neurapraxia)

**"14-y basketball, acute partial UCL, full ROM?"** → **activity avoidance 6 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: static ex-fix · arthroscopy

**"Extension exercises after MUCL vs LUCL?"** → **supination for MUCL · pronation for LUCL**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Multidirectional Shoulder Instability (MDI)

*Shoulder & Elbow · 28 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & epidemiology

**MDI** — symptomatic instability in **≥2 planes** (anterior, posterior, inferior) from **capsular redundancy**; **one plane must be inferior** (TO, OB)

**Age** — peaks **2nd–3rd decades**

**Who** — overhead athletes: **swimmers, volleyball, gymnasts, throwers**, dancers

**Generalised laxity** — in **45–75%** of MDI patients undergoing surgery

### Torn loose / worn loose / born loose (TO)

| Type (TO) | Mechanism | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **Torn loose** | Trauma | Traumatic dislocation (TUBS) |
| **Worn loose** | **Microtrauma / overuse** | Overhead athletes → **MDI** |
| **Born loose** | **Lax ligaments / capsule** | **Collagen disorders: Ehlers-Danlos, Marfan** → **MDI** |

### TUBS vs AMBRI (Matsen) (TO)

| **TUBS** | **AMBRI** |
|---|---|
| **T**raumatic dislocation → recurrent instability | **A**traumatic (minimal trauma) |
| **U**nidirectional | **M**ultidirectional |
| **B**ankart lesion most common | **B**ilateral (frequently) |
| **S**urgery mainstay | **R**ehabilitation first |
| — | **I**nferior capsular shift for nonresponders |

### Pathoanatomy

**Cause (TO)** — **inferior capsular redundancy**\
✗ Wrong options: Bankart · Hill-Sachs · cuff tear

**Hallmarks** — **patulous inferior capsule** (IGHL anterior + posterior bands) on MRI · **rotator interval deficiency**

**Add-on lesions (TO)** — from traumatic events: **Bankart** (anteroinferior labrum) · **Kim lesion** (posteroinferior labral avulsion) · glenoid erosion · Hill-Sachs, chondral, SLAP — **less often** than in unidirectional; rarely change treatment

### Glenohumeral stability

| Static restraints | Dynamic restraints |
|---|---|
| Glenohumeral ligaments | **Rotator cuff** — compresses head into glenoid (primary role) |
| Glenoid labrum | Biceps |
| Articular congruity + version | **Periscapular muscles** |
| **Negative intra-articular pressure** (release → head subluxes inferiorly) | |

**MDI = static deficiency** → therapy builds **dynamic** stabilisers to compensate

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **pain > instability** often · weakness · **paraesthesia / transient numbness** · crepitus · instability **during sleep** · atraumatic, self-reduced dislocations, never needed ED

**Impingement signs <20 y** — think **MDI** (TO)

**Voluntary dislocator** — "popping shoulders in and out" → **PT**; **contraindication to surgery**

### Diagnosis – sulcus sign, generalised laxity & capsular redundancy

| Test | MDI finding |
|---|---|
| **Sulcus sign** | **≥2+**; inferior traction at **0° and 45° abduction** (TO) → dimple below acromion; **most specific** for MDI (**2 cm → up to 97% specificity**) |
| **ER with arm adducted** (and at 90° abduction) | **↑ ER** = **incompetent rotator interval** |
| Anterior + posterior **load and shift** | **≥2+** both directions; less specific |
| Apprehension / relocation | Common in **both** traumatic + MDI |
| **Jerk / Kim tests** | Posterior traumatic **and** MDI |
| Neer / Hawkins | Impingement in **<20 y** → MDI |

![Sulcus sign – dimple below acromion with inferior traction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3052-sulcus-sign.jpg)

| Sulcus grade (TO) | Acromiohumeral interval |
|---|---|
| **1+** | **<1 cm** |
| **2+** | **1–2 cm** |
| **3+** | **>2 cm** |

**2+/3+ sulcus + ↑ ER at side (TO)** — **pathologic rotator interval** + patulous inferior capsule

**Sulcus reflects** — **patulous inferior capsule + rotator interval** (TO, Orthobullets) · "incompetent rotator interval" (OB)

### Beighton score (generalised laxity)

| Item (9 points) | Points |
|---|---|
| **Palms to floor**, knees straight | 1 |
| **Genu recurvatum** | 2 |
| **Elbow hyperextension** | 2 |
| **Small-finger MCP hyperextension** (passive >90°) | 2 |
| **Thumb to ipsilateral forearm** | 2 |
| **Not included** | **Excessive forearm supination** |

**Cut-offs** — generalised laxity **>4/9** (Orthobullets) · **>6** associated with **connective tissue disorder** (Marfan, Ehlers-Danlos; OB)

![MCP hyperextension – generalised ligamentous laxity](../../MCQ/attachments/q937-a-mp-hyperextension.jpg)

**✗ Exam distractors** — cross-body adduction (**AC joint**) · O'Brien (**SLAP**) · **axillary webbing** (**pec major rupture**) · Jobe's (**supraspinatus**)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Finding |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** | Trauma series (**AP-IR, AP-ER, true AP, axillary, scapular Y**); **often normal** |
| **MR arthrogram** | Needed to assess **capsular volume**; **patulous inferior capsule** / capsular redundancy (e.g. posterior), **intact labrum** |
| MRI add-ons | Bankart (traumatic anterior) · Kim lesion (traumatic posterior) · glenoid bony erosion (chronic anterior) |
| **Arthroscopy** | **Drive-through sign**: scope passes easily between head + glenoid at **anterior band of IGHL** (also laxity) |

### Diagnostic criteria

| MDI requires | |
|---|---|
| Instability in **≥2 planes** | One **inferior** |
| **Sulcus ≥2+** | ± ↑ ER at side |
| **Load and shift ≥2+** | Anterior + posterior |

### Differential diagnosis

| Diagnosis | Clue |
|---|---|
| Anterior / posterior (traumatic) instability | TUBS, Bankart, **glenoid bone loss**, inverted pear |
| **Long thoracic nerve palsy** | Serratus weakness, **medial winging** |
| **Buford complex** | Normal variant: **absent anterosuperior labrum + cordlike MGHL** to base of biceps anchor |
| **GIRD** | IR deficit **25°** (OB) · **>20°** (TO, Orthobullets) vs contralateral → sleeper stretches |
| **Little leaguer's shoulder** | **Salter-Harris I** proximal humeral physis, excessive pitch counts |
| C-spine disease, brachial plexitis, TOS | Neurologic |

## 5 Management

```
MDI
├─ All patients first → dynamic stabilisation PT (cuff + periscapular)
│      └─ still within PT trial (e.g. 8 wk) → continue PT
├─ Failed extensive PT + pain/instability limiting ADLs/sport → capsular shift / plication + RI closure
├─ Voluntary dislocator → no surgery
└─ Collagen disorder / refractory → allograft capsular reconstruction
```

### Initial treatment – prolonged rotator cuff & periscapular strengthening

**First line** — **all patients**, incl. bilateral, voluntary, collagen disorders: **dynamic stabilisation PT** → **rotator cuff + periscapular** (serratus anterior, rhomboids) strengthening, proprioception, humeral head control, scapulohumeral rhythm + **home exercises**

**Early rehab** — **closed kinetic chain exercises** → safe co-contraction of scapular + cuff muscles

**Duration** — **3–6 months** (Orthobullets, TO, OB) · **≥6 months** (several OB) · **6–12 months** (one OB) — read the options

**✗ Initial distractors** — **immobilisation / sling** (not effective; comfort only) · posterior capsular stretching (= GIRD) · bracing · steroid injections · labral repair · HAGL repair · thermal capsulorrhaphy · open/arthroscopic shift before PT trial

**Outcome** — most patients satisfactory with nonop (pain + recurrence)

### Operative criteria

| Surgery for MDI | |
|---|---|
| **Indications** | **Failure of extensive PT** (sole indication, TO) · pain + instability interfering with ADLs / sport |
| **Contraindication** | **Voluntary dislocators** |
| Procedure of choice | **Inferior capsular shift** (open or arthroscopic) |
| Results | Good, but **less predictable** than traumatic anterior stabilisation |

### Surgery – capsular shift & rotator interval closure

**Inferior capsular shift** — capsule shifted **superiorly** → addresses patulous inferior capsule

**Capsular plication** — loose capsule to labrum, **balanced anterior + posterior** → ↓ capsular volume

**Rotator interval closure** — plicate **SGHL to MGHL** (anterosuperior capsule) → **eliminates sulcus sign** / inferior subluxation · greatest effect = **↓ ER with arm at side (0° abduction)** (~10°) \
✗ Wrong options: restricts ER in cocking · IR at 90° · flexion/cross-body

**Capsule rule** — tight **anterior** capsule → ↓ **ER** · tight **posterior** capsule → ↓ **IR**

**Do not eliminate sulcus** — SLAP repair · Bankart repair (anteroinferior only) · supraspinatus repair · SAD

**Open approach** — deltopectoral; subscapularis tenotomy vs split; also fix any labral pathology

**Allograft capsular reconstruction** — rare; refractory, collagen disorders

**Arthroscopic vs open** — similar outcomes; arthroscopic advantages

| Post-op rehab | |
|---|---|
| **0–4/6 wk** | Sling / immobiliser |
| **6–10 wk** | ADLs, **45° limit** abduction + ER |
| **10–16 wk** | Gradual ROM |
| **>16 wk** | Strengthening |
| **>10 months** | **Contact sports** (only after normal strength + motion) |

### Recurrent instability with glenoid bone loss – bony augmentation

**Clues to bone loss** — multiple recurrences · long history · instability in **mid-range (45–60° abduction + ER)**

| Glenoid bone loss | Management |
|---|---|
| **10%** | Arthroscopic repair still OK |
| **15%** + labral tear, first-time (contact athlete) | **Surgical stabilisation** (✗ Wrong options: prolonged PT) |
| **>20–25%** | **Bony augmentation (Latarjet**: coracoid + conjoint tendon) — arthroscopic/open Bankart fail |

**✗ Bone loss distractors** — arthroscopic Bankart · subscapularis advancement · open capsular shift · hemiarthroplasty (no arthritis)

## 6 Complications

### Thermal capsulorrhaphy – complications & failure

**Status** — **historical / contraindicated**; abandoned for high complication + failure rates

**Failure** — **high failure** for **anterior, posterior and MDI** at 2 + 5 y · recurrence peaks **2–3 wk** post-op (capsule weakest) · good 1-y result does not protect from later recurrence

**Capsule** — revision finds capsule **thin + patulous** · **capsular necrosis / attenuation / insufficiency**\
✗ Wrong options: thick fibrotic

**Shrinkage** — depends on **total thermal energy + rate of delivery** · denatured tissue **does heal**\
✗ Wrong options: fixed for peak temp

**Revision after thermal failure** — **open revision** → capsular attenuation = **contraindication to arthroscopic revision**\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopic

**Axillary nerve injury** — **heat-induced**; can't actively raise arm, full passive ROM, normal X-ray

**Chondrolysis** — heat from **monopolar RF / laser** → progressive pain, ROM never regained, joint space loss

![Glenohumeral chondrolysis after thermal capsulorrhaphy](../../MCQ/attachments/q7888-a-100.jpg)

| ✗ Chondrolysis distractor | Actually causes |
|---|---|
| Supraspinatus tear | Cuff tear arthropathy, superior head migration |
| **Subscapularis dehiscence** | Open deltopectoral → **anterior instability** |
| **Anterior humeral circumflex coagulation** | **Osteonecrosis** (main head supply) |
| Non-compliance with PT | Stiffness, pain (✗ Wrong options: chondrolysis) |

### Surgical complications

**Recurrence** — **most common** after arthroscopic or open capsulorrhaphy

**Subscapularis deficiency** — after **open anteroinferior shift**; **+ lift-off**, excessive ER; late **anterior subluxation** on axillary view

**Loss of motion** — over/asymmetric tightening → ↓ ER; treat **Z-lengthening subscapularis**; rare

**Axillary nerve** — iatrogenic neurapraxia → observe; **abduction + ER moves nerve away** from glenoid

**Capsulorrhaphy arthropathy** — **posterior glenoid wear**, posterior head subluxation, **glenoid retroversion**, **IR contracture**; **Putti-Platt, Magnuson-Stack**

## X vs Y

| | **TUBS (traumatic)** | **AMBRI (MDI)** |
|---|---|---|
| Onset | Trauma | **Atraumatic** |
| Direction | Unidirectional | **≥2 incl. inferior** |
| Lesion | **Bankart**, bone loss | **Capsular redundancy, RI deficiency** |
| Side | Unilateral | **Bilateral** often |
| Treatment | **Surgery** | **Rehab first** → inferior capsular shift |

| | **MDI** | **GIRD / internal impingement** |
|---|---|---|
| Exam | Sulcus, ↑ ER, load-shift both ways, Beighton + | **IR deficit ≥25°** vs other side |
| Treatment | **Cuff + periscapular strengthening** | **Posteroinferior capsular (sleeper) stretching** |

## How it's asked

**"Cause of MDI?"** → **inferior capsular redundancy** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: Bankart · Hill-Sachs · cuff tear

**"Atraumatic recurrent dislocator, hyperextensible MCP — etiology?"** → **capsular redundancy**\
✗ Wrong options: inverted pear · LTN palsy · Bankart · Buford

**"Connective tissue feature EXCEPT?"** → **excessive forearm supination** (not Beighton)

**"Gymnast, self-reduced dislocations, normal MRA — exam finding?"** → **↑ ER in adduction**\
✗ Wrong options: cross-body · O'Brien · axillary webbing · Jobe

**"Most specific exam for MDI?"** → **sulcus sign**\
✗ Wrong options: apprehension · load-shift · jerk · Kim

**"Voluntary popper, 2 cm bilateral sulcus — initial treatment?"** → **cuff + periscapular strengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: posterior stretching · plication · thermal · HAGL repair

**"Swimmer, 2+ load-shift, 1.5 cm sulcus — next?"** → **dynamic stabilisation therapy**\
✗ Wrong options: bracing · labral repair

**"Bilateral MDI volleyball, impingement signs?"** → **physical therapy**\
✗ Wrong options: injections · staged surgery

**"17-y ♀ MDI — initial?"** → **PT + home exercises**\
✗ Wrong options: sling · thermal

**"Swimmer, 2+ translation, elbow hyperextension, thumb to forearm?"** → **PT cuff + scapulothoracic**\
✗ Wrong options: open shift · thermal · sling

**"13-y pitcher, Beighton signs, sulcus, 10° IR deficit?"** → **MDI; periscapular training**\
✗ Wrong options: GIRD sleeper stretches · little leaguer's shoulder

**"Who gets prolonged PT rather than surgery?"** → **17-y swimmer, Beighton 5 + sulcus**\
✗ Wrong options: 15% bone loss dislocator · cuff tears

**"Dancer, grade III sulcus, 8 wk PT done?"** → **continue PT**\
✗ Wrong options: shift · Bankart · Latarjet

**"Rotator interval closure — true?"** → **restricts ER at 0° abduction**

**"Failed nonop MDI — successful surgery includes?"** → **rotator interval closure**\
✗ Wrong options: SLAP repair · capsular advancement into Hill-Sachs · IGHL repair

**"Procedure that eliminates sulcus sign?"** → **rotator interval closure**\
✗ Wrong options: Bankart · SLAP · SAD

**"Contraindication to arthroscopic revision?"** → **capsular attenuation from thermal capsulorrhaphy**\
✗ Wrong options: 10% bone loss · ALPSA · GLAD · SLAP + Bankart

**"Can't actively elevate after thermal capsulorrhaphy, normal X-ray?"** → **axillary nerve injury**\
✗ Wrong options: chondrolysis

**"Stiff painful shoulder 3 y after MDI scope, joint space loss?"** → **monopolar RF thermal capsulorrhaphy** (chondrolysis)

**"Thermal shrinkage — true?"** → **high failure for anterior, posterior and MDI**\
✗ Wrong options: thick fibrotic capsule · no healing

**"6-y recurrent instability, mid-range apprehension, 3D-CT bone loss?"** → **bony glenoid augmentation**\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopic Bankart · hemiarthroplasty

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Neuropathic (Charcot) Joint of Shoulder

*Shoulder & Elbow · 29 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **very rare** (~70 cases reported)

**Demographics** — mean age **~50** · **♂:♀ 2:1**

**Sites** — shoulder · elbow · foot & ankle

**Syrinx → Charcot** — **25% of syrinx patients** develop neuropathic arthropathy, **80% in upper limb** (Orthobullets, OB) · **25% of all Charcot joints** due to syringomyelia (Orthobullets associated conditions) — read the options

### Syringomyelia – most common cause & MRI work-up

| Cause | Note |
|---|---|
| **Syringomyelia** | **#1 cause of upper-limb (shoulder) Charcot**; **cervical syrinx = 75%** of Charcot shoulders; monoarticular (**shoulder > elbow**); Hatzis: syrinx in **5/6** shoulders |
| **Arnold-Chiari malformation** | **#1 cause of syringomyelia** |
| **Hansen's disease (leprosy)** | **2nd** most common cause in upper limb |
| **Neurosyphilis (tabes dorsalis)** | Usually **knee**; can be **polyarticular**; no longer the commonest |
| **Diabetes** | **#1 cause in foot & ankle**; reported in shoulder but less common; always rule out |
| **Chronic alcoholism** | Reported (Hatzis: 1/6) |
| Others | MS, ESRD, adhesive/TB arachnoiditis, meningomyelocele, traumatic SCI, familial sensory neuropathy |
| ✗ Wrong option: **uremia** | **No documented association** (OB) |

### Pathophysiology

**Syrinx** — **fluid-filled cavity in spinal cord** → damages **decussating fibres of lateral spinothalamic tract** → **loss of pain + temperature**

**Dissociated anaesthesia** — pain/temperature lost · **proprioception + motor preserved** (early)

**Syrinx enlarges** — dorsal column + **anterior horn** → **areflexia**, weakness, atrophy

**Neurotraumatic theory** — ↓ sensation/proprioception → **repetitive microtrauma** + abnormal joint pressures

**Neurovascular theory** — dysregulated reflexes → **↑ blood flow (hyperaemia)** → **↑ osteoclastic resorption**

### Genes & lab markers

**Molecular** — **RANK/RANKL/OPG** pathway implicated

**Labs** — **ESR + WBC may be ↑** → hard to separate from osteomyelitis · can be **normal** (OB stem: CBC, ESR, CRP normal)

**Histology** — synovial hypertrophy · **detritic synovitis** (cartilage + bone fragments in synovium)

## 3 Presentation

**History** — trauma as inciting event in **30%**

**Symptoms** — swollen shoulder · **50% painless** · loss of function · instability · progressive **painless weakness** + ↓ ROM

**Inspection** — **swollen, warm, erythematous** (intermittent) → **mimics infection**

**Motion** — **loss of motion = most common finding (90%)** · crepitus · laxity/mechanical instability

**Neuro** — ↓ strength · **cape-like** sensory + temperature loss (back + arms) · asymmetric reflexes · **areflexia late**

**Most common findings (OB)** — **pain, loss of motion, instability**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (AP + scapular Y)** — **gold standard / initial** diagnosis

| Stage | X-ray finding |
|---|---|
| Early | Degenerative changes **mimic OA** |
| Late | **Superomedial flattening of humeral head** |
| Late | **Periarticular soft-tissue calcifications** |
| Late | **Glenoid sclerosis** |
| Late | **Extensive bone resorption** (humeral head "amputated"), fragmentation |
| Late | Destruction of **both sides** of joint → subluxation/dislocation |

![Charcot shoulder – resorption of humeral head, destruction both sides of joint](../../MCQ/attachments/q216787-a-shoulder-neuropathic2.jpg)

**MRI cervical spine** — **next step in every Charcot shoulder** → syrinx (signal ≥ CSF on T1)

![Cervical syrinx on MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob9044-syrinx-mri.jpg)

**CT** — if concern for osteomyelitis: intraosseous gas, cortical destruction, sequestra

| Scan | Charcot | Osteomyelitis |
|---|---|---|
| **Technetium bone scan** | **Hot** | Hot |
| **Indium-labelled WBC scan** | **Cold (negative)** | **Hot (positive)** |

**Best test to separate from osteomyelitis** — **indium WBC scan**

### Differential diagnosis

| ✗ Distractor (OB) | Why not |
|---|---|
| **AVN** (loss of humeral head blood supply) | **Sclerosis + flattening** of head only |
| **Metastasis** (distant primary) | Variable; no obliteration of **both sides** of joint |
| **Cartilage tumour** | ↑ calcification throughout lesion |
| **Bone-forming tumour** | ↑ ossification throughout lesion |
| **Osteomyelitis / septic joint** | Same swelling, warmth, ↑ WBC/ESR, hot Tc scan → **indium scan hot** |

**Others** — synovial chondromatosis · soft-tissue sarcoma · tumoral calcinosis · Winchester syndrome · **Gorham's disease** · **Milwaukee shoulder**

## 5 Management

```
Charcot shoulder
├─ All → MRI C-spine + treat underlying disease
│      └─ cervical syrinx → neurosurgical decompression
├─ First line → rest, NSAIDs, sling, activity restriction, gentle PT
│      └─ severe pain → GH corticosteroid injection
└─ Failed nonop
       ├─ classic → shoulder arthrodesis
       └─ underlying condition controlled → off-label arthroplasty
```

### Nonoperative

**First line** — rest, NSAIDs, **sling immobilisation** (slows ligamentous laxity), activity restriction, treat cause, gentle PT/passive stretching · **50% improve**

**Corticosteroid injection** — severe pain · **glenohumeral** most effective · temporary **80%** pain ↓ (case reports)

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication | Outcome / technique |
|---|---|---|
| **Neurosurgical decompression** | **Cervical syrinx present** | Slows progression, improves function + bone (**glenoid regrowth**); posterior fossa decompression, craniotomy, **syringoperitoneal shunt**, laminectomy |
| **Arthrodesis** | Severe pain, **failed nonop**; historically **only** option | **S-shaped incision** scapular spine → over acromion → anterolateral arm |
| **Arthroplasty** (hemi, TSA, rTSA, resurfacing) | Failed nonop **+ underlying condition treated** | **Off-label** (Charcot = **strict FDA contraindication**, loosening) · **70%** improved function at 5 y |

**Fusion position ("30-30-30")** — **20–30° abduction · 20–30° forward flexion · 20–30° internal rotation** → hand to mouth

**No emergent ORIF / I&D** — not indicated (afebrile, normal labs) → **C-spine MRI first**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Risk factor |
|---|---|
| **Infection** | Surgery **without managing underlying condition** |
| **Upper-limb DVT** | Any surgery |
| **Acromial stress fracture** | **Reverse TSA** for Charcot |
| **Progressive glenoid erosion** | **Hemiarthroplasty** |

**Missed diagnosis** — often missed → **inappropriate surgery** (Hatzis)

## X vs Y

| | **Charcot shoulder** | **Osteomyelitis** | **AVN humeral head** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pain | Often **painless** (50%) | Painful | Painful |
| Warm, red, swollen | Yes (intermittent) | Yes | No |
| X-ray | **Resorption + destruction both sides**, calcifications | Destruction, sequestra | **Sclerosis + flattening** (head only) |
| Indium WBC | **Cold** | **Hot** | — |
| Next step | **MRI C-spine** | Aspirate/CT | MRI shoulder |

| Charcot cause | Classic joint |
|---|---|
| Syringomyelia | **Shoulder** (upper limb) |
| Diabetes | **Foot & ankle** |
| Tabes dorsalis | **Knee** (polyarticular) |

## How it's asked

**"Chronic shoulder pain, X-ray of destroyed/fragmented humerus — most common cause?"** → **syringomyelia**\
✗ Wrong options: diabetes · syphilis · alcoholism · uremia

**"Most common cause of upper-extremity neuropathic arthropathy?"** → **syringomyelia**\
✗ Wrong options: Hansen's = 2nd · diabetes = foot · neurosyphilis = knee

**"Painless progressive weakness, SCI, normal CBC/ESR/CRP, Charcot X-ray — early management?"** → **cervical spine MRI**\
✗ Wrong options: HIV test · repeat labs · emergent ORIF · emergent I&D

**"Painless stiff shoulder, intermittent warmth, destroyed joint — work-up will show?"** → **fluid-filled cavity in spinal cord**\
✗ Wrong options: AVN · metastasis · cartilage/bone tumour

**"Tract damaged by syrinx?"** → **decussating fibres of lateral spinothalamic tract** (pain + temperature)

**"Most common cause of syringomyelia?"** → **Arnold-Chiari malformation**

**"Test to differentiate Charcot from osteomyelitis?"** → **indium WBC scan (cold in Charcot)**

**"Syrinx found — treatment?"** → **neurosurgical decompression**

**"Shoulder fusion position?"** → **20–30° abduction, flexion, IR each**

**"Complication of rTSA in Charcot shoulder?"** → **acromial stress fracture**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Olecranon Stress Fracture

*Shoulder & Elbow · 30 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & location

**Frequency** — **rare**; predominantly **throwing athletes** (**baseball pitchers**), other high-level athletes; overuse injury

**Location** — predominantly the **olecranon epiphyseal plate** (persistent apophysis); line propagates **transverse or oblique**

**Age** — adolescent pitchers; may persist into **late adolescence** (**delayed closure of olecranon epiphysis**, 16–20 y; other elbow physes closed)

**Associated throwing injuries** — **UCL tear** · **medial epicondyle avulsion fracture**

## 2 Mechanism

| Theory (3 accepted) | Note |
|---|---|
| **Posteromedial olecranon impingement** | — |
| **Triceps traction force** | Repetitive forceful triceps contraction |
| **Valgus extension overload** | Part of **valgus-extension overload syndrome** |

**Torg & Moyer** — triceps contraction + **varus and valgus torques** → **olecranon epiphysis separates from epiphyseal plate**

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — **posteromedial olecranon pain** of throwing arm; **worse with throwing** (after several innings), **better with rest**

**Exam** — mild swelling, **localised tenderness over proximal olecranon** (medial, distal to epicondyle); full ROM; no mechanical symptoms

**Valgus stress test** — **instability → associated UCL injury**; **normal** in isolated stress fracture

**Late picture** — acute worsening + **inability to throw** after failed nonop

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Detail |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** | AP, lateral, oblique; optional **valgus stress** + **contralateral comparison** |
| X-ray findings | **Physeal widening, delayed fusion, fragmentation**; medial joint space widening if UCL injured |
| **CT** | Early confirmation; **best on lateral (sagittal) views**; **originates at articular surface → runs dorsal-proximal** |
| **MRI** | Early confirmation; **suspected UCL tear** → coronal **T2 fat-sat**: **T-sign** = UCL tear |

![Lateral X-ray — olecranon stress fracture through persistent physis](../../MCQ/attachments/q1408-a-stress-fx-elb-olecranon-lat-2-v-spanos.jpg)

![CT — fracture from articular surface running dorsal-proximal](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3127-screen-shot-2014-07-30-at-9-29-26-pm.jpg)

### Differential

| ✗ Distractor | Why wrong (11-yo pitcher, posteromedial tenderness, normal valgus stress) |
|---|---|
| Olecranon bursitis | No swelling/infection |
| OCD capitellum | No clicking / mechanical symptoms |
| UCL insufficiency | **Valgus stress normal** |
| Medial epicondylitis | Tenderness distal to epicondyle, over olecranon |

## 5 Management

```
Olecranon stress fracture
├─ First line → NSAIDs + rest ± splint (4–6 wk), no throwing up to 3 months
│      └─ delayed union / failed rest → ORIF
├─ Undisplaced → single large compression (cannulated) screw
└─ Displaced → treat as olecranon fracture (screw ± TBW or plate)
```

### Nonoperative

**Regimen** — short-term **NSAIDs**, **rest ± temporary splint**; **4–6 wk rest**, **removal from sport up to 3 months**; progressive ROM; **avoid valgus loading (throwing)**

**Adjuncts** — **electrical bone stimulation**; **vitamin D + calcium**

### Operative criteria – compression screw fixation

| Indication | Fixation |
|---|---|
| **Delayed union** / failed rest (e.g. **9 months**, or **two 6-wk rest periods**) | **Large compression screw** (intramedullary **cannulated**) |
| Undisplaced | **Single large screw** (e.g. **7.0 mm**) |
| Displaced | Screw **± tension band wire**, or plate |

**Plate fixation** — option once displaced (OB) · **not indicated** for this injury (another OB) — read the options

**✗ Next-step traps** — elbow arthroscopy · open osteophyte removal · ulnar nerve transposition · **cast + strengthening** after failed rest · physiotherapy · **long-arm cast** (stiffness; only reasonable briefly at onset) · **hinged-elbow brace** (no healing benefit)

![Compression screw + tension band construct, healed](../../MCQ/attachments/q1408-illa-olecranon-stress-fracture-fixation.jpg)

### Outcomes

**Rettig (7.0 mm screw, 5 pitchers)** — union mean **15.4 wk**; return to throwing mean **29.4 wk**

**Charlton & Chandler (16–20 y, delayed epiphyseal closure)** — UCL intact in all; ORIF with **TBW, screw ± autograft** → all **asymptomatic at 32 months** despite **> 30 months** pre-op pain

**Surgery** — reliable results when rest fails

## X vs Y

| | **Olecranon stress fracture** | **UCL insufficiency** |
|---|---|---|
| Pain | **Posteromedial olecranon** | — |
| Valgus stress test | **Normal** (unless UCL also injured) | **Positive / instability** |
| Imaging | Physeal widening, CT fracture line | Medial joint widening; MRI **T-sign** |

| | **Undisplaced** | **Displaced** |
|---|---|---|
| Fixation | **Single large compression screw** | Screw ± TBW or plate (as olecranon fracture) |

## How it's asked

**"19-yo pitcher, posterior elbow pain 9 months despite nonop, acute worsening — next?"** → **internal fixation with compression screw**\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopy · osteophyte removal · ulnar transposition · cast

**"11-yo pitcher, posteromedial pain, tender over proximal olecranon, normal valgus stress — diagnosis?"** → **olecranon stress fracture**\
✗ Wrong options: UCL insufficiency · OCD · medial epicondylitis · bursitis

**"15-yo pitcher, two 6-wk rest periods failed, other physes closed — next?"** → **cannulated screw fixation**\
✗ Wrong options: plate · hinged brace · long-arm cast · physio

**"Mechanism theories?"** → **posteromedial impingement, triceps traction, valgus extension overload**

**"First-line treatment?"** → **NSAIDs + rest ± brief splint, no valgus loading**

**"Indication for ORIF?"** → **delayed union**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Os Acromiale

*Shoulder & Elbow · 31 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Definition

**Os acromiale** — **unfused secondary ossification centre** of the acromion (**failure of fusion of anterior acromial apophysis**, OB) → impingement, cuff tendonitis

### Epidemiology

**Prevalence** — **8%** (Orthobullets, OB) · **1–15%** (another OB) — read the options

**Bilateral** — **60%** (Orthobullets) · **33%** (Sammarco skeletons, OB) — read the options

**Race** — **Black > White** (**13.2% vs 5.8%**)

**Sex** — **♂ > ♀** (**8.5% vs 4.9%**)

**Usually incidental** — found on imaging for other reasons, often **trauma**

### Anatomy – failed fusion of meso- and meta-acromion

| Ossification centre | Position | Origin of |
|---|---|---|
| **Pre-acromion** | **Tip** | **Anterior deltoid + coracoacromial ligament** |
| **Meso-acromion** | **Mid** | **Middle deltoid** |
| **Meta-acromion** | **Base** | **Posterior deltoid** |
| Basi-acromion | (4th centre, OB) | — |

**Number of centres** — **3** (Orthobullets, OB) · **4: pre-, meso-, meta-, basi-** (another OB) — read the options

**Fusion age** — ossify medially toward clavicular facet **by 18 y** (OB) · **15–18 y** (Hurst, OB)

**Most common site** — **junction of meso- and meta-acromion** = commonest **symptomatic** form\
✗ Wrong options: meso–pre · meta–pre

**Blood supply** — **acromial branch of thoracoacromial artery** → preserve at surgery

![Ossification centres: pre- (PA), meso- (MSA), meta-acromion (MTA)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3036-os-acromiale-drawing.jpg)

### Pathological mechanism

**✓ Mechanism** — **failure of fusion between ossification centres**

| ✗ Mechanism distractor | Condition |
|---|---|
| Failure of mesenchymal segmentation | **Tarsal coalition** |
| Defect in chondrocyte proliferation | **Achondroplasia** |
| Glycine substitution, abnormal collagen cross-linking | **Osteogenesis imperfecta** |
| Failure of cartilaginous differentiation | **Congenital radioulnar synostosis** |

### Associated conditions

**Impingement + rotator cuff disease** — **meso-acromion** → cuff tendonitis + **full-thickness tears in 50%**

## 3 Presentation

**Impingement pain** — anterior fragment **flexes / displaces downward** with deltoid contraction + arm elevation → ↓ subacromial space

**Synchondrosis pain** — **motion at nonunion site**

**Symptoms** — **superior shoulder pain**, **overhead activity**, **lying on affected side**; tenderness **anterolateral / superior acromion**

**Trigger** — **trauma** can make a previously silent os symptomatic

**Diagnostic injection** — **1% lidocaine** into site when pain source unclear (Spiegl)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Use |
|---|---|
| **Axillary lateral X-ray** | **Best view** for diagnosis |
| **CT** | Nonunion site; **degenerative change (cysts, sclerosis, hypertrophy)** |
| **Axial MRI** | Shows os (superior pain + overhead vignette) |

**Os vs fracture** — os: **rounded edges + corticalisation of entire fragment** · fracture: acute

**Fused os acromiale** — may be mistaken for a free ossicle (Sammarco)

![Axillary lateral showing os acromiale](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3036-shoulder-ax.jpg)

![CT — unfused acromial fragment](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3036-ct.jpg)

| ✗ Diagnosis distractor | |
|---|---|
| Acute acromial fracture | Rounded corticated edges = os |
| Type IV AC separation | Posterior clavicle |
| Deltoid avulsion | — |
| AC OA / AC separation / PTRCT / SLAP | MRI shows os |

## 5 Management

```
Os acromiale
├─ Incidental / mild symptoms → observe, NSAIDs, PT, subacromial steroid
│      └─ fails 6–12 months → surgery
├─ Symptomatic os + impingement → two-stage fusion
├─ Impingement ± cuff tear, os incidental + nontender → ASD + acromioplasty
└─ Small symptomatic pre-acromion / failed ORIF → fragment excision
```

### Nonoperative

**Regimen** — observation, **NSAIDs**, activity modification, therapy, **subacromial corticosteroid** → **majority**

**Duration** — surgery only after **6–12 months** of nonop

### Operative criteria – treatment of symptomatic os acromiale (fusion)

| Procedure | Indication | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| **Two-stage fusion** | **Symptomatic os + impingement**, failed nonop | **Stage 1: fuse ± bone graft** (rigid fixation: **TBW, sutures, cannulated screws**) → **Stage 2: acromioplasty** |
| **Arthroscopic SAD + acromioplasty** | **Impingement ± cuff tear, os incidental + nontender** | — |
| **Fragment excision** (open/arthroscopic) | **Symptomatic pre-acromion, small fragment** · **salvage after failed ORIF** | Arthroscopic: less periosteal + deltoid detachment; **better results with pre-acromion** |

**Direct excision of large fragment** — **deltoid dysfunction** → prefer fusion

**Warner (15 shoulders)** — **cannulated screws + tension band + autograft** → union in **all but one**; 8 had cuff tears

**Harris** — commonest concurrent procedure **cuff repair (59%)**, then **distal clavicle excision (25%)**; all techniques improve outcomes

**Abboud** — meso-os + cuff pathology satisfactory in **4 of 8**; **80%** satisfactory overall vs **22%** in **workers' compensation**

**44-yo, failed PT after cuff repair, symptomatic os + impingement** — ✗ Wrong options: observation · more PT · ASD alone (os not incidental) · excision alone

## 6 Complications

**Deltoid weakness** — after **fragment excision**

**Persistent pain / weakness**

**Prognosis** — **poorer outcomes after cuff repair** with **meso-os acromiale**; os may worsen outcome of unrelated shoulder surgery (Hurst)

## X vs Y

| | **Two-stage fusion** | **ASD + acromioplasty** | **Excision** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Os status | **Symptomatic, tender** + impingement | **Incidental, nontender** | Small **pre-acromion** / failed ORIF |
| Deltoid | Preserved | Preserved | **Risk of dysfunction** (large fragments) |

## How it's asked

**"27-yo, shoulder pain, X-ray shows os — cause?"** → **failure of fusion meso- and meta-acromion**\
✗ Wrong options: meso–pre · acute fracture · type IV AC · deltoid avulsion

**"23-yo, superior shoulder pain overhead + lying on side, axial MRI?"** → **os acromiale**\
✗ Wrong options: AC OA · PTRCT · SLAP

**"Most common location?"** → **junction of meso- and meta-acromion**

**"Pathological mechanism?"** → **failure of fusion between ossification centres**\
✗ Wrong options: segmentation = coalition · glycine = OI

**"44-yo, failed PT after cuff repair, symptomatic os with impingement — treatment?"** → **two-stage fusion**\
✗ Wrong options: ASD · excision only

**"Os incidental + nontender, impingement ± cuff tear?"** → **arthroscopic SAD + acromioplasty**

**"Best view?"** → **axillary lateral**

**"Complication of excision?"** → **deltoid weakness**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Osteochondritis Dissecans of Elbow

*Shoulder & Elbow · 32 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Presentation & population (capitellum, gymnasts/throwers)

**Definition** — localised injury → **separation of articular cartilage + subchondral bone** of the **capitellum**

**Population** — **gymnasts + throwing athletes** (pitchers) · repetitive overhead or **upper-limb weight bearing** · also cheerleaders\
✗ Wrong options: swimmers/divers · linemen · rugby · cyclists

**Age** — **>10 y** (adolescent) (Orthobullets, OB) · "typically **>13 y**" (one OB explanation)

**Sex** — **boys > girls**

**Site** — **capitellum** of **dominant** arm (most common) · occasionally **radial head** (OB) \
✗ Wrong options: "capitellum or lateral trochlea"

**Juvenile vs adult** — **juvenile OCD better prognosis**

### Anatomy – blood supply

**Capitellum** — supplied by **2 end arteries**: **radial recurrent** + **interosseous recurrent** → vulnerable to ischaemia

![Capitellar blood supply – radial recurrent + interosseous recurrent arteries](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3085-blood-supply.jpg)

### Pathophysiology

**Mechanism** — **repetitive compression** (lateral compression + axial loading) of **immature capitellum** → **vascular insufficiency** + **repetitive microtrauma** → osteochondral necrosis → separation

**Throwers** — **repetitive valgus overload of radiocapitellar joint**, **late cocking** phase

**Panner's + OCD** — likely a **continuum** (OB)

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — insidious, **activity-related lateral elbow pain**, dominant arm · posterolateral pain with throwing

| Sign | Timing |
|---|---|
| **Loss of extension** (mild, ~15°) | **Early** sign = typical early presentation |
| **Catching / locking / grinding / clicking / popping** | **Late** — loose bodies; **poorer prognosis** |
| Lateral tenderness, mild **effusion**, ± crepitus | Any |
| Painful click on passive rotation, painful pronation/supination | Unstable lesion |

**Instability testing** — normal\
✗ Wrong options: MUCL laxity

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (AP + lateral; slightly flexed-elbow view)** — confirms diagnosis: **well-defined subchondral lesion** in capitellum · radiolucency, **flattening**, sclerosis, fragmentation, loose bodies · initial films may be **negative**

![Capitellar OCD – flattening and lucency](../../MCQ/attachments/q216869-c-capitellar-ocd.jpg)

**MRI** — best for **size, extent of oedema, cartilage status** · OCD: **separation of cartilage from capitellum + chondral fissuring** · stable: **intact cartilage + subchondral signal change**, T2 no signal around lesion

**MR arthrogram** — contrast **outlining margins** of lesion = **unstable** fragment → **poor** prognostic sign\
✗ Wrong options: good

**Os supratrochleare dorsale** — normal variant, incidental\
✗ Wrong options: prognostic

### Classification – radiographic & arthroscopic

| Type | Findings | Image |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Intact cartilage**; bony stability may or may not be present | ![Type I](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3085-type-1-ocd.jpg) |
| **II** | **Cartilage fracture** with bony collapse or displacement | ![Type II](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3085-ocd-elbow.jpg) |
| **III** | **Loose bodies** in joint | ![Type III](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3085-type-3-lesion-elbow.jpg) |

### Stability criteria (Takahara)

| **Stable** (heals with rest) — all of | **Unstable** (surgery better) — any of |
|---|---|
| **Open capitellar physis** | **Closed** capitellar physis |
| Localised flattening or radiolucency of subchondral bone | **Fragmentation** |
| Good elbow motion | **Restricted motion ≥20°** |

## 5 Management

```
Capitellar OCD
├─ Type I stable → stop activity 3–6 wk → graded return over 6–12 wk
├─ Unstable type I / stable type II → arthroscopic microfracture or drilling
├─ Large, incompletely displaced → arthroscopic reduction + fixation
├─ Unstable type II / type III → arthroscopic debridement + loose body excision
└─ Large type II–III engaging radial head → OATS
       └─ uncontained → size-matched fresh allograft
```

### Stable lesions – activity cessation

**First line** — **cessation of offending activity** ± immobilisation · **>90% success**

**Duration** — **cessation of activity 3–6 weeks**, followed by slow progression back to activities over next **6–12 weeks** (Orthobullets, OB) · "**minimum 6 wk**" / "**6 wk**" (other OB explanations) — read the options

**Return to play** — resumption when **asymptomatic** + examination shows **restoration of painless range of motion**\
✗ Wrong options: wait for complete radiographic healing (residual X-ray abnormality persists even when asymptomatic)

**No participation** until symptoms resolve + motion recovers\
✗ Wrong options: continue until worse

**Avoid casting 6–8 wk** — too long → stiffness

**Avoid immediate throwing programme** · no surgery before trial of nonop (stable, no loose body)

**Nonop healing** — >50% of stable lesions heal with activity restriction; **smaller lesions without cyst-like change** heal better

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication | Post-op |
|---|---|---|
| **Arthroscopic microfracture / drilling** (extra- or transarticular) | **Unstable type I** · **stable type II** · symptomatic lesion with **intact articular surface** (in situ drilling) | Protected ROM; strengthen 2 mo; throw/WB **4–6 mo** |
| **Fixation** (arthroscopic reduction) | **Large**, **incompletely displaced** (partial separation) | As above; results variable |
| **Arthroscopic debridement + loose body excision** | **Unstable type II** · **type III** · mechanical symptoms | Early ROM ± brace; no throwing/WB **3 mo** |
| **OATS** (autograft/allograft) | **Large type II–III engaging radial head**; uncontained → fresh allograft | Strengthen 3 mo; throwing 5–7 mo |
| Lateral column osteotomy | **Investigational**, salvage (offloads lateral compression) | — |

**Surgery overall** — failed extended nonop · mechanical symptoms · loose bodies on imaging · unstable lesions

**OATS (single plug)** — **93%** graft incorporation, high return to sport, low donor morbidity · large unstable lesions: **fixation/reconstruction > simple excision**

**Microfracture** — among the most commonly performed + studied

### Prognosis – skeletal maturity

**Best predictor** — **skeletal age / open physes**\
✗ Wrong options: symptom duration · gender · activity-related symptoms · anatomic variant

**Healing** — most heal in **6–18 months** of nonop (open physes)

**Poor nonop healing** — **radial head enlargement** · skeletal age difference · closed physis

**Long term** — **~50%** residual ADL symptoms (Takahara: 50% nonop, 46% fragment removal) · **up to 50% arthritis**

## 6 Complications

**Complications** — **stiffness** · pain · **failure to return to sport** · **arthritis**

## X vs Y

| | **OCD of capitellum** | **Panner's disease** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | **>10 y** (adolescent) | **<10 y** (first decade) |
| Lesion | **Focal, well-defined subchondral** lesion | **Entire capitellar ossific nucleus** fragmented; irregular epiphysis |
| MRI | Cartilage separation + chondral fissuring | — |
| Loose bodies | Common | Rare |
| Course | Variable; arthritis up to 50% | **Benign, self-limiting**, normal regrowth, no sequelae |
| Surgery | May be needed | **Contraindicated** |

| Distractor (X-ray / stem) | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| Calcification at common extensor origin | **Lateral epicondylitis** |
| **Widened medial epicondyle physis** | **Little Leaguer's elbow** |
| Incarcerated medial epicondyle fragment | Medial epicondyle fracture |
| **Olecranon osteophyte**, posterior pain in extension | **Valgus extension overload** |
| Medial pain + instability | UCL (Tommy John) |
| After dislocation | PLRI |
| Posterior pain in extension | Olecranon stress fracture |
| Lateral pain, rarely effusion/mechanical | Plica |

## How it's asked

**"13-y pitcher, lateral pain, effusion, painful click on rotation?"** → **OCD**\
✗ Wrong options: Tommy John · PLRI · stress fracture · plica

**"Population for capitellar OCD?"** → **gymnasts + throwing athletes**

**"Characteristic of elbow OCD?"** → **MRI: cartilage separation from capitellum + chondral fissuring**\
✗ Wrong options: whole ossific nucleus · normal regrowth · <10 y · MUCL laxity

**"Typical early presentation?"** → **loss of extension**\
✗ Wrong options: catching/locking = late · boys <10 = Panner · MR arthrogram outline = poor sign

**"14-y gymnast, can't fully straighten, popping — which X-ray?"** → **capitellar flattening + lucency**\
✗ Wrong options: extensor calcification · physeal widening · olecranon osteophyte

**"12-y gymnast, 15° extension loss, 3 × 7 mm stable lesion, no loose bodies?"** → **no gymnastics until symptoms resolve + motion recovers**

**"Little League pitcher, catching, lateral pain — initial management?"** → **rest; resume when asymptomatic + painless full ROM**\
✗ Wrong options: wait for X-ray healing · drilling · osteotomy

**"11-y pitcher, lacks terminal extension, first visit?"** → **stop pitching 3–6 wk + slow return**\
✗ Wrong options: medial epicondyle ORIF · OATS · debridement

**"Type I OCD on MRI, full ROM — initial treatment?"** → **cessation 3–6 wk then progression over 6–12 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: casting 6–8 wk · throwing programme

**"Intact cartilage + subchondral signal change?"** → **cessation of activity for 6 wk**\
✗ Wrong options: microfracture · fixation · OATS

**"Unstable type I or stable type II?"** → **microfracture / drilling**

**"Large lesion engaging radial head?"** → **OATS**

**"16-y cheerleader, closed physes — largest prognostic factor?"** → **skeletal age**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Pectoralis Major Rupture

*Shoulder & Elbow · 33 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — rare (**<1/100,000/y**) but rising · **75%** of reported cases since 1990

**Demographics** — **almost exclusively ♂, 20–40 y** · **weightlifters**, **bench press** · almost no reports in ♀ (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: equal ♂/♀ · old gym-goers

**Risk** — **anabolic steroids** / supplements

**Other sports (TO)** — rugby, football, wrestling, water skiing, boxing

**Most common site** — **tendinous avulsion at humeral insertion (65%)** > **musculotendinous junction (27%)** (TO) · intramuscular rare (direct trauma, stab)\
✗ Wrong options: "MTJ most common" · muscle belly

**Most common portion** — **sternocostal (sternal) head** (✓ TO "all except" answer) · clavicular head often intact

### Anatomy – sternal head & pectoral nerve innervation

| | **Clavicular head** | **Sternocostal head** |
|---|---|---|
| Origin | **Medial half of clavicle** (+ proximal sternum) | Sternum, **costal cartilages 1–6** (TO), external oblique aponeurosis |
| Size | Smaller; may look confluent with deltoid | **Larger, >80% of volume** |
| Tendon layer at insertion | **Anterior** | **Posterior** (lower sternal fibres) |
| Nerve | **Lateral pectoral (C5–C7)** | **Lateral + medial pectoral** (dual, C5–T1) |
| Rupture | Fails last | **Fails first**, often isolated |

**Innervation** — **lateral pectoral (C5–C7)** = clavicular + upper sternocostal · **medial pectoral (C8–T1)** = lower sternocostal + pectoralis minor (TO, Orthobullets)

**Insertion** — **lateral lip of bicipital groove** (humeral shaft lateral to groove), anterior + posterior layers (TO)

**Action** — **adduction + internal rotation** · forward elevation/flexion lesser (chiefly clavicular head) (TO) · not predominant forward flexion = **anterior deltoid + coracobrachialis**

**Thoracoappendicular muscles** — pec major, pec minor, subclavius, serratus anterior

![Brachial plexus: lateral pectoral (lateral cord) and medial pectoral (medial cord)](../../MCQ/attachments/q220091-illa-ill-1-pm.jpg)

| ✗ Nerve distractor | Supplies |
|---|---|
| Suprascapular (C5–C6) | Supraspinatus, infraspinatus |
| Thoracodorsal (C6–C8) | Latissimus dorsi |
| Medial brachial cutaneous (T1) | Sensation medial arm |
| Upper/lower subscapular | Subscapularis |
| Axillary / musculocutaneous | Deltoid, teres minor / biceps, brachialis |

## 2 Mechanism

### Mechanism – eccentric bench press

**Mechanism** — excessive tension on **maximally eccentrically contracted** muscle · **downward (deceleration) phase of bench press**\
✗ Wrong options: upward · max elevation · bar on chest

**Position** — **final 30° of humeral extension** (inferior sternal fibres at maximal stretch) · extension + abduction + ER · TO: **sudden forceful abduction + ER of contracted muscle**

**Failure sequence** — **inferior sternocostal fibres** (insert more posteriorly) → superior sternocostal → **clavicular head last**

## 3 Presentation

**History** — **sudden pop / tearing** with resisted adduction + IR · pain, weakness

**Inspection** — **swelling + ecchymosis anterolateral chest wall and/or proximal medial arm** · **loss of anterior axillary fold**, muscle retracted medially · palpable defect accentuated by **resisted adduction** · **"dropped nipple" sign**

**Ecchymosis location** — **anterior brachium/upper arm** → **humeral avulsion** · **chest wall** → mid-substance / MTJ tear (less benefit from surgery)

**Weakness** — **adduction + internal rotation** (± forward flexion); ER, abduction normal; apprehension negative

![Loss of axillary fold, chest asymmetry, upper arm ecchymosis](../../MCQ/attachments/q8788-c-pmchest.jpg)

| ✗ Exam-sign distractor | Lesion |
|---|---|
| Popeye | Proximal LHB tear |
| Medial winging | Long thoracic nerve / serratus |
| Belly-press | Subscapularis |
| ER lag | Infraspinatus |

**✗ Wrong option: axillary fold** — subscapularis and biceps injuries do **not** change the axillary fold

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnosis – MRI

**X-ray** — trauma series (true AP, scapular Y, axillary) · usually **normal** · lost pec shadow or bony avulsion

**MRI** — **investigation of choice / gold standard** · complete vs partial · tear site + retraction (muscle belly vs bone–tendon junction) · needs **dedicated chest-wall sequence** (standard shoulder MRI misses it) · **T2 acute**, **T1 chronic** · intact clavicular head may mask partial sternal tear

**EMG** — no role without nerve injury

### Modified Tietjen classification

| Type | Description |
|---|---|
| I | Contusion / sprain |
| II | Partial tear |
| III | **Complete** tear, by location ↓ |
| III-A | Muscle origin |
| III-B | Muscle belly |
| III-C | **Musculotendinous junction** |
| III-D | Intratendinous |
| III-E | **Tendon avulsion off humerus (no bone)** |
| III-F | Bony avulsion off humerus |

**Tietjen (TO)** — sprain · partial · complete (origin, belly, MTJ, tendon) + added **bony flake avulsion** + **intratendinous**

![Modified Tietjen tear locations](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3069-overview-of-modified-tietjen-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Pectoralis major rupture
├─ Low-demand, elderly, sedentary → sling, ice, NSAIDs, PT
│      └─ muscle belly / low-grade partial / chest-wall tear → nonop
├─ Acute complete avulsion or MTJ, young/active → open primary repair
└─ Chronic, can't mobilise → allograft reconstruction
       └─ primary repair still possible years later
```

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Avulsion from humeral insertion** (complete pecto-humeral tear) | **Primary repair** (✓ surgery indication) |
| Acute tear, young athlete / labourer | **Primary acute repair** = gold standard |
| MTJ tear | Repair (Orthobullets, OB) · therapy may be considered (one OB) · may need augmentation/reconstruction (one OB) — read the options |
| Muscle belly / partial / chest-wall ecchymosis | **Nonop** |
| Chest asymmetry alone, no deficit | not an indication |
| Chronic, inadequate excursion | **Reconstruction (allograft)** |

### Treatment – acute repair, chronic allograft reconstruction & nonoperative deficits

**Primary repair** — **open, deltopectoral** · reattach to humerus lateral to bicipital groove · **suture anchors or buttons** (TO) · transosseous with cortical trough · cortical button · anchors (PEAK/all-suture) · all comparably excellent; some evidence **button + trough > anchors**

**Acute vs delayed** — acute easier, less scar, avoids later reconstruction/allograft\
✗ Wrong options: delayed repair after failed nonop

**Best long-term result (sternocostal tear)** — **open repair of sternocostal portion**\
✗ Wrong options: PT/NSAIDs · steroid injection · LHB repair · clavicular repair

**Repair outcome** — reliable strength, return to sport, satisfaction; improves regardless of tear site · most can be repaired primarily, **even chronic**\
✗ Wrong options: majority need allograft

**Chronic reconstruction** — release adhesions superficial + deep (**protect medial + lateral pectoral nerves**) ± fascial release · graft: **Achilles allograft (most common)** (Orthobullets) · **semitendinosus weave** (OB) · gracilis weave · inferior to primary repair, better than nonop

**Chronic weakness 2 y after PT, young labourer** — **allograft reconstruction (semitendinosus weave)**\
✗ Wrong options: latissimus transfer · electrical stimulation · arthrodesis · arthroscopic repair

**Nonop deficits** — **weakness of adduction + IR** (esp. isokinetic adduction, peak torque) · cosmetic defect · delayed recovery · poor satisfaction · ↓ return to sport

**Nonop protocol** — sling in adduction + IR · immediate PROM · AAROM/AROM over 6 wk · strengthening/unrestricted at **2–3 mo**

**Tendon transfer** — not for acute tear (pec major is a **donor** for subscapularis insufficiency)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **Persistent pain** | **Most common** |
| **Re-rupture** | **5–7%**; fails at **suture–tendon interface** |
| Residual weakness | — |
| Cosmetic deformity | — |
| Nerve injury in reconstruction | Medial + lateral pectoral during deep release |

## X vs Y

| | **Pec major tear** | **LHB rupture** | **Subscapularis tear** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sign | **Lost anterior axillary fold**, dropped nipple | **Popeye** | **Belly-press**, ↑ passive ER |
| Weakness | **Adduction + IR** | — | — |
| Axillary fold | **Lost** | Normal | Normal |

| | **Sternocostal head** | **Clavicular head** |
|---|---|---|
| Torn | **Most often, first** | Last, often intact |
| Nerve | Lateral + medial pectoral | Lateral pectoral |

## How it's asked

**"Pec major tear — all false EXCEPT?"** → **sternal head most commonly ruptured** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: old gym-goers · MTJ most common · equal sexes

**"Bench press pop, upper medial arm pain, lost anterior fold?"** → **pectoralis major tear** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: LHB · axillary fold tear · cuff

**"Weightlifter, pop, MRI retracted muscle — nerve?"** → **lateral + medial pectoral**\
✗ Wrong options: axillary · musculocutaneous · subscapular · suprascapular

**"Labelled plexus — innervation of most commonly torn portion?"** → **A + E (lateral + medial pectoral)**

**"True about this injury?"** → **sternal head torn more than clavicular**\
✗ Wrong options: ♀ · allograft needed · flexion loss · belly

**"Bench press phase at greatest risk?"** → **downward deceleration**

**"38 y weightlifter, MRI arrow?"** → **pectoralis major tear**\
✗ Wrong options: biceps · pec minor · subscapularis

**"When is surgery indicated?"** → **avulsion from humeral insertion**\
✗ Wrong options: never · after failed PT · asymmetry only · chest-wall ecchymosis

**"30 y lifter, lost axillary contour, weak IR — definitive?"** → **primary acute surgical repair**

**"Bodybuilder, axillary + anterior brachium ecchymosis, MRI — best long-term?"** → **open repair of sternocostal portion**

**"Young electrician, lost fold worse with adduction, normal X-ray?"** → **open repair of pec major avulsion**\
✗ Wrong options: subscap repair · LHB tenodesis · brace

**"Chronic tear 2 y after PT, weak — treatment?"** → **allograft reconstruction (semitendinosus weave)**

**"Nonop treatment leaves weakness of?"** → **adduction + internal rotation**

**"Repair to red label; preop sign?"** → **lost axillary fold + chest asymmetry + arm ecchymosis (Figure C)**\
✗ Wrong options: Popeye · winging · belly-press · ER lag

**"42 y lifter, 3 days, MRI typical tear — next step?"** → **primary repair**\
✗ Wrong options: EMG · sling + PT · reconstruction · tendon transfer
