# Phases of Throwing

*Shoulder & Elbow · 34 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### The phases

**Number of phases** — **5**: wind-up · cocking (early + late) · acceleration · deceleration · follow-through (Orthobullets, TO) · **4**: wind-up · cocking · acceleration · deceleration/follow-through (one TO) — read the options

**Timing** — whole throw **~2 s**; wind-up + acceleration ≈ **75% (1.5 s)** (Orthobullets) · **wind-up + cocking = 80% (~1500 ms)**, acceleration **2% (50 ms)**, deceleration **18% (350 ms)** (TO) — read the options

![Phases – wind-up, early cocking, late cocking, acceleration, deceleration, follow-through](../../MCQ/attachments/q213064-illa-stages-of-throwing-labeled.jpg)

| Phase | Mechanics | Peak muscle activity | Associated pathology |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Wind-up** (500–1000 ms, TO) | Body rotates, hips + shoulders 90° to target; "controlled fall"; minimal shoulder force | **Rotator cuff inactive** | — |
| **Early cocking** | Arm loading; max valgus not yet reached | **Deltoid** | — |
| **Late cocking** (500–1000 ms, TO) | **Max shoulder ER** ("loaded", apprehension position), **high torque**; anterior capsule coiled | **Supraspinatus, infraspinatus, teres minor** (rotator cuff); subscapularis late cocking → early acceleration | **MUCL injury** (**max elbow valgus**) · **internal impingement** · **GIRD** · **SLAP (peel-back)** |
| **Acceleration** (**50 ms**, shortest, TO) | Max ER → **ball release (~ear level)**; elastic capsular recoil + IR muscles | Early **triceps** → late **pec major, latissimus dorsi, serratus anterior** | Enormous elbow valgus at release (TO) |
| **Deceleration** (350 ms, TO) | Eccentric braking; centre of gravity over plantar foot | **Eccentric contraction of all muscles**; **highest torque** | **Most harmful**: **SLAP**, **biceps**, **brachialis**, **teres minor** |
| **Follow-through** | Body rebalances, stops forward motion | Muscles **return to resting** | — |

**Most harmful phase** — **deceleration** (Orthobullets, TO) · "**late cocking + deceleration** put greatest force on shoulder" (TO)

**Rotator cuff greatest stress** — **deceleration** (eccentric slowing of arm)

**Minimal throughout** — short head of biceps + coracobrachialis

### Forces & kinematics (TO)

| Parameter (TO) | Value |
|---|---|
| Shoulder ER | **>160°** |
| IR angular velocity | **>6000°/s** |
| IR torque | **>60 N·m** |
| Anterior joint force, end of cocking | **>350 N**; **AIGHL + anteroinferior capsule** under greatest strain |
| Distraction force, deceleration | **>500 N** |

**IR muscles in acceleration (TO)** — subscapularis, pec major, latissimus dorsi, teres major

**Deceleration brakes (TO)** — eccentric **ER cuff**, **scapular stabilisers**, **posterior deltoid**; elbow flexors limit extension; spine = force attenuator

### Scapular kinematics & muscle activation

**Scapula + humerus** — must work **in concert** for GH stability

**Scapular rotation** — must **rotate in cocking + acceleration** → elevate / clear acromion → prevents cuff impingement

**Retraction / protraction** — **maximal retraction in late cocking** (acromion starts to elevate) · **protraction in acceleration + ball release** (**serratus anterior, long thoracic nerve**)

**✗ Scapula distractors** — max retraction at ball release · protracts in late cocking · fixed during throw (must be dynamic) · no effect on concavity-compression\
✗ Wrong options: — centres head → ↑ concavity-compression

**Kibler – 5 scapular roles** — stable GH articulation · retraction + protraction · **acromial elevation** · base for muscle attachment · **link in kinetic chain**

**Kinetic chain (TO)** — **~½ of kinetic energy** transferred through **scapulothoracic** joint

**SICK scapula (TO)** — **S**capular malposition · **I**nferior medial border prominence · **C**oracoid pain + malposition · dys**K**inesis → anterior shoulder pain, coracoid tenderness, AC pain, ↓ forward flexion

### Dynamic medial elbow stabilisers (FDS, FCU)

**Local dynamic elbow stability** — **FDS + FCU** in **cocking + acceleration** → dynamic compression; **protect UCL**\
✗ Wrong options: FDP · ECRL/ECRB · FCR

**Implication** — strengthen forearm flexors to protect UCL

## 2 Mechanism

### Phase of maximal valgus stress – late cocking/early acceleration

**Max elbow valgus** — **late cocking** (TO, Orthobullets, OB) → **MUCL** injury; MCQs combine as **late cocking/early acceleration**

**Medial elbow pain + valgus stress test** — reproduced in **late cocking**\
✗ Wrong options: early cocking (max valgus not yet) · acceleration/deceleration (more force at shoulder)

**Varus instability** — **LUCL**\
✗ Wrong options: valgus

### Throwing athlete adaptations (TO)

**Shoulder** — **↑ ER range** (anterior capsule/IGHL stretch) → anterior instability + secondary impingement

**IR:ER strength** — normal **3:2**; exaggerated in throwers → ER weakness ↑ injury risk

**Bony** — ↑ **humeral retrotorsion** (↓ anteversion) · ↑ **glenoid retroversion**

**Elbow** — breakdown of medial stabilisers (MCL, capsule, flexors) → **↑ carrying angle**; anterior capsular strain, posterior impingement, flexor strain → **fixed flexion deformity**

## 3 Presentation

### Internal impingement & GIRD (TO)

**Internal impingement (TO)** — late cocking (**ABER**): posterior cuff undersurface pinched between **greater tuberosity/humeral head and posterosuperior glenoid** → **posterosuperior labral tear** (hallmark) + **articular-sided (PASTA) supraspinatus** tear

**GIRD (TO)** — adaptive **loss of IR** in throwing shoulder; caused by **late cocking** (repetitive cocking)

**GIRD pathology (TO)** — **posteroinferior capsule + posterior band IGHL contracture** (thicker posterior capsule) ± posterior cuff tightness → ↑ GH contact pressure + impingement

**GIRD symptoms (TO)** — stiffness · prolonged warm-up · **loss of velocity ("dead arm")** · vague **posterior** pain · late cocking provocative

**GIRD associations (TO)** — posterior labral tears · posterosuperior PASTA tears · **SLAP (peel-back in ABER)** · scapular dyskinesia · **UCL tears**

**Sulcus sign in GIRD (TO)** — **~⅔ of pitchers**; rotator interval laxity (**CHL + SGHL**)

**Cuff tears in throwers (TO)** — mostly **degenerative partial/full** from impingement (**internal > external**) · minority **acute traumatic** from **deceleration** overload

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnostic criteria – GIRD

| GIRD | |
|---|---|
| **Definition** | IR loss **>20°** / **≥20°** vs contralateral (TO) · **25°** (OB, MDI topic) — read the options |
| **Measurement** | **Supine, 90° abduction + 90° elbow flexion**; max passive IR = point **just before scapula lifts**; goniometer; also TRM |
| **Posterior tightness (Tyler)** | Lateral decubitus, 90° abduction, neutral rotation, scapula stabilised → adduct; **1 cm loss ≈ 5° IR loss** |
| **Posterior impingement sign (Meister)** | Max passive **ABER (late cocking)** → **deep posterior pain** = posterosuperior labral / PASTA |

![Left GIRD – IR deficit on 90/90 exam](../../MCQ/attachments/TO21733265-1.png)

## 5 Management

```
Throwing shoulder with GIRD
├─ First line → posterior capsular stretching + scapular strengthening
│      └─ sleeper stretch, cross-body stretch, passive prone stretch
└─ Severe GIRD + ↓ TRM, failed extensive nonop → arthroscopic posteroinferior capsular release
```

### Throwing athlete rehabilitation (TO)

**Mainstay (TO)** — **posterior capsular stretching** + **scapular** strengthening

**Sleeper stretch (TO)** — lie on **affected side**, shoulder + elbow **90°**, scapula stabilised → other arm pushes forearm to floor (**IR**); **30 s × 5**

![Sleeper stretch](../../MCQ/attachments/TO21733265-3.png)

**Cross-body stretch (TO)** — standing/sitting, shoulder flexed 90° → other hand pulls arm across chest (**horizontal adduction**); **30 s × 5**

**Not appropriate for GIRD (TO)** — **latissimus dorsi stretch** (✓ sleeper, ✓ cross-body, ✓ passive prone shoulder stretch)

**Stretching benefits (TO)** — ↑ IR · ↑ TRM · ↑ **acromiohumeral distance**

**Surgery (TO)** — only after exhausted nonop; SLAP repair + articular cuff tear treatment **controversial** (variable RTP); **arthroscopic posteroinferior capsular release** for severe GIRD

### Follow-through & injury prevention (TO)

**Long follow-through (TO)** — **decreases** chance of muscle injury\
✗ Wrong options: increases injury · ↑ fatigue · no relation

**Why** — dissipates deceleration forces; forward torso rotation reacquires energy → ↓ burden on serratus + scapular stabilisers

### Ball-flight physics (Magnus effect) (TO)

**Magnus effect (TO)** — **spinning ball** drags boundary layer of air → **high pressure** where boundary layer opposes passing air · **low pressure** where they move together → **lift (Magnus) force** high → low → **curved flight path**

**Sports (TO)** — **tennis**, cricket, soccer, baseball, golf\
✗ Wrong options: sprints · swimming · basketball

![Magnus effect – spin creates pressure differential and curved flight](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17135941-1.png)

## X vs Y

| | **Late cocking** | **Deceleration** |
|---|---|---|
| Shoulder position | **Max ER**, ABER | Rapid IR, arm slowing |
| Muscles | Cuff (supra, infra, teres minor) peak | **Eccentric all**; cuff greatest stress |
| Elbow | **Max valgus → MUCL** | Elbow flexors (brachialis) |
| Shoulder pathology | **Internal impingement, GIRD, SLAP peel-back** | **SLAP, biceps, teres minor** |
| Label | High torque | **Highest torque, most harmful** |

| | **Early cocking** | **Acceleration** |
|---|---|---|
| Peak muscle | **Deltoid** | **Triceps** → **pec major, lat, serratus** |
| Scapula | — | **Protracts** (serratus) |
| Duration | Part of 500–1000 ms | **50 ms — shortest** |

## How it's asked

**"Scapula during throwing — true?"** → **must rotate in cocking + acceleration to prevent cuff impingement**\
✗ Wrong options: max retraction at release · protracts in late cocking · fixed · no concavity-compression effect

**"Peak muscle in early cocking (figure)?"** → **deltoid**\
✗ Wrong options: subscapularis = late cocking/early acceleration · supraspinatus/teres minor = late cocking

**"Most important dynamic medial elbow stabilisers in cocking + acceleration?"** → **FDS + FCU**

**"Phase of maximum valgus at elbow in bowling?"** → **late cocking** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: acceleration · deceleration

**"Phase causing GIRD?"** → **late cocking** (TO)

**"Medial epicondyle tenderness + valgus stress test — phase reproducing pain?"** → **late cocking**

**"Valgus instability after seasons of pitching — structure + phase?"** → **MUCL; late cocking (C)**\
✗ Wrong options: LUCL = varus

**"Young athlete, IR deficit on exam — exercise NOT appropriate?"** → **latissimus dorsi stretch** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: sleeper · cross-body · passive prone

**"Magnus effect important in?"** → **tennis** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: sprints · swimming · basketball

**"Long follow-through in bowling?"** → **decreases muscle injury** (TO)

**"Most harmful / highest torque phase?"** → **deceleration**

**"Shortest phase?"** → **acceleration (50 ms)** (TO)

# Posterior Labral Tear

*Shoulder & Elbow · 35 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — **reverse Bankart** lesion / attenuation of posterior capsulolabral complex

**Frequency** — relatively **rare** vs anterior labral pathology

**Athletes** — **weightlifters (bench press)** · **football linemen (blocking)** · **wrestlers** · swimmers · gymnasts · **baseball batters**

### Anatomy

**Posterior labrum** — **fibrocartilage** · cavity-compression · **50% of socket depth** · **posterior stability** · **anchors PIGHL**

**Static restraints to posterior translation (flexion, adduction, IR)** — **PIGHL**, augmented by **SGHL**

| ✗ Distractor | Actual restraint role |
|---|---|
| Supraspinatus | **Dynamic**; prevents **inferior** instability |
| MGHL | Static; anterior + posterior at **45° abduction** |
| Subscapularis | **Dynamic**; posterior translation in **ER** |
| AIGHL | Static; **anterior** at **90° abduction + ER** |

**Most important factors in posterior instability (Bäcker)** — **capsular lesions + IGHL rupture**

## 2 Mechanism

### Mechanism & provocative position (flexion–adduction–internal rotation)

**Most common mechanism** — **repetitive microtrauma** to posterior capsulolabral complex

**Provocative position** — **posteriorly directed force** with arm **flexed, ADducted, internally rotated** (net posterior vector)

**✗ Wrong option: flexion + ABduction + ER** — anterior instability → **anteroinferior** labral tear

**Batter's shoulder** — **acute posterior subluxation** on **adducted lead shoulder** while swinging a bat → acute posterior labral tear\
✗ Wrong options: recurrent anterior events · anabolic steroids

**Posterior capsular tightening** — → **GIRD**\
✗ Wrong options: cause of batter's shoulder

**Glenoid retroversion** — **~17° → 6× more likely** posterior instability vs ~7°

**Kim lesion** — **incomplete, sometimes concealed avulsion of posteroinferior labrum**

**First-time posterior dislocation** — **less likely to recur** than first-time anterior

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **vague, deep, nonspecific posterior shoulder pain** (most common) · **↓ strength** (bench press, **batting power**) · clicking/popping · instability less common

**Aggravated by** — posterior force: **pushing heavy doors, bench press, push-ups, blocking, grappling**

**Throwing pain** — **late cocking** (Orthobullets) · late cocking/early acceleration = **anterior** instability (OB) — read the options

**Exam** — posterior joint-line tenderness; **full ROM**; large patients hard to examine

### Diagnosis – jerk/Kim tests & MRI

| Test | Technique | Positive / meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **Posterior apprehension** | **90° flexion + adduction**, stabilise scapula anteriorly, push humerus posteriorly | Pain / instability |
| **Posterior load and shift** | Arm at side; posterior force on proximal humerus | Graded translation (below) |
| **Jerk (Jahnke) test** | **90° abduction + full IR** (arm flexed, IR), **axial load while adducting across body** (push elbow posteriorly, scapula anteriorly) | **Clunk** = head subluxes off posterior glenoid → **posterior labral tear** |
| **Kim test** | **90° abduction + 45° forward flexion**, axial load on elbow, **posteroinferior** force | **Pain** → **posteroinferior labral tear** (most sensitive) |
| Push-pull test | (OB) | Pain from posterior labrum |

**Jerk/Kim accuracy** — **highly sensitive + specific** (Orthobullets) · sensitivity/specificity **"under question"** (OB) — read the options

![Kim test: abduction with forward flexion, axial + posteroinferior force](../../MCQ/attachments/q213968-illa-kim-test.jpg)

| Load-and-shift grade | Translation |
|---|---|
| 0 | None |
| 1 | To **edge** of glenoid |
| 2 | **Over edge**, **spontaneously relocates** |
| 3 | Over edge, **does not relocate** |

| ✗ Exam distractor | Indicates |
|---|---|
| Forward elevation in scapular plane | **Impingement** |
| **ER + abduction** | **Anterior apprehension** |
| Flexion + abduction | Nonspecific |
| O'Brien active compression | SLAP / labrum |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **true AP + scapular Y + axillary**; **axillary required** (posterior dislocation missed on AP alone); often **normal**

**Chronic X-ray (axillary)** — **glenoid retroversion** · **posterior glenoid erosion / bone loss**

**MRI** — **study of choice**; **intra-articular contrast (MR arthrogram)** ↑ sensitivity · alternative **CT arthrogram**

**MR arthrography for labrum** — **sensitivity 89%, specificity 91%, accuracy 90%** (Bencardino); standard MRI often misses labral lesions → **next step after failed nonop + labral test**\
✗ Wrong options: diagnostic arthroscopy · CT · stress views

**SLAP on MRA** — coronal oblique: deep cleft between superior labrum + glenoid extending below biceps anchor

**Incidental** — posterior labral tears common on MRI of **asymptomatic throwers** → treat only if history + exam fit

![Axial MR arthrogram: posterior labral tear](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3057-posterior-labral-tear.jpg)

| ✗ Diagnosis distractor (bodybuilder, MRI arthrogram) | |
|---|---|
| Pectoralis major rupture · supraspinatus PT tear · SLAP · LHB tendinitis | Not on MRI |

## 5 Management

```
Posterior labral tear
├─ First line → activity mod + NSAIDs + PT
│      (cuff + deltoid strengthening, periscapular stabilisation)
└─ Failed extensive nonop / competitive athlete → arthroscopic repair + capsulorrhaphy
       ├─ posterior glenoid bone loss → address bone (augmentation)
       └─ excess retroversion → ± corrective osteotomy
```

### Treatment – therapy vs arthroscopic repair, recurrence & postoperative restrictions

**Nonop** — **first line**; cuff + deltoid strengthening, **periscapular stabilisation**

**Thrower with anterior follow-through pain, end-of-game, full ROM, small posterior labral contrast** — **rotator cuff deconditioning** → **PT with throwing program**\
✗ Wrong options: labral repair · debridement · capsular release · IR stretching (ROM is full)

**Operative indication** — **failed extensive nonop** (or competitive athletes)

**Arthroscopic > open** — avoids extensive posterior dissection; **more reliable return to play**

**Suture anchors + capsulorrhaphy** — **fewer recurrences + revisions** than non-anchored repair

**Probe posterior labrum** — to rule out subtle **Kim lesion**

**Post-op** — **avoid adduction + internal rotation** (stress posterior repair) → failure risk ↑ if not avoided

| Post-op restriction | Repair |
|---|---|
| **Avoid adduction + IR** | **Posterior labrum** |
| Avoid ER | **Subscapularis**, **anterior labrum** |
| No restriction (stiffness risk) | Calcific tendinitis decompression, distal clavicle resection |

**Outcome** — generally good; **overhead throwers** return less reproducibly

### Posterior glenoid bone loss

| Posterior glenoid bone loss | Failure of isolated capsulolabral repair |
|---|---|
| **11%** | **10.4×** |
| **15%** | **24.4×** |

**"Sub-critical" bone loss** — even small posterior loss → **recurrence after isolated arthroscopic repair**\
✗ Wrong options: "not critical, no bony procedure"

**Glenoid width vs version** — **width more predictive** of recurrence than version

**Obligate translation** — tightening posterior tissues → anterior translation = **therapeutic**\
✗ Wrong options: causes anterior instability

**Corrective osteotomy** — for excessive retroversion; **does not obviate bone block** when bone loss present

## 6 Complications

### Complications – axillary nerve injury

**Axillary nerve (posterior branch)** — within **1 mm of inferior capsule + glenoid rim** → injured by **suture passage at posteroinferior glenoid**

**Posterior branch gives** — **superolateral brachial cutaneous** nerve + **nerve to teres minor** → **lateral arm burning/numbness** + **teres minor ER weakness**

**Overtightening posterior capsule** — **anterior subluxation** or **coracoid impingement**

**Recurrence** — ↑ if **posterior bone loss** not addressed

## X vs Y

| | **Posterior labral tear (reverse Bankart)** | **Anterior labral tear (Bankart)** |
|---|---|---|
| Position | **Flexion + adduction + IR**, posterior force | **Abduction + ER**, anterior force |
| Who | Linemen, lifters, wrestlers, batters | — |
| Painful activity | **Bench press, push-ups, blocking** | Late cocking, spiking, overhead serve |
| Test | **Jerk, Kim, load-and-shift** | Apprehension, relocation |
| Ligament | **PIGHL** (+ SGHL) | **AIGHL** |
| Post-op avoid | **Adduction + IR** | **ER** |

| | **Jerk test** | **Kim test** |
|---|---|---|
| Position | 90° abduction + IR, adduct across body with axial load | 90° abduction + 45° flexion, axial + posteroinferior force |
| Positive | **Clunk** | **Pain** |
| Best for | **Posterior** labral tear | **Posteroinferior** labral tear |

## How it's asked

**"Bodybuilder, vague deep pain, weak bench press, MR arthrogram — etiology?"** → **posterior labral tear**\
✗ Wrong options: pec major · SLAP · LHB tendinitis

**"Old 'pop', never reduced, axial MRI posterior labrum — painful activity?"** → **heavy bench press**\
✗ Wrong options: late cocking · volleyball spike = anterior · curl = SLAP

**"Wrestler, + Kim + jerk — structure augmenting PIGHL in flexion-adduction-IR?"** → **superior glenohumeral ligament**\
✗ Wrong options: MGHL · AIGHL · subscapularis

**"Wrestler, posterior labral tear MRI — position increasing incidence?"** → **flexion, ADduction, internal rotation**

**"Baseball player, ↓ batting power, + Kim/jerk — etiology?"** → **acute posterior subluxation while batting**\
✗ Wrong options: posterior capsular tightening = GIRD

**"Ex-pitcher, failed nonop, + O'Brien — next diagnostic step?"** → **MRI-arthrography**\
✗ Wrong options: diagnostic arthroscopy · CT

**"Lineman, pain with blocking, axial MRI — manoeuvre reproducing pain?"** → **flexion, adduction, internal rotation**\
✗ Wrong options: ER + abduction = apprehension

**"Flexed IR arm pushed posteriorly, girdle anteriorly, clunk — injured structure?"** → **posterior labrum (C)** (jerk test)

**"Quarterback, anterior pain in follow-through, end of game, small posterior labral contrast — initial?"** → **PT with throwing program**

**"Post-op: avoid adduction + IR — initial diagnosis?"** → **posterior labral tear (Figure C)**

**"NFL tackle, posterior glenoid bone loss on CT — true?"** → **capsulolabral repair alone may lead to recurrence**\
✗ Wrong options: obligate anterior instability · version more predictive than width · osteotomy obviates bone block

**"Burning lateral arm pain after arthroscopic posterior labral repair — nerve?"** → **axillary**\
✗ Wrong options: suprascapular · radial

# Posterior Shoulder Instability & Dislocation

*Shoulder & Elbow · 36 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **2–5%** of unstable shoulders (Orthobullets) · **2–12%** of instability (OB) — read the options

**Missed** — **50%** of traumatic posterior dislocations in ED undiagnosed · Hawkins & Neer: 41 locked dislocations, **50% with fracture**, 20 missed, mean **1 y** to diagnosis

**Prior dislocation** — up to **50%** of patients evaluated for posterior instability report a prior posterior dislocation · frank dislocation in only **2–5%** of posterior labral tears

**Risk factors** — **ligamentous laxity** · **glenoid retroversion** / hypoplasia (less common) · not excessive glenoid **anteversion** = anterior instability

**Seizures + electric shock** — tetanic contraction pulls head out; posterior dislocation unlikely without such force · anterior **still more common** with seizures (Orthobullets, OB) · posterior "more common after seizure" / **as common as anterior in epilepsy** (OB) — read the options

**Atraumatic (esp. posterior) dislocation** — warrants **neurological workup**

### Anatomy – posterior restraints

| Restraint | Role |
|---|---|
| **Posterior band IGHL** | **Primary restraint in IR**; most important at **90° flexion + IR** |
| **SGHL + CHL** | Primary **static** stabiliser to posterior subluxation in **flexion, adduction, IR** · also inferior translation of adducted arm + ER |
| **Subscapularis** | **Primary dynamic restraint against posterior subluxation** (and in ER) |
| Labrum | Deepens glenoid **50%** |

**Restraint conflict** — posterior band IGHL (OB, Orthobullets) vs SGHL (OB, Orthobullets) for flexion + IR — read the position in the stem

| ✗ Restraint distractor | Role |
|---|---|
| Anterior band IGHL | Anterior/inferior at **90° abduction + max ER** (late cocking) |
| MGHL | Anterior + posterior at **~45° abduction** in ER |
| Anterior labrum | Anterior; IGHL anchors here → Bankart |
| Superior labrum | **Biceps anchor** |

**Dynamic stabilisers by direction** — posterior labral tear → **subscapularis** · anterior labral tear / HAGL → **infraspinatus + teres minor**

### Tubercle of Assaki (TO)

**Tubercle of Assaki (TO)** — ridge / **focal elevation of subchondral bone at centre of glenoid** · **normal variant** · **focal thinning of overlying cartilage** · not a cartilage defect \
✗ Wrong options: "depression of humeral head cartilage"

## 2 Mechanism

**High-risk position** — arm **flexed, adducted, internally rotated** + **axial load**\
✗ Wrong options: abduction + ER = anterior · hyperabduction = **luxatio erecta**

| Type | Mechanism | Presentation |
|---|---|---|
| **Acute (dislocation)** | Trauma (~50%), **seizure, electric shock** | Dramatic |
| **Chronic (instability)** | **Repetitive microtrauma** → labral tear, incomplete avulsion, erosion, **patulous posterior capsule** | Insidious, vague **pain > instability** |

**At-risk athletes** — **football linemen** (blocking), linebackers, **weightlifters/bench press**, push-ups, weighted dips, rugby, swimmers, overhead athletes · stretches posteroinferior GHL

**Classification** — acute vs chronic · voluntary vs involuntary

| Associated lesion | Association |
|---|---|
| Posterior band IGHL avulsion | Acute subluxation |
| **Reverse Bankart** | Detached posteroinferior capsulolabral complex |
| **Reverse Hill-Sachs** | **Locked**, hard-to-reduce dislocation |
| Posterior labral cyst / posterior glenoid rim fracture | Chronic reverse Bankart |
| **Lesser tuberosity fracture** | Acute posterior dislocation |
| Large capsular pouch | Chronic instability (MR arthrogram) |

**Reverse Hill-Sachs (TO)** — **anteromedial** humeral head · Hill-Sachs = **posterosuperolateral**\
✗ Wrong options: posterolateral

**Cuff tears** — **not** commonly associated with posterior dislocation

## 3 Presentation

**Acute dislocation** — **prominent posterior shoulder + coracoid** · **locked in IR + adduction** · comfortable in "sling position"

**Most consistent sign** — **lack of ER** (mechanical block: anterior head defect on posterior glenoid) · ER loss differential: adhesive capsulitis, GH OA

**Chronic instability** — may look normal · pain with **flexion, adduction, IR** · slipping when pass-blocking, pain bench pressing/push-ups

### Recurrent posterior instability – Kim lesion/labral tear, jerk & Kim tests, restraints

**Kim lesion (TO)** — **incomplete + concealed avulsion of posteroinferior labrum** (superficial labrum intact, deep labrum detached) in posterior or multidirectional posteroinferior instability · usually **diagnosed at arthroscopy** · needs **arthroscopic repair**\
✗ Wrong options: ALPSA = anterior labral periosteal sleeve avulsion · meniscal root avulsion

**Posterior labral tear site** — **posteroinferior quadrant**: **6–9 o'clock right**, **3–6 o'clock left**

| Test | Technique | Positive / use |
|---|---|---|
| **Jerk** | 90° abduction/flexion + IR, elbow bent · **axial load** along humerus → adduct into forward flexion (bring back to extension → reduces) | **Clunk** ± pain · posterior labral tear in **mid-range** glenoid |
| **Kim** | Seated, 90° abduction → **flex to 45°** + axial load at elbow + posteroinferior force (Orthobullets) · arm elevated **125°** + IR, downward + posterior force (OB) | **Pain** (± clunk) · more sensitive for **posteroinferior** tears |
| **Jerk + Kim** | Combined | **97% sensitive** for posterior labral tear |
| Posterior stress | Stabilise scapula, push posteriorly | Pain common, **not specific** |
| **Load & shift** | Supine, neutral rotation, 40–60° abduction + flexion | Grade translation |

![Kim test](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3051-kim-test-moved.jpg)

| Load & shift grade | Translation |
|---|---|
| 1+ | Apparent, not to rim |
| 2+ | **To glenolabral rim** |
| 3+ | **Over** rim |
| 4+ | Complete dislocation |

| ✗ Test distractor | Assesses |
|---|---|
| Apprehension / relocation (abd + ER) | **Anterior** instability, Bankart |
| Hornblower; ER weakness at 90° abduction | **Teres minor** (axillary n.) |
| Jobe | Supraspinatus |
| Speed / Yergason | Biceps |
| O'Brien | SLAP |
| Belly-press, lift-off, IR lag | Subscapularis |
| Sulcus | Rotator interval |
| Pinch grip | AIN |

| ✗ Lesion distractor | Feature |
|---|---|
| Bankart / Hill-Sachs | Anterior instability, apprehension |
| **GIRD** | Throwers, **tight posteroinferior capsule**, ↓ IR → posterior impingement, **anterior subluxation** |
| AC separation | Piano key sign |
| SLAP | Overhead pain, dead arm |
| PASTA | Partial articular supraspinatus avulsion |
| Pec major tear | ↓ adduction + IR, lost axillary fold |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Locked posterior dislocation – diagnosis (axillary/Velpeau view, loss of ER, seizures, electrocution)

**AP** — **unreliable** · **"lightbulb" sign** (head internally rotated) · humeral head **overlaps glenoid**

![Lightbulb sign on AP](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3051-light-bulb-sign.jpg)

**Axillary lateral** — **best view**; mandatory (needs **20–30° abduction**) · **more sensitive than trans-scapular** · any suspected dislocation → full trauma series (AP, scapular Y, axillary)

**Can't abduct (pain/spasm)** — **Velpeau view**\
✗ Wrong options: Zanca = AC/clavicle · Stryker notch = Hill-Sachs · MRI not first-line in trauma · EUA is for locked dislocation

**Scapular Y** — humeral articular surface **posterior** to glenoid

**CT** — chronic dislocation (**>2–3 wk**): size of **reverse Hill-Sachs** + bone loss · posterior glenoid bone loss (% diameter)

**MRI / MR arthrogram** — chronic instability without dislocation · posterior labral tear, reverse Hill-Sachs, cuff · **Kim lesion**: incomplete avulsion/cyst with intact chondrolabral junction · **posteroinferior capsulolabral separation**

![MR arthrogram: posterior labral tear](../../MCQ/attachments/q216832-b-kim-lesion.jpg)

**Next step pattern** — any "light bulb" / overlapping head / ER to neutral after seizure or fall → **axillary view**\
✗ Wrong options: PT for frozen shoulder · sling · cuff repair · EMG · injection

**Glenoid hypoplasia** — **posteroinferior glenoid deficiency**, enlarged distal clavicle, glenoid indentation · **usually bilateral** → X-ray other side · rarely syndromic · **PT first** · glenoid osteotomy variable · early OA\
✗ Wrong options: echo / abdominal US / skeletal survey unless Holt-Oram, Apert stigmata

## 5 Management

```
Posterior shoulder instability
├─ Acute traumatic dislocation → reduce, immobilise ER 4–6 wk, then PT
├─ Chronic instability → PT (cuff + periscapular)
│      └─ fails, pain with loading → posterior labral repair
├─ Chronic locked dislocation → by duration + reverse Hill-Sachs size
└─ Uncontrolled seizures → neurology control first
```

### Chronic locked dislocation – reverse Hill-Sachs management (McLaughlin, arthroplasty)

| Source | Closed reduction | McLaughlin / lesser tuberosity transfer | Hemiarthroplasty |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Orthobullets** | All acute (most reduce spontaneously) · disimpaction + graft if **<3 wk** | Chronic **<6 mo** + RHS **<40%** | **>6 mo**, RHS **>40%**, severe head arthritis, **head collapse on reduction** |
| **Hawkins & Neer (OB)** | **<6 wk + <20%** | **6 wk–6 mo + 20–45%** | **>6 mo or >45%** (glenoid normal) |
| **OB (other)** | **<3 wk + <25%** under GA; stable if IR to abdomen → 6 wk orthosis in slight extension + ER | Defect **<~40%** | Larger; **>50%** or several months |

**TSA** — significant **glenoid arthritis** + an HA indication · glenoid intact → no glenoid component

**Closed reduction after ~3 wk** — exceedingly difficult · may **displace unrecognised surgical neck fracture**

**McLaughlin** — **subscapularis** into anteromedial defect · **modified (Neer/Hawkins)** = **lesser tuberosity + subscapularis** (used by most) · deltopectoral

**Modified McLaughlin candidate** — young man, **locked posterior dislocation after electric shock / seizure**\
✗ Wrong options: AVN · RA = arthroplasty · subscap insufficiency = repair or pec transfer · anterior Hill-Sachs = graft/remplissage

**6-wk locked dislocation, 38 y** — **open reduction ± lesser tuberosity transfer** (CT first)\
✗ Wrong options: PT · closed manipulation · cuff repair · HA

**12-wk locked post-seizure, large RHS + lesser tuberosity fracture, 60 y** — **hemiarthroplasty + subscapularis repair** · younger → **osteochondral allograft** + subscap repair

**6 mo after motorcycle fall, 52 y** — **hemiarthroplasty** most reliable\
✗ Wrong options: PT · MUA · capsular release/plication

**HA version** — **anatomic version (20–30° retroversion)** · not excessive anteversion (re-dislocation, per source)

**7 mo locked, 63F, light bulb** — first **further radiographs (axillary)** → then HA

**Open complications** — stiffness · **AVN** · OA

![CT: locked posterior dislocation with anteromedial reverse Hill-Sachs](../../MCQ/attachments/q3587-illa-locked-post-dislocation.jpg)

**Uncontrolled epilepsy, 9th dislocation, humeral + glenoid bone loss** — **neurology + social work to control seizures first** · later: **posterior bone block** (iliac crest autograft or **distal tibial allograft**) ± modified McLaughlin / head grafting · HA ± glenoid reconstruction for chronic locked\
✗ Wrong options: urgent HA · reconstruction before control · anterior procedure alone

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Posterior labral repair** (open/arthroscopic) | **Recurrent instability despite PT** · pain loading arm in forward flexion (bench, blocking) · **negative Beighton** |
| **Capsular shift + rotator interval closure** | **Positive Beighton** (laxity) · shift less desirable in throwers · interval closure controversial |
| **Posterior bone block** | Posterior glenoid bone loss **≥11%** in contact athlete (**subcritical** by anterior standards) |
| **Opening-wedge glenoid osteotomy** | Excessive **congenital retroversion** (limited data) |
| Modified McLaughlin | Chronic locked, RHS <40%, <6 mo |
| HA / TSA | RHS >40%, >6 mo, collapse; TSA if glenoid arthritis |

### Treatment – therapy vs arthroscopic capsulolabral repair & postoperative protocol

**Acute reduction** — immobilise **10–20° ER, elbow at side, 4–6 wk** → PT (cuff + periscapular) + avoid high-risk position

**After reduction, avoid** — **cross-body adduction at 90° flexion + IR** (tensions SGHL + posterior IGHL)

**Chronic instability first line** — **PT** (cuff + periscapular; in-season athlete) · esp. **generalised laxity** · ± brief immobilisation, steroid injection

**Failed rehab, positive jerk, grade 2 load-shift, lineman** — **arthroscopic posterior capsulolabral repair** (arthroscopic ≈ open)\
✗ Wrong options: thermal · open anterior capsulorrhaphy · injection · ER immobilisation for recurrent

**Lineman, clunk, bench-press pain** — **posterior labral tear → arthroscopic labral repair**\
✗ Wrong options: thermal capsulorrhaphy · SLAP debridement/tenodesis

**Thermal capsulorrhaphy** — **contraindicated** · breaks collagen cross-links at **65–75 °C** · capsular necrosis, **chondral damage**, axillary nerve injury, recurrence

**Technique** — **lateral decubitus** · **high lateral / posterosuperior portal** for anchor trajectory

**Results** — open **80–85% success at 5–7 y** · arthroscopic similar (shorter follow-up)

**Post-op** — sling in **neutral rotation** (ER sling) or standard, up to 6 wk · **avoid flexion, adduction, IR** · full contact/heavy labour **6 mo**\
✗ Wrong options: no elbow flexion = biceps tenodesis · avoid abd + ER = anterior/HAGL repair · PROM ≥6 wk = cuff repair · unrestricted ROM

**Posterior glenoid bone loss** — **lower critical value than anterior**; **11%** of glenoid diameter ↑ recurrence after isolated labral repair → **bone block in contact athletes**\
✗ Wrong options: modified McLaughlin = chronic dislocation · only with large RHS · isolated repair better · augmentation doesn't change recurrence

**First-time traumatic dislocation, young athlete with recurrent subluxations** — **arthroscopic capsulolabral repair reasonable** · recurrence up to **90%** in young active · immobilisation (IR or ER) may not reduce recurrence · arthroscopic ≈ open with modern anchors if no glenoid loss/engaging lesion

## 6 Complications

| Rank | Complication after labral repair |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Stiffness** (↓ **IR**) |
| **2** | **Recurrence** (7–50%) · ↑ with glenoid bone loss ≥11%, uncontrolled seizures |
| **3** | **DJD** |
| Other | Adhesive capsulitis · **overtightening → anterior subluxation / coracoid impingement** · **axillary / suprascapular** nerve |

**Not after posterior repair** — anterior instability not expected · acromial stress fracture not typical

## X vs Y

| | **Posterior instability** | **Anterior instability** |
|---|---|---|
| Position | **Flexion, adduction, IR** | Abduction + ER |
| Symptom | **Pain** > instability | Instability, apprehension |
| Labral lesion | **Reverse Bankart / Kim** (posteroinferior) | Bankart, ALPSA |
| Head lesion | **Reverse Hill-Sachs (anteromedial)** | Hill-Sachs (posterosuperolateral) |
| Tests | **Jerk, Kim**, load & shift | Apprehension, relocation |
| Dynamic stabiliser | **Subscapularis** | Infraspinatus + teres minor |
| Post-op avoid | **Flexion, adduction, IR** | Abduction + ER |
| Critical glenoid bone loss | **Lower (~11%)** | Higher |

| | **Jerk test** | **Kim test** |
|---|---|---|
| Positive | **Clunk** | **Pain** |
| Best for | Posterior (mid-range) | **Posteroinferior** |

## How it's asked

**"63F, 7 mo after fall, ↓ ER, light-bulb AP — next?"** → **further radiographs (axillary view)**\
✗ Wrong options: PT for frozen shoulder · arthroplasty first · ORIF

**"Seizure 1 wk ago, ER to neutral, sling — next?"** → **axillary lateral radiograph**

**"Posterior dislocations as common as anterior in?"** → **epilepsy patients**

**"Drunk, woke with pain, 'cuff tear' sling 6 wk — next?"** → **axillary radiograph**

**"Acute posterior dislocation — shoulder locked in?"** → **internal rotation**

**"Electrician, no axillary possible due to spasm?"** → **Velpeau view**\
✗ Wrong options: EUA · MRI · Zanca · Stryker notch

**"Exam finding of fixed posterior dislocation?"** → **lack of external rotation**\
✗ Wrong options: apprehension · relocation · sulcus · jerk

**"ICU head injury, ER −30°, overlap on AP?"** → **posterior shoulder dislocation**

**"Light-bulb sign — predisposing factor?"** → **seizures**\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ IR = GIRD · inflammatory · anteversion · anterior IGHL injury

**"Epileptic teen, 9th dislocation, can't afford levetiracetam, bone loss on CT?"** → **social work + neurology to control seizures**

**"Reverse Hill-Sachs location?"** → **anteromedial** humeral head (TO)

**"NOT true about tubercle of Assaki?"** → **depression of humeral head cartilage** (TO)

**"Who benefits from modified McLaughlin?"** → **locked posterior dislocation after electric shock**

**"52 y, locked posterior dislocation 6 mo — most reliable?"** → **hemiarthroplasty**

**"Seizure 12 wk ago, no ER, large RHS + lesser tuberosity fracture?"** → **HA + subscapularis repair**\
✗ Wrong options: sling · closed reduction · labral repair · LT transfer

**"38 y, posterior dislocation 6 wk old?"** → **open reduction ± lesser tuberosity transfer**

**"61 y, seizure 5 mo, half head lost?"** → **HA in anatomic version**\
✗ Wrong options: anteversion · TSA · closed reduction · LT transfer

**"After surgery for posterior dislocation, most common complication besides stiffness?"** → **instability (recurrence)**

**"Kim's lesion is?"** → **incomplete, concealed avulsion of posteroinferior labrum** (TO)

**"Lineman, posterior pain after block in flexion + IR?"** → **posterior labral tear**\
✗ Wrong options: Bankart · SLAP · HAGL · AC

**"Lineman subluxes blocking, positive jerk — pathology?"** → **Kim lesion (reverse Bankart)**\
✗ Wrong options: Hill-Sachs · GIRD

**"Positive jerk + Kim test?"** → **posterior labral tear**\
✗ Wrong options: SLAP · ALPSA · PASTA

**"After posterior dislocation, 'arm will pop out' — positive test?"** → **Kim test**\
✗ Wrong options: Hornblower · Jobe · apprehension · Speed

**"Arthroscopy shows posteroinferior capsulolabral tear — preop exam?"** → **painful clunk with arm elevated 125° + IR, downward/posterior force (Kim)**

**"Basketball player, posterior labral tear on MR arthrogram — test?"** → **jerk test**\
✗ Wrong options: O'Brien · Speed · Neer · apprehension

**"Lineman slipping when pass blocking, no apprehension?"** → **posterior labral tear**

**"Primary restraint to posterior displacement in flexion + IR?"** → **posterior band IGHL**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior band · MGHL · anterior/superior labrum

**"Football player, axial load flexed + IR = pain + clunk?"** → **jerk test**\
✗ Wrong options: Hornblower · Speed · apprehension · O'Brien

**"Powerlifter, pain bench + push-up, normal exam — MRI?"** → **posteroinferior capsulolabral separation**

**"Subscapularis is critical stabiliser in which lesion?"** → **posterior labral tear (C)**\
✗ Wrong options: SLAP · HAGL · spinoglenoid cyst · Bankart

**"Lineman, Kim test pain — matching MRI?"** → **posterior labral tear (B)**

**"Mechanism of posterior labral tear + reverse Hill-Sachs?"** → **axial load on flexed, adducted, IR shoulder**

**"Quarterback after reduction of posterior dislocation — avoid?"** → **cross-body adduction at 90° flexion + IR**

**"Swimmer, clicking, glenoid hypoplasia on X-ray — next?"** → **physical therapy**\
✗ Wrong options: echo · abdominal US · skeletal survey · osteotomy

**"Lineman, failed rehab, positive jerk, grade 2 load-shift — best?"** → **arthroscopic posterior capsulolabral repair**

**"16 y, first dislocation + 2 subluxations — true?"** → **arthroscopic capsulolabral repair is reasonable**

**"Lineman, bench pain, clunk — MRI + surgery?"** → **posterior labral tear; arthroscopic labral repair**

**"Pitcher, positive Kim, labral repair — likely complication?"** → **decreased internal rotation (stiffness)**\
✗ Wrong options: posterior instability = 2nd · DJD = 3rd

**"Post-op protocol after posterior labral repair?"** → **avoid flexion, adduction, IR**

**"Rugby player, posterior tear + 11% glenoid loss, bone block — true?"** → **subcritical bone loss is an indication in contact athletes**

# Quadrilateral Space Syndrome

*Shoulder & Elbow · 37 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — compression of **axillary nerve + posterior humeral circumflex artery (PHCA)** in the quadrilateral (quadrangular) space → **posterolateral shoulder pain**

**Incidence** — **rare**; often **misdiagnosed as subacromial impingement**

**Age** — **20–40 y** (Orthobullets) · **20–35 y** (TO) — young adults

**Side** — **dominant shoulder** most commonly (TO)

**Risk factors** — **overhead athletes** (basketball, baseball pitchers), contact/throwing sports, **weightlifting** (muscle hypertrophy)

### Anatomy – quadrilateral space

| Boundary | Structure |
|---|---|
| **Superior** | **Teres minor** (inferior border) |
| **Inferior** | **Teres major** (superior border) |
| **Medial** | **Long head of triceps** (lateral border) |
| **Lateral** | **Surgical neck of humerus** (medial cortex) |
| **Contents** | **Axillary nerve** (C5 root, **posterior cord**) + **PHCA** (+ vein, TO) |

**Location** — posterior compartment of arm; **lateral to triangular space**, **medial to triangular interval** (with lateral + medial triangular spaces, TO)

![Quadrangular space, triangular space and triangular interval](../../MCQ/attachments/q519-illb-shoulder-spaces.jpg)

**Axillary nerve supplies** — **deltoid + teres minor** + sensation **lateral arm** (upper lateral brachial cutaneous)

**Long head of triceps** — **can have axillary innervation** (case report, OB)

**Teres minor motor nerve** — 2 variants of fascial envelopment; nerve takes **highly angulated course at the fascial sling** → **stout fascial sling** may compress it (OB)

## 2 Mechanism

### Etiology – causes of compression

| Cause | Note |
|---|---|
| **Fibrous bands** | **Oblique fibrous bands = most common cause** (TO); compress nerve + artery in abduction/ER |
| **Muscular hypertrophy** | Weightlifting after a break (OB) |
| **Paralabral cyst** | **Most commonly inferior labral tears** (Orthobullets, TO) · **posterior labrum** ("occasionally", TO) — read the options |
| Trauma | Scapular fracture, shoulder dislocation |
| Masses | Benign or malignant |

**Position of max compression** — **late cocking phase** of throwing = **abduction + external rotation** (TO)

**Throwers** — repetitive motion irritates nerve → dysfunction; usually **self-limited** (OB)

## 3 Presentation

### Symptoms

**Pain** — **poorly localised posterior/lateral shoulder pain**; **worse at night**, overhead activity, late cocking/acceleration

**Paraesthesia** — **non-dermatomal**, lateral shoulder + **lateral arm**

**Weakness** — **external rotation** weakness, esp. **arm abducted 90° (throwing position)**; **5/5 ER with arm at side** (OB)

**Aggravated by** — active + resisted **abduction + ER**; forward flexion (OB)

### Examination

**Palpation** — **point tenderness over quadrilateral space** (near teres minor insertion, TO)

**Atrophy** — **teres minor + deltoid** (✓ **deltoid atrophy**, TO)\
✗ Wrong options: supraspinatus atrophy · positive apprehension · positive dynamic labral shear

**Provocation (TO)** — **arm abducted + externally rotated for 1 min** reproduces symptoms\
✗ Wrong options: abducted + internally rotated

**Detecting weakness (TO)** — weakness/atrophy **difficult to detect unless significant deltoid involvement**

**Neuro exam** — usually **normal**; mild sensory change in axillary distribution

**Typical negatives (OB)** — full ROM, negative Hawkins/Neer, belly press/lift-off, O'Brien, normal radiographs, no cuff/labral tear on MRA

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnosis – axillary nerve compression & teres minor atrophy

**Diagnosis** — **clinical** (point tenderness ± teres minor atrophy); MRI may show compression

### Key radiology findings

| Test | Finding |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** (AP, lateral, axillary) | **Usually normal**; rules out other pathology |
| **MRI** | **Fatty atrophy of teres minor** (✓ OB; T1 streaked white + atrophied) · deltoid fatty infiltration · compression of space · **inferior paralabral cyst** with labral tear |
| **Arteriogram** | **PHCA occlusion in abduction + ER, normal with arm at side** (TO) |
| **EMG** | **Confirms diagnosis**; axillary nerve involvement |
| **Lidocaine block** | Confirms diagnosis (see Management) |

**Selective teres minor atrophy on MRI** — **highly suggestive** of QSS (TO)

**Sanders** — teres minor fatty atrophy in **3% of shoulder MRIs** (OB)

**Next step to confirm (OB)** — **subclavian arteriogram** with arm **adducted + abducted/ER**

**MRI ✗ distractors** — ↓ intra-capsular volume (= **adhesive capsulitis** on arthrogram) · ↑ capsular volume · infraspinatus atrophy (suprascapular) · latissimus atrophy

**Isolated teres minor atrophy (OB)** — **common**, unclear aetiology (axillary nerve variants); **clinically distinct** from QSS

![Fatty atrophy of teres minor on sagittal MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3066-fatty-atrophy.jpg)

### Brown classification (TO)

| Type | Cause | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **Vascular QSS** | **Repetitive trauma to PHCA** in abduction + ER | **Professional/collegiate overhead athletes** |
| **Neurogenic QSS** | **Fixed anatomic anomalies** — **fibrous bands most common** | |

## 5 Management

```
Quadrilateral space syndrome
├─ First line → NSAIDs + activity modification + PT
│      (most improve in 3–6 months)
├─ Diagnosis unclear → lidocaine block into space
└─ Fails 3–6 mo / significant weakness / space-occupying lesion → open decompression
       └─ labral tear + paralabral cyst → ± arthroscopic labral repair
```

### Nonoperative

**Regimen** — NSAIDs, activity restriction, physiotherapy (GH mobilisation + strengthening, **posterior capsule stretching**, massage)

**TO regimen** — activity modification, NSAIDs, **cortisone injections**, PT for **rotator cuff strengthening** — indicated initially for **all** patients

**Outcome** — **most improve with 3–6 months** nonoperative

### Diagnostic lidocaine block

**Technique** — plain lidocaine into quadrilateral space; start **2–3 cm inferior to standard posterior arthroscopy portal**

**Positive** — **no point tenderness + pain-free full ROM** after injection

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Failed nonop (3–6 mo)** | Open release of quadrilateral space |
| **Significant weakness + functional disability** | Open release |
| **Space-occupying lesion** (paralabral cyst) | Decompression **± arthroscopic labral repair** |
| **Isolated teres minor atrophy** | **Release of fascial sling** to teres minor → ↓ pain, **Hornblower's** weakness resolves (OB) |

### Open quadrilateral space decompression

**Approach** — **lateral decubitus**; **posterior approach**, 3–4 cm incision over space; reflect posterior border of deltoid superolaterally; fat between teres minor + major

**Technique** — find axillary nerve using **humeral neck** as reference · **avoid cutting PHCA** · free fibrous bands (**lysis of fibrous bands**) · confirm release in **abduction + ER**

**Post-op** — sling for comfort · **early pendulums** (avoid new adhesions) · supervised full active ROM

**Results** — good results in overhead athletes after failed nonop (McAdams, 4 athletes, OB); usually symptom relief (TO)

## 6 Complications

**Prognosis** — long-standing cases → **atrophy/weakness of teres minor + deltoid**

## X vs Y

| Lesion | Atrophy | Weakness |
|---|---|---|
| **QSS (axillary n.)** | **Teres minor** ± deltoid | **ER with arm abducted 90°** |
| **Suprascapular notch** | **Supraspinatus + infraspinatus** | Abduction + ER **at side** |
| **Spinoglenoid notch** (cyst) | **Infraspinatus only** | **Isolated ER at side** |
| Chronic cuff tear | Infraspinatus (retracted tear on MRI) | ER |
| Subscapularis | Rare (**upper + lower subscapular** nerves) | IR |

**QSS vs subacromial impingement** — QSS often **misdiagnosed as impingement**; in QSS **Hawkins + Neer negative**, MRI shows **teres minor atrophy**

**✗ Wrong options (other diagnoses)** — Parsonage-Turner · long thoracic neuritis · internal impingement · external impingement · scapular dyskinesia

## How it's asked

**"Posterior shoulder pain, worse in abduction + ER, paralabral cyst in quadrilateral space — exam finding?"** → **deltoid atrophy** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: supraspinatus atrophy · apprehension · dynamic labral shear

**"True about quadrilateral space syndrome?"** → **most common cause is oblique fibrous band** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: anterior pain · abducted + internally rotated · middle-aged diabetic female

**"MRI in chronic QSS?"** → **fatty atrophy of teres minor**\
✗ Wrong options: infraspinatus · latissimus · loss of capsular volume

**"Pitcher, pain + lateral arm paraesthesia, T1 fatty teres minor + deltoid — diagnosis?"** → **quadrilateral space syndrome**\
✗ Wrong options: Parsonage-Turner · long thoracic neuritis · internal/external impingement

**"EMG shows QSS — muscle affected along with deltoid?"** → **teres minor**\
✗ Wrong options: teres major · supraspinatus · subscapularis

**"Weightlifter, weakness of resisted ER at 90° abduction only, isolated teres minor atrophy?"** → **QSS**\
✗ Wrong options: suprascapular notch · spinoglenoid notch · scapular dyskinesia

**"Next step to confirm QSS?"** → **subclavian arteriogram, arm adducted + abducted/ER**

**"Pitcher, weak ER at 90° abduction — muscle and aetiology?"** → **teres minor; axillary nerve at quadrilateral space**

**"Boundaries of quadrilateral space?"** → **teres minor above, teres major below, long head triceps medial, surgical neck humerus lateral**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Reverse Shoulder Arthroplasty

*Shoulder & Elbow · 38 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Concept & history

**Design** — **convex glenoid hemisphere (glenosphere)** + **concave humeral cup** (semiconstrained)

**History** — early reverse designs failed (**COR too lateral** → torque → glenoid loosening) · **Grammont 1985** = first successful reverse (TO) · popular in Europe; North America since **1990**

**Grammont's 2 innovations (OB)** — **large glenoid hemisphere with no neck** + **small, almost horizontal humeral cup at 155°** (non-anatomic valgus) covering **< half the glenosphere**

**Indications summary** — **cuff tear arthropathy (CTA)**, comminuted **4-part proximal humerus fractures**, **failed arthroplasty**

## 2 Mechanism

### Biomechanics – medialised/inferior centre of rotation & deltoid tensioning

**COR shift** — **medial + inferior (distal)** (✓ TO, OB)\
✗ Wrong options: superior · lateral · ↑ lateral humeral offset · posterior · proximal

**Standard prosthesis without cuff (TO)** — deltoid contraction → humeral head **subluxes superiorly**; reverse **corrects the abnormal vector** and re-creates a **fulcrum**

**Medial COR** — ↑ **deltoid moment arm** (less force for same torque) · ↓ **torque/shear at glenoid interface** (✓ glenoid stability; force vector passes through component) · ↓ articular shear stress

**Distal COR** — **lengthens/tensions deltoid** (↑ resting length + tone) · humerus lowered relative to acromion · recruits **anterior + posterior deltoid** as abductors

**Grammont advantages (OB)** — **medialisation of COR + lengthening deltoid + ↑ deltoid moment arm**\
✗ Wrong options: lateralisation · shortening deltoid

**Matched radius of curvature** — glenosphere + humeral cup → **stable fixed fulcrum** for elevation; shear converted to **compression**

**Motions gained** — **forward elevation + abduction** ↑ · **IR, ER (at side or at 90°), adduction** not significantly improved → **latissimus dorsi transfer** for ER\
✗ Wrong options: not normal

**↑ deltoid moment (OB)** — **place glenosphere more inferiorly** (✓) · anything moving COR medial/inferior or lengthening deltoid\
✗ Wrong options: constrained poly (no effect) · short stem (no effect) · **smaller NSA (varus) shortens deltoid**

![Grammont biomechanics: medial COR lengthens deltoid lever arm (R > r) and deltoid (L)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3076-biomechanics-of-rtsa.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

**Pseudoparalysis** — **inability to actively elevate arm** with **free passive ROM**, **no neurological lesion**; irreparable cuff tear (± GH arthritis)

**Pseudoparalysis without arthritis (TO)** — loss of fulcrum when cuff fails → head translates superiorly → deltoid loses lever · **first line = rehabilitation** (recruit accessory muscles) → **RSA only after PT fails**

**Anterosuperior escape** — **incompetent coracoacromial arch** → humeral head escapes into subcutaneous tissue (esp. after **HA + acromioplasty**); **anterosuperior bulging** on attempted elevation

**Failed HA (cuff failure)** — pseudoparalysis, lateral deltoid pain, **no crepitus**, near-normal passive ROM · weakness of ER at side\
✗ Wrong options: infection → passive ROM also ↓

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Investigation | Purpose / finding |
|---|---|
| **True AP (Grashey)** | Extent of arthritis, **superior migration of humerus**, acetabularisation of acromion |
| **Axillary lateral** | **Posterior glenoid wear** |
| Scapular-Y | |
| **CT** | **Glenoid version + bone stock** (if axillary inadequate), osteopenia |
| **MRI** | **Cuff integrity + fatty infiltration** |

**Anterosuperior migration on X-ray** — cuff tear + **CA ligament deficiency** (OB)

**Hamada ≥3** — **acetabularisation of acromion**\
✗ Wrong options: not linked to post-op acromial fracture

**RA glenoid** — **central wear, glenoid medialisation**\
✗ Wrong options: posterior wear = primary OA

## 5 Management

```
Massive irreparable cuff tear ± GH arthritis
├─ Balanced shoulder (isolated SST tear + arthritis) → unconstrained TSA
├─ Full active elevation retained → no RSA
├─ Pseudoparalysis, no arthritis → PT first
│      └─ PT fails → RSA
└─ CTA / pseudoparalysis, elderly, working deltoid → RSA
       └─ also no active ER (abducted) → RSA + latissimus dorsi transfer
```

```
Failed hemiarthroplasty / aTSA
├─ Infection signs → 2-stage revision
├─ Cuff failure / anterosuperior escape → convert to RSA
│      (not RCR, aTSA or tendon transfer — poor results)
└─ Intractable pain / persistent infection → resection arthroplasty
```

### Indications – cuff tear arthropathy, pseudoparalysis, failed arthroplasty or cuff repair, fracture

| Indication | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Cuff tear arthropathy** | Severe GH arthritis + **superior escape** + massive cuff tear; **best results** |
| **Pseudoparalysis** | Irreparable cuff tear (± arthritis, TO) |
| **Anterosuperior escape** | Incompetent CA arch |
| **Proximal humerus fracture in elderly** | **3- or 4-part, age >70** · head-splitting · osteopenia (GT poor healing) |
| **Cuff insufficiency "equivalent"** | **Tuberosity nonunion/malunion** after trauma or arthroplasty |
| **Failed arthroplasty** | Prior HA or aTSA with **cuff insufficiency**; options exhausted |
| **Failed cuff repairs** | Irreparable tear after ≥2 repairs |
| **Rheumatoid arthritis** | **Only if glenoid bone stock sufficient** |
| Others (OB) | Proximal humeral tumours, B2 glenoid |

**Patient factors** — **low demand** · **physiological age >70** ("exclusively >70 y", OKU 9) · **sufficient glenoid bone stock** · **working deltoid (intact axillary nerve)**

**Not ideal** — **40-y labourer** (glenoid loosening in young/active) · 40-y painful malunion · arthritis with **active overhead motion** · intact cuff → **aTSA** · 30-y locked posterior dislocation

**Not indications (TO)** — **massive cuff tear + arthritis with full active elevation** · **isolated supraspinatus tear + arthritis** → balanced → **unconstrained TSA**

**Revision options — wrong (OB)** — revision SST repair after 2 failed (doesn't address subscap) · **HA in subscap + SST insufficiency → instability + anterosuperior escape** · **latissimus transfer = younger, no arthritis** · cortisone/PT (doesn't restore function)

**HA best results** — marked glenoid bone loss, younger, **RA with forward elevation >90°**

**HA failure reasons** — **progressive glenoid degeneration + cuff deficiency** (✓ cuff failure; not advanced glenoid wear when pseudoparalytic without crepitus)

**Instability after HA** — elderly fall → **rotator cuff injury** → **convert to RSA**\
✗ Wrong options: labrum

**Humeral stem loosening** — most commonly linked to **infection**

**Sirveaux (OB)** — 80 shoulders: **96% no/minimal pain**, 3 revisions; ↑ forward elevation at 44 mo

### Proximal humerus fracture – RSA vs alternatives

| Option | Elderly 3/4-part fracture |
|---|---|
| **Nonoperative** | **Majority** of geriatric fractures |
| **rTSA** | **Best surgical option** in osteoporotic elderly; **better outcomes + lower reoperation than HA**; doesn't rely on tuberosity healing |
| HA | Outcome **depends on tuberosity healing** (cuff function) |
| ORIF | High demand + expected tuberosity healing; elderly → **= nonop function, ↑ complications** |
| IMN | 2/3-part; ≈ ORIF; **high failure in osteoporosis** |
| PHR | **Last resort** — no bone stock; failed revisions, tumour |

### Fracture RSA – greater tuberosity healing

**GT fixation** — ↑ **forward elevation + ER** (✓ OB); **anatomic GT repair** → ↑ ER, function, satisfaction vs resection/malunion · not mandatory (deltoid still powers elevation)

**No effect on outcome** — press-fit vs cemented stem · inlay stem · LTO (LT already fractured) · suprascapular decompression

**Humeral height in fracture** — calcar/tuberosity; if calcar missing → **pectoralis major insertion 5.6 cm** below top of humeral head

### Contraindications – deltoid dysfunction & axillary neuropathy

| Contraindication | Note |
|---|---|
| **Deltoid denervation / axillary nerve dysfunction** | **Absolute**; separate permanent vs temporary |
| **Global deltoid deficiency** | Absolute · **partial = relative** (reasonable results) |
| **C5–C6 root avulsion · posterior cord palsy** | → complete deltoid palsy |
| **Acromion deficiency** | Contraindication (Orthobullets) · **relative** (OB) |
| **Glenoid osteoporosis / insufficient bone stock** | |
| **Active infection** (glenoid osteomyelitis) | |
| Complete brachial plexus injury · prior arthrodesis | |
| Severely impaired deltoid · isolated SST tear · full active elevation (TO) | |

**NOT contraindications** — **RA** (sufficient glenoid stock; excellent short/midterm results) · **pseudoparalysis** (= indication) · **prior infection** (if not active) · **prior partial acromioplasty** · **absent GH joint-space narrowing** · **age >70** · **incompetent CA arch** (= indication)

**Deltoid wasting + scapular dysrhythmia** — **not a candidate**\
✗ Wrong options: "candidate due to CTA" · "not candidate due to age/massive tear"

### Operative criteria

| Factor | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Deltoid | **Functioning** (intact axillary nerve) |
| Age | **Physiological >70**, low demand |
| Glenoid | **Sufficient bone stock** |
| Cuff | Irreparable / deficient (pseudoparalysis) |
| Failed nonop | PT, injections |
| Fracture | **3/4-part, >70 y**, osteoporosis |

### Approach

| | **Deltopectoral** | **Anterosuperior (superolateral)** |
|---|---|---|
| Deltoid | **Preserved** | **Anterior deltoid split/violated** |
| Subscapularis | **Take-down needed** | **Preserved** |
| Glenoid | **Inferior pole exposed** (✓ baseplate position) | ↑ exposure; **risk excess height / superior tilt** |
| Instability | Extensive capsular release → ↑ instability (TO: ↑ vs superolateral) | ↓ (anterior stabilisers kept) |
| Axillary nerve | **↓ risk** | **↑ risk** (distal branches) |
| Notching | Not associated | **↑ notching** (insufficiently inferior baseplate) |
| Other | Extensile; **LD transfer possible**; poor posterior glenoid access; stiffness (immobilise for subscap) | Easier humeral axial prep + **GT fixation** |

### Technique

**Humerus** — osteotomy **0–30° retroversion (typically 20°)**; more retroversion → ↑ post-op ER · LHB tenotomy/tenodesis · ream + broach as TSA\
✗ Wrong options: no effect on notching

**Glenoid** — excise labrum, **circumferential capsular release**, expose **inferior glenoid** subperiosteally · central guidewire by best bone stock · **baseplate as inferior as possible + inferior tilt** (↓ loosening + notching) · navigation

**Screws** — **superior → coracoid base**, **inferior → scapular body** · unicortical screws to coracoid may ↓ acromial fracture; 2 screws may suffice

**Glenosphere size** — **36 mm** females/smaller · **40 mm** large men

**Newer prostheses (OB)** — **locking peripheral screws + 15° inferior tilt** · NSA **155° → 143° or 135°** → ↓ notching, ↓ instability, ↓ need for subscap repair

### Humeral component design (inlay vs onlay)

| | **Inlay** (Grammont) | **Onlay** |
|---|---|---|
| Stem | Straight, horizontal inlay tray | Curved |
| Bone | **More metaphyseal reaming** → **↑ GT fracture risk**; ↑ bony contact | **Preserves proximal bone** |
| Cut | | **More varus cut** → preserves GT, **minimises cuff damage** |
| Offset | | **↑ humeral offset / lateralisation** |
| ROM | | **↑ adduction, extension, ER** |
| Conversion | | **Convertible to/from HA + aTSA** |
| Scapular spine fracture | **↓ risk** (✓ prevention, OB) | **↑ risk** (humeral lateralisation) |

**TO trap** — "onlay has more **valgus** cut and more cuff damage" = **FALSE** (onlay = **varus** cut, less damage)

![Inlay vs onlay humeral components](../../MCQ/attachments/q219911-illa-inlay-vs-onlay-diagram.jpg)

### Latissimus dorsi transfer with RSA

**Indication** — **loss of active elevation + ER** → RSA restores elevation, **LD restores ER**; primary or revision (✓ **failed HA, no active ER with arm abducted**)

**✗ Wrong options** — deltoid dysfunction (RSA contraindicated) · brachial plexus injury/arthrodesis · CTA with **active ER >50°** (posterior cuff functioning → RSA or CTA head alone) · AVN + partial infraspinatus tear (nonop → resurfacing) · OA with 10° retroversion (TSA, ream anterior glenoid)

### Rehabilitation

**Early** — sling; passive / active-assisted only

**Sling duration** — **3 wk if subscap NOT repaired** · **6 wk if repaired**; limit passive ER + active IR

**Avoid** — **pushing out of chair** (extension + adduction + IR) · TO: avoid these **8–12 wk**

**Early ROM** — not ↑ dislocation vs 6 wk immobilisation (OB)

**Subscap re-tear** — **anterior instability** → **early exploration + repair**

### Outcomes

**Best indication** — **CTA** (failed arthroplasty = **highest complications**)

**Deterioration** — radiographic after **6 y**, clinical after **8 y**

**Survivorship** — **~90% at 10 y**

### Surgeon learning curve & postoperative surveillance

**Learning curve** — early complications ↓ after **18–45 cases** (✓ 40; ✗ Wrong options: 10, 75, 100, 200)

**Asymptomatic notching** — **observe, routine follow-up with serial X-rays** (✓) · (✗ Wrong options: aspirate for C. acnes · sling for acromial fracture · revise baseplate) · IR to **L5 = excellent** for RSA

## 6 Complications

### Overview – which is "most common"

| Claim | Source |
|---|---|
| **Scapular notching** = most common (radiographic) complication | OB, TO, Orthobullets incidence |
| **Dislocation** = most common complication | OB (2 questions) — read the options |
| **Instability/dislocation** = most common cause of **early failure / early revision** | Orthobullets, TO, OB |
| **Glenoid loosening** = most common **mechanism of failure / long-term** complication | Orthobullets, TO |
| **Glenoid loosening/fracture** = most common after **revision RSA** | OB |

**TO ranking (early)** — **notching > instability > acromion/scapular spine fracture > infection > axillary neurapraxia**; overall complication rate **~15%**

**Complications ∝ indication** — highest for **failed shoulder arthroplasty** (dislocation, loosening, ↓ function); low for CTA / failed RCR

### Scapular notching – superior baseplate, neck-shaft angle & lateralisation

**Definition** — erosion of **axillary border of inferior scapular neck** (scapular pillar) from **medial rim of humeral cup in adduction**; unique to RSA

**Incidence** — **44–96%** Grammont (Orthobullets) · **51–96%** (OB) · **68%** (Lévigne, OB) · **62%** (Lévigne, 326 pts, OB); appears early (**1–2 y**), variable progression

**Most common technical error** — **superior placement of glenoid component** (✓) → **inferior** scapular notching\
✗ Wrong options: "superior notching"

| ↑ Notching | ↓ Notching (protective) |
|---|---|
| **Superior baseplate placement** (upper half) | **Inferior placement / inferior overhang** — **greatest reduction**, least adverse effect |
| **Superior tilt** of glenosphere | **Inferior tilt** (more even force, impingement-free arc) |
| **155° NSA** (Grammont; medialises humeral component) | **Varus NSA (135°)** — **most important factor** (Gutiérrez) |
| **Medialised COR** / medialised glenosphere | **Lateralised offset** (bone/metal augment) — **protective** (✓ TO "all except") |
| **Superior glenoid erosion (E2)** → base plate in valgus | **Larger glenosphere** (OB) |
| **Anterosuperior / superolateral approach** | ↓ **poly depth** (↑ ROM, ↓ notching) |
| ↓ **scapular neck length** · CTA with narrow AHD · **infraspinatus FI grade 3/4** · high BMI · ↑ activity · duration | |

**Gutiérrez (sawbones)** — **NSA** = largest effect on **inferior impingement** → then glenosphere position → lateral offset → glenosphere size · **COR offset** = largest effect on **ROM** · **15° inferior tilt** → most uniform compression, least micromotion

**No effect on notching** — humeral retroversion · stem length · horizontal humeral cut · retentive liner · cemented stem · locking screws · over-reaming

**Source conflicts** — **inferior/posterior tilt**: protective (Orthobullets, TO, most OB) · **minimal effect, may ↑ notching** (OB7681; low position = biggest effect) · **glenosphere size**: larger ↓ notching (OB) · **no effect** (OB214573) — read the options

**Consequences** — **↓ ROM, strength, Constant scores** (✓ with preop superior erosion, Lévigne) · "not linked to detrimental outcomes" (same OB item) · particulate debris → osteolysis → **implant failure**; association with **baseplate failure** · rarely needs revision · not ↑ dislocation · not ↑ aseptic loosening

**Baseplate lateralisation** — ↓ notching but **↑ baseplate loosening** (more stress)

| Sirveaux grade | Extent |
|---|---|
| 0 | No notch |
| 1 | **Limited to scapular pillar** |
| 2 | **Contact with inferior screw** of baseplate |
| 3 | **Beyond inferior screw** |
| 4 | **Under baseplate, approaching central peg** |

![Sirveaux grades 0–4 of scapular notching](../../MCQ/attachments/q217839-illa-sirveaux-glenoid-notching-classification.jpg)

### Instability/dislocation – risk factors & positions to avoid

**Incidence** — **2–3.4%** (Orthobullets) · **3.4% avg, 2–14%** (TO) · **2–4%** (OB) · **2–3%** (OB) · **1.5–31%** (OB) — read the options

**Direction (TO)** — **exclusively anterior / anterolateral**

**Position** — **extension + adduction + internal rotation** (pushing out of chair, tucking shirt) · TO keys: **adduction + extension** and **adduction + IR** · not abduction + ER (native shoulder; deltoid "wrap effect" stabilises RSA)

**✗ Position distractors** — flexion + abduction (RCR) · flexion + IR + adduction (**posterior labral repair**) · ER + abduction (**anterior labral repair**) · IR at 90° abduction (face/mouth hygiene)

| Risk factor | Note |
|---|---|
| **Irreparable / inadequate subscapularis** | **Strongest** (Orthobullets); ↑ with medialised prosthesis (OB) · **less of an issue with newer implants** (OB4801) — read the options |
| **Proximal humeral bone loss** / loss of tuberosities | |
| **Failed prior arthroplasty / revision** | **Most significant** in revision vignette |
| **Proximal humeral nonunion / malunion** | Tuberosity failure |
| **Fixed preop GH dislocation** | |
| **Male sex** · **BMI >30 / obesity** | (✗ Wrong options: female) |
| Previous shoulder surgery (open, extensive capsulotomy) | Not arthroscopic / mini-open biceps tenodesis |
| Axillary nerve injury · Grammont-style implant | |
| Deltopectoral (vs superolateral) · subscap **FI > Goutallier 3** (TO) | |
| **Inadequate humeral length (↓ compression)** → shallow socket | **Main biomechanical** parameters (TO) |
| ✗ Wrong option: **rheumatoid arthritis** | Not a risk factor |

**Stability determinants (TO)** — soft-tissue balance (humeral length), glenosphere size, humeral joint-line inclination, humeral version, metaglene position

**Prevention** — **lateralise baseplate/glenosphere** (✓) · ↑ glenosphere size · **eccentric baseplate** · lateralise/distalise humeral component · **constrained implant**\
✗ Wrong options: valgus stem (medialises humerus, ↓ deltoid wrapping) · subscap tenotomy without repair

**Treatment** — **immediate closed reduction + sling** (OB) · CR can be tried but **redislocation usual → open reduction + correct the surgical error** (TO) — read the options

**✗ Dislocation treatment distractors** — EMG (deltoid weakness) · I&D + poly exchange (**acute infection**) · I&D + antibiotic spacer (**chronic infection**) · ORIF (periprosthetic fracture)

```
RSA dislocation (OB algorithm)
├─ Closed reduction successful → immobilise in abduction 4–6 wk
├─ CR fails → open reduction
│      ├─ soft-tissue laxity → thicker PE ± metal spacer ± retentive PE ± glenosphere change
│      └─ adequate tension → immobilise in abduction 4–6 wk
└─ Recurrent → work-up: full-length humerus X-ray, CT, WBC, ESR/CRP
```

```
Recurrent RSA instability – cause
├─ ↑ ESR/CRP + positive aspirate → <3 mo: I&D + PE exchange · >3 mo: 1- or 2-stage revision
├─ Humeral shortening
│      ├─ <15 mm → thicker PE ± metal spacer ± retentive PE
│      └─ >15 mm → humeral revision ± proximal humeral allograft
├─ Humeral medialisation
│      ├─ <15 mm → larger glenosphere ± ↑ lateral offset
│      └─ >15 mm → baseplate revision with augment / lateralising graft
└─ Acromial stress fracture → immobilise in abduction 4–6 wk
```

### Acromial & scapular spine stress fractures

**Incidence** — **4%** (Orthobullets, OB) · **4–5%** / **~5%** (OB) · **4–8%** (OB) · **10.2%** with **lateralised glenosphere** (Levy, TO)

**Mechanism** — ↑ deltoid tension/lever arm → stress through acromion (deltoid origin) → **stress/fatigue fracture**; propagates from **superior or posterior screw** at base of acromion/scapular spine; **2–48 mo** post-op (TO) · OB: "most often occur during implantation" — read the options

**✓ Glenoid** — **universally stable glenoid implant** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: "fall on abducted shoulder"\
✗ Wrong options: loose glenoid

| Risk factor | Source |
|---|---|
| **Female sex** (up to 100% of series) · age 70–80 | Orthobullets, OB, TO |
| **Osteoporosis** | ✓ OB |
| **Medialised preop COR** (✓ OB) → **RA** (central wear) | Orthobullets, OB |
| **Superior baseplate screw** (✓ TO) · posterior screw | TO, OB |
| **Lateralised glenosphere / glenoid** · superior glenoid placement | TO, OB |
| **Onlay humeral component** (humeral lateralisation) | OB |
| ↑ NSA (larger deltoid moment arm) | OB |
| Acromial thinning (end-stage CTA acetabularisation) · inflammatory arthritis · revision · massive cuff tear · prior surgery | TO, OB |

**Deltoid length conflict** — **less change in deltoid length post-op** = risk (OB215540) · **distalisation / over-tensioning** = risk; superior baseplate ↓ risk (OB217851, OB218963) — read the options

**Not risk factors** — male · **Hamada ≥3** · RSA for fracture · age <50 · **inferior** baseplate screw

**Presentation** — acute **superior shoulder pain** + loss of function, often **atraumatic** or after fall; **missed on X-ray in up to 20% → CT**

**Outcome** — **inferior to RSA without fracture**; **elevation** most limited · better than preop · chronic pain **not** usual · **↑ revision**\
✗ Wrong options: ER

**Treatment** — **nonop: sling (abduction pillow) 6 wk** · nonop union only **40–50%** · **ORIF / tension band** → ↑ union · TO: types I–II nonop (late ORIF if symptomatic nonunion); **type III → ORIF** if displaced, loss of function, nonunion\
✗ Wrong options: nonunion <5%

**Rare sequel** — new instability (glenosphere orientation change, ↓ deltoid tension)

| Levy type (Orthobullets) | Location | Periprosthetic scapular fracture (TO) |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | Acromion **anterior** to posterolateral corner | **Anterior acromion** |
| **2** | **Mid acromion** (base → posterolateral corner) | Acromion **posterior to AC joint, anterior to spine** |
| **3** | **Acromial base** | **Scapular spine** |

![Levy zones of acromial fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3076-levy-classification.jpg)

### Periprosthetic fracture after a fall

**Fall 8 mo post-RSA → acromial fracture** — ✓ **osteoporosis is a risk factor**\
✗ Wrong options: incidence 15% (≈5%) · chronic pain likely · nonunion <5% nonop · equal revision rate

### Infection & complications after revision

**Incidence** — **1–2%** deep infection; large **subacromial dead space**

**Organisms** — **C. acnes + staphylococci** · C. acnes → **vancomycin + clindamycin**

**Risk factors** — **younger age (<65) + male = greatest** (✓ 45-y male RSA) · **primary reverse > aTSA** · **arthroplasty for trauma** · failed arthroplasty\
✗ Wrong options: BMI/obesity, race, ASA, smoking, RA, diabetes

**Treatment** — **2-stage revision = gold standard** · acute → I&D + poly exchange · chronic → I&D + antibiotic spacer

**Revision RSA** — complication rate **11–36%** after failed arthroplasty; **glenoid loosening/fracture ↑ (~25% at 5 y)** (✓) — high metal : small glenoid bone ratio; **periprosthetic glenoid** fractures commonest · also dislocation/recurrent instability\
✗ Wrong options: humerus

**Glenoid loosening treatment** — **staged**: fill glenoid with **autograft** + **hemiarthroplasty** → await incorporation → new glenosphere

### Nerve injury during glenoid preparation

**Most commonly injured** — **axillary nerve** (✓ label B)\
✗ Wrong options: radial (A), musculocutaneous (C), median (D), ulnar (E)

**Neurapraxia** — **0.5–1%**; risks **anterosuperior approach** (deltoid retraction/levering) + **humeral lengthening** · direct laceration during glenoid implantation · **usually transient → observe**

**Lädermann** — subclinical EMG changes (mainly axillary) at 3.6 wk; **8 of 9 resolved <6 mo**; arm lengthening implicated

### Baseplate failure – central peg error

**Scenario** — RSA + **iliac crest graft behind baseplate** → early baseplate migration → **central peg error** (peg in graft, not native glenoid; peg too short) (✓)\
✗ Wrong options: tilt · dissociation · small glenosphere (larger = instability option)

**Glenosphere dissociation** — rare; intra-op failure to engage glenosphere on baseplate

## X vs Y

| | **Anatomic TSA** | **Hemiarthroplasty** | **Reverse TSA** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cuff | **Must be functional** | Tuberosity healing needed | **Not needed** (deltoid) |
| Anterosuperior escape | **Contraindicated** | Performs poorly | **✓ Treatment** |
| Fracture elderly | | Old standard; ↑ revision | **Best** |
| Infection risk | Lower | | **Higher** (dead space) |
| Scapular spine fracture | | | **Highest (~5%)** |

| | **Grammont 155°** | **135° (varus) / lateralised** |
|---|---|---|
| Humeral component | Medialised | ↑ lateral offset |
| Notching | **↑** | **↓** |
| Instability | ↑ (↓ deltoid wrap) | ↓ |
| Deltoid length | | Varus shortens deltoid (OB) |
| Acromial stress | | Lateralisation ↑ (TO, OB) |

| Technical change | Notching | Dislocation | Acromial fracture | Baseplate loosening |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Inferior baseplate** | **↓** | | ↑ (distalisation, OB) | not ↑ |
| **Superior baseplate** | **↑** | no direct effect | ↓ (OB) | |
| **Lateralised glenosphere** | **↓** | **↓** | **↑** | **↑** |
| **Larger glenosphere** | ↓ | ↓ | | |
| **Superior screw** | | | **↑** | |

## How it's asked

✗ Wrong options: CTA · pseudoparalysis without arthritis · CTA + pseudoparalysis

✗ Wrong options: 40-y labourer · active overhead motion · failed HA from glenoid wear

✗ Wrong options: smaller NSA · constrained poly · short stem

✗ Wrong options: ER · IR · adduction

**"Notching 2 y post-op — implicated factor + sequela?"** → **preop superior glenoid erosion; ↓ ROM, strength, Constant**

✗ Wrong options: acromion fracture · baseplate failure · dissociation

✗ Wrong options: smaller glenosphere · retentive liner · horizontal cut

✗ Wrong options: inferior baseplate · larger glenosphere · lateral offset · retroversion

✗ Wrong options: male · Hamada ≥3 · RSA for fracture · age <50
