<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Revision Total Elbow Arthroplasty

*Shoulder & Elbow · 39 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Lifetime revision rate** — **~13%** · much **higher than TKA/THA**

**TEA survival** — **92% (5 y)**, **81% (10 y)**, **71% (15 y)**, **61% (20 y)**

**Shift in indication** — originally **RA**; with anti-rheumatics (DMARDs) → now mostly **post-traumatic** (distal humerus fractures)

**Risk factors for TEA failure** — **smoking** · **medical comorbidities** (RA, hypothyroidism, uncontrolled DM, iron-deficiency anaemia, obesity) · **non-compliance with activity restrictions** · **non-constrained** (dislocation) or **highly constrained** (loosening) systems

### Failure modes & revision rates by indication

| Mode of failure | Rate | Key facts |
|---|---|---|
| **Aseptic loosening** | **15%** | ↑ with **cementation + highly constrained** designs; **anterior impingement** of coronoid into anterior humeral flange in max flexion · **most common in OA** |
| **Component failure** | **15%** | Mostly **bushing wear** (linked/semiconstrained); **younger** patients or **pre-existing deformity** |
| **Infection** | **~8% → ~3%** since DMARDs (Orthobullets) · **3–5%** (OB) — read the options | **Higher than other joint arthroplasties** |
| **Periprosthetic fracture** | **5%** | Intra-op (preparation, reaming, implantation) · post-op (non-compliance, ground-level falls) |
| **Instability** | — | **Most common failure of unconstrained (unlinked) TEA** — late ligament insufficiency or malposition |

**Revision rate by indication** — **traumatic < inflammatory arthritis < OA** (lowest → highest)

**OA patients** — **aseptic loosening = most common complication** (higher demand) \
✗ Wrong options: infection · fracture

**RA patients** — ↑ **tendon + ligament insufficiency**

**Design** — unconstrained/unlinked · **semiconstrained/linked** (e.g. **Coonrad-Morrey**) = **best longevity + function** · constrained

**Polyethylene / bushing wear** — linked + semiconstrained · not the main failure of unconstrained · **component fracture uncommon**

**Gill & Morrey (OB)** — RA, Coonrad-Morrey, 10–15 y: **97%** no/mild pain · arc **28–131°** · **92.4% survival** · 14% reoperation · 86% good/excellent

![Aseptic loosening of TEA](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422838-loosening-tea.jpg)

### Infection – organisms, risk factors & staged revision

| Organism | Fact |
|---|---|
| **S. aureus** (GPC clusters, catalase +, **coagulase +**) | **Most common (~40%)** |
| **S. epidermidis** (GPC clusters, catalase +, **coagulase −**) | **2nd (30–35%)**; **worst outcomes / lowest reimplantation** — **biofilm / encapsulation** → **most difficult to eradicate** |
| **Cutibacterium (P.) acnes** (anaerobic aerotolerant GP bacillus) | **Most common in TSA**, not common in TEA |
| GPC chains, α-haemolytic, optochin-sensitive | S. pneumoniae (pneumonia) |
| GPC chains, β-haemolytic, bacitracin-sensitive | **Group A strep** → necrotising soft-tissue infection |

**Infection risk factors** — **RA on steroids** · **hypothyroidism** (independent) · **diabetes** · **thin soft-tissue envelope** · **prior elbow infection** · **prior elbow surgery** · **psychiatric illness**

**Not risk factors** — female gender · Nordic heritage · **isolated +RF with −anti-CCP** · HbA1c <6.0%\
✗ Wrong options: RA

### Genes & lab markers

| Test | Value / meaning |
|---|---|
| **TSH ↑ + free T4 ↓** | **Hypothyroidism** = independent PJI risk (TSH normal 0.5–5.0; free T4 normal 0.7–1.9) |
| **ESR, CRP, WBC** | Usually **↑** in PJI (WBC may be normal) |
| IL-6, alpha-defensin | **Not studied** in elbow PJI |
| **Synovial WBC** | **No accepted cut-off** for elbow |
| **Aspiration culture** | **+ = chronic infection**; **very high PPV, very low NPV** → **negative aspirate does not rule out** |
| **Intra-op histology** | **Most effective** diagnostic test; **specificity 93%**, **NPV 90%**, **sensitivity 51%** |
| **Intra-op cultures** | **Negative in 10%** of infected TEA; chronic **~15%** vs acute **3%** |

**No definitive test** — diagnosis needs **high clinical suspicion**

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **squeaking / crepitus** (bushing wear) · pain · swelling · stiffness

**Red flags for infection** — **pain at night or rest** · **progressive loss of motion without cause** · **early unexplained loosening** · erythema, **sinus tract**, persistent wound drainage

**Exam** — diffuse tenderness, swelling, **deformity** (significant bushing wear), painful limited ROM

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — **AP + lateral of humerus, elbow, forearm** → loosening, periprosthetic fracture, **bushing wear**

**Bushing wear angle** — angle between ulnar and humeral components · **>10° = mild–moderate wear** (Orthobullets text) · figure labels **7° measurable, 10° extensive** — read the options

![Bushing wear angle](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422838-bushing-wear.jpg)

**CT** — periprosthetic **osteolysis / loosening**; **cement mantle intact or broken** in fracture

**MRI** — **metal subtraction + contrast** → abscess / soft-tissue infection

### Mayo (O'Driscoll & Morrey) classification of periprosthetic fracture

| Type | Location / features | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I** | **Periarticular** — humeral condyle or olecranon; osteolysis + muscle distraction | **Undisplaced** → immobilisation / soft-tissue repair (**fibrous union OK**) · **Displaced** → ORIF with heavy non-absorbable sutures or **tension band** · intact extensor + well-fixed stem → **fragment excision** |
| **II1** | Along stem, **well-fixed implant** | **ORIF + component retention ± strut allograft** (unicortical screws + cerclage cables) |
| **II2** | Along stem, **loose implant, good bone stock** | **Revision with long-stem** ± strut allograft + impaction grafting (± locking plate / cerclage) |
| **II3** | Along stem, **loose implant, severe bone loss** | Revision with **extensive allograft**; often **resection arthroplasty** |
| **III** | **Distal to prosthesis** | Well-fixed → **humerus: immobilise**, **ulna: ORIF (plate)** · loose → treat as **II2** · CT/X-ray to confirm implant + cement mantle |

![Mayo classification of periprosthetic TEA fracture](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422838-mayo-classification-of-periprosthetic-fracture.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Failed TEA
├─ Bushing wear, stable implants, no infection → I&D + bushing exchange (retain)
├─ Acute PJI (<90 d) → I&D + component retention (63%)
├─ Chronic PJI → explant + 2-stage revision (90%)
│      └─ unfit / severe bone loss / poor soft tissue → resection arthroplasty
└─ Aseptic loosening / Mayo II2 → single-stage long-stem revision
```

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| **Nonop** (immobilise 2–4 wk → brace) | **Type I** with stable prosthesis · **Type III humerus** with stable implant (→ Sarmiento) · unfit for surgery | — |
| **I&D + bushing exchange, retain** | **Squeaking/crepitus**, stable implants, no infection · **acute PJI <90 d** | Bushing exchange **75% good at 5 y** · acute PJI **63%** eradicated · chronic only **31%** |
| **ORIF, retain ± excision ± strut** | **Type I olecranon** · **II1** · **III ulna** | **80–90%** no complications |
| **Single-stage revision ± ORIF/allograft** | **Type II2** · **aseptic loosening** | Chronic infection only **66%** eradicated |
| **Explant + 2-stage revision** | **Infected TEA** · Mayo II2 | Chronic infection **90%** eradicated |
| **Resection arthroplasty** | **Salvage**: resistant PJI, can't tolerate multiple surgeries, **severe bone loss**, poor soft tissue · **II3** not reconstructable | **71%** eradication; **lowest MEPS** function |

**Chronic P. acnes (6 mo), well-fixed, good bone stock, healthy** — **2-stage revision**\
✗ Wrong options: IV antibiotics alone · arthroscopic I&D · I&D + poly exchange · single stage

### Technique pearls

**Bushing exchange** — prior approach · ulnar nerve **explore/decompress if symptomatic**, else identify + protect · **uncouple** components → inspect integrity, loosening, rotation → exchange bushing

**ORIF** — humerus: **posterior paratricipital** muscle-sparing · ulna: posterior + subcutaneous border extension · **radial nerve crosses lateral IM septum 14 cm proximal** to lateral epicondyle · Type I with extensor disruption → **tension band, K-wires dorsal to implant** engaging cement + figure-of-8

**Single-stage revision** — remove all loose implant + cement · **long stem in all**, **2 cortical widths beyond fracture** · **cement stem BEFORE struts** (extravasation under strut → no incorporation, nerve irritation) · humerus: **longer posterior + shorter anterior strut** · ulna: dorsal strut under **anconeus** or **FCU**\
✗ Wrong options: flexion impingement

**2-stage (infection)** — **posterior triceps-sparing** · humeral removal: burr + flexible osteotomes, slap hammer; stuck → **posterior humeral cortical split** · well-fixed ulna → **extended olecranon osteotomy** (repair with cables) · **antibiotic spacers/beads** in both canals; no cement in subcutaneous tissue · ± static ex-fix · **≥6 wk IV culture-specific antibiotics** → reimplant when markers normalise, **long stem + antibiotic cement**

**2-stage (fracture)** — stage I: union with **ICBG + plate** · stage II: **longer stem + impaction graft**

**Resection arthroplasty** — thick flaps; nerves in unpredictable places; remove all cement; stabilise with heavy sutures/wires if needed

![Resection arthroplasty](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422838-ap-resection-arthroplasty.jpg)

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Rate / risk factors | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Persistent deep infection** | **~10% after 2-stage**, **~30% after single stage** · **polymicrobial**, **S. epidermidis**, **RA on steroids**, **extensive bone loss** | **Resection arthroplasty** |
| **Ulnar nerve palsy** | **Cement extravasation**, **not visualising nerve** | — |
| **Radial nerve palsy** | **Cement extravasation**, **long plates** impinging | — |
| **Symptomatic hardware** | **ORIF of periprosthetic ulna** fractures | Remove after union |

**Prognosis** — depends on **cause of failure**, **comorbidities**, remaining **bone stock**

## X vs Y

| | **Unconstrained (unlinked)** | **Semiconstrained (linked)** | **Highly constrained** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Main failure | **Instability** | **Bushing / poly wear**, loosening | **Aseptic loosening** |
| Longevity | — | **Best** (Coonrad-Morrey) | — |

| | **Single-stage revision** | **Two-stage revision** | **Resection arthroplasty** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Chronic infection eradication | **66%** | **90%** | **71%** |
| Persistent infection | ~30% | ~10% | — |

## How it's asked

**"Chronic P. acnes TEA, 6 mo, well-fixed, good bone, healthy — treatment?"** → **two-stage revision arthroplasty**

**"RA, cemented semiconstrained TEA 8 y, ↑ ESR/CRP, lysis — hardest organism to eradicate?"** → **Staphylococcus epidermidis**\
✗ Wrong options: S. viridans, E. coli, Vibrio, C. difficile

**"History item most linked to TEA PJI (TSH 10.6, free T4 0.23)?"** → **TSH and free T4 levels** = hypothyroidism\
✗ Wrong options: female · Nordic · HbA1c 5.9% · +RF with −anti-CCP

**"Draining TEA at 12 wk — organism with worst reimplantation prognosis?"** → **gram-positive cocci in clusters, catalase-positive, coagulase-negative** = S. epidermidis\
✗ Wrong options: coagulase + = S. aureus, most common

**"Most common failure of unconstrained TEA?"** → **instability**\
✗ Wrong options: poly/bushing wear · humeral loosening · component fracture

**"TEA revision rates lowest → highest?"** → **traumatic < inflammatory arthritis < osteoarthritis**

**"TEA for OA — most common complication (figure)?"** → **aseptic loosening (Figure A)**\
✗ Wrong options: fracture · infection · normal X-ray

**"Most effective test for TEA infection?"** → **intra-op histology**\
✗ Wrong options: negative aspirate excludes

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Rotator Cuff Arthropathy

*Shoulder & Elbow · 40 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Demographics** — **♀ > ♂** · **7th decade** · **dominant** shoulder

**Risk factors** — **rotator cuff tear** · **RA** · **crystal-induced arthropathy** · **haemorrhagic shoulder** (haemophilia, elderly on anticoagulants)

**History** — term **cuff tear arthropathy** coined by **Neer (1983)**

### Defining features

**3 common features (all patients)** — **non-functioning rotator cuff** · **GH degenerative changes** · **superior migration of humeral head**

**Full picture** — + **subchondral osteoporosis** · **humeral head collapse** · **femoralisation** of head · **acetabularisation** of coracoacromial arch

**Why diagnosis matters** — definitive treatment depends on whether **cuff functions**

## 2 Mechanism

### Pathogenesis – loss of force couples/concavity compression & anterosuperior escape

**Concavity compression** — cuff compresses head into glenoid → concentric rotation → **primary mechanism centring head against upward pull of deltoid**

**Force coupling** — muscle balance centres the net force vector on glenoid → stable **fulcrum** for deltoid + periscapular muscles · loss → **instability, eccentric wear, CTA**

**Supraspinatus + infraspinatus** — depress head caudally; dynamic stabilisers

**Pathway** — cuff deficiency → **loss of dynamic compression** → abnormal joint forces → **superior migration** → **mechanical cartilage wear** → **acetabularisation of acromion**

| ✗ Concept trap | Truth |
|---|---|
| Absent supraspinatus → centring impossible | **Subscapularis + infraspinatus** can still centre head |
| Cuff repair restores stability after concavity lost | **False** — cannot fully restore once **effective glenoid concavity lost** |
| Intact biceps prevents migration | **False** — biceps is **not a head depressor** |
| Cuff vector optimally depresses head | **False** — not optimally oriented |

**Mechanical factors** — loss of concavity (compression effect), ↓ ROM + function, head migration, instability ± recurrent dislocation

**Nutritional factors** — **loss of watertight joint space** → ↓ joint fluid → **cartilage atrophy** (↓ water + GAG) + **subchondral collapse** (disuse osteoporosis)

**Crystal-induced** — synovial degradation proteins destroy cuff + cartilage → end-stage **calcium phosphate crystal** deposits

### Pseudoparalysis mechanism

**Pseudoparalysis** — inability to **actively forward flex/elevate** with **full passive ROM** · cuff **can't DEPRESS** head → deltoid pulls humerus up → **anterosuperior subluxation / escape** · deltoid lever arm ↓\
✗ Wrong options: can't elevate

**Injection test** — **no change** in active elevation after GH local anaesthetic → **pain is NOT the cause** → mechanical (RSA)

| ✗ Pseudoparalysis distractor | Why wrong |
|---|---|
| Posteroinferior / inferior subluxation | Deltoid pulls **anterosuperiorly** |
| Deltoid atrophy | Not the classic chronic-tear mechanism |
| Suprascapular nerve palsy | Weak supra + infraspinatus but **cuff still checkreins** head → no pseudoparalysis |
| **C5 radiculopathy** | Weak deltoid + cuff, **paraesthesias**, no pseudoparalysis |
| Fibroblastic capsular thickening | **Adhesive capsulitis** (passive ROM lost) |

| ✗ Pathogenesis distractor | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| Haemosiderin, synovial hyperplasia, cytokines | **Haemophilic arthropathy** (can cause massive cuff tears) |
| GPC proliferation, PMNs | **Septic arthritis** (P. acnes = **gram + bacillus**, may have **normal markers**) |
| Microcirculation occlusion, osteocyte death | **AVN** (subchondral lucency/collapse, no superior migration) |
| T-cell autoimmune | **RA** (**central** glenoid erosion, periarticular osteopenia) |

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — pain incl. **night pain** · subjective weakness + stiffness

**Inspection** — **supra/infraspinatus atrophy** · **anterosuperior escape** (head prominence anteriorly on elevation) · **subcutaneous effusion** (capsular fluid leak, "geyser")

**ROM** — ↓ active ± passive · **crepitus** GH/subacromial · **pseudoparalysis**

**Acute-on-chronic** — low-energy injury → **acute decompensation** of compensated chronic tear → pain + disability

| Test | Finding | Structure |
|---|---|---|
| **ER lag sign** | Can't hold passive ER, elbow 90° | **Massive infraspinatus** tear |
| **Hornblower** | Can't ER / hold ER at **90° abduction + 90° elbow flexion** | **Teres minor** dysfunction |
| Belly press | Normal → subscapularis intact | Subscapularis |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Views** — AP, axillary, **Grashey (true AP)**

**X-ray** — **↓ acromiohumeral interval** · **acromial acetabularisation** · **femoralisation** of head · **asymmetric superior glenoid wear** · **lack of osteophytes** · **osteopenia** · **"snowcap" sign** (subchondral sclerosis) · **anterosuperior escape**

![Cuff tear arthropathy, high-riding head](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3044-rotator-cuff-arthropathy.jpg)

**MRI** — irreparable tear: **massive fatty infiltration** + **severe retraction** · **not needed** if X-ray already shows anterosuperior escape

**Labs** — normal WBC/ESR/CRP (vs septic, RA)

### Hamada classification

| Grade | Findings |
|---|---|
| 1 | **AHI ≥6 mm** |
| 2 | **AHI ≤5 mm** |
| 3 | AHI ≤5 mm + **acetabularisation of acromion** |
| 4A | **GH arthritis without acetabularisation** (AHI <7 mm) |
| 4B | **GH arthritis with acetabularisation** (AHI ≤5 mm) |
| 5 | **Humeral head collapse** |

![Hamada classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3044-hamada-classification.jpg)

**Hamada versions** — stage 2+ AHI **≤5 mm**, stage 5 **head collapse** (Orthobullets) · AHI **<7 mm**, stage 5 **osteonecrosis** (TO) — read the options

### Seebauer classification

| Type | Pattern | Features |
|---|---|---|
| **IA** | **Centred, stable** | Intact anterior restraints; minimal superior migration; dynamic stabilisation; **femoralisation + acetabularisation** |
| **IB** | **Centred, medialised** | Intact/compensated anterior restraints; compromised stabilisation; **medial glenoid erosion** |
| **IIA** | **Decentred, limited stability** | Compromised anterior restraints; **superior translation**; minimal CA-arch stabilisation |
| **IIB** | **Decentred, unstable** | Incompetent anterior restraints; **anterosuperior escape**; no dynamic or CA-arch stabilisation |

![Seebauer classification](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3044-seebauer-classification.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Rotator cuff arthropathy
├─ Function preserved / first visit → NSAIDs, steroid injection, PT (deltoid, scapular, cuff)
├─ Pain only, intact CA arch + deltoid, young active → hemiarthroplasty
├─ Pseudoparalysis / elderly >70 / anterosuperior escape → reverse TSA
│      ├─ + ER pseudoparalysis (Hornblower +, ER lag) → + latissimus dorsi transfer
│      └─ deltoid / axillary nerve dysfunction → no RSA
└─ Infection / poor soft tissue → resection arthroplasty (salvage)
```

### Nonoperative care when function is preserved

**Indication** — **first-line**; esp. **good active elevation** (e.g. 150°), first presentation

**Regimen** — activity modification · **NSAIDs** · **subacromial steroid injection** · PT (**deltoid**, scapular, cuff strengthening) · not straight to surgery

**Acute decompensation** — rest, PT, injection often settle the painful phase

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indications / facts |
|---|---|
| **Arthroscopic debridement** | **Controversial**, unpredictable; **preserve CA arch** (✗ Wrong options: acromioplasty · CA ligament release) |
| **Hemiarthroplasty** | **Anterior deltoid preserved** + **CA arch intact** (else **anterosuperior / subcutaneous escape**) · **younger, active** · relieves pain (good–excellent **~75%**) but **not function** (elevation **40–70°**) |
| **Reverse TSA** | **Pseudoparalytic CTA** · **elderly (>70)**, low demand · **anterosuperior escape** · needs **functioning deltoid (axillary nerve)** + **good bone stock** · improves **pain + function** |
| **Latissimus dorsi transfer** | **ER pseudoparalysis** · with RSA |
| **Pectoralis major transfer** | **IR deficiency / subscapularis insufficiency** (± aTSA for massive anterior tear + arthritis) |
| **Resection arthroplasty** | **Salvage** — chronic osteomyelitis, infection, poor soft tissue |
| **Glenoid resurfacing / anatomic TSA** | **Contraindicated** — superior glenoid **shear / edge loading** ("**rocking horse**") → **glenoid loosening** (#1 cause of aTSA revision) |
| **Cuff repair** | Only if no end-stage changes, active, **minimal fatty infiltration**; **not with supraspinatus atrophy** |
| **SCR / tendon transfer** | Younger, irreparable tear, must follow strict rehab |
| **Ream-and-run** | **Young labourer, GH arthritis, intact cuff** (✗ Wrong options: CTA) |

### Reverse arthroplasty for pseudoparalysis & outcomes

**Biomechanics** — moves **centre of rotation inferior + medial** → ↑ **deltoid mechanical advantage / lever arm** → deltoid powers elevation

**Outcome** — **improves pain and range of motion even in setting of preoperative pseudoparalysis** · low loosening with modern constructs · durability > hemi

**Can't elevate after successful local anaesthetic** → **RSA**\
✗ Wrong options: biceps tenolysis, open debridement, hemi (pain-reducing only)

**Young vs old** — younger: cuff repair, **SCR**, or hemi · **older, less active: RSA**

**Implant options** — convertible platform stem, central post or screw baseplate, **short cementless stem** (osteopenia) all OK · **severe osteoporosis / low deltoid tuberosity index → cemented stem** · **metal-backed polyethylene glenoid (aTSA) = contraindicated**

### RSA with latissimus/teres major transfer for loss of external rotation

**Indication for LDT with RSA** — **pseudoparalysis of ER** (deltoid restores elevation but **not ER**) from infraspinatus + teres minor dysfunction

**ER dysfunction criteria** — **+ Hornblower** · **ER lag >15° vs contralateral** · **teres minor fatty degeneration ≥ stage 2** (Goutallier / Fuchs)

**Not LDT indications** — negative Hornblower · <5° ER lag · **IR pseudoparalysis** · supraspinatus retraction medial to glenoid · isolated supraspinatus tear\
✗ Wrong options: RSA either

**RSA contraindications** — **axillary nerve neurotmesis** · **severe deltoid dysfunction** (e.g. stroke)

**Post-traumatic arthritis, normal motor** — TSA, no transfer

### Pectoralis major transfer for subscapularis insufficiency – nerve risk

**Technique** — upper part or whole pec major tendon → near **lesser tuberosity**; route **deep to conjoint tendon, superficial to musculocutaneous nerve**

**Nerve at risk** — **musculocutaneous** (tension neurapraxia) → **↓ elbow flexion** (biceps, brachialis)

| ✗ Wrong option: weakness | Nerve |
|---|---|
| Elbow extension | Radial |
| Shoulder ER | Suprascapular (infraspinatus), axillary (teres minor) |
| Shoulder adduction | Medial/lateral pectoral, thoracodorsal, lower subscapular |
| Shoulder abduction | Suprascapular, axillary |

### Traumatic subscapularis tear mechanism

**Mechanism** — fall, grasping to stop fall → **forced external rotation** against resistance\
✗ Wrong options: IR · extension · abduction · flexion

## 6 Complications

### Implant choice & complications (superior glenoid wear, metal-backed glenoid)

**Scapular notching** — humeral poly abuts **inferior glenoid in adduction** → bone loss, osteolysis, loosening, **lower outcome scores**

| Notching | Factor |
|---|---|
| **↑ Risk** | **CTA with superior glenoid wear** → glenosphere too **superior** / **superior tilt** · **anterosuperior (deltoid-splitting) approach** |
| **↓ Risk** | **Lateralised humeral implant** with **lower neck-shaft angle 132.5°** (vs Grammont **155°**) · **~10° inferior tilt** · **inferior-offset glenosphere** with **~3 mm inferior overhang** · deltopectoral approach |

![Scapular notching after RSA](../../MCQ/attachments/q217907-a-figure-a-notching.jpg)

**Acromial / scapular spine stress fracture** — mechanism = **deltoid tensioning by implant design** · **~4–5%**, mean **9 mo** · ↑ **onlay humeral component**, **lateralisation**, **osteoporosis**, **female**, medialised pre-op COR · **nonop union 40–50%** \
✗ Wrong options: bisphosphonate mechanism · C. acnes · superior tilt (= notching)

| Complication | Note |
|---|---|
| **Instability** | **More common after hemi**, rare after RSA |
| Infection | C. acnes → indolent infection |
| Neurovascular injury | Musculocutaneous with pec major transfer |
| **Deltoid dysfunction** | — |
| **Glenoid loosening** | aTSA in CTA (rocking horse) |

## X vs Y

| | **Hemiarthroplasty** | **Reverse TSA** | **Anatomic TSA** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Patient | Younger, active | **Elderly >70**, low demand | Not in CTA |
| Needs | **Intact CA arch + anterior deltoid** | **Functioning deltoid (axillary n.)**, bone stock | Functioning **supraspinatus + subscapularis** |
| Pain | Relieved (~75%) | Relieved | — |
| Function | **Poor (40–70°)** | **Improved**, even pseudoparalysis | Glenoid loosening |

| | **CTA** | **RA** | **AVN** | **Septic (P. acnes)** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Glenoid wear | **Superior**, asymmetric | **Central** erosion | — | — |
| X-ray | **↓ AHI**, acetabularisation, **no osteophytes**, snowcap | **Periarticular osteopenia** | Subchondral lucency / **head collapse**, no superior migration | — |
| Labs | Normal | ↑ inflammatory markers | — | **May be normal** |

## How it's asked

**"Remote cuff repair, normal labs, high-riding head — pathogenesis?"** → **loss of dynamic compression → abnormal forces → mechanical cartilage wear**\
✗ Wrong options: haemosiderin · GPC · AVN · T-cell

**"Untreated progression of cuff tear on MRI (figure)?"** → **cuff tear arthropathy (Figure E)**\
✗ Wrong options: chondrosarcoma · OA · dislocation · fracture

**"True about CTA before RSA (Hamada 4a)?"** → **concavity compression is primary centring mechanism vs deltoid**

**"Full passive ROM, can't flex, no change after injection — pathomechanics?"** → **anterosuperior subluxation of humeral head**\
✗ Wrong options: posteroinferior · deltoid atrophy · SSN · failure to elevate

**"Pseudoparalysis after ladder fall, acetabularisation — process?"** → **inability of cuff to depress head**\
✗ Wrong options: capsulitis, SSN, C5, inferior subluxation

**"75 y, subcutaneous fluid, 80° active / 170° passive, CTA X-ray — next?"** → **reverse TSA**\
✗ Wrong options: biopsy · aspiration · anatomic TSA · syrinx MRI

**"75 y, high-riding head, severe arthritis — treatment?"** → **reverse shoulder arthroplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: resurfacing · hemi · TSA · debridement

**"30° active elevation, AHI 0 mm — best function?"** → **reverse shoulder arthroplasty**

**"Can't raise arm even after injection relieved pain?"** → **reverse TSA**

**"82 y fall, acute-on-chronic cuff tear, failed 4 mo — surgery?"** → **reverse TSA**\
✗ Wrong options: cuff repair · ream-and-run · hemi · aTSA

**"72 y, walks + housework, CTA — implant (figure)?"** → **reverse TSA (Figure F)**

**"True about best option for CTA, 70° elevation?"** → **improves pain + ROM even with pseudoparalysis**\
✗ Wrong options: needs CA arch · pain only · high loosening

**"Pain on top of shoulder 9 mo after RSA, CT fracture — mechanism?"** → **deltoid tensioning by implant design**\
✗ Wrong options: bisphosphonates · ↓ lateralisation · C. acnes · superior tilt

**"RSA + latissimus transfer most appropriate?"** → **70 F, pseudoparesis of elevation + ER, acetabularisation**

**"Pseudoparalysis + Hornblower +, belly press normal — treatment?"** → **RSA + latissimus dorsi transfer**\
✗ Wrong options: aTSA + pec transfer = anterior tears

**"Indication for concurrent latissimus transfer?"** → **pseudoparalysis of external rotation**\
✗ Wrong options: axillary neurotmesis · negative Hornblower · IR

**"82 F, 1 mo pain, elevates to 150° — management?"** → **trial of anti-inflammatory medication or cortisone injection and/or deltoid strengthening**

**"Iatrogenic nerve injury after pec major transfer — weak movement?"** → **elbow flexion** (musculocutaneous)

**"Traumatic subscapularis tear mechanism?"** → **forced external rotation**

**"Risk factor for scapular notching?"** → **CTA with superior glenoid wear**\
✗ Wrong options: lateralised 132.5° · 10° inferior tilt · inferior offset · deltopectoral

**"Implant feature contraindicated in CTA?"** → **metal-backed polyethylene glenoid component**\
✗ Wrong options: convertible stem · central post · medialised COR · short cementless stem

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Rotator Cuff Tears

*Shoulder & Elbow · 41 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**FT tear prevalence** — **28% >60 y · 65% >70 y** (Orthobullets, OB) · ~1/3 >60 y, >60% at 70 y (OB) · ~30% >60 y (OB) — read the options

**Risk factors** — **age** · **smoking** · **hypercholesterolaemia** · **family history**

**Burden** — leading cause of shoulder disability; ~**250,000 RCR/y** (USA); **2nd most common reason manual labourers miss work**

**Age pattern** — mostly **>40 y**; degenerative/attrition in elderly · acute trauma in young

### Genes & lab markers

**Inherited / metabolic** — **family history** + **hypercholesterolaemia** = risk factors (no gene named in sources)

**Indolent post-RCR infection labs (OB)** — WBC borderline (11.0) · **ESR ↑ (40)** · **CRP mildly ↑ (1.5)** · Gram stain negative · culture positive at **day 7–21** → **Cutibacterium acnes**

### Natural history – asymptomatic prevalence & tear progression

| Fact | Value |
|---|---|
| Asymptomatic tears (FT + PT) **>60 y** on MRI/US | **30–55%** (Tempelhof US **30%** · Sher MRI **54%** = 28% FT + 26% PT) |
| Asymptomatic ≥60 y (Orthobullets) | MRI **55%** · US **23%** of asymptomatic |
| Asymptomatic → symptomatic | **50% in 2–3 y** (Orthobullets) · **30–50% in 2–3 y** (OB) — read the options |
| Symptomatic FT tear enlarges | **50% at 2 y** (Orthobullets) · ~**50% within 2–3 y** (OB) · **20–50% at ~2 y, >50% at 5 y** (OB) |
| Asymptomatic tear enlarges | ~**1/3** within 2–3 y (OB) |

**Progression rules** — **larger initial tears progress faster** (**linear correlation size ↔ rate**) · **FT > PT** (faster, more atrophy) · ↑ pain = marker of progression · ↑ fatty atrophy / ↓ muscle quality ↔ ↑ pain

**✗ Progression traps** — FT tears don't progress · symptoms unrelated to size · pain independent of muscle quality · **all** asymptomatic tears become symptomatic (subset never painful) · "10–15% FT enlarge by 2 y" · "20% symptomatic in 3 y" · PT tears progress faster

**Incidental FT tear, minimal symptoms (72 y, 7 mm)** — **observation**\
✗ Wrong options: repair · debridement · tenotomy

**Atraumatic FT tear + PT** — **subjective functional improvement** (↑ PROMs) · ~**75% avoid surgery at 2 y** · ↑ ROM (forward elevation) · minimal pain long-term · PT aim = strengthen surrounding muscles, **not tendon healing**

### Anatomy – footprint, rotator cable, biceps pulley, innervation & CA ligament

**Function** — **dynamic stability** by **balancing force couples** → stable fulcrum; **coronal**: inferior cuff (IS, TM, SSc) vs deltoid's superior moment · **transverse**: SSc (anterior) vs IS + TM (posterior); goal of treatment = restore equilibrium

| Footprint | Value |
|---|---|
| **Supraspinatus ML width** | **12.7 mm** (Orthobullets) · **12.1–12.7 mm** (Dugas, Ruotolo; OB) · **14–16 mm** (OB) · ~12–13 mm (OB) — read the options |
| Infraspinatus · teres minor · subscapularis | 13.4 · 13.9 · **17.9 mm** (SSc widest) |
| AP dimension | **20 mm** (SS + anterior IS) |
| Max insertional length × width | 23 × 16 mm (OB) |
| Articular cartilage → medial footprint | **1.6–1.9 mm** |
| Tendon thickness | 10–12 mm |
| **Usual tear site** | **15 mm posterior to biceps**, near **SS/IS junction** |

**Footprint ↔ % tear** — **6–7 mm** exposed ≈ **50%** PT tear

**5 histologic layers** — **articular side = half the strength of bursal side** → **most tears articular-sided** · I CHL fibres (1 mm) · II dense parallel fibres (3–5 mm) · III loose 45° bundles (3 mm) · IV loose CT + CHL · V capsule (2 mm)

**Bursal vs articular tissue** — bursal = tendon bundles → greater deformation + tensile strength · articular = tendon + ligament + capsule complex

**Blood supply** — subscapular, suprascapular, humeral circumflex aa (layers II–III); **bursal > articular** (hypovascular); critical zone next to lateral SS insertion

**Rotator interval** — capsule + **LHB** + **SGHL** + **CHL**; gap between SS leading edge and SSc superior border

**Rotator crescent** — thin sheet = distal SS + IS insertions (asterisk on arthroscopy)

**Rotator cable** — **thickened CHL** near its **avascular zone**, **perpendicular to SS fibres**, spans SS + IS insertions · **>2.5× thicker** than crescent · "**suspension bridge**" · cable disruption → destabilises tendon, tear propagates

**Biceps pulley (medial sling)** — **SGHL + CHL** (rotator interval tissue) · SSc = **anterior wall of bicipital groove** → SSc tear tears pulley → **medial LHB subluxation**

**CA ligament** — extra-articular · suspension function · restrains anterior translation (with CHL) · contains head in cuff arthropathy · resection → **↑ GH translation** (anterior/inferior)

**✗ CA resection distractors** — ↑ passive IR · ↑ axillary recess volume · ↓ AC joint forces · ↓ LHB resting tension

| Nerve | Muscles |
|---|---|
| **Upper + lower subscapular** | **Subscapularis** (lower also **teres major**) |
| Suprascapular | Supraspinatus + infraspinatus |
| Axillary | **Teres minor** + deltoid |
| Thoracodorsal (middle subscapular) | Latissimus dorsi |
| Musculocutaneous | Biceps, brachialis, coracobrachialis |
| Lateral pectoral · medial pectoral | Pec major (± minor) · pec major + minor |
| Long thoracic · dorsal scapular | Serratus anterior · rhomboids + levator scapulae |

**Biceps subluxation on MRI → innervation of injured muscle?** — **subscapular nerves** (upper + lower) — SSc tear

**Sagittal MRI marked muscle** — **infraspinatus** (TO, FNB 2022/2023) · **teres minor** (OB, sagittal T1 = muscle quality)

**Lateral subacromial portal view through FT tear** — glenoid, labrum, **biceps tendon** visible

**MGHL** — anterior restraint at **45° abduction**; **deep to SSc** (can be mistaken for it); **absent MGHL** → suspect **SSc tear** (MGHL translates medially with retracted SSc)

**Posterior lever push** — **IR arm + posterior stress on head** → tensions SSc → shows **SSc insertion on lesser tuberosity**

## 2 Mechanism

### Etiology

| Mechanism | Features |
|---|---|
| **Chronic degenerative** (intrinsic = primary aetiology) | Older; **SIT**; large tears extend to superior SSc |
| Chronic impingement (extrinsic) | Starts **bursal surface** or intratendinous |
| **Acute avulsion** | **SSc in young** (hyperabduction/ER, fall) · **SIT >40 y with dislocation** |
| Iatrogenic | Failed repair, esp **SSc after open anterior surgery** (open Bankart) |

**Disease continuum** — subacromial impingement → subcoracoid impingement → calcific tendonitis → **RCT** → cuff arthropathy

**Associated** — AC pathology · proximal biceps subluxation/tendonitis · **internal impingement** (throwers; PT tears)

**PT tear causes (OB)** — age-related metabolic/vascular degeneration · subacromial impingement · **anterior instability** · internal impingement · repetitive microtrauma · acute trauma less often · **articular side = poorer blood supply**

**Articular vs bursal origin** — articular = **intrinsic** degeneration · bursal = **extrinsic** (acromial spurs, AC arthritis); degenerative tears "start bursal side" (one OB explanation) vs articular commoner (Orthobullets, TO) — read the options

### Throwing athletes – mechanism & management

**Phase** — **deceleration** (label E): cuff = principal decelerator → **eccentric tensile failure**

**Site** — **articular side of posterior SS or IS**; **posterior pain after ball release**

**Overhead-athlete cuff dysfunction** — primary impingement · primary tensile overload · secondary impingement/tensile overload from GH instability; microtrauma (overhead) vs macrotrauma (contact)

**FT tear in thrower** — **must be repaired**\
✗ Wrong options: debrided

**Adolescent pitcher, undersurface PT tear, never treated** — **cessation of all throwing 6 wk** → cuff + periscapular strengthening → slow supervised return, pitching mechanics\
✗ Wrong options: ↓ pitch count · continued play · arthroscopic/mini-open repair

### Cuff tear after shoulder dislocation in older patients

**Rule** — **>40 y** anterior dislocation → **rotator cuff tear** (pre-existing degeneration, ↓ elasticity) ≫ recurrent instability; often **missed**

**Which tendon** — **supraspinatus** commonest (TO, OB) · **SIT** (SS, **IS**, TM) > SSc · SSc tears more with **posterior** dislocation or young hyperabduction/ER

**Incidence** — **35–86%** >40 y (OB) · **54%**, rising to **100% >70 y** (Simank) · **15%** >40 y, **up to 40% >60 y** (OB) — read the options

**Bimodal instability** — peak <40 y (Bankart, HAGL) · 2nd peak >60 y (cuff tears, fractures); Hill-Sachs common, usually not addressed

**Clue (TO)** — pain lifting in **scapular plane (scaption)** after 6 wk immobiliser → **US/MRI** for cuff\
✗ Wrong options: LHB entrapment · loose body

**Axillary nerve** — **7–10%**; far less common than cuff tear; usually too mild for gross weakness; **lateral arm sensation intact** → nerve palsy/radiculopathy unlikely

(✗ Wrong options: **Distractors** — posterior Bankart (posterior dislocation)) · HAGL (only ~11% of first dislocations >35 y) · SLAP (throwers, no link to anterior dislocation) · SSN injury (uncommon)

```
>40 y, first anterior dislocation, can't elevate / weak ER
├─ Next step → MRI shoulder (arthrogram if pacemaker)
│      ├─ FT cuff tear → early rotator cuff repair
│      └─ MRI negative → EMG (axillary nerve)
└─ not CT · sling 6 wk · PT first · diagnostic scope · observation
```

**Arthrogram** — dye leaks **GH → subacromial space** (geyser) = cuff tear; normal = dye contained in capsule

**Treatment** — **rotator cuff repair**; improved pain + function vs nonop; delay → atrophy + retraction\
✗ Wrong options: hemiarthroplasty · ORIF · TSA · RTSA · coracoid transfer · labral repair · PT alone

**Most likely to need surgery (52 y)** — **supraspinatus**\
✗ Wrong options: labrum · GH ligaments · humeral head · biceps

**Massive but completely repairable** — **acute tear after dislocation, 45 y** (no retraction/atrophy yet) · (✗ Wrong options: RA (attrition, steroids) · prior repair (revision) · SS fossa atrophy · superior humeral migration (→ arthropathy)) · chronic massive repairs retear **~50%**

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — insidious, deltoid region, **overhead** activity · **night pain** = poor predictor for nonop

**Weakness** — **active ROM lost, passive ROM preserved** · acute pain + weakness after trauma

### Physical examination – external rotation lag sign

| Muscle | Weakness | Test | Technique / positive |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Supraspinatus** | Elevation in Jobe position | **Jobe / empty can**, **drop arm** | Abducted, IR, **30° forward in scapular plane, thumb down** → resist pressure · drop test: lowers slowly → sudden drop |
| **Infraspinatus** | ER at **0° abduction** | **ER lag sign** | **Elbow 90°, passive ER → can't actively maintain** |
| **Teres minor** | ER at **90° abd + 90° ER** | **Hornblower** | 90° abd, 90° elbow flexion, 90° ER → **arm falls into IR** |
| **Subscapularis** | IR at 0° abduction | **Belly press**, **lift-off**, **bear hug** (upper border), **IR lag**, **↑ passive ER** | Belly press: **elbow drops back** · lift-off: can't lift hand off back |

**✗ Wrong options (non-cuff tests)** — Yergason (resisted supination, elbow 90°) = biceps · Jerk = posterior labrum · Wright = **TOS** · relocation = anterior instability · Hawkins/Neer = impingement · O'Brien = SLAP

### Subscapularis tears – belly-press/lift-off tests, biceps instability

**Who** — **young**, acute avulsion, **hyperabduction/ER** (fall, wave, door handle) · iatrogenic after **open Bankart** · degenerative = superior portion; largest cuff muscle, prevalence under-recognised

**Symptoms** — **anterior shoulder pain** (mimics **subcoracoid impingement**)

**Signs** — **↑ passive ER** (e.g. 90° vs 45°, 100° vs 70°) · **↓ active IR** · belly press, lift-off, bear hug, IR lag

**✗ ROM traps** — ↑ passive flexion/extension/abduction · ↑ active IR · ↑ passive IR

**MRI** — SSc discontinuity off **lesser tuberosity** + **LHB out of groove (medial subluxation/dislocation)** = near-pathognomonic

**LHB dislocated medially → also injured?** — **subscapularis**; rare lesser tuberosity injury\
✗ Wrong options: MGHL · Bankart (not biceps stabilisers)

**Biceps dislocation routes** — beneath SSc · within SSc · extra-articular

**Arthroscopic findings (all of the above)** — uncovered lesser tuberosity · retraction to glenoid · avulsed SGHL · medial biceps subluxation

**Godenèche (218 scopes)** — pathologic LHB with intact SGHL/CHL in only **11%**; even then **medial sheath wall torn in 92%** → SGHL/CHL lesion = LHB nearly always unstable

**Comma sign** — **SGHL + CHL** complex attached to **superolateral corner of retracted SSc** (Lo & Burkhart) · trace it **inferiorly** → SSc; chronic SSc retracts medially, scarred to deltoid fascia

![Comma sign: SGHL/CHL complex (asterisk) on superolateral SSc border](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3043-comma-sign.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Finding / fact |
|---|---|
| **X-ray** | True AP, AP IR/ER, axillary, **outlet view** (acromion); GT cysts, calcific tendonitis, CHL calcification, **type III acromion**, **AHI <7 mm** = proximal migration (chronic RCT) |
| **Arthrogram** | When **MRI contraindicated** (pacemaker); dye **GH → subacromial** = tear; MR arthrogram ↑ sens/spec |
| **MRI** | **Diagnostic standard**; size, shape, retraction; **fatty atrophy best on sagittal**; **medial biceps subluxation = SSc tear**; humeral head cysts in almost all chronic RCT |
| **ABER MRI** | ↓ effacement of articular surface → contrast enters defect → **↑ sensitivity for PT (PASTA) tears** |
| **Ultrasound** | Dynamic, cheap, confirms injections; **user dependent**; = MRI accuracy for cuff |

**Tangent sign** — sagittal oblique: line **scapular spine top → coracoid top**; SS belly **below line = positive** → **SS fatty atrophy → irreparable** / poorer outcome after repair (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: SSc atrophy · retraction medial to glenoid · biceps dislocation

![Tangent sign on sagittal MRI](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3043-sagittal-shoulder-mri-showing-tangent-sign.jpg)

**PASTA on coronal T2** — **articular-sided SS tear**; intra-articular contrast delineates

| Lesion | Imaging / definition |
|---|---|
| **PASTA** | Partial articular supraspinatus tendon avulsion |
| ALPSA | Labroligamentous complex **medial + inferior**, rolled under **intact periosteum** |
| GLAD | Nondisplaced anteroinferior labral tear + **glenoid cartilage** avulsion |
| HAGL | IGHL avulsed from **humeral neck** |
| Bankart | Anterior scapular periosteum **disrupted** |
| SLAP | Superior labrum anterior → posterior to biceps root |

### Diagnostic criteria – irreparable cuff

| Marker | Threshold |
|---|---|
| Size | **>5 cm** (Cofield) or **≥2 tendons** |
| Fatty atrophy | **Goutallier 3–4** |
| AHI | **<6 mm** (TO) · **<5–7 mm** (OB) · <7 mm = proximal migration (Orthobullets) |
| Tangent sign | **Positive** |
| Retraction | Medial to glenoid |
| Clinical | **Chronic pseudoparalysis** · **profound ER weakness** · long tear duration |
| Pattern | **Posterosuperior (SS + IS) > anterosuperior (SS + SSc)** |

**Pseudoparalysis** — can't actively elevate **>90°** (some authors **>45°**, TO), **full passive**; **no improvement after lidocaine** · **SSc + SS complete tear → 80% pseudoparalysis**

### Anatomic classification

| Type | Features |
|---|---|
| **SIT** (SS, IS, TM) | **Majority**; subacromial impingement; degenerative older or **dislocation >40 y** |
| **Subscapularis** | More prevalent than thought; **subcoracoid impingement**; acute avulsion young (hyperabduction/ER) or **iatrogenic failed repair** |

### Cuff tear size

| Size | cm |
|---|---|
| Small | 0–1 |
| Medium | 1–3 |
| Large | 3–5 |
| Massive | **>5** or **≥2 tendons** (Orthobullets) · ">2 tendons" (one OB) |

### Cuff tear shape

| Shape | Repair |
|---|---|
| **Crescent** | Little retraction, mobile ML → direct to bone, minimal tension |
| **U-shape** | Apex at/medial to glenoid → **margin convergence** side-to-side first (↓ strain on lateral margin) |
| **L-shape** | One leaf more mobile → margin convergence |
| Massive immobile | U or longitudinal → **interval slide** |

### Ellman classification of partial-thickness tears

| Grade | Depth |
|---|---|
| I | **<3 mm (<25%)** |
| II | **3–6 mm (25–50%)** |
| III | **>6 mm (>50%)** |
| Location | **A** articular · **B** bursal · **C** intratendinous |

**Snyder** — also classifies PT tears by location + depth

### Goutallier classification of fatty atrophy

| Grade | Fat vs muscle | MRI |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Normal | ![Goutallier 0–4](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3043-goutallier.jpg) |
| 1 | **Some fatty streaks** | |
| 2 | More muscle than fat | |
| 3 | Equal fat and muscle | |
| 4 | **More fat than muscle** | |

### Lafosse classification of subscapularis tears (TO)

| Type | Lesion | Surgical approach |
|---|---|---|
| I | **Partial**, superior third (articular) | Debridement or single-row |
| II | Complete, superior third | Single-row |
| III | Complete, **superior two-thirds** | Double-row / footprint restoration |
| IV | Complete, **entire tendon**, head centred (concentric) | **Extensive 3-sided release** + robust repair |
| V | Complete + **head subluxation** (eccentric; coracoid impingement) | Often open repair or **pec major transfer** |

### Acromial morphology & impingement (TO)

| Bigliani type | Shape | Note |
|---|---|---|
| I | Flat | ![Bigliani acromion types I–III](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17120975-1.png) |
| II | Curved | **Most common type** |
| **III** | **Hooked** | ↑ anterior + inferior extension → **most associated with impingement + RCT** |
| IV | Convex (upturned), 3% | no impingement |

| Parameter | View | ↑ RCT risk |
|---|---|---|
| Acromion type | **Outlet** | Type III |
| Acromial slope | **Outlet** | Normal 23–45°; **small angle** (steep) |
| **Acromial tilt** | **Outlet** | **↑ tilt** (keyed); **>35°** (normal 29–34°) · ">8.5 mm" (other TO copy) · "lower tilt angle → ↑ tears" (one TO text) — read the options |
| Lateral acromial angle | True AP (TO) · oblique coronal MRI (Banas, TO) | Normal 64–99°; **<70°** → high likelihood FT tear |
| **Acromion index** | **True AP** (✗ Wrong options: outlet) | **>0.7** (deltoid vector more superior) |
| Critical shoulder angle | — | **>35°** |
| Acromiohumeral interval | **Outlet** | **<7 mm** |

**Direction summary** — ↑ RCT with **type III**, **↑ AI**, **↑ tilt**, **↑ CSA**, **↓ AHD**, **↓ LAA**

**Bursal tears** — invariably associated with severe degenerative acromial change (friction/rubbing)

### Cuff tear arthropathy – radiographic signs (TO)

**Sourcil (eyebrow) sign** — **sclerosis/erosion of acromion undersurface**; superiorly migrated head abuts acromion → **rotator cuff tear arthropathy** (anterosuperior escape) · sens/spec/PPV/NPV all **poor**\
✗ Wrong options: calcific tendinitis · SS tendinitis · adhesive capsulitis · SLAP · instability

| Hamada stage | Finding |
|---|---|
| 1 | AHI **>6 mm** (TO) · ≥6 mm (OB figure) |
| 2 | AHI **<7 mm** (TO) · **≤5 mm** (OB figure) — read the options |
| 3 | + **acetabularisation** of acromion |
| 4a | GH arthritis **without** acetabularisation |
| 4b | GH arthritis **with** acetabularisation |
| 5 | Humeral head **osteonecrosis** (TO) · **collapse** (OB figure) |

![Hamada grades of cuff tear arthropathy](../../MCQ/attachments/q214106-illc-hamada.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Rotator cuff tear
├─ Asymptomatic / incidental → observation
├─ Atraumatic FT or PT → PT + NSAIDs + subacromial CSI, 3–6 mo
│      └─ fails → arthroscopic debridement or repair
├─ Acute traumatic FT (young; >40 after dislocation) → early repair
│      (do not delay for PT; better within 3 wk – 3/4 mo)
└─ Massive irreparable → partial repair / SCR / transfer / RTSA
```

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Low-grade PASTA** (<50%, <5–7 mm footprint) + impingement, failed nonop | **SAD + cuff debridement** |
| **Bursal >3 mm (>25%)** | Release, debride degenerative tissue + **repair** |
| **Articular >50%** (>6 mm (OB) · >7 mm (Orthobullets, OB) exposed footprint) | **Completion + repair** |
| PASTA, young, acute, **≥25% healthy bursal tissue** | **In situ repair** (bursal tissue left intact) |
| Intrasubstance tear, intact footprint | **Trans-tendon repair** |
| **Acute (<3 mo) traumatic tear, <60 y** | Repair (relative indication) |
| FT or PT failing nonop | Repair (relative) |
| Massive irreparable, intact SSc, no arthritis | SCR / LD / lower trapezius transfer |
| Massive + GH arthritis, **intact deltoid** | **RTSA** |
| **Relative CI to repair** | Advanced fatty infiltration/atrophy · significant GH arthritis |

### Operative vs nonoperative indications (acute traumatic vs atraumatic tears)

**Nonop regimen** — PT + NSAIDs + subacromial CSI = **first line**; avoid overhead; aggressive **cuff + scapular stabiliser** strengthening **3–6 mo**

**Partial tear, improving, never had formal PT (70 y)** — **formal PT**\
✗ Wrong options: repair · debridement · RTSA · repeat MRI unless unexplained progression

**PASTA, full strength, 6 wk (carpenter)** — **physical therapy** first\
✗ Wrong options: PRP · repair · SLAP repair · SAD

**Failed 12 wk PT + injection, PASTA** — **repair or debridement**\
✗ Wrong options: repeat injection · continued PT · capsular release (full passive ROM) · rotator interval release (for adhesive capsulitis or retracted SSc)

**Low-grade PASTA + impingement, 5/5 strength, failed nonop** — **SAD + debridement**\
✗ Wrong options: SR/DR repair · labral repair · tenodesis

**Subacute FT SS tear, weakness, type 1 acromion, no fatty change (47 y)** — **rotator cuff repair alone**\
✗ Wrong options: repeat CSI · porcine SIS xenograft · + acromioplasty · + remplissage/tenodesis/DCE

**AAOS points** — exercise + NSAIDs for PT tears · **routine acromioplasty not required** · **non-cross-linked porcine SIS xenograft not to be used** · workers' comp → less favourable outcome

**Purpose of repair** — restore muscle function; then ↓ pain, prevent irreversible atrophy

**Massive tear SS + SSc, 40 y, 5 mo, no atrophy** — **rotator cuff repair**; attempt repair before salvage; surgery **within 6 mo** → better outcome\
✗ Wrong options: debridement/SAD/DCE · pec major transfer · 6 wk more PT · SCR

**Traumatic FT tear, young — outcome driver** — **time to surgical repair** (repair within **3–4 mo**; acute repair **within 3 wk** best) · (✗ Wrong options: BMI · age · male gender) · higher preop ASES → ↑ VAS improvement only

**Atraumatic FT tear — strongest predictor of surgery** — **patient expectation of PT** (also higher activity, non-smoker) · pain not related to anatomic severity · pain ↔ comorbidities, lower education, race

### Partial-thickness tears – PASTA, bursal vs articular, debridement vs completion & repair

**PASTA (TO)** — **articular side**; far more common than bursal or intratendinous

**Articular vs bursal** — articular **more common** · bursal **more symptomatic** (subacromial bursitis) · **bursal = higher risk of progression** · articular fares better → higher % tolerated before repair

**Repair thresholds** — **bursal >25% (>3 mm)** · **articular >50%**\
✗ Wrong options: bursal <25% · articular >25%

**Articular >50% tear but poor lateral tissue** — **complete the tear → repair**\
✗ Wrong options: fascial graft · allograft · trans-tendon · biopsy

**Bursal tear 4 mm deep, 1.5 cm AP, softening** — **convert to FT + repair with suture anchors**\
✗ Wrong options: debride + acromioplasty · abort · incidental

**Footprint exposed 3 mm, bursal intact** — **debridement + SAD** (<5 mm exposure ≈ <50% thickness; ✗ Wrong options: completion · transtendinous repair · tenotomy · synovectomy)

**6 mm ML width tear** — **50%** of SS tendon

**Rotator cable involved** — **bursal 4 mm tear involving anterior cable → repair** · intact cable 3–4 mm → less likely to need repair · articular <7 mm → debridement · SSc upper-border fraying without retraction → no repair (but often-missed tear)

**High-grade PASTA (>50%), failed 9 mo PT + PRP (tennis, 51 y)** — **tear completion + repair to footprint**\
✗ Wrong options: isolated DCE (negative cross-body) · more nonop · SAD ± tenodesis · trans-tendon + DCE

```
Partial-thickness tear, failed nonop
├─ Articular <50% → debridement ± SAD
├─ Articular >50% → completion + repair
│      └─ young, acute, healthy bursal tissue → in situ repair
├─ Bursal >25% (>3 mm) → completion + repair
└─ Rotator cable disrupted → repair
```

### Subscapularis repair – biceps, 3-sided release (TO)

```
Subscapularis tear
├─ Repairable → SSc repair + biceps tenodesis/tenotomy
│      (not biceps recentering, groove deepening, tenosynovectomy)
├─ Chronic retracted, atrophied → subcoracoid pec major transfer
└─ Elderly, irreparable → RTSA
```

**Biceps with SSc tear** — unstable → **tenodesis or tenotomy** (recentering unpredictable; prevents recurrent biceps pain/failure) · tenodesis **low in groove**; address biceps **first** (rubs/fails repair)

**SSc tear after open Bankart (6 mo)** — **subscapularis repair** + biceps tenotomy/tenodesis\
✗ Wrong options: PT · tenolysis → cosmetic deformity · pec major (tendon repairable)

**Which lesion → RCR + biceps tenodesis?** — **SSc tear with dislocated biceps**; no significant GH arthritis\
✗ Wrong options: Hill-Sachs · GT fracture · bony Bankart · Bankart

**Chronic retracted atrophied SSc (55 y carpenter)** — **pectoralis major transfer** under conjoined tendon · Achilles allograft alternative for degenerated SSc\
✗ Wrong options: fusion = salvage · CSI · arthroscopic/open repair

**Lafosse IV — release from coracoid base (TO)** — **coracohumeral ligament**\
✗ Wrong options: CA ligament · transverse humeral ligament · conjoined origin

| 3-sided release (TO) | Structure |
|---|---|
| **Anterior** (subcoracoid space) | **CHL** + adhesions to **base of coracoid** — key for lateral excursion |
| **Superior** | Rotator interval; free **comma tissue** from coracoid base |
| **Posterior** (intra-articular) | Capsulotomy from **capsule + MGHL** to **6 o'clock** |

**Repair steps (TO)** — biceps first → **70° scope, posterior portal** for lesser tuberosity → traction stitch at comma via accessory anterolateral portal → anchors **inferior first → superior** → tie in **internal rotation** · III/IV → modified double-row

**Arthroscopic vs open SSc repair** — arthroscopic superior motion + pain (Orthobullets)

### Repair technique – single vs double row, transosseous-equivalent & failure mode

**Mini-open** — deltoid-splitting; ↓ deltoid avulsion vs open; immediate PROM; active ~6 wk · arthroscopic = equivalent results

**Arthroscopic vs open (medium tear)** — **no difference in retear rate** or function · arthroscopic → earlier rehab (no deltoid to protect) · mini-open → worse early pain/function only

**Interval slides** — **anterior** = release SS from rotator interval (incise CHL) · **posterior** = release SS from IS, then margin convergence

**Double-row (TOE)** — medial **mattress** + lateral **simple** → **more anatomic footprint**, larger contact area

| DR vs SR | Finding |
|---|---|
| **Retear** | **DR lower** (esp tears/retraction **>3 cm**); SR higher (Millett meta-analysis, 7 level I RCTs) |
| Healing rate | DR higher |
| Biomechanics | DR **higher ultimate tensile load**; = peak-to-peak elongation, stiffness, conditioning elongation |
| Function, pain, time to heal (short/mid term) | **No difference** (esp <3 cm) |
| Long term | DR may improve function (one RCT) |
| Cost | DR more (more anchors) |
| Blood loss | No difference |

**Lowest retear risk** — **Goutallier 1 + DR TOE**\
✗ Wrong options: Goutallier 3–4 · SR

**Two DR constructs (B, C)** — equivalent retear; SR (A) possibly higher; **A and B = equivalent outcome scores at 1 y**

**Vascularity** — **anchor drilling** (holes in GT) + **peribursal tissue** → main supply to repair; ↑ with exercise

**✗ Wrong options (false repair "truths")** — motion restriction ↑ blood flow · DR better clinical results · SAD ↑ healing · non-healing always → poor outcome (**~half don't heal**, outcomes preserved)

**GT trough (cancellous)** — **not stronger** repair (animal models)

**Failure mode** — **suture pull-out through tendon = tissue failure** (most common, early) → gap formation → poor tendon–bone healing

| Less common failure | Note |
|---|---|
| Anchor breakage | Biocomposite fatigues less than metal |
| Anchor pull-out | Poor BMD, improper placement |
| Eyelet abrasion suture rupture | Metal anchors (sharper) |
| Knot failure | |
| Infection | ~1.9% |

**Causes of failed RCR** — failure to heal (**19–94%**: poor tissue, vascularity, bone) · surgical (deltoid, infection, foreign body, stiffness, nerve) · diagnostic (missed interval, LHB, SSc) · technical (excess tension, anchor placement); arthroscopic RCR complication rate ~10%

**Traumatic retear 3 wk post-op, 1 cm, <65 y** — **revision RCR** (healing >95%)\
✗ Wrong options: PT · CSI · sling · debridement (for chronic irreparable)

**Revision RCR vs RTSA** — RTSA favoured by older age, **static proximal humeral migration**; also aetiology of retear, tissue/MRI quality

### Massive/irreparable tears – partial repair, SCR & tendon transfers

```
Massive irreparable tear
├─ Elderly / CTA / pseudoparalysis → RTSA
├─ Young, posterosuperior, intact SSc + deltoid, no OA → LD or lower trapezius transfer / SCR
│      └─ SSc irreparable → no SCR or LD transfer
├─ Chronic irreparable SSc → pec major transfer
└─ Part of cuff reaches GT → partial repair
```

**Partial repair** — SS can't reach GT, posterior cuff can → **partial repair** (balances coronal + axial force couples) · pain options: acromioplasty + CA release, **reverse acromioplasty (tuberoplasty)**, biceps tenotomy · ✗ RTSA in 48 y · other young options: SCR, transfers, **subacromial balloon spacer** (none superior)

**Pseudoparesis vs pseudoparalysis** — **active FE >90° (e.g. 100°) after lidocaine injection → pseudoparesis** (pain) · no change → pseudoparalysis · arthritis/retraction occur in both

**Young (35 y), pseudoparalysis, AHD <6 mm, Goutallier 3, minimal arthritis — least preferred (TO)** — **RSA** (✓ LD, ✓ lower trapezius, ✓ SCR)

| SCR (TO, Mihata) | Detail |
|---|---|
| Mechanism | Passive constraint to **superior head translation**; space-occupier + tether; restores force couples |
| Graft | **Folded fascia lata 6–8 mm** · acellular dermal **3–4 mm**; anchors **superior glenoid rim → medial GT** |
| **Indications** | Failed nonop massive irreparable **SS + IS**, **Hamada 1–2**, **intact/reparable SSc**, **functional deltoid**; intolerable pain; pseudoparalysis |
| **Contraindications** | **Irreparable SSc** (subscapular nerves) · **Hamada ≥3** · GH OA |
| Best candidate | **59 y, SS + IS, normal X-ray** |

**SCR caveat** — irreparability confirmed only after full diagnostic arthroscopy (Hartzler & Burkhart); short-term data only, graft failure risk

| Transfer | Indication | Key facts |
|---|---|---|
| **Pectoralis major** (subcoracoid, **under conjoined**) | **Chronic irreparable SSc** | Replicates SSc vector; restores IR; **4–6 wk rigid immobilisation**; not posterosuperior |
| **Latissimus dorsi** (± teres major) | **Irreparable posterosuperior (SS + IS)**, **young labourer**, Goutallier ≥3 | To cuff/SSc/GT; brace **6 wk, 45° abd, 30° ER**; restores ER + elevation |
| **Lower trapezius** (± **Achilles allograft**) | Irreparable posterosuperior, middle-aged labourer | More anatomic; **superior outcomes vs LD** (Orthobullets); restores ER |

**Transfer counselling** — pain relief more reliable than function (Omid & Lee)

**LD transfer — best candidate** — **young labourer, massive posterosuperior tear + SS atrophy/fatty infiltration** · **some active elevation (90–100°)** · intact SSc + deltoid, no OA (e.g. 50 y, FE 100°, ER −10°)

**LD transfer — worse outcomes** — **positive lift-off / SSc deficiency** (strongest; Gerber, Irlenbusch) · **deltoid weakness/detachment** · **nonsynergistic** (out-of-phase) action · stage 3–4 fatty degeneration, poor tendon · **female** · preop elevation **<90°** · **complete loss of ER** · superior escape · salvage after failed repair (vs primary)

**Not a worse-outcome factor** — **absence of CA ligament** · other prior surgery

**LD relative CI** — SSc deficiency · deltoid deficiency · pseudoparalysis · advanced GH arthritis · CTA (stays painful) · >80 y pseudoparalytic · acute tear in young (repair first) · thoracodorsal palsy · disability claimant

**Belly press elbow drops back** — SSc dysfunction → **↓ LD transfer benefit**\
✗ Wrong options: ↑ passive IR · drop sign = SS · Hornblower = TM · axillary fold ecchymosis = pec major rupture

**Posterosuperior deficiency + intact belly press → transfer?** — **latissimus dorsi**\
✗ Wrong options: pec major = SSc · pec minor · conjoined = instability · LHB

**LD nerves at risk** — **radial nerve** deep (anterior) to LD tendon, **~3 cm medial to insertion** → **IR arm** at tenotomy; injury → **EPL (extensor pollicis longus)** + wrist/digit extensors · posterior branch **axillary** (deep fascia of posterior deltoid, tendon passage)

**Lower trapezius best for** — **45 y manual labourer, irreparable posterosuperior, loss of ER**\
✗ Wrong options: pitcher SLAP · irreparable SSc → pec major · GH OA → TSA · CTA → RTSA

**Retear 2 mo after massive repair (rough PT), ER lag** — **revision repair SS/IS**; failure: intrinsic (age, size, tissue, systemic disease, smoking) + extrinsic (construct, aggressive rehab)\
✗ Wrong options: SSc revision · LD first · interval release · anatomic TSA

**RTSA mechanism** — **lengthens deltoid lever arm**; COR **medial + inferior**; convex glenoid + concave humerus; ↓ shear · survival **90–95% at 10 y** · most reliable for 65 y painter with SS + IS atrophy, no OA\
✗ Wrong options: dermal augmentation · SCR · partial repair · trapezius transfer

**Other grafts (Orthobullets)** — xenograft (bovine dermis/intestine; mixed) · allograft (human skin/fascia; good short term) · synthetic (foreign body reaction) · **lateral acromionectomy = contraindicated** (historic)

### Concomitant procedures (acromioplasty, distal clavicle excision, suprascapular nerve release)

| Add-on to RCR | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Acromioplasty / SAD** | No difference in PROs, symptomatic retear, ROM, strength, pain · **lower long-term reoperation** (Level 1, esp **type 2–3** acromion; OB) · "routine acromioplasty not recommended" (AAOS, OB) — read the options |
| **Distal clavicle excision** | **No effect on 2-y/long-term outcomes**, ROM, revision |
| **SSN release** | **No difference** in pain or function; SSN dysfunction in **8–27%** of massive tears (traction from retraction → fatty infiltration) |
| LHB tenotomy vs tenodesis | Negligible difference |
| CA ligament release | ↑ anterior/inferior GH translation |

**No long-term effect either** — mini-open vs arthroscopic · starting PT at 6 wk · SR vs DR

**Negative predictors of RCR outcome** — smoking · severe obesity · tear size · fatty infiltration · **age 70+** · pre-existing arthropathy · **workers' comp**

### Postoperative rehabilitation – early vs delayed motion

**Healing** — tendon-to-GT healing **8–12 wk** = rate-limiting step · protect ≥**6 wk**, ~**8 wk** for large 2-tendon tear

**Protocol** — **early PROM** (prevents adhesive capsulitis) → **AROM at 6 wk** → strengthening **≥2–3 mo** · no active strengthening at 1 mo\
✗ Wrong options: isometric ER · eccentric SS · resistance · deltoid (can't isolate from cuff)

**EMG** — **passive forward flexion = minimal cuff activation** · active, active-assisted, isometric, **pulley-assisted** all activate cuff (avoid early)

| Early (<6 wk) vs delayed (6 wk sling) | Result |
|---|---|
| Functional outcomes | **Equivalent** |
| Terminal ROM / stiffness at 1 y | **No difference** |
| Retear / healing | No difference (Cuff & Pupello, 68 pts) · **delayed may ↑ healing** |
| Short-term PROs | Not better with early; early may ↑ pain |

**PT not available first 6 wk** — **won't affect ROM at 1 y**\
✗ Wrong options: peer-to-peer · home band strengthening · strength testing · prophylactic CSI

**Scapular plane forward elevation, prone retraction, 2 mo post-RCR (TO)** — **lower trapezius** (LT > MT + SA) · SA activity ↑ with elevation angle; **LT ↑ steeply at end range** · deficit = endurance/control/timing, not weakness → low–moderate repetitive resistance

| Trapezius part (TO) | Exercise |
|---|---|
| Upper | Shrug ± retraction; overhead shrug |
| Middle | Overhead retraction against wall |
| **Lower** | **Prone scapular setting** (retract + depress, lift arm off table) |
| Lower + middle | Prone scapular retraction, arm to ~120° |

**Return to work** — **full duty 6–10 mo** · light duty after progressive strengthening (earliest **8–10 wk**) · labourers later + lower outcomes than desk jobs; most return to full · PROM PT starts 2–6 wk

**Analgesia** — NSAIDs improve pain at 1 wk (Orthobullets)

## 6 Complications

### Healing & retear risk factors (age, fatty infiltration, workers' comp, injections)

**Retear rate** — **10–90%** · massive chronic ~**50%**\
✗ Wrong options: <5%

| Factor | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Age >65** | **Highest risk of non-healing** (✗ Wrong options: protective); >70 → inferior outcomes |
| **Goutallier grade** (3–4) | **Greatest retear risk** (vs age, rehab, technique, gender) |
| Tear **>5 cm**, multiple tendons, retraction **medial to glenoid** | ↑ retear |
| Diabetes · smoking · severe obesity | ↑ retear |
| Poor post-op compliance | ↑ retear |
| Concomitant AC / biceps procedure | ↑ retear (Orthobullets) |
| **Workers' comp / work-related** | **↓ functional improvement + ↓ satisfaction**, later RTW |
| **Frequent CSI** | **↑ revision surgery** (frequency-dependent) |
| Not risk factors | Female gender · DR technique · delayed rehab · hand dominance · BMI/age/sex (young traumatic tear) |

**Timing of surgery** — repair **<3 mo** from acute tear → better · operative time >45 min → not better (longer = overnight stay, SSI)

### Complications – infection, positioning & regional anaesthesia

**Suprascapular nerve (TO)** — on **undersurface of SS + IS**; **~2.9 cm from superior glenoid rim**, **1.8 cm from scapular spine** → **no sharp dissection >1 cm medial to glenoid rim** · tethered at supraspinous fossa + notch → **tension on lateral SS advancement** (and medial retraction) · injured during **posterosuperior cuff mobilisation** in massive RCR, posteriorly directed glenoid-neck sutures (anterior labral repair), glenoid osteotomy\
✗ Wrong options: thoracoacromial a. · axillary n. · PCHA

**Infection** — **<1%** (Orthobullets) · ~**1.9%** (OB) · **0.006–3.4%** (OB) — read the options; S. aureus, strep, **C. acnes** (formerly *P. acnes*)

**C. acnes** — **delayed/indolent** (10 wk), healed portals, mild ↑ ESR/CRP, Gram negative → anaerobe, cultures positive **7–21 days**\
✗ Wrong options: Pseudomonas (water-borne, not shoulder)

**Pre-op steroid injection** — **within 1 month → highest infection risk** (**65–75% ↑**) · 1–3 mo ↑ · **>3 mo = no ↑ risk** → delay surgery ≥3 mo · not dose, location, number, steroid type

**6 injections, surgery 3 mo later** — **↑ revision, no ↑ infection**

**Pneumothorax** — after **interscalene/supraclavicular block** or arthroscopy · risk = **smoking/COPD** (hyperinflated lungs, high pleural dome) · dyspnoea also from block **diaphragm paralysis** → CXR\
✗ Wrong options: DM · revision · obesity · SSc repair

**Beach chair** — **LFCN palsy** up to **2%**; resolves <6 mo; padding, seatbelt, slight reverse Trendelenburg; **BMI >35** risk\
✗ Wrong options: femoral · obturator · pudendal (perineal post/hip scope) · sciatic

**Others** — **deltoid detachment** (open) · AC pain · axillary nerve injury · stiffness (early PT/ROM doesn't reduce at 1 y)

## X vs Y

| | **Articular-sided (PASTA)** | **Bursal-sided** |
|---|---|---|
| Frequency | **Far more common** | Less |
| Cause | Intrinsic degeneration, weaker hypovascular side | Extrinsic (acromial spur, AC arthritis) |
| Symptoms | Less | **More** (bursitis) |
| Progression | Lower | **Higher** |
| Repair threshold | **>50%** (>6–7 mm footprint) | **>25%** (>3 mm) |

| | **Supraspinatus/SIT tear** | **Subscapularis tear** |
|---|---|---|
| Patient | Older, degenerative; >40 y dislocation | Young, hyperabduction/ER; post-open Bankart |
| Pain | Lateral, overhead, night | **Anterior** |
| Signs | Jobe, drop arm, ER lag | **↑ passive ER**, belly press, lift-off, bear hug, IR lag |
| MRI | Tear 15 mm behind biceps | **Medial LHB subluxation** |
| Impingement | Subacromial | **Subcoracoid** |
| Nerve | Suprascapular | **Upper + lower subscapular** |

| | **Pseudoparalysis** | **Pseudoparesis** |
|---|---|---|
| Active elevation | **<90°**, full passive | Limited by pain |
| After lidocaine | **No change** | **Improves >90°** |

| | **SCR** | **LD transfer** | **Pec major transfer** | **RTSA** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Tear | SS + IS | SS + IS | **SSc** | Any massive + CTA |
| Patient | Younger | **Young labourer** | Younger | **Elderly**, low demand |
| Needs | Intact SSc, deltoid, Hamada 1–2 | Intact SSc + deltoid, some elevation | Under conjoined | **Intact deltoid** |

| | **Dislocation <40 y** | **Dislocation >40 y** |
|---|---|---|
| Main lesion | Bankart, HAGL | **Cuff tear** (SS, SIT) |
| Problem | Recurrent instability | **Weakness**; redislocation low |
| Sling 6 wk | | Unnecessary |
