# Scapular Winging

*Shoulder & Elbow · 42 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Definition & types

**Definition** — dysfunction of **scapular stabilising muscles** → imbalance + abnormal scapular motion; rare, debilitating

**Named by** — direction of the **superomedial corner** of the scapula

| | **Medial winging** | **Lateral winging** |
|---|---|---|
| Muscle | **Serratus anterior** | **Trapezius** (rarely rhomboids) |
| Nerve | **Long thoracic (C5, C6, C7)** | **Spinal accessory (CN XI)** |
| Unopposed pull | **Medialising retraction (rhomboids)** + **elevation (trapezius)** | **Lateralising protraction** (serratus, pec major + minor) |
| Who | **Young athletes**; **more common** | **Iatrogenic** (neck surgery) |
| Commonest cause | **Repetitive stretch/traction** (>50%) | **Iatrogenic** (**up to 70%**) |

![Lateral (trapezius/CN XI) vs medial (serratus/LTN) winging](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3062-winging.jpg)

### Anatomy – scapulothoracic stabilisers

**Scapulothoracic articulation** — **not a true joint**; attached via **AC joint** ligaments; **suction** by serratus anterior + subscapularis holds scapula to thorax

**Scapula** — attachment for **17 muscles**

| Muscle | Origin → insertion | Action | Nerve |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Serratus anterior** | **Ribs 1–8** (Orthobullets) · top **8–9 ribs** (OB); 7–10 slips → anteromedial (vertebral) border | **Protraction** (+ upward rotation, stabilises vertebral border) | **Long thoracic (C5–7)** |
| **Trapezius** | Superior nuchal line, EOP, nuchal ligament, **C7–T12** spinous → lateral ⅓ clavicle, acromion, spine | **Upward rotation + elevation** | **Spinal accessory (CN XI)** |
| **Rhomboid major** | **T2–T5** spinous → medial border below minor | **Retraction** (+ elevation, OB) | **Dorsal scapular** |
| **Rhomboid minor** | **C7–T1** spinous → medial border at base of spine | Retraction | **Dorsal scapular** |
| **Levator scapulae** | **C1–C4 transverse processes** → medial border at spine level | Elevation + downward rotation | **C3–C4 cervical plexus** + dorsal scapular |

**Serratus parts** — upper: downward rotation (glenoid down), stabilises superior scapula · middle: protraction · lower: **upward rotation + abduction**

**Serratus blood supply** — long thoracic artery (superior) · **thoracodorsal artery** branches (inferior)

**Trapezius parts** — upper: upward rotation + elevation · middle: **retraction + adduction** · lower: downward rotation + depression

### Long thoracic nerve

**Roots** — **ventral rami C5, C6, C7** · **C4 contribution 13%** · **no C7 in 8%**\
✗ Wrong options: C8–T1 · C4–6 · C6–8

**Course** — **first nerve to branch** off brachial plexus · posterior to axillary vessels + plexus · **superficial to serratus**, branch to each slip

**Vulnerability** — **long course, small diameter, little connective tissue** · easily injured in **axillary dissection**

**Crow's foot** — where LTN crosses the dominant inferior **serratus branch of thoracodorsal artery**

### Spinal accessory nerve

**CN XI** — spinal + cranial parts → accessory trunk → **jugular foramen** → crosses **lateral to IJV** → **SCM** → **posterior triangle** → trapezius · also ventral rami **C2–C4** (OB)

**Vulnerability** — **superficial course in posterior triangle**

### Dorsal scapular nerve – rhomboid & levator

**Medial scapular muscles** — innervated by **dorsal scapular nerve** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: median · ulnar · musculocutaneous · superior/inferior subscapular · thoracodorsal

**Dorsal scapular nerve (TO)** — **anterior ramus of C5** → **levator scapulae + rhomboids** · OB: **C4 + C5 (mostly C5)**; less commonly causes winging

**Medial-border origins (TO)** — posterior surface: **levator scapulae, rhomboid major + minor** (deep extrinsic) · anterior surface: **teres major** (TO) · **teres minor = lateral border** (TO)

**Attachments (TO)** — levator → **transverse processes of upper cervical** · rhomboids → **spinous processes of upper thoracic**

**Fossa muscles (TO)** — supraspinatus, infraspinatus, subscapularis from their fossae

| Nerve (TO, OB) | Origin | Muscles | Winging? |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Dorsal scapular** | **C5 anterior ramus** | **Levator, rhomboids** | Less common |
| **Suprascapular** | **Superior trunk** (C5–6) | Supraspinatus, infraspinatus | No |
| **Upper (superior) + lower (inferior) subscapular** | **Posterior cord** (C5–6) | Subscapularis, **teres major** | No |
| **Thoracodorsal** | **Posterior cord** (C6–8) | **Latissimus dorsi** | No |
| **Axillary** | **Posterior cord** | Deltoid, teres minor | No |
| Long thoracic | C5–7 | Serratus | **Medial** |
| Spinal accessory | CN XI | Trapezius | **Lateral** |

## 2 Mechanism

### Medial winging – causes

| Cause | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Traction (>50%)** | **Repetitive stretch = most common** (gradual) — head tilted away during overhead activity, **throwing, swimmers, weightlifters, volleyball**, prolonged abduction · acute high-energy (motorcycle) |
| **Compression – acute** | Blunt chest/head/neck trauma (contact sports, **MMA blows**), sudden shoulder depression (fall) |
| **Compression – chronic** | **Scalenes** · **subcoracoid** (coracoid–1st/2nd rib; carrying loads) · inflamed bursae · anteroinferior scapular border |
| **Iatrogenic (10%)** | **Radical mastectomy / axillary node dissection**, thoracic surgery (**flail chest fixation**), **chest tube** |
| Penetrating | Chest wall |
| **Neuralgic amyotrophy (Parsonage-Turner)** | **Isolated LTN palsy in 22%**; no trauma, antecedent immune/inflammatory insult |
| **Mechanical** | **Serratus avulsion** · displaced **inferior pole scapula fracture** |
| Muscle inhibition | Underlying GH pathology (cuff, labrum — Kibler dyskinesia), fatigue from repetitive tensile use |

**Most common cause conflict** — **repetitive traction** (Orthobullets, OB) · **Parsonage-Turner = most often associated** (OB6211) · Parsonage-Turner **"very rarely"** (OB7315) — read the options; nerve = **long thoracic** either way

**Vastamäki (OB)** — 92 isolated serratus palsies: **exertion 40%**, trauma 24%, surgery/anaesthesia 11% · **infection → better recovery**, **surgery/anaesthesia → poorer**

### Lateral winging – causes

**Iatrogenic (most common)** — **cervical lymph node biopsy**, **radical neck dissection**, posterior cervical surgery, carotid endarterectomy

**Traumatic** — traction (**sudden lateral neck flexion**, MVA), blunt (**deep tissue massage**), penetrating neck, fall in elderly

## 3 Presentation

### Medial winging – long thoracic nerve/serratus anterior

**Symptoms** — **vague, nonspecific shoulder girdle pain + fatigue** (base of neck, scapula, deltoid) · spasms · weak **lifting away from body/overhead** · discomfort sitting against chair · subjective instability (no stable platform) · ± traction plexus symptoms

**Deformity** — **inferomedial scapula elevates, protrudes posteriorly + medially**; scapula superior + medial with **inferior pole rotated medially** · **worse with forward flexion / wall push-up**

**Mechanism of deformity** — **compensatory overpull of rhomboids (major + minor) + levator scapulae** (✓ OB) · unopposed **trapezius** pulls medially (antagonist)

**Exam** — abduction often **≤90°** · weak **forward flexion + abduction** · wasting of anterior scalene triangle (SCM atrophy)

**Scapular compression test** — examiner pushes scapula anteriorly + stabilises chest during flexion → **positive = ↑ motion** with support

**Scapular assistance test (OB)** — upward pressure on **inferomedial angle** during elevation → **↓ pain + ↑ arc** = positive

**✗ Exam distractors** — ER lag/hornblower (teres minor) · **belly press** (subscapularis) · **apprehension** (anterior instability) · **Wright's** (TOS)

### Lateral winging – spinal accessory nerve/trapezius & Eden-Lange transfer

**Symptoms** — **similar to medial** + **impingement** (CA arch translates inferiorly as scapula depresses)

**Deformity** — **superomedial scapula drops + protrudes posterior and lateral**; **inferior angle rotates laterally** · **worse with abduction + resisted ER**

**Exam** — **drooping/depressed shoulder girdle** · **trapezius atrophy** (flattened superior trapezius) · weak flexion + abduction · **manual scapular stabilisation ↓ pain**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**EMG medial** — LTN palsy vs **mechanical** (serratus avulsion); **serial every 3 mo** to follow recovery

**EMG lateral** — isolated CN XI vs wider neurological injury; **repeat every 3–6 mo**

**EMG → which nerve** — medial winging + abnormal EMG = **long thoracic (C5–7)**; trapezius denervation = **spinal accessory**

## 5 Management

```
Medial winging (serratus / LTN)
├─ Serratus avulsion / inferior pole fracture → early repair
├─ Neurapraxia → PT, observe ≥6 mo (ideally 18–24 mo)
│      └─ no recovery + EMG compression → supraclavicular neurolysis
└─ Fails 1–2 y, better with manual stabilisation → split pec major transfer
       └─ failed transfer / neuromuscular → scapulothoracic fusion
```

```
Lateral winging (trapezius / CN XI)
├─ Elderly, sedentary, no identifiable injury → nonop
├─ Identifiable injury, early (<20 mo) → explore, neurolysis, repair
└─ Late (>20 mo) / permanent → Eden-Lange transfer
       └─ salvage → scapulothoracic fusion
```

### Nonoperative – medial

**Regimen** — observation, PT (**serratus anterior strengthening**, stretching), avoid heavy lifting · modified thoracolumbar brace (**poor compliance, little benefit**)

**Duration** — **≥6 months**, ideally **18 mo–2 y**

**Recovery** — majority resolve **by 2 y** (Orthobullets; **within 24 mo**, Martin, OB) · **within 1 y** (OB) · **~25% need surgery** (OB)

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Early serratus repair** | **Mechanical avulsion** / inferior pole fracture + symptomatic winging |
| **LTN neurolysis** | Failed nonop **≥6 mo** + EMG **compression** (distal latency, denervation) |
| **Split pec major transfer** | Failed nonop **1–2 y** + **relief with manual scapular stabilisation** |
| Surgery (OB) | Symptomatic serratus winging **>1 y + EMG denervation** → fusion, fascial sling, muscle transfer |
| **Eden-Lange** | CN XI injury **diagnosed late (>20 mo)** |
| **CN XI exploration/repair** | **Identifiable injury diagnosed early** (<20 mo) |
| **Scapulothoracic fusion** | **Diffuse neuromuscular disorders**, **failed muscle transfer**; not first choice; goal **pain relief** |

### LTN neurolysis

**Technique** — **supraclavicular decompression** where nerve traverses scalenes

**Outcome** — **98%** pain relief + winging resolution after failed nonop · **better strength (flexion, abduction) than muscle transfer**

### Split pectoralis major transfer

**Technique** — **sternal head** of pec major → **inferior (inferomedial) border/pole of scapula**; direct (± bone block) or with **hamstring/allograft** extension · **most effective** for medial winging · dynamic stabiliser

**Predictor of success** — **symptom relief with preop manual scapular stabilisation**

**Outcome** — persistent **abduction weakness** common

**Complications** — transfer failure at scapula · **breast asymmetry** · infection · adhesive capsulitis

![Split pectoralis major transfer](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3062-pec-transfer.jpg)

### Nerve transfer – medial

**Donors** — **lateral branch of thoracodorsal nerve → LTN** · **medial pectoral + sural graft → LTN**; preserves muscle biomechanics · nerve/muscle transfers **mixed results** (OB)

### Lateral – nonoperative

**Role** — **controversial** (most injuries = iatrogenic direct → surgery) · nonop for **elderly, sedentary, no identifiable injury** · **less effective than for serratus palsy**

**Poor prognosis with nonop** — **can't raise arm above shoulder** at presentation · **dominant arm**

**Delay** — marked delay in recognition common

### Eden-Lange transfer

**Classic** — **levator scapulae → lateral scapular spine** + **rhomboid minor + major → infraspinatus fossa** (lateralises: medial border → lateral)

**Triple tendon transfer** — all three to **scapular spine** (Rm medial → RM → LS lateral) to reconstruct trapezius

**Results** — **reliably restores ROM + function** · **better than scapulothoracic fusion** (OB)

(✗ Wrong options: **Distractors** — split pec major (= serratus/medial)) · long head triceps / infraspinatus transfers (rarely used) · latissimus transfer · levator only

![Eden-Lange: levator, rhomboid minor and major transferred laterally](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3062-eden-lange.jpg)

### Scapulothoracic fusion

**Technique** — anterior scapula to posterior ribs (wire cables, plates/screws)

**Outcome** — **~20° gain in abduction** · **82% satisfied at 5 y**

**Complications** — nonunion · **pleural effusion** · adhesive capsulitis · symptomatic hardware

## X vs Y

| | **Medial winging** | **Lateral winging** |
|---|---|---|
| Scapula | Inferior pole **medial**, scapula elevated | **Drooping**, inferior angle **lateral**, depressed |
| Worse with | **Forward flexion / wall push-up** | **Abduction + resisted ER** |
| Atrophy | — | **Trapezius** |
| Nonop | **Effective** (most recover) | **Less effective** |
| Muscle transfer | **Split pec major (sternal head)** | **Eden-Lange (levator + rhomboids)** |
| Nerve transfer | Thoracodorsal lateral branch → LTN | |

| Nerve injury | Result |
|---|---|
| Long thoracic | **Medial winging** |
| Spinal accessory | **Lateral winging** |
| Thoracodorsal | Latissimus weakness, no winging |
| Suprascapular | Supra/infraspinatus wasting, no winging |
| Upper/lower subscapular | Subscapularis/teres major, no winging |
| Axillary | Deltoid/teres minor, no winging |
| C8–T1 | No winging |

## How it's asked

**"Long thoracic nerve injury causes?"** → **medial scapular winging**\
✗ Wrong options: lateral · teres minor/infraspinatus atrophy

**"Swimmer, medial winging, abnormal EMG — roots?"** → **C5, C6, C7**

**"MMA fighter, blows to torso, medial winging — deficient muscle?"** → **serratus anterior (D)**\
✗ Wrong options: trapezius · teres major · latissimus · infraspinatus

**"Medial winging + abnormal EMG — nerve?"** → **long thoracic**\
✗ Wrong options: suprascapular · axillary · thoracodorsal

**"Chest wall stabilisation for flail chest, inferior angle translates medially?"** → **long thoracic nerve**\
✗ Wrong options: spinal accessory · C8–T1 · subscapular

**"Quarterback, medial winging, PT — additional finding?"** → **inferomedial scapular support ↓ pain, ↑ ROM (scapular assistance test)**

**"Most common condition causing winged scapula — nerve?"** → **long thoracic**

**"Clinical photo of medial winging — nerve/muscle?"** → **long thoracic / serratus anterior**\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal scapular/trapezius

**"Nerve to muscle not functioning in medial winging?"** → **long thoracic**\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal scapular · thoracodorsal · lower subscapular

**"Swimmer, wall push-up winging, EMG — nerve on plexus diagram?"** → **A = long thoracic**\
✗ Wrong options: medial pectoral · thoracodorsal

**"True of wall push-up winging?"** → **compensatory overpull of rhomboids + levator scapulae**\
✗ Wrong options: C8–T1 · Eden-Lange · node biopsy · retraction deficit

**"Winging after neck exploration, trapezius denervated?"** → **lateral winging, spinal accessory injury**

**"72-y fall, lateral winging — nerve?"** → **cranial nerve XI**\
✗ Wrong options: XII · C3–5

**"Cervical node biopsy, winging at rest + at 90° flexion?"** → **spinal accessory**

**"Posterior cervical surgery, lateral winging — best restores function?"** → **rhomboids + levator transfer (Eden-Lange)**\
✗ Wrong options: split pec · fusion

**"Node biopsy 2 y ago, trapezius denervated — surgery?"** → **lateral transfer of levator + rhomboid minor + major**\
✗ Wrong options: pec transfer · levator only · latissimus

**"75-y after ENT node biopsy, winging, untreated — nerve?"** → **spinal accessory**

**"Medial scapular muscles innervated by?"** → **dorsal scapular nerve** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: subscapular · thoracodorsal · musculocutaneous

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Scapulothoracic Dyskinesis

*Shoulder & Elbow · 43 / 60*

## 1 Basics

**Definition** — **abnormal scapular motion** during coordinated scapulohumeral movement → impingement + dysfunction · rare

**Who** — athletes, **baseball pitchers** · sequela of prior shoulder injury disrupting **scapular stabiliser activation**

### Aetiology

**Causes (multifactorial)** — neurologic injury · thoracic **kyphosis** · **periscapular muscle fatigue** · **poor throwing mechanics** · pain (shoulder, neck)

## 2 Mechanism

**Pathoanatomy** — scapulothoracic **power imbalance** → **scapular protraction** → altered GH mechanics → ↑ stress on **anterior capsule + posterosuperior labrum**

**Injury risk** — labrum · rotator cuff (**articular-surface tears**) · capsule

**Thrower's cascade** — **posteroinferior capsular tightness** + scapular dyskinesis → articular-side cuff tear + **posterosuperior labral tear**

### SICK scapula

**SICK** — muscular **fatigue syndrome** of throwers

| Letter | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **S** | **Scapular malposition** |
| **I** | **Inferior medial border prominence** |
| **C** | **Coracoid pain + malposition** (tender coracoid = **pectoralis minor contracture**) |
| **K** | dys**K**inesis of scapular movement |

**GIRD** — **loss of internal rotation** (e.g. 15°) = posterior capsule tightness

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — shoulder pain/dysfunction **worse with arm elevation** · **loss of throwing velocity** · posterosuperior ± anterior pain

**Exam** — **scapulothoracic crepitus** · scapula **lower + protracted** · **symptoms relieved by scapular stabilisation** (diagnostic)

**Associated** — coracoid tenderness · IR deficit · positive relocation test

![Wall push-up – protracted, winged scapula](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3134-protractionwalltest.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

**Diagnosis** — **clinical**

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (complete shoulder series)** — **usually unremarkable**

**MRI** — often no intra-articular lesion

![Scapular asymmetry with arm abduction](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3134-topicimage.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Scapular dyskinesis
├─ Mainstay → NSAIDs, PT, local injections
└─ Rehab (Kibler, proximal → distal)
       ├─ Stage 1 → full scapular motion + coordinate scapula with trunk + hips
       ├─ Stage 2 → strengthen scapular muscles
       └─ Stage 3 → shoulder flexibility, closed-chain, sport-specific
```

### Rehabilitation – kinetic chain, pectoralis minor & posterior capsule stretching

**Principle** — **proximal → distal** (kinetic chain); progress by **function, not timetable**; acute → recovery → maintenance phases

**Initial stage** — **coordination of scapular motion with trunk + hip movements**\
✗ Wrong options: isometric · isokinetic · closed chain · axial loading (later)

**PT emphasis** — **core** strengthening · scapular stabilisers (**serratus anterior, trapezius**) · rotator cuff · proper mechanics in throwers

**SICK / GIRD initial regimen** — **stretch posterior capsule** (IR deficit) **+ pectoralis minor** (coracoid tenderness)

| ✗ Regimen distractor | Why wrong |
|---|---|
| Posterior capsule + **subscapularis strengthening** | Misses pec minor (coracoid pain) |
| Posterior capsule + **plyometrics** | Misses pec minor (coracoid pain) |
| **Anterior capsule** stretching (any) | IR deficit = **posterior** capsule tightness |

## How it's asked

**"Pitcher, posterior pain, scapular dyskinesis, normal MRI — emphasise first in rehab?"** → **coordination of scapular motion with trunk + hip movements**\
✗ Wrong options: isometric · isokinetic · closed chain · axial loading

**"Pitcher, 15° IR loss, coracoid tenderness, + relocation, normal MRI — initial regimen?"** → **stretch posterior capsule + pectoralis minor**\
✗ Wrong options: subscapularis strengthening · plyometrics · anterior capsule stretching

**"SICK scapula — coracoid pain due to?"** → **pectoralis minor contracture**

**"Diagnostic exam finding?"** → **symptoms relieved by scapular stabilisation**

**"Scapular position in dyskinesis?"** → **lower + protracted**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Shoulder Arthrodesis

*Shoulder & Elbow · 44 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Goal

**Aim** — **stable base** for the upper limb → optimise **hand + elbow** function

**Ideal patient** — **flail shoulder** (deltoid + cuff paralysis) with **functional hand, wrist + elbow**; young, esp. **labourer**

### Anatomy

**GH fusion surface** — small → decorticate **GH articular surfaces + humeral head–acromion undersurface** to ↑ fusion area

**Fixation strength** — only **glenoid fossa + base of coracoid** strong enough for screws

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **specific to underlying condition**; symptomatic GH dysfunction

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Test | Purpose |
|---|---|
| **X-ray AP, lateral, axillary** | Bone stock for fusion, deformity |
| **CT** | **Glenoid bone loss** (esp. failed arthroplasty) |
| **EMG** | When **neurological status of scapular muscles** is ill-defined |

## 5 Management

### Indications – deltoid and rotator cuff paralysis

| Indication | Note |
|---|---|
| **Paralytic disorders** | **Deltoid + cuff paralysis** (post-traumatic), **brachial plexus palsy**, paralysis in infancy |
| Irreparable deltoid + cuff deficiency with arthropathy | |
| **Failed TSA / failed revision arthroplasty** | Salvage |
| **Tumour resection** | Bone deficiency |
| **Chronic infection** | Painful ankylosis |
| **Recurrent instability** | Failed previous repairs (severe, refractory) |

**Rühmann (OB)** — 43 cases: paralysis **74%**, OA/humeral head necrosis 35%, infection 16%, instability

### Contraindications

**List** — **ipsilateral elbow arthrodesis** · **contralateral shoulder arthrodesis** · **no functional scapulothoracic motion** · **trapezius, levator scapulae or serratus anterior paralysis** · **Charcot** in acute inflammatory stage (**Eichenholtz 0–2**) · **elderly** · **progressive neurological disease**

### Operative criteria – who benefits vs alternatives

| Patient | Best option |
|---|---|
| **Flail shoulder (deltoid + cuff paralysis), hand works** | **Arthrodesis** ✓ |
| No cuff function | No **TSA** (contraindicated) |
| No deltoid function | No **reverse TSA** (contraindicated) |
| Chronic lateral winging | Eden-Lange (levator + rhomboids) |
| Irreparable cuff tear, overhead motion | Latissimus dorsi transfer |
| Young AVN | Hemiarthroplasty |
| Recurrent MDI after arthroscopy | Therapy, revision arthroscopic/open stabilisation |
| Infected TSA removed (70-y) | Treat infection → **2nd-stage TSA / HA / reverse** |
| **Cleidocranial dysostosis** | **Scapulothoracic** (✗ Wrong options: glenohumeral) arthrodesis |

### Fusion position

**Goal** — **hand to mouth** (feeding) + **groin** (hygiene)

| Source | Abduction | Forward flexion | Internal rotation |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Orthobullets "30-30-30"** | **20–30°** | **20–30°** | **20–30°** |
| OB typical | **~40°** | **~35°** | **~20°** |
| Rühmann range (OB) | 20–60° | 20–40° (anteversion) | 0–50°; **no ER** |
| Orthobullets GH anatomy note | 15–20° | 20–25° | 40–50° |

**Positions conflict** — 30-30-30 (Orthobullets) · 40-35-20 (OB) — read the options

![Arthrodesis position: reach mouth, head, back](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3128-shoulder-arthodesis.jpg)

### Technique

**Approach** — **S-shaped incision**: scapular spine → over acromion → anterolateral arm

**Steps** — resect cuff from proximal humerus · **tenodese biceps** · decorticate glenoid, humeral head + acromial undersurface · position 30-30-30

**Fixation** — **10-hole 4.5 mm pelvic reconstruction plate** along **scapular spine → acromion → humeral shaft** · **compression screws across GH into glenoid fossa** · screw into **base of coracoid**

![Plate fixation of shoulder arthrodesis](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3128-image.jpg)

### Postoperative care

**0–6 wk** — **thermoplastic orthosis** from day 1

**6 wk** — **sling** if no radiographic loosening

**3 mo** — **mobilisation + thoracoscapular strengthening** if no loosening

**Recovery** — **6–12 months**; marked ↑ pain + function in selected patients

## 6 Complications

**List** — **infection** · **nonunion** · **malposition** · **prominent hardware** (pain) · **humeral shaft / periarticular fracture**

## How it's asked

**"45-y, deltoid + cuff paralysis 5 y after motorcycle injury, hand/wrist work?"** → **shoulder arthrodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: Eden-Lange · TSA · reverse TSA · latissimus transfer

**"Who benefits most from GH arthrodesis?"** → **30-y labourer with deltoid + cuff paralysis**\
✗ Wrong options: AVN · MDI · infected TSA removed · cleidocranial dysostosis

**"Position of shoulder fusion?"** → **30° abduction, 30° flexion, 30° IR** — hand to mouth (OB: ~40/35/20)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Shoulder Arthroscopy: Indications & Approach

*Shoulder & Elbow · 45 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Indications

**Indications** — diagnostic · loose bodies · **cuff repair/debridement** · labral/SLAP/instability repair · **subacromial decompression** · AC joint / **distal clavicle resection** · **suprascapular nerve release** · capsular release (contracture) · synovectomy · **biceps tenotomy/tenodesis**

### Diagnostic scope – normal anatomy & variants

**Diagnostic scope** — **30° scope via posterior portal**: biceps, SS/IS/TM, **rotator interval**, MGHL/IGHL, **subscapularis recess (loose bodies)**, anterior labrum, glenoid, humeral head

**Rotator interval** — **biceps tendon + superior edge of subscapularis + glenoid**

**Anterosuperior labrum + MGHL** — **widest anatomic variability**

| Variant | Labrum | MGHL |
|---|---|---|
| **Attached labrum** | Attached | **Broad** — **most common** |
| **Sublabral hole (foramen)** | Sublabral hole | **Cord-like** |
| **Buford complex** | **Absent** anterosuperior labrum | **Cord-like** |

![Sublabral foramen, sublabral recess, Buford complex](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12064-labralvariant.jpg)

**Normal bare areas** — **central glenoid** + **posterior humeral head**\
✗ Wrong options: pathology

## 5 Management

### Positioning

| | **Beach chair** | **Lateral decubitus** |
|---|---|---|
| Orientation | Upright, **anatomic** | Glenoid parallel to floor (non-anatomic) |
| Traction | **None** → **↓ neurapraxia** | **10–13 lb** (avoid over-distraction) → **traction neurapraxia** (NV + soft tissue) |
| Arm | Mobile; arm holder possible | **30–60° abduction + 20–30° forward flexion** |
| Conversion to open | **Easy** (deltopectoral; no re-prep) | Must reposition/redrape |
| Bleeding | **↓ venous pressure + bleeding** | — |
| **Cerebral perfusion** | **↓ (hypoperfusion, CDEs, stroke, death)** | **Better** |
| Hypotension / bradycardia | **↑ risk** | No ↑ |
| Posterior portals | **Difficult** (head in the way) | Easy |
| Labrum | Less capsular distortion | **Traction accentuates labral tears** |
| Anaesthesia | **Regional + sedation possible** | Not ideal if regional not tolerated |
| 5 o'clock portal | **Safer** (Gelber) | **Axillary + MCN closer** |

**Lateral position (TO)** — **vacuum beanbag + kidney rest**; **10–13 lb** traction; **30–60° abduction, 20–30° forward flexion**

**Lateral modification (TO)** — tilt patient **20–30° posteriorly** → glenoid parallel to floor → **less traction** (↓ plexus neurapraxia), **labral tears accentuated** (pulled away from bed), **better access to inferior third** of labrum/capsule

**Beach chair advantages (TO)** — faster positioning · **↓ neurapraxia (no traction)** · less capsular distortion · arm mobility · easy open conversion

**Beach chair disadvantages (TO)** — difficult posterior portals · **↓ cerebral perfusion with hypotensive anaesthesia → stroke, death**

✗ Wrong options: **EXCEPT (TO)** — "beach chair → **more** brachial neurapraxia" is **false**

### Primary portals

| Portal | Location | Function | Passes / risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Posterior** | **2 cm inferior, 1 cm medial** to posterolateral acromion; aim at **coracoid tip** | **Primary viewing**; **first portal** | Between **infraspinatus (SSN)** + **teres minor (axillary)** or through IS |
| **Anterior** | **Lateral to coracoid**, anterior to AC joint; spinal needle under vision | Viewing + SAD | Between **pec major (pectoral nn.)** + **deltoid (axillary)** |
| **Lateral** | **1–2 cm distal** to lateral acromion | **SAD** | Through **deltoid (axillary)** |

![Portal sites: posterior (P), lateral (L), anterolateral (AL), anteromedial (AM)](../../MCQ/attachments/q4837-illa-3.jpg)

### Secondary portals

| Portal | Location | Function | Risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Anteroinferior (5 o'clock)** | Slightly inferior to coracoid | **Anterior labral repair anchors** | **Axillary (> 7 o'clock risk, Meyer)** + MCN |
| **Posteroinferior (7 o'clock)** | Under vision, spinal needle | **Posterior labral repair anchors** | **Axillary nerve** (low posterior) |
| **Neviaser (supraspinatus)** | **Just medial to lateral acromion** | Anterior glenoid view, **SLAP repair** | Through **supraspinatus (SSN)**; SSN if into **suprascapular notch** |
| **Port of Wilmington (posterolateral)** | **Just anterior to posterolateral acromion** | **Posterior SLAP + cuff lesions** | — |

**Trans-subscapularis portal** — **avoid** in either position (Gelber)

**Placement** — secondary portals under direct vision from posterior portal with **spinal needle**

## 6 Complications

### Portal-related nerve injury

| Structure | Anatomy | At risk when |
|---|---|---|
| **Axillary nerve** | Quadrangular space → deep surface deltoid, **~7 cm below acromion** | Posterior portal **too inferior** (**7 o'clock**) → pain, weakness, ↓ **lateral arm/shoulder** sensation |
| **Suprascapular nerve** | Supraspinatus → infraspinatus fossa | Posterior portal **too medial**; Neviaser into notch |
| **Cephalic vein** | Deltopectoral groove | Anterior portal **too lateral** — **structure most at risk** (Lo, Meyer) |
| **Musculocutaneous nerve** | Enters muscle **2–8 cm distal to coracoid tip** | Anterior portal **too inferior** |
| Phrenic nerve | — | **Interscalene block** |

**Lo (outside-in)** — all NV structures **> 20 mm** from all portals

**No neural structure at risk** — lateral superior portal · posterior superior portal

### Positioning complications (beach chair, lateral decubitus, obesity)

| Pressure point (beach chair) | Nerve | Deficit |
|---|---|---|
| **A – forehead** (tight face mask) | **Supraorbital** (terminal **CN V**) | Paraesthesia **forehead + anterior scalp** |
| **B – mastoid process** (mask straps) | **Great auricular (C2–C3)** | Paraesthesia **ear, posterior auricular area, angle of mandible** |
| C – chin strap | — | **Skin breakdown lip/chin** |
| D – contralateral elbow | **Ulnar** | Ulnar neurapraxia |
| **E – lateral abdominal belt / ASIS** | **LFCN** | Burning/numbness **anterolateral thigh**, above knee, **no weakness** |

![Beach chair pressure points A–E](../../MCQ/attachments/q8940-a-beach.jpg)

| Neck position | Complication |
|---|---|
| **Hyperextension** (± rotation) | **Stroke** + **CN XII (hypoglossal)** palsy — traction + compression by angle of mandible (✗ Wrong options: face-mask compression) |
| **Hyperflexion** | **Spinal cord ischaemia → paraplegia** |

**Not reported with beach chair** — CN III (ptosis, mydriasis, diplopia) · **AIN** (FPL + index/long FDP) · **femoral** (quadriceps, anterior thigh, medial leg)

### Cerebral hypoperfusion

**CN VI palsy post-op** — **lateral rectus** paralysis (can't abduct eye) → cerebral hypoperfusion in beach chair

**↓ Risk** — **lateral decubitus** · **regional anaesthesia + sedation** (fewer CDEs) · **SBP > 90 mmHg**

**Increases or no effect (not protective)** — **general anaesthesia (↑ CDE)** · **SBP < 70 mmHg** (↑) · open vs arthroscopic · metal vs bioabsorbable anchors

**Stroke** — also reported after lateral decubitus (risk small in both)

### LFCN palsy (obesity)

**Path** — LFCN crosses near **ASIS** → compressed by belt / waist flexion / pannus

**Risk factors** — **obesity (BMI > 30; highest-risk option BMI > 35)** · **prolonged operative time** · **inadequate ASIS padding**

**Not risk factors** — age > 50 · type of surgery (open/arthroscopic/arthroplasty) · **diabetes** · foam between pannus + thighs (= **protective**)

**Prevention** — hips **centred at bed break**, **hip flexion ~45°** (less waist flexion), **padding under belt** + between pannus and thighs

**Holtzman (397 pts)** — LFCN palsy **5.5%**, mostly **transient**; weight + BMI significant

**Levy protocol (BMI > 30)** — LFCN **5 → 1** case (OB) · **none, even BMI > 40** (another OB)

**Obese patients** — ↑ nerve palsies; post-op ↑ **DVT, infection, wound breakdown, hardware failure, mortality**

## X vs Y

| | **5 o'clock (anteroinferior)** | **7 o'clock (posteroinferior)** |
|---|---|---|
| Use | **Anterior** labral anchors | **Posterior** labral anchors |
| Nerve | **Axillary (more risk, Meyer)** + MCN | **Axillary** |

| | **Sublabral foramen** | **Buford complex** |
|---|---|---|
| Labrum | **Sublabral hole** | **Absent** anterosuperiorly |
| MGHL | Cord-like | **Cord-like** |

## How it's asked

**"Lateral arm numbness + weakness after arthroscopy — portal?"** → **posterior inferior (7 o'clock)**\
✗ Wrong options: Neviaser = SSN · anterior = MCN · lateral/posterior superior = none

**"All true about positioning EXCEPT?"** → **beach chair has more brachial neurapraxia** (TO)

**"Lateral decubitus traction?"** → **10–13 lb**, 30–60° abduction, 20–30° flexion (TO)

**"Beach chair pad at mastoid — deficit?"** → **great auricular: ear, posterior auricular, angle of mandible**\
✗ Wrong options: CN III · CN XII · AIN · femoral

**"PACU, can't gaze left after beach chair — prevention?"** → **lateral decubitus position**\
✗ Wrong options: GA · SBP < 70 · open · metal anchors

**"Obese, burning outer thigh above knee, no weakness — pad where?"** → **lateral abdominal belt / ASIS (LFCN)**

**"Anterolateral thigh numbness after beach chair — biggest risk factor?"** → **BMI > 35**\
✗ Wrong options: age · diabetes · surgery type

**"Hypoglossal palsy after beach chair — cause?"** → **neck hyperextension**

**"Absent anterosuperior labrum + cord-like MGHL?"** → **Buford complex**

**"Cephalic vein at risk — portal error?"** → **anterior portal too lateral**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Shoulder Exam

*Shoulder & Elbow · 46 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Exam components

**Sequence** — inspection · palpation · ROM · neurovascular · provocative tests · always **compare both sides**

**Inspection** — skin, scars, symmetry, swelling, atrophy, hypertrophy, **scapular winging**

**Palpation** — C-spine spinous processes, SC joint, clavicle, **AC joint**, acromion, coracoid, scapular spine · paraspinals, periscapular, deltoid, **greater tuberosity (cuff insertion)**, **LHB in groove**

### Range of motion

| Plane | Normal |
|---|---|
| **Forward elevation** | **180°** |
| **Abduction (scapula stabilised)** | **90°** |
| **ER at side** | **80°** |
| ER at 90° abduction | Compare sides |
| **IR to vertebral level** | **T4–T8** |
| IR at 90° abduction | Compare sides |

**ROM rule** — active vs passive, both sides; normal relative to contralateral

### Neurovascular

| Root | Motion | Muscle | Reflex |
|---|---|---|---|
| C4 | Scapular stabilisation (winging) | Upper serratus anterior | — |
| **C5** | Shoulder abduction · elbow flexion palm up | Deltoid · biceps | **Biceps** |
| **C6** | Elbow flexion thumb up · wrist extension | Brachioradialis · ECRL | **Brachioradialis** |
| **C7** | Elbow extension · wrist flexion | Triceps · FCR | **Triceps** |
| **C8** | **Finger flexion, grip, thumb extension** | **FDS** | — |
| T1 | Finger abduction | Interossei (ulnar) | — |

**Sensation** — C4–T1 dermatomes · **pulses** brachial, radial, ulnar

### Anterior band of IGHL – restraint in abduction/external rotation

| Ligament | Restrains |
|---|---|
| **Anterior band IGHL** | **Anterior translation at 90° abduction + full ER** (moves anteriorly) |
| **MGHL** | Anterior translation at **45° abduction** |
| **SGHL + CHL** | **Inferior translation of adducted arm** + ER in adduction |
| **Posterior band IGHL** | **Posterior translation in IR** |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Impingement & biceps tests (Hawkins, Speed)

**Impingement syndrome (TO)** — anterior pain in **60–120° abduction** (painful arc) · greater tuberosity slides under acromion, bursa smooths it · causes: **subacromial bursitis**, **hooked acromion**, cuff pathology (supraspinatus tendinitis, calcific tendinitis, partial tear), **GT fracture** (TO)

| Test | Technique | Positive / meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **Neer sign** | Stabilise scapula, **passive forced forward elevation** | Anterior pain (GT under acromion **70–110°**) · needs **full ROM** · sens **75–86%**, spec **50%** · also + in stiffness, OA, instability, bone lesions |
| **Neer test** | Repeat Neer after **subacromial lidocaine** | **Marked pain relief** = subacromial impingement |
| **Hawkins-Kennedy** | **Flex 90°, elbow 90°, forcible IR** (GT under CA ligament) | Pain · **sensitive, not specific** · sens **75–92%**, spec **45%** · = **subacromial bursitis** (TO) |
| **Scapular assistance (TO)** | Push **inferomedial scapular angle** during elevation (upward rotation + posterior tilt) | **Relief** of impingement pain + ↑ arc |
| **Internal impingement** | 90° abduction + max ER + extension (late cocking) | **Posterior** throwing pain · relieved by relocation / elbow in front of body |

![Hawkins-Kennedy test](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17140070-1.png)

**Neer test injectate** — **5 mL 1% lidocaine** (TO) · **4 cc 1% lidocaine + 4 cc 0.5% bupivacaine + 2 cc steroid** (Orthobullets) — read the options

| Biceps test | Technique | Positive |
|---|---|---|
| **Speed** | Resisted forward elevation, **elbow extended, forearm supinated** | Pain in **bicipital groove** |
| **Yergason** | Elbow 90°, pronated → **resisted supination** | Groove pain = biceps tendon pathology |
| **Popeye** | Bulge in arm | **LHB proximal rupture** |
| Groove tenderness | Palpation | Inflamed LHB or **SLAP** |

**Speed + / empty can −** — **bicipital tendinitis** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: supraspinatus tendinitis · SLAP · impingement

### Rotator cuff strength & lag tests (lift-off, hornblower, ER lag)

| Muscle | Tests |
|---|---|
| **Subscapularis** | **Lift-off**, **belly-press / Napoleon**, **bear hug**, **IR lag**, ↑ passive ER |
| **Supraspinatus** | **Jobe empty can**, **drop arm (Codman)** |
| **Infraspinatus** | **ER lag sign** (specific), drop sign (Hertel), ER resistance stress |
| **Teres minor** | **Hornblower (Patte)**, ER strength at 90° abduction |

**Lift-off (Gerber) (TO)** — dorsum of hand on lumbar spine (IR) → can't lift off = **subscapularis tear** · specific for **IR deficit** (TO) · best for **inferior subscapularis** · more accurate if hand reaches opposite scapula · confounded by other muscles\
✗ Wrong options: abduction · ER deficit · anterior instability

![Lift-off test](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17140081-1.png)

**Belly-press (Gerber) / Napoleon (TO)** — palm presses abdomen, elbow in front of trunk · weak → **elbow drops behind trunk** (shoulder extension) = belly-press · **wrist flexes** = **Napoleon sign** · best for **superior subscapularis**

![Belly-press test](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3037-belly-press.jpg)

**Bear hug (Burkhart & De Beer) (TO)** — palm on **opposite shoulder**, elbow in front, examiner pulls hand away **anteriorly** · can't resist = positive · **most sensitive**: detects tears **<50%** (upper third) missed by lift-off/belly-press (TO) · positive if **≥20% weaker** than other side (Orthobullets)

**Most sensitive + specific subscap test** — **IR lag sign** (Orthobullets, OB) · **bear hug** most sensitive (TO) — read the options

**IR lag sign** — behind patient, elbow 90°, shoulder 20° elevation + 20° extension, lift hand off lumbar spine to near-max IR, release wrist → lag

**Not subscap testing** — **isolated IR at side** (pec major + latissimus contribute)

**Subscapularis insertion** — **lesser tuberosity** → **positive bear hug after trauma = lesser tuberosity avulsion**\
✗ Wrong options: GT fracture = drop arm · superior migration = CTA · dislocation can't be positioned · AC separation

**Jobe empty can (TO)** — **90° abduction in scapular plane (30° forward), IR thumb down**, resist downward push · **weakness = supraspinatus tear** (TO) · **pain only** = subacromial bursitis/irritation, tear only with true drop arm (Orthobullets)

**Drop arm (Codman) (TO)** — arm elevated overhead → lower slowly · sudden pain or uncontrolled lowering = **supraspinatus tear** · passive 90° scapular plane → drop = **most specific for full-thickness tear (98%)** (Orthobullets)

**ER resistance stress (TO)** — arms at side, elbows 90°, **ER 60°**, resist examiner's IR push → pain/weakness = **infraspinatus + teres minor**

**ER lag sign (TO)** — seated, elbow 90°, shoulder **20° elevation in scapular plane**, full ER → hold · angular drop = **infraspinatus tear** (specific)\
✗ Wrong options: subscapularis · teres minor · adhesive capsulitis

**Drop sign (TO)** — same as ERLS but **90° elevation** in scapular plane · infraspinatus · ERLS slightly more sensitive + specific · not the Orthobullets "drop sign" (= drop arm, supraspinatus)

**Hornblower (Patte) (TO)** — arm **90° abduction in scapular plane**, elbow 90° → attempt ER against resistance · can't = hornblower posture · **most accurate for teres minor** · TO option: "test for **infraspinatus and teres minor**" · Orthobullets: hold 90° abd + 90° ER → **arm falls into IR** · also + in **posterior supraspinatus** tears

![Positive hornblower's sign](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17140078-1.png)

**Teres minor** — **axillary nerve**, ER

| ✗ Distractor | Tests |
|---|---|
| Jobe | Supraspinatus weakness ± impingement |
| Belly-press, IR lag | Subscapularis |
| Hawkins | Impingement |
| "Jones test" | not supraspinatus test |

### Lesser tuberosity fracture – subscapularis testing

**Bear hug after ATV accident** — can't hold palm on opposite shoulder vs anterior pull → **lesser tuberosity fracture** on X-ray

### Labral tests (Porcelli)

| Test | Technique | Positive |
|---|---|---|
| **O'Brien (active compression)** | **90° flexion, elbow extended, 10–15° adduction, pronated (thumb down / full IR)** → resist downward force · repeat supinated/ER | **Deep GH pain pronated, not supinated = SLAP** · **superficial AC pain = AC joint** |
| Crank | Abducted, passive rotation + axial load | Click/pain = labrum |
| **Modified dynamic labral shear (TO)** | Standing, **abduct 120°** scapular plane, elbow 90°, ER → lower arm keeping ER | **Painful click posterior joint line 120°→90°** = SLAP |
| **Uppercut (TO)** | Neutral rotation, elbow 90°, supinated fist → punch to chin vs resistance | Pain = **SLAP** |
| **Porcelli (TO)** | See below | **Posterior labral tear** |

**Porcelli test (TO)** — Morey 2016 · **posterior labral tear** · **sens 100%, spec 99%**, inter-examiner reliability **>0.8** · outperformed jerk, Kim, load-and-shift · **not yet validated**\
✗ Wrong options: SLAP · subscapularis · PASTA

**Porcelli technique (TO)** — part 1: **90° flexion, 10–15° adduction, max IR (thumb down)**, examiner behind stabilises scapula, resisted elevation → note pain/strength · part 2: same, examiner's **thumb on posterior joint line** pushing anteriorly (prevents posterior subluxation) · **pain ↓/gone in part 2 = positive** · pain persists = **AC joint** pathology

### Instability tests

| Direction | Test | Technique / positive |
|---|---|---|
| Anterior | **Apprehension** (Jobe, TO) | Supine, **90° abduction + full ER** (behind coronal plane, elbow 90°) → apprehension |
| Anterior | **Relocation** | Posterior force on head → apprehension relieved |
| Anterior | **Anterior release** | Release hand in abd/ER → subluxation · **"3 in 1"** (apprehension + relocation also +) |
| Anterior | Anterior load & shift / drawer | Supine, **40–60° abd** (TO drawer: 60°, neutral rotation, axial load) · grade = laxity, pathologic only if symptomatic |
| Posterior | **Jerk (Jahnke)** | Sitting/standing, **flex 90°, IR, adducted**, axial load at elbow → posterior jerk; abduct → reduces (TO) · clunk or pain |
| Posterior | **Kim** | Seated, 90° abduction → flex to 45° + axial load + posteroinferior force · **pain** |
| Posterior | **Posterior stress** | Flexion, adduction, IR + posterior force → pain + instability |
| Posterior | Posterior load & shift / drawer | Increased translation vs other side |
| Posterior | **Loss of ER** | Locked in IR = posterior subluxation/dislocation |
| Inferior / MDI | **Sulcus** | Pull arm down (TO: at **0° and 45° abduction**) · persists at **2+/3+ with ER at side = pathologic rotator interval** |

| Translation grade (drawer / load & shift) | Finding |
|---|---|
| 1+ | **To glenoid rim** |
| 2+ | **Over rim, reduces spontaneously** |
| 3+ | **Dislocates / locks out**, no spontaneous reduction |

| Sulcus grade | Acromiohumeral interval |
|---|---|
| 1+ | **<1 cm** |
| 2+ | **1–2 cm** |
| 3+ | **>2 cm** |

### AC joint & other tests

| Test | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Cross-body adduction** | AC joint pain |
| AC tenderness | AC pathology |
| **Wright's** | **TOS**: abduct + ER, head turned away → **radial pulse lost** |
| **Medial winging** (wall push at 90° flexion) | **Serratus anterior / long thoracic nerve** |
| **Lateral winging** | **Trapezius / spinal accessory (CN XI)** |
| **Axillary webbing defect** | **Pectoralis major rupture** |

## X vs Y

| | **Lift-off** | **Belly-press** | **Bear hug** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Subscap portion | **Inferior** | **Superior** | Upper third, partial <50% |
| Position | Hand on lumbar spine | Palm on abdomen | Palm on opposite shoulder |
| Positive | Can't lift off | Elbow drops back / wrist flexes (Napoleon) | Can't resist anterior pull |

| | **ER lag sign** | **Drop sign (TO)** | **Hornblower** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Elevation | **20°** scapular plane | **90°** scapular plane | **90°** abduction |
| Muscle | **Infraspinatus** | Infraspinatus | **Teres minor** |

| | **Neer** | **Hawkins** |
|---|---|---|
| Manoeuvre | Passive forced elevation, scapula fixed | 90° flexion + **forced IR** |
| Sens / spec | 75–86% / 50% | 75–92% / 45% |

| | **O'Brien — SLAP** | **O'Brien — AC** |
|---|---|---|
| Pain site | **Deep** in GH joint | **Superficial** over AC |
| Forearm | Pronated, not supinated | Pronated, not supinated |

## How it's asked

**"Full-thickness teres minor tear — positive test?"** → **Hornblower's sign**\
✗ Wrong options: Jobe · belly-press · IR lag · Hawkins

**"90° abduction + full ER — ligament resisting anterior translation?"** → **anterior band IGHL**\
✗ Wrong options: CHL · SGHL · MGHL · posterior band

**"ATV crash, positive bear hug — X-ray?"** → **lesser tuberosity fracture**

**"Correct statement for test in figure (arm in hornblower posture)?"** → **Hornblower's test for infraspinatus and teres minor** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: Jones · O'Brien · IR lag

**"Muscle tested in figure (hand behind back)?"** → **subscapularis** (lift-off) (TO)

**"Lift-off test is specific for?"** → **internal rotation deficit** (TO)

**"ER lag sign suggests?"** → **infraspinatus tear** (TO)

**"Anterior pain on IR, positive lift-off — muscle?"** → **subscapularis** (TO)

**"Diagram: 90° flexion + forced IR?"** → **Hawkins-Kennedy test** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: Neer · empty can · ER lag

**"Positive Hawkins sign indicates?"** → **subacromial bursitis** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: cuff tear · subscap tear · brachial plexus

**"Positive Speed + negative empty can?"** → **bicipital tendinitis** (TO)

**"Porcelli test detects?"** → **posterior labral tear** (TO)

# Shoulder Hemiarthroplasty

*Shoulder & Elbow · 47 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Definition

**Hemiarthroplasty (HA)** — humeral articular surface replaced with **stemmed humeral component**; glenoid left native

**Most common indication** — GH arthritis with **inadequate glenoid bone stock** for TSA

**Mechanics** — relies on **rotator cuff compression** for motion + strength; **CA arch** = barrier to proximal migration

**Use** — less common since RSA; still for young patients/laborers, CTA, osteonecrosis, complex proximal humerus fractures

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Use |
|---|---|
| **True (Grashey) AP** | **30–40° oblique** to coronal plane; extent of DJD, fracture pattern |
| **Axillary** | **Posterior glenoid wear**; fracture displacement |
| **CT** | **Glenoid version + glenoid bone stock**; unclear fracture pattern |
| **MRI** | **Rotator cuff** status |

**↓ Acromiohumeral distance** — superior head migration → **cuff deficiency** → evaluate cuff (MRI / CT arthrogram)

![Axillary view — posterior glenoid wear](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3074-posterior-glenoid-wear.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Indications & limitations (cuff integrity, osteonecrosis, CTA head, ream-and-run)

| Indication | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Primary arthritis** | **Cuff deficient** · glenoid stock inadequate · **high glenoid loosening risk (young, active laborers)** |
| **Rotator cuff arthropathy** | **HA > rTSA if forward flexion > 90°** |
| **Osteonecrosis** | **Without glenoid involvement** |
| **Proximal humerus fracture** | 3-part with poor bone · **4-part** · **head-split** · major articular destruction |

| Contraindication | Note |
|---|---|
| Infection | — |
| **Neuropathic (Charcot) joint** | e.g. syrinx |
| Unmotivated patient | — |
| **CA ligament deficiency** | CA arch + cuff deficient → **superior (anterosuperior) escape** |
| RA · capsulorrhaphy arthropathy | Listed as contraindications (one OB) |

**RA / capsulorrhaphy arthropathy** — contraindication (OB) · RA results "satisfactory in most, ↓ ROM" (TO) · ream-and-run works in capsulorrhaphy arthropathy (OB) — read the options

### Outcomes by diagnosis

**Best results (TO)** — **humeral head AVN / osteonecrosis**: consistent pain relief in **90%**, near-normal ROM

**Less good (TO)** — **RA, OA, glenoid dysplasia, post-traumatic arthrosis**: satisfactory in most, **↓ ROM**

**Most influential factor on function** — **status of the rotator cuff**\
✗ Wrong options: CA ligament · glenoid wear · inferior osteophyte · AC arthritis

**Irreparable cuff** — **TSA contraindicated** → HA (limited function) or **RSA**

**Fractures** — **excellent pain relief** in majority; outcome **inversely proportional to age + time from injury to surgery**

### HA vs TSA for GH OA

| Statement | Truth |
|---|---|
| **HA outcomes with eccentric glenoid wear** | **Inferior to TSA** ✓ |
| HA function overall | Inferior to TSA |
| HA revision rate | **Equal or greater** than TSA (mid–long term); higher with eccentric wear |
| HA outcomes eccentric vs concentric | **Worse with eccentric** wear |
| Glenoid | **Progressive erosion** may occur |

### Extended (CTA) head

**Theoretic advantage** — **metal-to-bone articulation with the acromion** → greater arc of smooth metal contact with glenoid + acromion

**Evidence** — unproven; results **comparable to standard HA**; **no other biomechanical advantage**\
✗ Wrong options: superior stability · fixed fulcrum · ↑ deltoid moment arm · ↑ offset

### Ream-and-run

**Definition** — HA + **concentric glenoid reaming** (**non-prosthetic glenoid arthroplasty**) with **nubbed spherical reamer** → single smooth concavity

**Advantage** — avoids **prosthetic glenoid failure** → **activity beyond that recommended for TSA**

**Ideal patient** — **young, active, motivated** (e.g. 42-yo weightlifter)

**Works in** — OA, **capsulorrhaphy arthropathy**, **post-traumatic arthritis**\
✗ Wrong options: contraindicated

**Rehab** — **therapy is critical**\
✗ Wrong options: "TSA needs more therapy"

**Outcomes** — excellent 2-year functional + radiographic results

### Technique

**Approach** — **deltopectoral**

**Head cut** — start at **medial insertion line of supraspinatus**

### Humeral stem version

| Version | Result |
|---|---|
| **30° retroversion** ✓ (broach + stem, TO) | **Ideal**; lateral fin **slightly posterior to bicipital groove** |
| **Excess anteversion** | **Anterior dislocation** |
| **Excess retroversion** (e.g. 45°, TO) | **Posterior dislocation** (✗ Wrong options: anterior instability) |
| ✗ Wrong options: neutral · 10° anteversion · neutral–10° retroversion | Wrong (TO) |

**45° retroversion (TO)** — FNB 2024 item, answer not provided by source; options: anterior instability · limit IR · best function · shaft fracture

### Height, head size & fixation

**Greater tuberosity height** — **7–8 mm below top of head** (Orthobullets) · **6–8 mm** (OB) — read the options

**Why** — maintain **cuff + biceps tension**; recreate **medial calcar** contour

**Height landmark** — top of head → **upper border of pectoralis major = 56 mm**; cement prosthesis proud

**Head size** — contralateral X-ray or **measure resected head**; too large → **overstuffing**

**Fixation** — **cemented = standard of care** (better QoL, ROM, strength vs uncemented)

### Tuberosity malposition after fracture hemiarthroplasty

**Importance** — **tuberosity migration = commonest cause of failure** in fracture HA; non-anatomic placement → variable outcomes, **rotation deficit**

**Technique** — secure tuberosities **to each other + to shaft**; **autograft from resected head** → ↓ pull-off, ↑ healing

| Malposition | Consequence |
|---|---|
| **GT too proximal** (above head) | **GT impingement on acromion** in abduction |
| GT too distal | **Over-tensioned cuff**, stiffness, ↓ motion |
| **LT too lateral** (close to GT) | **↑ Subscapularis tension → ER deficit** |

**✗ GT position traps** — does **not** change nonunion risk · does **not** cause overstuffing

![Tuberosity fixation around hemiarthroplasty](../../MCQ/attachments/q216885-illa-hemi-repair.jpg)

### Rehab

**Fracture HA** — **early passive motion 6–8 wk** until healed → **strengthening once tuberosity healed**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Detail | Consequence / treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **Progressive glenoid arthrosis** | Young, active | **Conversion to TSA** |
| **Tuberosity displacement / malunion** | Commonest in fracture HA | Reposition + bone graft |
| **Overstuffing** | Head too large | → **stiffness**, **accelerated glenoid arthritis** |
| **Subcutaneous (anterosuperior) escape** | **Cuff + CA arch deficient** | **RSA > anatomic TSA** |

![Anterosuperior escape after hemiarthroplasty](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3074-anterosuperior-escape.jpg)

### Failed hemiarthroplasty – glenoid erosion & conversion to TSA/RSA

```
Painful hemiarthroplasty
├─ Infection excluded? (WBC 1900, 20% PMN, cultures −ve 3 wk) → aseptic
├─ Intact cuff + glenoid wear → keep stem, revise head + add glenoid (TSA)
└─ Pseudoparalysis + superior escape → convert to RSA
```

**Glenoid arthritis, intact cuff, well-fixed stem** — **modular**: retain stem, **revise head + prosthetic glenoid**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI · two-stage spacer · explant stem · RSA

**Cuff failure + superior escape (pseudoparalysis)** — **conversion to RSA**\
✗ Wrong options: MRI (cuff failure implied) · cuff repair + TSA · fusion if glenoid stock good · revision HA · PT

**Why not TSA for cuff-deficient failed HA** — poor function, **continued superior migration**, **glenoid loosening (rocking-horse)**

**Cuff arthropathy after HA** — bony erosion, superior head migration, **acromion + AC joint erosion**

## X vs Y

| | **Hemiarthroplasty** | **Anatomic TSA** | **Reverse TSA** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Glenoid | Native | Prosthetic | Prosthetic (reverse) |
| Cuff needed | **Yes** | **Yes** | No |
| Best for | **AVN, 4-part fracture**, young/laborer, poor glenoid stock | OA, **eccentric glenoid wear** | Cuff deficiency, **superior escape** |

## How it's asked

**"Best results of hemiarthroplasty?"** → **humeral head AVN** (TO)

**"Recommended stem position?"** → **30° retroversion** (TO)

**"Lesser tuberosity placed too close to GT — problem?"** → **ER deficit** (subscap over-tension)

**"GT above superior head after fracture HA — risk?"** → **tuberosity impingement**\
✗ Wrong options: nonunion · overstuffing

**"Function after HA or TSA most affected by?"** → **rotator cuff integrity**

**"Extended CTA head advantage?"** → **metal-to-bone articulation with acromion**

**"66-yo — hemiarthroplasty most indicated for?"** → **humeral head osteonecrosis**\
✗ Wrong options: Charcot · CA ligament deficiency · RA · capsulorrhaphy arthropathy

**"42-yo with GH OA — HA vs TSA?"** → **HA inferior with eccentric glenoid wear**

**"42-yo weightlifter — ream-and-run?"** → **allows activity beyond TSA limits**

**"Painful HA 10 y, intact cuff, aseptic aspirate — next?"** → **revise head + prosthetic glenoid, retain stem**

**"Pseudoparalysis + superior escape after fracture HA?"** → **convert to RSA**

**"Contraindication to HA?"** → **CA ligament deficiency** (superior escape)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Shoulder Imaging

*Shoulder & Elbow · 48 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### MRI physics – field strength

**Field strength** — stronger magnet → ↑ intrinsic **signal-to-noise ratio**

**3 T vs 1.5 T** — **3 T = 9× proton energy** of 1.5 T → **1.5 T ≈ 11% of 3 T**\
✗ Wrong options: 33% · 66%

| Sequence | TR / TE | Bright | Dark | Shoulder use |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **T1** | **Short TR, short TE** | **Fat** | Fluid, bone, ligament, marrow, fibrocartilage | **MR arthrogram**; **Hill-Sachs** |
| **T2** | **Long TR, long TE** | **Fluid** (inflammation), marrow | Bone, ligament, muscle, fibrocartilage | **Cuff pathology** — full-thickness tear |
| **STIR** | Fat saturation (suppression) | Fluid + oedema ↑ | Fat ↓ | **Oedema vs fatty infiltration** of cuff muscles |

### CT vs MRI

| | **CT** | **MRI** |
|---|---|---|
| Best for | **Cortical + trabecular bone**, **bony defects** | **Soft tissue**, **bone contusion / trabecular microfracture** |
| Cost | **↑ Radiation** | — |
| Artefact | **No magnification artefact** (unlike X-ray) | — |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Radiographic views (Zanca, West Point)

| Complete trauma series | Focus |
|---|---|
| Shoulder AP | GH joint space, DJD |
| **Grashey (true AP)** | GH joint space, DJD, **proximal migration of humerus** |
| **AP in ER / AP in IR** | **Hill-Sachs** |
| **Axillary lateral** | **Anterior + posterior dislocation**, acromion |
| **Velpeau** | Axillary modification **if can't abduct arm** |
| **Scapular Y lateral** | **Anterior + posterior dislocation** |

| Additional view | Focus |
|---|---|
| **Supraspinatus outlet** | **Acromion morphology** — type I flat, II curved, **III hooked** (→ impingement + cuff pathology) |
| **Zanca** | **AC joint** — AC disease, **distal clavicle osteolysis**, distally projecting osteophytes |
| **Stryker notch** | **Hill-Sachs** |
| **West Point axillary** | **Anteroinferior glenoid — bony Bankart**, proximal humerus fracture |
| **Garth** | Anteroinferior glenoid — **bony Bankart** |
| **Hobbs** | **Anterior + posterior SC dislocation** |
| **Serendipity** | **Anterior + posterior SC dislocation** |

**Zanca technique (TO)** — beam angled **10–15° superiorly (cephalad)** + **kV ↓ to 50%** of standard

**Bony Bankart (TO)** — **West Point axillary** = popular view for **glenoid bone defect**\
✗ Wrong options: IR/Stryker = Hill-Sachs · serendipity · Greenspan

**Hill-Sachs (TO)** — **internal rotation view + Stryker notch view**

![West Point prone axillary (A) and Stryker notch (B) positioning](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17125111-1.png)

![Supraspinatus outlet view technique](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3038-supraspinatous-outlet-view-moved.jpg)

### Acromion morphology (outlet view)

| Type | Shape | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| I | **Flat** | — |
| II | **Curved** | — |
| **III** | **Hooked** | **Impingement + cuff pathology** |

### X-ray views to remember (TO)

| View | Use (TO) |
|---|---|
| **Axillary** | Lateral view of shoulder |
| **West Point axillary** | **Bankart lesion** |
| **IR view + Stryker notch** | **Hill-Sachs** |
| **Zanca** | **AC joint** |
| **Serendipity** | **SC joint** dislocation |
| **Greenspan** | **Radial head + capitellum** fractures |
| **Jones (AP) elbow** | Reduction of **supracondylar humerus** fracture |
| **Oblique + PA wrist in ulnar deviation** | **Scaphoid** fracture |
| **Brewerton** | **Metacarpal head** fractures |
| **Robert's** | **Thumb CMC** joint |
| **Carpal tunnel view** | **Hook of hamate** fracture |
| **Ball catcher's** | **RA hand erosions** |
| **Judet (obturator + iliac oblique)** | **Acetabular** fractures |
| **Frog leg** | Intra-op lateral hip |
| **Von Rosen** | **CDH** |
| **Axial / skyline / Merchant** | **Patellar subluxation** |
| **Mortise (AP ankle in 15° IR)** | Ankle injuries |
| **Canale** | **Talar neck** fractures |
| **Harris (hindfoot)** | **Calcaneus** fractures |
| **Broden** | Intra-op **calcaneal** fracture assessment |
| **Swimmer's** | Lateral C-spine with shoulder pulled down → **C6, C7, T1** |
| **Ferguson** | **20° caudocephalic AP lumbar** → L5 transverse process vs sacrum (**far-out syndrome**) |

### Key radiology findings – CT

| CT plane | Best for |
|---|---|
| **Axial** | **Reverse Hill-Sachs** |
| Coronal | **Fractures** |
| **Sagittal** | **Anteroinferior glenoid insufficiency** (bone loss) |
| **3D reconstruction** | **Glenoid version** for TSA |

### Key radiology findings – MRI & MR arthrogram

**ABER position** — sequence beyond coronal/sagittal/axial · hand **behind head** · **tensions anteroinferior GHL + labrum, relaxes capsule** → **Bankart**, **partial/full-thickness cuff tears**, **internal impingement**\
✗ Wrong options: true 90–90

**MR arthrogram** — percutaneous **dilute gadolinium** into joint → **labral + ligament** pathology: **Bankart**, **SLAP**, **GLAD**, **ALPSA**, **HAGL**

![MR arthrogram, Bankart lesion](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3038-shows-bankart-lesion.jpg)

## X vs Y

| Lesion | Best X-ray view | Best cross-sectional |
|---|---|---|
| **Hill-Sachs** | **AP in IR / ER**, **Stryker notch** | T1 MRI |
| **Reverse Hill-Sachs** | — | **Axial CT** |
| **Bony Bankart** | **West Point**, **Garth** | **Sagittal CT** (glenoid insufficiency) |
| **Soft-tissue Bankart / SLAP / HAGL** | — | **MR arthrogram**, ABER |
| **AC joint / DCO** | **Zanca** | — |
| **SC dislocation** | **Serendipity**, **Hobbs** | — |
| Cuff tear | Grashey (proximal migration) | **T2 MRI**; STIR (fatty vs oedema) |

## How it's asked

**"1.5 T proton energy as % of 3.0 T?"** → **11%** (3 T = 9×)

**"Best view for AC joint?"** → **Zanca view** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: serendipity · Greenspan · Brewerton

**"Best view for bony Bankart lesion?"** → **West Point axillary view** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: IR + Stryker notch · serendipity · Greenspan

**"Hill-Sachs best seen on?"** → **IR view / Stryker notch** (TO)

**"SC joint dislocation view?"** → **serendipity** (or Hobbs)

**"Can't abduct arm for axillary view?"** → **Velpeau**

**"Glenoid version before TSA?"** → **3D CT**

**"Reverse Hill-Sachs?"** → **axial CT**

**"MRI position for internal impingement / anteroinferior labrum?"** → **ABER**

**"Distinguish cuff muscle oedema vs fatty infiltration?"** → **STIR**
