# Shoulder Periprosthetic Fracture

*Shoulder & Elbow · 49 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — **0.6–3%** of shoulder arthroplasty (Orthobullets, OB) · **1.5–3%** in stemmed anatomic TSA (OB) · age **50–85**

**Share of complications** — **20% of all complications** (intra-op fractures, Orthobullets) · overall arthroplasty complication rate **11%**; post-op periprosthetic fractures = **6.6%** (reverse) vs **1.2%** (anatomic) of complications

**Timing** — **most are intra-operative**, at the index implant (anatomic + reverse); stemless: few post-op reports

**Location** — **adjacent to stem tip** · **greater tuberosity** (esp. **revision** TSA)

**Risk factors** — **osteoporosis** · **aggressive reaming** · endosteal notching · incorrect reaming entry (ideal **9 mm lateral** to anatomic centre of rotation) · **press-fit > cemented** stems · revision · soft-tissue contracture · instability history · ♀ · post-traumatic arthritis · ↑ comorbidity · osteonecrosis (**steroid** > alcohol, post-traumatic)

## 2 Mechanism

### Intra-operative

**Revision TSA** — highest risk: greater exposure, subacromial/cuff scarring → ↑ retraction + torque

| Intra-op step (revision) | Share of intra-op fractures |
|---|---|
| **Removing previous implant** | **Up to 81%** |
| Reaming / broaching | Up to 31% |
| Trialing / insertion | Up to 19% |
| Exposure (forceful retractor) | Up to 15% |

**Implant removal** — metaphyseal-filling → GT fracture (cement removal, interface disengagement) · diaphyseal-engaging → GT fracture (**metaphyseal stress shielding**) · prevent: clear lateral soft tissue, implant-specific tools, **controlled corticotomy**

**Primary TSA** — lower risk · **endosteal notching from reaming** · **excessive humeral ER during exposure** · cortical breach, esp. osteopenic bone

### Post-operative

**Fall (FOOSH)** — **stem tip = stress riser** → most post-op fractures **at/adjacent to stem tip** · fatigue fracture through stress riser

**Atraumatic** — **loosening** → implant "rattling" weakens cortex

**Nonunion risk ↑** — prosthesis **disrupts endosteal blood supply** · **stem distracts fragments** (no end-to-end healing)

**Nonunion factors** — osteoporosis · **RA** · ♀ · **displacement >2 mm** · **cement at fracture site**

## 3 Presentation

**History** — fall on outstretched hand · **prodromal / worsening pain** = mechanical loosening

**Exam** — **varus/valgus deformity**, ecchymosis, shortening, diffuse tenderness · can't move elbow/shoulder (**pain**) · document full NV exam

**Radial nerve** — check status; palsy common with distal humerus fracture, **mostly neurapraxia**\
✗ Wrong options: surgery for it

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — orthogonal humerus: **AP, axillary lateral, Grashey, scapular Y** · fracture at **stem tip**, usually **oblique** · decide on **stem stability** + **zones of lucency** (no ingrowth / osteolysis)

**CT (metal suppression)** — concern for **loose stem** (pattern, prodromal pain) or comminution · morphology, bone stock, cuff muscle quality, glenoid version (if revising)

![Loose humeral stem — axillary lateral](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422742-axillary-lateral-radiograph-of-the-right-shoulder-with-loose.jpg)

### Wright and Cofield classification

| Type | Fracture | Diagram |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | Centred near **stem tip**, extends **proximally** ("proximal to stem tip" — OB) | ![Wright and Cofield](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422742-diagram-depicting-the-wright-and-cofield-periprosthetic-hume.jpg) |
| **B** | Centred near **stem tip**, extends **distally** (at level of tip) | |
| **C** | **Distal to stem tip** (into distal metaphysis) | |

**Wright series (Mayo)** — 499 arthroplasties → **9 fractures** (1 A, 5 B, 3 C) · 3 splinted → **failed to heal**

## 5 Management

```
Post-operative periprosthetic humerus fracture
├─ Loose stem (any type) → revision arthroplasty + supplementary fixation
│      (poor stock → long stem bypassing 2–3 cortical diameters; good stock → shorter stem + fixation)
├─ Stable stem, type A/B → ORIF: hybrid locking plate + cerclage cables
│      (long spiral/oblique A/B → bracing possible, union <50%)
└─ Type C → fracture brace (nonop)
       └─ fails / nonunion → ORIF ± allograft strut
```

```
Intra-operative fracture
├─ Type A → stem spanning fracture by 2 cortical diameters
├─ Type B / C → proximally porous-coated long stem, 2–3 cortical diameters
└─ Type C unsuitable for long stem → ORIF
```

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Long oblique / spiral A or B, stable stem** · **type C** | Immobilisation (coaptation → **functional brace**); **union <50%** |
| **Intra-op type A** | Longer stem spanning **2 cortical diameters** ± supplementary fixation |
| **Intra-op type B or C** | **Proximally porous-coated long stem**, **2–3 cortical diameters**; distal cement |
| **Post-op transverse A/B, stable stem** · C failed nonop · can't tolerate nonop | **ORIF** |
| **Loose prosthesis, any type** | **Revision arthroplasty + supplementary fixation** |
| Poor bone stock (loose) | **Long stem** bypass ≥2–3 cortical diameters ± allograft |
| Good bone stock (loose) | **Short stem** (ends proximal to original) + fixation; tip doesn't distract fracture |

**Source nuance** — long spiral **type B**, stable stem: bracing possible (Orthobullets) · **ORIF hybrid plate + cables** best for active patient (OB) — read the stem

### Stable-stem fractures – ORIF with plate/cerclage & radial nerve risk

**Best for stable stem A/B** — **ORIF hybrid locking plate + cerclage cables**\
✗ Wrong options: revision (stem well fixed) · **lag screw + neutralisation** · nonop near stem tip → nonunion

![Hybrid locking plate and cerclage cables](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob422742-screen-shot-2020-04-12-at-5-28-43-pm.jpg)

**Construct** — plate placed **eccentrically** → locking screws skive past stem (bicortical) · **cables proximal** to stem tip · **screws distal** · usually **4.5 mm LC-DCP** · alternatives: dual plates, **3.5 mm attachment plates** · deficient cortex → **cortical strut allograft** + cables (2 hemicylinders = "sarcophagus")

**Approach** — **anterolateral** (extend deltopectoral: coracoid → DP groove → lateral biceps) · biceps–brachialis interval, **split brachialis** (medial = musculocutaneous, lateral = radial) · **posterolateral** for distal fracture with stable stem

**GT / calcar fracture** — suture or cable fixation

**No nonop with sling / hanging arm cast** — bracing is the nonop method; active patient wants fast return → ORIF

**No APC (allograft-prosthetic composite)** — not with good bone quality + no bone loss

**Evidence** — Kurowicki: 7 type B ORIF without revision → **all united** · Andersen: ORIF 17/17, revision 18/19 united · stable IM stem + supplementary fixation > loose stem

### Radial nerve and cerclage

| Landmark | Distance to radial nerve at spiral groove |
|---|---|
| Coracoid | ~13 cm |
| Distal lesser tuberosity | ~10 cm |
| Superior latissimus insertion | ~8 cm |
| **Inferior latissimus insertion** | **~4 cm** |

**Cable 4 cm below inferior LD insertion** → **iatrogenic radial nerve injury**\
✗ Wrong options: median (medial septum) · axillary (proximal) · subscapularis · brachial artery (superficial, anteromedial)

**Safe zone** — cables **just inferior / proximal to inferior edge of LD insertion**

![Radial nerve in spiral groove](../../MCQ/attachments/q213101-illa-radial-nerve.jpg)

### Long stem technique

**Porous-coated long stem** — proximal coating = metaphyseal stability · **pack cement distally**, **avoid cement extrusion into fracture** · strut + cables if deficient · **olecranon fossa** may prevent it

## 6 Complications

**Complications** — **nonunion** · delayed union · **GH stiffness** · **radial nerve injury** (fracture or **iatrogenic cerclage**)

**Prognosis** — high union when treated appropriately; union **2–8 months** · **significant ↓ shoulder ROM**

## X vs Y

| | **Stable stem** | **Loose stem** |
|---|---|---|
| Clues | No lucency, no prodromal pain | Lucent zones, **prodromal pain**, "rattling" |
| Type A/B | **ORIF hybrid plate + cables** | **Revision long stem** + fixation |
| Type C | Brace → ORIF if fails | Revision |

## How it's asked

**"Fall, stable rTSA stem, type B fracture — best treatment?"** → **ORIF hybrid locking plate + cerclage cables**\
✗ Wrong options: revision cemented/cementless long stem · lag screw · nonop

**"Cable placed 4 cm inferior to latissimus insertion — complication?"** → **iatrogenic radial nerve injury**\
✗ Wrong options: median · axillary · brachial artery

**"Active 69-year-old, T-score −0.45, long spiral B, stable stem — treatment?"** → **ORIF plate + screws/cerclage cables**\
✗ Wrong options: sling · hanging cast · revision · APC

**"Loose stem with any fracture type?"** → **revision arthroplasty + supplementary fixation**

**"Intra-op fracture — stem should bypass by?"** → **2 cortical diameters** (2–3 for type B/C)

**"Why high nonunion near stem tip?"** → **disrupted endosteal blood supply** + stem distracts fragments

**"Most common step causing intra-op fracture in revision?"** → **implant removal** (up to 81%)

**"Type C fracture, stable stem?"** → **fracture brace**\
✗ Wrong options: sling

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Shoulder Prosthetic Joint Infection

*Shoulder & Elbow · 50 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Significance** — **major cause of revision within first 2 years** after shoulder arthroplasty

**Incidence** — **~4% primary TSA + RSA** (Orthobullets, OB) · 3–4% · 0–5% · up to 4–5% (other OB explanations) — read the options · **~15% revision** TSA/RSA

**aTSA vs rTSA** — **no difference** in primary shoulders without prior surgery · ↑ in primary **rTSA after ≥1 prior non-arthroplasty operation** (Florschütz)

### Risk factors & prevention (smoking, steroid injection, nasal decolonisation)

| Risk factor | Note |
|---|---|
| **Male** | **2.5×** PJI rate · ↑ C. acnes colonisation |
| **Arthroplasty for fracture / trauma** | **3×** vs elective |
| **Revision surgery** | ~15% |
| **Intra-articular steroid injection within 3 months** | Injection **4 months** (or 15 months) before = **not** a risk factor |
| **Smoking** | ↑ infection + fracture + loosening |
| **Young age** | — |
| Postoperative haematoma | — |
| **Coagulopathy**, **ESRD**, **lupus**, **RA**, uncontrolled comorbidities | Medical comorbidities |

**Pre-op smoker** — **smoking cessation programme next** (smoking = suggested contraindication to TSA/RSA) \
✗ Wrong options: PT trial (pseudoparalysis CTA) · tramadol (= 1st line knee OA) · CBC/ESR/CRP (infection unlikely) · rheumatology referral

**Complication risk (patient)** — younger age · ↑ BMI · RA · diabetes · smoking

**C. acnes colonisation correlates** — **male** · **native hair at surgical site** · **humeral loosening**\
✗ Wrong options: age >70 · CKD · female · glenoid lucent lines

| ✓ Validated prevention | Detail |
|---|---|
| **MRSA (methicillin-resistant Staphylococcus aureus) screening + nasal decolonisation** | If nares colonized → application of **5% povidone-iodine solution to each nostril** pre-operatively (**10 s/nostril, 1 h pre-op**) · or **2% mupirocin BID × 5 days** |
| IV antibiotic prophylaxis | Within **30 min** of incision, continue **24 h** |
| Penicillin allergy prophylaxis | **Clindamycin for C. acnes** (Orthobullets, OB) · **vancomycin, not clindamycin** (OB) — read the options |
| Skin prep | **Chlorhexidine (ChloraPrep)** · hydrogen peroxide prep |
| Hair removal | **Electric clippers** |
| Intra-op | Frequent **saline irrigation** · **dilute povidone-iodine** before closure |
| Closure / dressing | Dermabond · **silver-impregnated** dressings |

| Not validated | Note |
|---|---|
| **Axillary hair shaving** | No meaningful ↓ |
| **Sterile helmets/hoods** | May **↑** infection |
| **Laminar flow** | Not validated |

## 2 Mechanism

### C. acnes – microbiology, prolonged culture & antibiotic choice

**Most common organism** — **Cutibacterium acnes** (formerly Propionibacterium acnes) · others: S. aureus, S. epidermidis

**Description** — **gram-positive, anaerobic rod** (OB) · **gram-positive rod, facultative anaerobe** (OB) · "gram-positive, facultative, aerotolerant, anaerobic rod" (Orthobullets) · **ferments lactose → propionic acid** · non-spore-forming

**Habitat** — **axilla**, dermal **sebaceous glands**; commensal skin flora

**Biofilm** — forms in **18–90 h** on implant + synovium (>> planktonic) → **aspiration only 17% sensitive**

**Behaviour** — indolent, low-grade, low virulence · usually **no swelling, erythema, fever, pus** · inflammatory markers **often normal**

**Culture** — mean incubation **7–21 days** · usually negative in first **3–5 days** · may take **up to 3 weeks**

![C. acnes – gram-positive rods](../../MCQ/attachments/q218951-e-figure-e.jpg)

| ✗ Organism distractor | Description |
|---|---|
| **S. aureus** | GP cocci, facultative anaerobe, **coagulase +** · commonest in **hip/knee** PJI |
| **S. epidermidis** | GP cocci, facultative anaerobe, **coagulase −** |
| **E. coli** | GN rod, facultative anaerobe · rare in shoulder |
| **Klebsiella** | GN **encapsulated** rod |
| N. gonorrhoeae | GN diplococci |
| C. diphtheriae | GP **club-shaped**, nonmotile, noncapsulated bacillus |
| Streptococci · fungi | Rare · exceedingly rare |

### Biofilm & timing

**Biofilm (all bacteria)** — on implant **within 4 weeks** · **15% cells + 85% polysaccharide (glycocalyx)** → adherence + immune protection · no safe removal → **explant if >4 wk**

**Source** — **direct** (sinus tract, wound dehiscence) · **haematogenous** (dental work, gallbladder) in longstanding infection-free joint

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **persistent pain (most common)** · **draining sinus (2nd)** · systemic (fever, chills, sweats) · **stiffness**

**Sinus tract to joint** — **diagnostic**

**Typical C. acnes stem** — months–years after arthroplasty, **gradually ↑ pain**, normal WBC/ESR/CRP, humeral lucency

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Classification – time of onset

| | **Acute** | **Chronic** |
|---|---|---|
| Timing | **<3–6 wk** (CDC **<90 days**) | **>3–6 wk** (CDC **>90 days**) |
| Biology | Confined to joint; no bone–implant invasion; less biofilm | **Biofilm**; invades **prosthesis–bone interface** |
| Treatment | **I&D + retention (DAIR)** | **2-stage revision** |

**DAIR window** — **<4 wk** (Orthobullets treatment) · **within 5–6 wk** (OB) · <3–6 wk (Orthobullets classification) — read the options

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray** — normal early · osteopenia · **periprosthetic lucencies** · pseudosubluxation of head · effusion · **endosteal scalloping** · bony resorption

**Humeral vs glenoid loosening** — **glenoid loosening = most common cause of TSA failure**, not specific for infection · **humeral loosening → suspect PJI**

![Glenoid radiolucency after TSA](../../MCQ/attachments/q216841-a-tsa-poly.jpg)

**US / MRI** — fluid collections (US: loculated away from GH joint)

**Bone scan** — not routinely (efficacy not shown)

**PET** — adjunct; **98% sensitivity + 98% specificity**

### Genes & lab markers

| Marker | Fact |
|---|---|
| WBC | Not sensitive/specific; **normal in C. acnes** |
| **CRP** | Peaks **2–3 d** post-op → normal **14–21 d**; may be normal in C. acnes |
| **ESR** | Peaks **5–7 d** → normal **90 d**; may be normal |
| ESR/CRP overall | **Insufficient sensitivity/specificity** for PJI |
| Alpha-defensin | Investigational |
| Synovial WBC | **>50,000** |
| Crystals | **Don't exclude** infection |
| Gram stain | Often **negative**; + in ~75% |

### Diagnostic work-up – aspiration

**Synovial tissue culture** — **gold standard** (+ ~80%) · **≥4 specimens** at surgery · **aerobic + anaerobic + broth** media

**Hold cultures** — **14–28 days** (Orthobullets) · **14–21 days / 2–3 weeks** (OB) — read the options · fungal **4 wk** · mycobacterial **8 wk**

**Aspiration** — **low sensitivity + low NPV** · **dry tap common** → **does not rule out** · **+ S. aureus culture = high specificity + PPV**

**Fluoroscopic / US guidance** — ensures intra-articular placement · **no ↑ PPV/NPV or yield**, operator-dependent, ↑ cost

**Antibiotics** — stop **2 weeks before aspiration**\
✗ Wrong options: current antibiotics don't affect yield

**No growth at 5 days** — **observe cultures additional 2 weeks** before revision\
✗ Wrong options: revision · arthroscopic I&D

## 5 Management

```
Shoulder PJI
├─ Unfit / unwilling → antibiotic suppression (fails 60–75%)
├─ Acute (<4 wk; 5–6 wk OB) → I&D + component retention (DAIR)
│      └─ exchange mobile parts (glenosphere, poly liner, tray)
└─ Chronic
       ├─ fit, unknown organism, adequate bone → 2-stage exchange (gold standard)
       ├─ low-virulence known organism, healthy tissue → 1-stage revision
       └─ frail / poor bone / recalcitrant → resection arthroplasty
```

### Antibiotics

**C. acnes drug of choice** — **penicillin** (low MIC) · penicillin G, amoxicillin \
✗ Wrong options: TMP-SMX · meropenem · vancomycin (not 1st line)

**Penicillin allergy** — **ceftriaxone or clindamycin**

**Duration** — prolonged IV; guided by cultures + ID consult

| Mechanism of action | Antibiotic |
|---|---|
| **Binds transpeptidase (PBP) → inhibits cell wall synthesis** | **Penicillin** ✓ |
| Reversibly binds **50S** | Macrolides |
| **Irreversibly** binds **30S** | Aminoglycosides |
| Blocks **30S–50S initiation complex** | **Linezolid** |
| Binds cell wall, disrupts peptidoglycan **cross-linking** | **Vancomycin** |

### Operative criteria

| Option | Indications | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| **Antibiotic suppression** | Severely ill, unwilling, multiple comorbidities | **Fails 60–75%** |
| **I&D + retention** | **Acute** infection | **50% failure** |
| **Single-stage revision** | Uncommon; **low-virulence** organism with good sensitivity; healthy patient + soft tissue | Up to **90%**; ↓ stay, cost, antibiotic time · consider **RSA** (cuff sacrificed) |
| **2-stage exchange** | **Gold standard**; medically fit; **unknown organism**; adequate bone stock; eradication before stage 2 | **60–90%**; some keep spacer |
| **Resection arthroplasty** | Frail/complex, **insufficient bone stock**, recalcitrant | Poor function; **pain relief >50%**; **tuberosity preservation** predicts better result |
| Arthrodesis | Rare (bone stock poor) | Pain ↓ |
| Amputation | **Life-threatening** infection | — |

**Spacer** — antibiotic-PMMA; local antibiotics, **pseudocapsule**, maintains soft-tissue tension, ↓ pain, allows PT · stem from **Steinmann pin + chest tube**

![Resection arthroplasty](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob12757-resection-arthroplasty.jpg)

**Resection** — **antero-superior subluxation** if tuberosities + cuff removed

**Arthrodesis** — S-shaped incision · resect cuff, biceps tenodesis, decorticate glenoid/head/acromion undersurface · **10-hole 4.5 mm pelvic recon plate** · **30° abduction, 30° flexion, 30° IR** · complications: infection, nonunion, malposition, prominent hardware, humeral shaft fracture

**Approach** — **deltopectoral** for all

## 6 Complications

**Complications** — **failure to eradicate** (inadequate clearance after suppression, I&D, 1-stage) · recurrent infection (2-stage) · humerus/glenoid fracture · poor bone stock

## X vs Y

| | **Shoulder PJI** | **Hip/knee PJI** |
|---|---|---|
| Commonest organism | **C. acnes** | **S. aureus / S. epidermidis** |
| Inflammatory markers | **Often normal** (C. acnes) | ↑ with S. aureus / MRSA |
| Aspiration value | Less clear: low sensitivity, dry taps | Clearer |
| Gold-standard treatment | 2-stage | 2-stage |

## How it's asked

**"rTSA, drainage at 4 wk, I&D + poly exchange, cultures negative at 10 d — MOA of best antibiotic?"** → **binds transpeptidase, inhibits cell wall synthesis (penicillin)**\
✗ Wrong options: 50S macrolide · 30S aminoglycoside · linezolid · vancomycin

**"Normal labs, painful aTSA, aspiration — hold cultures 2–3 wk for?"** → **gram-positive, anaerobic bacteria**\
✗ Wrong options: GP aerobic cocci · GN rods · diplococci · club-shaped

**"18-mo TSA, lucent lines, normal labs, no growth at 5 d — next step?"** → **observe cultures additional 2 weeks**\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopic I&D · revision

**"Independent correlate of P. acnes colonisation?"** → **native hair at surgical site**\
✗ Wrong options: age >70 · CKD · female · glenoid lucent lines

**"aTSA 2 mo ago, infection confirmed — organism characteristics?"** → **gram-positive rod, facultative anaerobe**\
✗ Wrong options: coagulase + / − cocci · GN rods

**"C. acnes grown — antibiotic of choice?"** → **penicillin**\
✗ Wrong options: TMP-SMX · clindamycin = allergy only · meropenem · vancomycin

**"Smoker with CTA planned for arthroplasty — next step?"** → **smoking cessation programme**

**"rTSA infection — NOT a risk factor?"** → **steroid injection 4 months before surgery** (✓ risk: coagulopathy, fracture, male, smoking)

**"Validated PJI prevention before rTSA?"** → **5% povidone-iodine to each nostril if MRSA-colonised**\
✗ Wrong options: axillary shaving · helmets · laminar flow · clindamycin for allergy

**"rTSA 2 y, ↑ ESR/CRP — most common organism (Gram picture)?"** → **gram-positive rod (C. acnes)**\
✗ Wrong options: staph · strep · E. coli · fungus

**"Shoulder aspiration — true statement?"** → **positive S. aureus culture = high specificity**\
✗ Wrong options: high sensitivity/NPV · dry tap rules out · fluoroscopy ↑ PPV/NPV · antibiotics don't matter

**"Gold standard for chronic shoulder PJI?"** → **2-stage exchange**

**"Acute PJI within weeks of surgery?"** → **I&D + component retention (DAIR)**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# SLAP Lesion

*Shoulder & Elbow · 51 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — uncommon: **<5%** of shoulder injuries · yet a common cause of pain + weakness in throwers

**Who** — **dominant shoulder of overhead/throwing athletes** · older patients after traumatic falls

**Risk factor** — **GIRD**

**Associations** — internal impingement · GIRD · **articular-sided** cuff tears · (subtle) instability · scapular dyskinesis · paralabral cyst · sublabral clefts, Buford complex

### Anatomy – biceps anchor, Buford complex & vascularity

**LHB attachment** — **~50% to supraglenoid tubercle, ~50% to labrum** (predominantly posterior labrum) · tubercle **>6 mm medial** to glenoid surface

**Posterior / posterior-dominant attachment** — **~70%** of shoulders (✗ Wrong options: 15% · 30% · 100%) · most common: **posterior to 12 o'clock** (✗ Wrong options: "anchored anteriorly")

| Tuoheti type | Biceps attachment | Share |
|---|---|---|
| I | Completely posterior | I + II **>70%** |
| II | Predominantly posterior | |
| III | Anterior + posterior | 25% |
| IV | Anterior | 5% |

**Biceps function** — torsional + horizontal stability in **late cocking** · **poorest blood supply** in intra-articular course

**Labrum** — fibrocartilage; **chock block** to subluxation · superior labrum attaches more **medially** + anchors biceps = **weak link**

**Labral blood supply** — **suprascapular, circumflex scapular, posterior humeral circumflex** arteries via **capsule + periosteum** · **anterosuperior labrum = poorest** → degenerative tears, poor healing\
✗ Wrong options: bone · single source

| Variant | Feature | Pitfall |
|---|---|---|
| **Sublabral recess / foramen** | ± thickened MGHL | Mistaken for tear on MRI |
| **Buford complex** | **Cord-like MGHL + absent anterosuperior labrum**; **normal variant**, **1.5%** | **Do not repair → significant loss of ER** + pain |
| **Meniscoid labrum** | **1%** | — |
| Superior MGHL attachment | On glenoid | — |

![Buford complex — arthroscopic view](../../MCQ/attachments/q101-illa-buford-comples-scope-view.jpg)

**Arthroscopy tell** — erythema + tearing **under** labrum = tear (vs normal recess)

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanisms** — **repetitive overhead activity** · FOOSH with **tensed biceps** · traction on arm

**Thrower pathoanatomy** — tight **posterior IGHL** → GH contact shifts **posterosuperiorly** → ↑ shear on superior labrum → **"peel-back"**

**Consequence** — SLAP **↑ strain on anterior band IGHL** → ↓ stability

## 3 Presentation

**History** — "pop" during overhead activity or trauma · **lag** between injury + symptoms

**Symptoms** — **vague deep shoulder pain** · popping/clicking · weakness, fatigue, ↓ performance

**Exam** — bicipital groove tenderness · check **IR + total arc** vs contralateral · SS ± IS **atrophy** (suprascapular neuropathy from cyst) · apprehension positive in **85%**

**Tests** — **no single specific test**

| Test group | Tests |
|---|---|
| Biceps provocation | **Speed's**, **Yergason's**, Kim biceps load |
| SLAP | **O'Brien (active compression)**, **crank** (full abduction, load + rotate), **dynamic labral shear**, Kibler anterior slide |

### Related topics (distal clavicle excision, biceps dislocation)

**Arthroscopic vs open DCE** — advantage = **evaluate GH joint** · failed open DCE: **15/20 had SLAP** (9/15 good–excellent after arthroscopic treatment)\
✗ Wrong options: fewer complications · infection · time

**Arthroscopic DCE** — sacrifices **inferior** AC ligament, **preserves superior** AC ligament

**Medial LHB dislocation** — **subscapularis tear**\
✗ Wrong options: supraspinatus (LHB rupture) · transverse ligament · shallow groove · type I SLAP; type II SLAP can cause instability

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Diagnosis – MR arthrogram

**X-ray** — usually normal

**Study of choice** — **MR arthrogram** · **gold standard = arthroscopy**\
✗ Wrong options: US · EMG · labs · PT first when suspicious

**MRI sign** — T2 signal between superior labrum + glenoid, **lateral to glenoid rim, posterior to biceps**

**Accuracy** — sens **~50%**, spec **~90%**, ↑ with arthrogram (Orthobullets) · linear high signal on oblique coronal T2: sens **41%**, spec **86%**, accuracy 63% (OB) — read the options

**Paralabral ganglion cyst** — **highly specific for labral tear**; usually **spinoglenoid notch**\
✗ Wrong options: suprascapular notch

**Overdiagnosis** — concern (↑ SLAP repairs in ABOS case logs)

**Arthroscopic peel-back test** — labrum peels back at **90° abduction + ER**

### SLAP classification

| Type | Orthobullets description (share) | Clock face (TO) | TO description |
|---|---|---|---|
| **I** | Labral + biceps **fraying, anchor intact** (11%) | 11–1 | Fraying superior labrum |
| **II** | Fraying + **detached biceps anchor** (**41%**, most common) | 11–1 | Detachment of biceps anchor |
| **III** | **Bucket handle, intact anchor** (33%); biceps separates from fragment | 11–1 | **Bucket handle, intact biceps** |
| **IV** | **Bucket handle + detached anchor** (15%); biceps stays with fragment | 11–1 | Bucket handle with **biceps extension** |
| V | II + **anteroinferior extension (Bankart)** | **11–5** | Bankart + SLAP |
| VI | II + **unstable flap** | 11–1 | Flap tear of bucket-handle SLAP |
| VII | II + **MGHL injury** | **11–3** | Extension into MGHL |
| VIII | II + **posterior extension** | **11–7** | Posterior extension |
| IX | **Circumferential** | **11–11** | Pan-labral |
| X | II + posteroinferior extension (**reverse Bankart**) (Orthobullets) | **11–1+** | SLAP + **noncontiguous** posterior labral tear (TO) |

![SLAP types I–IV](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3053-classification-illustration-moved.jpg)

**Systems** — **Snyder** = I–IV · **Maffet** added V–VII · high inter/intra-observer variability

**Type X wording** — "posteroinferior extension / reverse Bankart" (Orthobullets) · "noncontiguous posterior labral tear" (TO) — read the options

### Classification & variants (type III, Andrews lesion) (TO)

**Dynamic labral shear + tear 11–1 o'clock, intact biceps (TO)** — **type III** SLAP\
✗ Wrong options: type II = biceps anchor detached · Bankart

**Andrews lesion (TO)** — **anterior + superior labral detachment** **without extension posterior to biceps** footplate · **throwers** \
✗ Wrong options: SLAP II · posterosuperior · anterior + posterior · posterior IGHL avulsion

## 5 Management

```
SLAP lesion
├─ First line → rest + PT + NSAIDs (scapula, cuff; treat GIRD)
├─ Failed nonop, type I / III / IV <1/3 biceps → arthroscopic debridement
├─ Failed nonop, type II, young overhead athlete → SLAP repair
│      └─ >40 y (failure >36 y) → debridement + biceps tenodesis/tenotomy
└─ Type IV >1/3 biceps → biceps tenodesis/tenotomy ± labral repair/debridement
```

### Treatment – nonoperative vs repair vs tenodesis (age, type IV) & return to sport

**Initial ✓** — **rest + rehab for scapular mechanics + cuff strengthening** (+ NSAIDs; address GIRD)\
✗ Wrong options: aggressive **concentric biceps** strengthening (aggravates) · surgery first

**Labral tear in thrower** — initial: **posterior capsular stretching + cuff strengthening**

### Operative criteria

| Situation (failed nonop, severe symptoms) | Procedure |
|---|---|
| **Type I, III, IV <1/3 biceps** | **Arthroscopic debridement** (III → debridement ± repair of fragment) |
| **Type II, young overhead athlete** | **Labral repair** (most amenable to repair) |
| **Type II, >40 y** · biceps fraying **>50%** | **Debridement + biceps tenotomy/tenodesis** (✗ Wrong options: repair (stiffness)) |
| **Type IV with >1/3 (>30%) biceps** | **Biceps tenodesis** ± labral repair/debridement |
| Type V (II + Bankart) · II + posterior extension | **SLAP + Bankart repair** (active patient) |
| Paralabral cyst + SLAP II | **Cyst decompression + SLAP repair** |

**Age thresholds** — tenodesis consensus **>40 y** (Orthobullets, OB) · repair failure **>36 y** (Orthobullets, OB) · stiffness risk **>35 y** (OB) — read the options

**Tenodesis in young** — viable even **<25 y** athletes (recent studies)

**Repair failure factor** — **age >36**\
✗ Wrong options: recreational swimming · male · sedentary job · type II

### Return to sport

| Group / treatment | Rate |
|---|---|
| Same level after repair (overall) | **60–70%** |
| Return to prior play after repair | **50–60%** |
| Non-pitchers returning to baseball after repair | **65–85%** |
| Nonop: return to play | **~40%** |
| Nonop: return to prior play | **20–30%** |

**Pitchers vs non-pitchers** — **non-pitchers return at higher rates** after repair · RTP similar for repair vs tenodesis · elite throwers: variable, unpredictable

### Repair technique

**Steps** — debride flaps · **decompress cysts** · accessory/percutaneous portals for anchors near rim · **sutures anterior to biceps → stiffness**

**Tenodesis** — all-arthroscopic or mini-open; tenotomise near labrum

### Postoperative rehabilitation after SLAP repair

| Period | Allowed | Avoid |
|---|---|---|
| **Wk 0–4** | **Passive + active-assisted flexion in scapular plane**; passive **0–90° FE** | **Passive ER at 90° abduction** (~4 wk), extremes of abduction + ER, **resisted biceps** |
| Wk 4–6 | Active ROM, isometrics; **isotonic cuff** 4–6 wk | **Resisted elbow flexion until 6 wk** |
| Wk 6–12 (to 8) | Functional + light strengthening | — |
| Wk 8–16 / 12+ | **Sport-specific** strengthening | — |
| **~6 months** | Return to sport | — |

**Limit after repair** — **abduction + ER** — max tension on repair\
✗ Wrong options: IR

**✗ Rehab traps** — immediate full active ROM · **6 wk sling, no motion** (stiffness) · cuff strengthening by wk 2 · eccentric biceps at wk 2 · latissimus pull-downs

## 6 Complications

### Stiffness

**Most common** after SLAP repair (~78%, Orthobullets) · risk: incidental SLAP repaired in **>45 y** during cuff repair · **post-surgical adhesive capsulitis**

**Treatment** — early pendulum/AAROM from wk 1 → **capsular release** if fails >6 months PT

### Suprascapular nerve – anchor placement & paralabral cyst

**Mechanism** — deep drilling / anchors from **anterior or anterosuperior portal** · instrumentation **medial to glenoid** · overdrilling glenoid

**Distance** — SSN **1.5–2.0 cm** from glenoid cortex at scapular spine level

**Origin** — SSN from **superior (upper) trunk**\
✗ Wrong options: middle trunk (no branches) · C5–7 roots = long thoracic · posterior/medial cord

**✗ At risk distractors** — axillary nerve, thoracoacromial artery, thoracodorsal nerve, superior thoracic artery

![Spinoglenoid paralabral cyst](../../MCQ/attachments/q9037-illa-mr-spinoglenoid-cyst.jpg)

| Cyst location | Muscles affected |
|---|---|
| **Suprascapular notch** | **SS + IS** |
| **Spinoglenoid notch** | **IS only** |

**Cyst management** — no structural lesion → rehab · with labral tear → **decompression + labral repair**

### Failed repair

**Causes** — chondral injury, hardware failure, **anchor pullout** · age **>36** → tenodesis better

## X vs Y

| | **Buford complex** | **Sublabral foramen** | **SLAP II** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Labrum | **Absent** anterosuperior | Present; recess/foramen beneath | Detached at biceps anchor |
| MGHL | **Cord-like** | ± thickened | — |
| Key point | **Do not repair** (↓ ER) | MRI pitfall — not a tear | Repair / tenodesis |

| | **SLAP III** | **SLAP IV** | **Andrews (TO)** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Labrum | Bucket handle | Bucket handle | Anterosuperior detachment |
| Biceps | **Intact** | **Torn into** | Not posterior to footplate |

| Distractor lesion | Key feature |
|---|---|
| **HAGL** | Humeral avulsion of anterior IGHL |
| **ALPSA** | Capsulolabral periosteal sleeve displaced **medially** on glenoid neck (axial) |
| **Bankart** | Anteroinferior labrum, after anterior dislocation |
| Hill-Sachs | Anterior instability; engaging defects → surgery |
| Reverse Hill-Sachs | Posterior instability; surgery if **>20%** of articular surface |

## How it's asked

**"MRI tear 11–1 o'clock, intact biceps, positive dynamic labral shear?"** → **SLAP type III** (TO)

**"Andrews lesion?"** → **anterior + superior labral tear** (TO)

**"Buford complex?"** → **normal variant: cord-like MGHL + absent anterosuperior labrum**

**"% posterior/posterior-dominant biceps attachment?"** → **70%**

**"True about the superior labrum (SLAP on MRI)?"** → **anterosuperior portion most susceptible to vascular insufficiency**\
✗ Wrong options: anterior anchor · single artery · suprascapular notch cyst · limit IR after repair

**"FOOSH, overhead athlete, MRI superior labral signal?"** → **SLAP tear**\
✗ Wrong options: HAGL · ALPSA · Bankart

**"Pitcher, O'Brien + Jobe relocation, total arc 180° — next?"** → **MR arthrogram**

**"47-year-old, type II SLAP, biceps fraying >50%, failed 8 months nonop?"** → **debridement + biceps tenotomy/tenodesis**\
✗ Wrong options: SLAP repair

**"Factor associated with SLAP repair failure?"** → **age >36**

**"Third-baseman, type II SLAP — counsel?"** → **better return to same level than pitchers**

**"38-year-old, O'Brien/Speed/Yergason +, initial treatment?"** → **rest + rehab for scapular mechanics + cuff**

**"Finding favouring tenodesis over repair?"** → **type IV with 40% biceps involvement**

**"Rehab after type II SLAP repair?"** → **limited passive 4 wk → active to 8 wk → sport-specific to 12–16 wk**

**"Avoid at 2 weeks after SLAP repair?"** → **passive ER at 90° abduction**

**"Immediate rehab after SLAP repair?"** → **passive + active-assisted flexion in scapular plane**

**"Anchor drilling from anterosuperior portal — structure at risk?"** → **suprascapular nerve**

**"SLAP + cyst on MRI — treatment?"** → **supraglenoid cyst decompression + SLAP repair**

**"Instrument passed medial to glenoid — nerve origin?"** → **superior trunk**

**"Arthroscopic DCE advantage?"** → **evaluation of GH joint**

**"Medial biceps dislocation cause?"** → **subscapularis tear**

# Sternoclavicular Joint

*Shoulder & Elbow · 52 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Joint anatomy

**Type** — **diarthrodial saddle** joint, **incongruous (~50% contact)**; **fibrocartilage**; contains **intra-articular disc**\
✗ Wrong options: hyaline/synovial cartilage

**Motion** — arm elevation to **90° → 30° rotation** at SC joint

![SC joint: disc, anterior/posterior SC, costoclavicular, interclavicular ligaments](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3034-sternoclavicular-joint.jpg)

### Stabilising ligaments

| Ligament | Function |
|---|---|
| **Posterior SC capsular ligament** | **Primary restraint to anterior–posterior stability** |
| **Anterior SC ligament** | Primary restraint to **superior displacement** of medial clavicle |
| **Costoclavicular (rhomboid)** — anterior fasciculus | Resists **superior rotation + lateral displacement** |
| **Costoclavicular** — posterior fasciculus | Resists **inferior rotation + medial displacement** |
| **Intra-articular disc ligament** | **Prevents medial displacement**; secondary restraint to superior displacement |

### Medial clavicle anatomy

**First to ossify** — **clavicle**, **5th–6th week of gestation**

**Last to fuse** — **medial clavicular epiphysis**, closes **20–25 y** (last physis)

**Before 25 y** — most "SC dislocations" are **medial clavicular physeal fractures** → distinguish

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**Serendipity view** — **40° cephalic tilt** of sternum + clavicles; best plain radiograph

![Serendipity view: 40° cephalic tilt](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3034-serendipity-view.jpg)

**CT** — **most sensitive + specific**

## 5 Management

### Instability after medial clavicle resection – figure-of-eight reconstruction

**Cause** — medial clavicle resection (e.g. for **SC degenerative joint disease**) compromising **costoclavicular ligament** → **medial clavicular instability**: ↑ pain, **medial bump**, digit tingling

**Treatment** — **semitendinosus figure-of-eight graft** through **clavicle + manubrium** → best **peak load-to-failure**

(✗ Wrong options: **Distractors** — subclavius tendon transfer) · medial clavicular osteotomy · further medial resection · SC arthrodesis

## X vs Y

| | **SC dislocation** | **Medial clavicle physeal fracture** |
|---|---|---|
| Age | Adult (physis closed) | **<25 y** (physis closes 20–25) |
| Frequency <25 y | Less common | **Most injuries** |

## How it's asked

**"Instability after medial clavicle resection (medial bump, tingling) — procedure?"** → **semitendinosus figure-of-eight graft**\
✗ Wrong options: subclavius transfer · further resection · arthrodesis

**"Primary restraint to AP translation of SC joint?"** → **posterior SC capsular ligament**

**"Ligament preventing medial displacement of clavicle?"** → **intra-articular disc ligament**

**"Best plain view of SC joint?"** → **serendipity view (40° cephalic tilt)**; **CT** most sensitive

**"Last physis to close?"** → **medial clavicle, 20–25 y**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Subacromial Impingement

*Shoulder & Elbow · 53 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Frequency** — **most common cause of shoulder pain** · **44–65%** of shoulder disorders · with biceps tendinitis, among commonest causes

### Anatomy – acromion ossification

**Ossification centres** — **pre-acromion** (tip) · **meso-acromion** (mid) · **meta-acromion** (base)

**Os acromiale** — failure of centres to fuse

![Acromial ossification centres](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3041-shows-the-ossification-centers-of-the-acromion.jpg)

### Pathophysiology – metalloproteases

**Extrinsic compression** — cuff between humeral head and **anterior acromion**, **CA ligament**, **AC joint**

**Intrinsic degeneration** — supraspinatus attrition → can't balance head → **superior migration** → narrowed subacromial space

**Bursitis** — abutment → inflamed subacromial bursa → anti-inflammatory treatment rationale

### Genes & lab markers

| Marker | Condition |
|---|---|
| **Metalloproteases (MMP)** ↑ | **Subacromial bursitis / impingement** ✓ |
| TNF, **COX-1 + COX-2** ↑ | Subacromial bursitis |
| TSH (hypothyroid), **HbA1c** (diabetes) | Wrong — tests for **adhesive capsulitis** |
| WBC | Wrong — tests for **septic bursitis** |
| ALP | Wrong — tests for osteoblastic tumours, Paget, hyperparathyroidism |
| AFP, PSA, CEA, CA-125 | Wrong — tumour markers |

**Histology (tendinopathy)** — **disorganised collagen** · **mucoid degeneration** · inflammatory cells · bursa: high MMP + cytokines

## 2 Mechanism

**Continuum (Neer)** — **impingement + bursitis (stage 1)** → tendinitis/fibrosis → partial → full-thickness → **massive tear** → **cuff tear arthropathy**

**Associated conditions** — **hooked (type III) acromion** · **os acromiale** · **posterior capsular contracture** · **scapular dyskinesia** · tuberosity malunion · instability

## 3 Presentation

**Pain** — **insidious** · **overhead activity** + lifting away from body · **night pain** = **poor predictor of nonop success**

**Strength** — **usually normal**; full passive ROM

| Test | Technique | Positive / meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **Neer sign** | Passive FF **>90°** | Pain |
| **Neer test** | Subacromial **lidocaine** then repeat | **Pain relieved** |
| **Hawkins** | **IR + passive FF to 90°** | Pain |
| **Jobe (empty can)** | Resisted pronation + FF 90° | **Supraspinatus** pathology |
| **Painful arc** | Abduction in scapular plane | Pain **60–120°** |
| **Yocum** | Hand on opposite shoulder, raise elbow | **Sensitive, nonspecific** |
| Internal impingement | ABER | Pain |
| Speed's | — | Biceps tendinitis |

**O'Brien pain not relieved in neutral** — equivocal → **not labral**; don't order MRA first

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Shows |
|---|---|
| **True AP** | **Acromiohumeral interval** — normal **7–14 mm** |
| **30° caudal tilt** | **Subacromial spur** |
| **Supraspinatus outlet** | **Acromial morphology** (Bigliani) |
| **Axillary lateral** | **Os acromiale** (best) |

**X-ray findings** — proximal humeral migration (CTA) · traction osteophytes · **CA ligament calcification** · GT cysts · **type III hooked acromion** · os acromiale

**MRI** — degree of cuff pathology; subacromial/subdeltoid bursitis · CT arthrogram + US also image cuff accurately

**MRI indications** — acute injury · failed nonop · **weakness** / suspected large tear\
✗ Wrong options: first with benign exam

### Bigliani classification of acromion morphology

| Type | Shape | Diagram |
|---|---|---|
| I | **Flat** | ![Bigliani acromion types](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3041-shows-the-bigliani-classification-of-acromion-morphology.jpg) |
| II | **Curved** | |
| **III** | **Hooked** → associated with impingement | |

**Reliability** — **poor inter-observer**

### Acromial morphology – os acromiale

**Types** — pre- · **meso-** · meta- os acromiale (named by unfused centre)

**MRI (axial + sagittal)** — unfused segment ± **edema** in skeletally mature adult → **meso os acromiale**\
✗ Wrong options: type I/III acromion

![Os acromiale on outlet view](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3041-os-acromiale-supraspinatus-outlet-view.jpg)

## 5 Management

```
Subacromial impingement (no cuff tear)
├─ First line → PT (cuff + periscapular) + NSAIDs ± subacromial steroid
│      (scapular dyskinesia → integrated kinetic-chain rehab)
├─ Failed ≥4–6 months nonop → subacromial decompression ± acromioplasty
└─ Os acromiale → stage 1 fuse with graft → stage 2 acromioplasty
```

### Nonoperative management – physical therapy & PRP

**Mainstay** — **PT** (aggressive cuff strengthening + periscapular stabilisation), NSAIDs, subacromial injection

**Benign exam (full strength, Jobe/Speed painful)** — **PT** before imaging\
✗ Wrong options: AC injection · ASD · MRA · MRI

**Outcome** — **most improve with PT alone** (even with type III acromion) · but **tendinitis tends to recur** \
✗ Wrong options: **"most fail nonop"**

**PT vs surgery** — PT **as effective** as subacromial decompression ± debridement (tendinitis, partial tears) · ASD + PT not better at 2 y

**PRP** — **no effect** on pain, function, ROM at **1 y** vs therapy alone · not harmful · does not prevent arthropathy · meta-analysis: insufficient evidence

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Subacromial decompression / acromioplasty** | Impingement **failed ≥4–6 months** nonop |
| **Two-stage os acromiale** | Fuse with bone graft → later acromioplasty (✗ Wrong options: direct excision → deltoid dysfunction) |
| **Avoid acromioplasty + CA ligament release** | **Massive irreparable cuff tear** (preserve CA arch → avoid anterosuperior escape) |

### Technique – acromioplasty

**Step 1** — **anterior acromionectomy** (arthroscopic: limited by **anterior deltoid origin**, keep intact)

**Step 2** — **anteroinferior acromioplasty** smooths undersurface (rasp open; shaver/burr arthroscopic) · open → meticulous **deltoid repair** to bone

### Subacromial decompression outcomes (workers' compensation)

**Lower satisfaction** — **workers' compensation** (also longer return to work) · **anxiety + depression** \
✗ Wrong options: dominant arm · male · smoker · age <60

### Massive cuff tears – irreparability & SCR contraindications (TO)

**Irreparable (TO)** — can't reach tuberosity despite release (size, retraction, atrophy, fatty infiltration)

| Irreparability marker (TO) | Value |
|---|---|
| Massive tear | **>5 cm** (Cofield) or **≥2 tendons** |
| Pattern | **Posterosuperior (SS + IS)** > anterosuperior (SS + subscap) |
| Fatty atrophy | **Goutallier III–IV** |
| **Acromiohumeral distance** | **<6 mm** |
| Function | **Chronic pseudoparalysis** (can't elevate **>90°**; some say >45°) |
| MRI | **Positive tangent sign** |

**Tangent sign (TO)** — sagittal oblique; line **top of scapular spine → top of coracoid** · SS belly **below line = positive** → **fatty atrophy, irreparable supraspinatus** / poor repair outcome\
✗ Wrong options: subscapularis · retraction medial to glenoid · biceps dislocation

![Tangent sign](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17140126-1.png)

### Hamada classification of cuff tear arthropathy (TO)

| Stage | Finding (TO wording) | Diagram |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | AHI **>6 mm** | ![Hamada stages](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17140116-2.jpg) |
| 2 | AHI <7 mm | |
| 3 | AHI <7 mm + **acetabularisation** of acromion | |
| 4a | AHI <7 mm + **GH arthritis**, no acetabularisation | |
| 4b | AHI <7 mm + acetabularisation + GH arthritis | |
| 5 | AHI <7 mm + **humeral head osteonecrosis** | |

**Hamada versions** — AHI **<7 mm**, stage 5 **osteonecrosis** (TO) · stage 2+ AHI **≤5 mm**, stage 5 **head collapse** (Orthobullets) — read the options

### Superior capsular reconstruction (TO)

**Concept (TO)** — passive restraint to **superior head translation** → restores force couples, ↓ subacromial impingement, ↓ pain

**Graft (TO)** — folded **fascia lata 6–8 mm** or **acellular dermal 3–4 mm** · anchors on **superior glenoid rim** + **medial tuberosity**

| SCR (TO) | Criteria |
|---|---|
| **Indications** | Failed nonop **massive irreparable** tear (SS + IS) · **Hamada 1–2** · **intact/reparable subscapularis** · **functional deltoid** · intolerable pain · pseudoparalysis |
| **Contraindications** | **Irreparable / torn subscapularis** ✓ · **Hamada ≥3** · GH OA |

**✗ Contraindication distractors (TO)** — Hamada I · pseudoparalysis · massive tear failed conservative treatment (= indications)

### Young patient, massive irreparable posterosuperior tear (TO)

| Option (TO) | Indication |
|---|---|
| **Latissimus dorsi transfer** | Retracted posterosuperior tear, **Goutallier ≥3** SS + IS; not if subscap insufficient or deltoid atrophy |
| **Lower trapezius transfer** | **More anatomical** for posterosuperior; promising |
| **Pectoralis major transfer** | **Anterosuperior** (SS + subscap) — most common |
| **SCR** | Young, no significant arthritis |
| **Reverse TSA** | **Older** patients with cuff arthropathy → **least preferred in 35-year-old** ✓ |

**Tendon transfer prerequisites (TO)** — young, active, no significant GH arthritis, **intact subscapularis**

## 6 Complications

**Deltoid dysfunction** — failed repair after open acromioplasty · **excessive acromionectomy** arthroscopically · **direct excision of os acromiale**

**Anterosuperior escape** — acromioplasty + CA ligament release in **massive irreparable** tear

## X vs Y

| | **Subacromial impingement** | **Adhesive capsulitis** | **Septic bursitis** |
|---|---|---|---|
| ROM | **Full passive** | ↓ | — |
| Marker | **MMP, TNF, COX** | TSH, HbA1c (associations) | **WBC** |

| | **Latissimus transfer (TO)** | **Pectoralis major transfer (TO)** |
|---|---|---|
| Tear | **Posterosuperior** | **Anterosuperior** |
| Requires | Intact subscapularis, deltoid | — |

## How it's asked

**"Insidious overhead pain, full ROM, MRI bursitis — elevated marker?"** → **metalloproteases**\
✗ Wrong options: TSH · HbA1c · WBC · ALP

**"Markers contributing to subacromial bursitis pain?"** → **metalloproteases**\
✗ Wrong options: AFP, PSA, CEA, CA-125

**"Partial SS tear, lidocaine relieved 90%, asks for PRP?"** → **no effect on pain, function, ROM at 1 year**

**"Type III acromion, Hawkins + empty can +, 5/5 — true about nonop?"** → **most improve with PT alone**\
✗ Wrong options: most fail · ASD better at 2 y · PRP better · never recurs

**"Golfer, Jobe + Speed's painful, full strength — next?"** → **PT for cuff strengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: ASD · MRI · MRA · AC injection

**"Lower satisfaction after ASD?"** → **workers' compensation**

**"Axial + sagittal MRI unfused acromion with edema?"** → **meso os acromiale**

**"Contraindication to SCR?"** → **irreparable subscapularis tear** (TO)

**"35-year-old, pseudoparalysis, AHD <6 mm, Goutallier 3 — least preferred?"** → **reverse shoulder arthroplasty** (TO)

**"Positive tangent sign predicts?"** → **irreparable supraspinatus (fatty atrophy)** (TO)

**"Best view for os acromiale?"** → **axillary lateral**

**"Night pain significance?"** → **poor predictor of nonop success**

**"Massive irreparable tear — avoid?"** → **acromioplasty + CA ligament release** (anterosuperior escape)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Suprascapular Neuropathy

*Shoulder & Elbow · 54 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Anatomy – suprascapular nerve course

**Origin** — **C5–C6**, **superior (upper) trunk** of brachial plexus → across **posterior triangle of neck** → superior border of scapula

**Course** — under **superior transverse scapular (suprascapular) ligament** at **suprascapular notch** → supraspinous fossa (**supraspinatus**) → around lateral border of scapular spine through **spinoglenoid notch** (under **spinoglenoid ligament**) → infraspinous fossa (**infraspinatus**)

**Suprascapular ligament** — from medial base of coracoid over notch · **artery ABOVE, nerve BELOW**

**Function** — **motor only** (SS + IS) · **no sensory** → no sensory deficit

**Transglenoid fixation risk** — nerve lies **1.5 cm medial** to glenoid labrum at spinoglenoid notch

![Suprascapular nerve — sites of compression](../../MCQ/attachments/q213127-illa-suprascapular-nerve-sites-of-compression.jpg)

| Site | Level | Muscles weak |
|---|---|---|
| **Suprascapular notch** | **Proximal** | **Supraspinatus + infraspinatus** |
| **Spinoglenoid notch** | **Distal** (SS branch already given off) | **Infraspinatus only** |

**TO wording** — "infraglenoid notch" compression → infraspinatus only (= spinoglenoid notch)

**Atraumatic compression sites** — **suprascapular + spinoglenoid notches** (fascial bands, ganglion cysts) \
✗ Wrong options: scalenus anterior · cervical rib · conjoined tendon · subcoracoid

### Epidemiology & associations

**Who** — **overhead athletes**: **volleyball** (classic), baseball pitchers/catchers, tennis · also weightlifters, rowers, linebackers

**Associated** — **SLAP tears** · posterior labral tears · internal impingement

## 2 Mechanism

### Suprascapular notch – causes

**Causes** — **ganglion cyst** (often with labral tear) · **transverse scapular ligament** entrapment/thickening · **fracture callus**

### Spinoglenoid notch – causes

**Causes** — **posterior labral tear → cyst** · spinoglenoid ligament · spinoglenoid notch ganglion · **traction** (**45% of volleyball players**) · transglenoid fixation

**Cyst mechanism** — labral tear = **one-way valve** → synovial fluid exits joint, can't drain → paralabral cyst

**Cyst frequency** — ganglion cysts at **spinoglenoid notch** > **less commonly suprascapular notch** · cyst origin: transverse scapular ligament, scapular fibrous tissue, or GH joint

**Labral source** — **SLAP** most often, posterior labrum less (OB) · **posterior labral tear** (Orthobullets, OB) — read the options; posterior SLAP / **posterosuperior** labral tear fits both

## 3 Presentation

**Symptoms** — **deep, diffuse, posterolateral shoulder pain** · weakness, fatigue with overhead activity · ↓ velocity · no numbness

**Suprascapular notch** — tender notch · **supraspinatus weak**: abduction 90° + 30° FF + IR (**Jobe +**) · **infraspinatus weak**: **ER with elbow at side** · atrophy above + below spine

**Spinoglenoid notch** — **isolated weak ER with arm at side** · **atrophy below scapular spine** (posterior shoulder) · **supraspinatus normal** (empty can negative) · O'Brien + (associated SLAP)

![Isolated infraspinatus atrophy](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3063-clinical-image-infraspinatus-atrophy-smaller.jpg)

**Most specific exam finding (spinoglenoid cyst)** — **weakness of ER**\
✗ Wrong options: impingement sign · apprehension · active compression · abduction weakness

**ER in elevation** — isolated IS palsy: **full ER with arm elevated** · combined SS + IS: **can't ER in forward elevation**

### Distinguishing shoulder tests

| Test | Muscle / lesion | In SSN entrapment |
|---|---|---|
| **Jobe / empty can** | **Supraspinatus** | Abnormal at **suprascapular notch** ✓ |
| **ER with arm at side** | **Infraspinatus** | Abnormal at **both** sites ✓ |
| **Hornblower** (90° abduction + 90° ER, falls into IR) | **Teres minor** (axillary) | Normal |
| **Belly press · bear hug · lift-off** | **Subscapularis** | Normal |
| **Anterior load and shift** (supine, 40–60° abduction + FF) | Anterior instability | Normal |
| **Kim test** (seated, 90° abduction → 45° FF + axial + posteroinferior force) | **Posterior instability** | Normal |
| **Jerk test** | **Posterior labral tear** | May be + (cyst source) |

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**MRI** — identify **compressive mass / cyst** + associated **labral lesion** · cyst: well-defined, **low T1, high T2** · denervation: ↓ bulk + **fatty infiltration** (SS ± IS)

**Paralabral cyst** — most commonly with **labral tears**

![Spinoglenoid notch cyst](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3063-spinoglenoid-notch-cystplain.jpg)

![Suprascapular notch cyst](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3063-suprascapular-notch-cyst.jpg)

### Diagnosis – EMG & compression sites

**EMG/NCV** — **diagnostic**; with MRI = investigations of choice · localises compression

**Isolated ER weakness + atrophy, MRA normal (no cyst, no tear)** — **EMG/NCV first** → found notch entrapment with early SS denervation\
✗ Wrong options: cervical MRI first · subacromial steroid · notch release before EMG · CRP/ESR

**C5 radiculopathy** — weakness out of proportion; not when specific SSN pattern

| Lesion (distractor) | Nerve / site | Deficit |
|---|---|---|
| **Quadrangular (quadrilateral) space** | **Axillary + PHCA** | **Teres minor** atrophy, deltoid (forward elevation, abduction) |
| **Triangular interval** | **Radial nerve + profunda brachii** | Elbow + wrist extension |
| Subscapular recess | Anterior to subscap, below coracoid | Loose bodies |
| Upper/lower subscapular nerves | Subscapularis | IR (lift-off) |
| Long thoracic (C5–7 roots) | Serratus anterior | Not affected by spinoglenoid cyst |

**Brachial plexus figure** — suprascapular = **A (upper trunk)** · dorsal scapular · medial pectoral · lower + upper subscapular = wrong options

## 5 Management

```
Suprascapular neuropathy
├─ No structural lesion on MRI → activity modification + rehab ≥6 months
│      └─ fails ~1 year → surgical decompression (notch release / spinoglenoid ligament release)
├─ Suprascapular notch cyst → decompression at suprascapular notch
└─ Spinoglenoid cyst + labral tear → arthroscopic labral repair + cyst decompression
```

### Operative criteria

| Situation | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **No structural lesion** | **Nonop ≥6 months** (posterior capsule stretching for spinoglenoid) |
| No lesion + failed **~1 year** nonop | Suprascapular notch release · or **spinoglenoid ligament release** (posterior approach) |
| **Structural lesion (cyst) at suprascapular notch** | **Nerve decompression at notch** |
| **Labral tear + spinoglenoid cyst** | **Arthroscopic labral repair + cyst decompression** ✓ |

### Treatment – labral repair with cyst decompression

**Young competitive athlete, posterior labral tear + spinoglenoid cyst** — **cyst decompression + posterior labral repair**\
✗ Wrong options: 6 wk rest · PT · suprascapular notch decompression · cuff repair + acromioplasty

**Catcher, posterosuperior tear + large cyst, severe ER weakness** — **arthroscopic repair + decompression**\
✗ Wrong options: aspiration + steroid · rest · acromioplasty · rehab

**Labral tear key** — address tear (cause of cyst) · **no isolated cyst decompression** when tear present

**Labral repair alone** — not enough (doesn't decompress cyst, atrophy may progress — OB) · "good outcomes with labral repair alone; needle aspiration also used" (one OB explanation) — read the options; best answer = **repair + decompression**

**Failed 6 wk PT with cyst + IS atrophy** — operate\
✗ Wrong options: another 6 wk PT · cervical MRI + EMG · tenodesis

**Mechanical compression** — surgical decompression usually required

## 6 Complications

### Iatrogenic injury

**Arthroscopic SSN release at suprascapular notch** → injured nerve → weak **abduction, forward elevation + ER**, wasting **above + below scapular spine** = suprascapular notch level\
✗ Wrong options: spinoglenoid = IS only · axillary · spinal accessory · dorsal scapular

**SLAP repair** — anchors/instruments **medial to glenoid** → SSN injury

## X vs Y

| | **Suprascapular notch** | **Spinoglenoid notch** |
|---|---|---|
| Muscles | **SS + IS** | **IS only** |
| Weakness | **Abduction (Jobe) + ER** | **ER at side** only |
| Atrophy | Above + below spine | **Below spine** |
| Usual cause | Cyst, **transverse scapular ligament**, callus | **Paralabral cyst (SLAP/posterior labrum)**, traction |
| Treatment (lesion) | Decompress at notch | **Labral repair + cyst decompression** |

| | **Spinoglenoid cyst** | **Quadrilateral space syndrome** |
|---|---|---|
| Nerve | Suprascapular | **Axillary** |
| Atrophy | **Infraspinatus** | **Teres minor** (± deltoid) |
| Test | ER at side weak | Hornblower; forward elevation/abduction weak |

## How it's asked

**"Burning pain, SS + IS atrophy, weak abduction + ER — site?"** → **suprascapular notch** (TO)

**"Volleyball, isolated IS atrophy, axial MRI cyst — compression?"** → **suprascapular nerve in spinoglenoid notch**\
✗ Wrong options: quadrangular space · triangular space

**"Volleyball, MRI cyst in suprascapular notch — weakness?"** → **abduction + external rotation**

**"SLAP + cyst on sagittal MRI — extra exam finding?"** → **weak external rotation**

**"Weak ER at side, atrophy below spine, O'Brien + — MRI?"** → **SLAP tear + spinoglenoid notch cyst**

**"Isolated IS atrophy on clinical photo + MRI — cyst location?"** → **spinoglenoid notch**\
✗ Wrong options: quadrangular space · subscapular recess

**"Isolated ER weakness, normal flexion/abduction, 44 y?"** → **spinoglenoid notch cyst**\
✗ Wrong options: CN XI · Parsonage-Turner

**"Normal empty can + belly press, weak ER — cause?"** → **SSN entrapment at spinoglenoid notch**

**"Suprascapular notch cyst — weakness on exam?"** → **ER with arm at side**\
✗ Wrong options: scapular protraction · IR

**"Volleyball, posterosuperior pain — most specific exam finding?"** → **weakness of external rotation**

**"Weak ER at side, normal lift-off — nerve + site?"** → **suprascapular nerve, spinoglenoid notch**

**"Pitcher, O'Brien +, ER weak, empty can 5/5 — muscle at risk?"** → **infraspinatus**

**"Posterior labral tear + paralabral cyst — structure affected (MRI figure)?"** → **infraspinatus**\
✗ Wrong options: supraspinatus · subscapularis

**"Spinoglenoid cyst — exam?"** → **weak ER with arm adducted**\
✗ Wrong options: Jobe · belly press · Hornblower

**"Linebacker, weak ER at side, spinoglenoid cyst — arthroscopic finding?"** → **SLAP tear**\
✗ Wrong options: Bankart · PASTA · Hill-Sachs

**"Cyst in suprascapular notch on MRI — which muscles?"** → **supraspinatus + infraspinatus**

**"Spinoglenoid cyst on MRI — which muscle weak (figure)?"** → **infraspinatus (B) only**

**"Weak ER at side, full ER when elevated — MRI?"** → **spinoglenoid cyst + IS fatty atrophy**

**"Weak forward elevation + ER at 0° abduction — commonest compression site (figure)?"** → **suprascapular nerve (A)**

**"Rower, IS atrophy, negative empty can — cause?"** → **spinoglenoid notch cyst**\
✗ Wrong options: Bankart · subscapularis tear

**"Volleyball, suprascapular notch cyst — abnormal test?"** → **Jobe's test**\
✗ Wrong options: load-and-shift · bear hug · Hornblower · Kim

**"Weightlifter, weak ER, posterior labral tear + cyst — treatment?"** → **spinoglenoid cyst decompression + posterior labral repair**

**"Catcher, cyst + posterosuperior labral tear — management?"** → **arthroscopic repair + decompression**

**"Jerk test +, labral tear + spinoglenoid cyst — procedure?"** → **labral repair + cyst decompression**\
✗ Wrong options: isolated decompression

**"Posterosuperior tear, cyst, mild IS atrophy, failed 6 wk PT — next?"** → **labral repair + cyst decompression**\
✗ Wrong options: repair only · cervical MRI

**"MRI cyst, abnormal EMG — nerve?"** → **suprascapular**

**"Tennis player, paralabral cyst, ER weakness — cause?"** → **suprascapular nerve compression**\
✗ Wrong options: C5 radiculopathy · IS tear

**"Superior labral cyst — clinical finding?"** → **atrophy of posterior shoulder**\
✗ Wrong options: any sensory deficit

**"Weak ER, no tear or cyst on MRA — next?"** → **EMG/NCV**

**"Atraumatic SSN compression sites?"** → **suprascapular + spinoglenoid notches**

**"After arthroscopic notch release: weak abduction + ER, wasting above and below spine?"** → **suprascapular nerve at suprascapular notch**

# Thoracic Outlet Syndrome

*Shoulder & Elbow · 55 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — compression of **brachial plexus ± subclavian vessels** between neck and axilla (first rib–clavicle); heterogeneous, potentially disabling

**Incidence** — **1–2%** of population

**Demographics** — **♀ > ♂ (3:1)**; **thin, long neck, drooping shoulders**; age **20–60**

| Type | Frequency | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **Neurogenic** | **95% (most common)** | Lower plexus |
| **Venous** | **4%** | Paget-Schroetter; athletic ♂ > ♀ |
| **Arterial** | **<1%** | Aneurysm, emboli; athletic ♂ > ♀ |

### Anatomy – thoracic outlet spaces

**Boundaries (OB)** — **clavicle, first rib, subclavius, costoclavicular ligament, anterior scalene**

| Space | Borders | Contents | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Interscalene triangle** (proximal) | **Ant: anterior scalene · Post: middle scalene · Inf: 1st rib** | **Plexus trunks + subclavian artery** (✗ Wrong options: vein (runs **beneath anterior scalene**)) | **Adson**, supraclavicular pressure |
| **Costoclavicular space** (middle) | Ant: clavicle + **subclavius** · Post: 1st rib + scalenes · Med: **costoclavicular ligament** · Lat: upper scapular border | **Divisions + subclavian artery + vein** | **Costoclavicular manoeuvre** |
| **Retropectoralis minor** (subcoracoid; distal) | Sup: **coracoid** · Ant: **pectoralis minor** · Post: **ribs 2–4** | **Cords + axillary artery + vein** | **Wright** |

![Three spaces of the thoracic outlet](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3064-tosanatomy.jpg)

**Most affected (OB)** — **subclavian artery, vein + lower trunk (C8–T1)**

## 2 Mechanism

**Pathogenesis** — **anatomic predisposition + superimposed neck trauma** (acute or repetitive)

**Split** — **soft tissue 70%** · **osseous 30%**

| Soft tissue | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Scalene abnormalities** | **Anterior scalene hypertrophy** · plexus **through** anterior scalene · **middle scalene anterior insertion** on 1st rib |
| **Scalenus minimus** | Accessory; **30–50%** of TOS; C-transverse process → 1st rib **between subclavian artery + T1 root** |
| **Fibromuscular bands** | ↑ stiffness, ↓ compliance; congenital band → lower trunk |
| **Costoclavicular ligament** | Abnormal insertion → **Paget-Schroetter** |
| **Tumours** | **Pancoast** (1–3% lung cancers; **lacks cough/haemoptysis/dyspnoea**), neuroblastoma, plexus schwannoma |
| Pectoralis minor | Abnormal |

| Osseous | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Cervical rib** | **<1%** population; from **C7** |
| **Prominent C7 transverse process** | |
| Clavicle / 1st rib | Displaced fracture, **hypertrophic callus**, malunion |
| AC / SC injury | Dislocation |
| Tumours | 1st-rib metastasis (**breast, prostate, kidney**), osteoid osteoma |

### Cervical rib types

| Type | Description |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Complete**, articulates with 1st rib or manubrium |
| 2 | Incomplete, **free bulbous tip** |
| 3 | Incomplete, **fibrous band** distally |
| 4 | **Short bar** (mm) beyond C7 transverse process |

**Overuse** — repetitive overhead lifting, **hyperabduction**; **weightlifting, rowing, swimming**

**Vascular damage** — aneurysm, thrombosis, emboli, limb-threatening ischaemia

### Paget-Schroetter syndrome

**Definition** — **venous TOS** in **well-developed young athletes** → **upper-limb DVT**

**Cause** — intermittent **subclavian vein** obstruction in **costoclavicular space** by **abnormal costoclavicular ligament** or **anterior scalene hypertrophy**

## 3 Presentation

### Neurogenic

**Pain** — neck, **trapezius (92%)**, chest, shoulder, arm

**Paraesthesia** — **98%**; **non-radicular**, **wide plexus distribution** · **lower plexus (C8–T1) or combined (C5–T1) in 90%** → **ring + small fingers**, medial forearm

**Other** — weakness, numbness, **heaviness with overhead activity** · activity-related + **night symptoms** (↓ pressure → returning sensation felt as pain) · uni- or bilateral

**Posture** — rounded shoulders, ↑ kyphosis, depressed/downward-rotated scapula

### Gilliatt-Sumner hand (TO)

**Definition (TO)** — rare, severe sign of **true neurogenic TOS**; **chronic lower-trunk (C8–T1)** compression by **cervical rib or congenital fibrous band**

**Pattern** — **preferential APB atrophy, relative hypothenar sparing** (TO, ✓) · atrophy of **APB + hypothenar + interossei** (Orthobullets, TO Elvey list) — read the options

**Why APB (TO)** — **T1-innervated** muscles hit harder (median + ulnar both get lower-trunk fibres)

**Red flag (TO)** — thenar wasting usually = CTS, but in TOS **+ medial forearm sensory change** + milder ulnar intrinsic involvement

(✗ Wrong options: **Distractors (TO)** — symmetric interossei/hypothenar wasting sparing thenar) · claw hand from ulnar compression at humerotricipital groove · thumb/index C6 sensory loss

**Diagnosis (TO)** — EDX (neurogenic changes) + MRI/US

**Treatment (TO)** — **surgical decompression** → stops progression of atrophy + pain; partial motor recovery possible

**Differential (TO)** — **CTS, ALS (motor neuron disease), Hirayama disease**

![Gilliatt-Sumner hand: thenar (APB) wasting](../../MCQ/attachments/TO24921390-1.png)

### Vascular

**Venous** — **episodic cyanosis + swelling**, distended veins, deep arm ache, **heaviness after activity**

**Arterial** — **unilateral Raynaud-type**: coolness + **pallor → cyanosis → erythema**, worse in cold; **hand** predominantly; ulcers/microemboli (rare)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Provocative tests & neurogenic TOS

**All provocative tests** — **high false-positive rate**

| Test | Technique | Positive | Space |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Supraclavicular pressure** | Squeeze upper trapezius + anterior scalene **30 s**, arm at side | Pain/paraesthesia | Interscalene |
| **Adson** | Arm slightly abducted + ER (TO: abducted + **extended**), elbow extended; **neck extended + rotated TOWARD tested side**; inhale + hold | ↓/absent radial pulse ± symptoms; **51% of normals** ↓ pulse; TO: **low sensitivity + PPV** (modified: tilt to opposite side / symptom aggravation) | **Interscalene** |
| **Costoclavicular (military / "attention")** | Shoulders retracted + depressed, chest out; shoulder extended ~30° **1 min** | ↓ pulse ± symptoms | **Costoclavicular** |
| **Wright (hyperabduction)** | Neck rotated **away**; shoulder ER + **max abduction above head** **1 min** (TO: 1–2 min) | ↓ pulse (**7% of normals**) ± symptoms | **Retropectoralis minor** |
| **Roos / EAST** | Orthobullets: **abduct 90° + elbow 90°**, open/close hands **3 min** · TO: arms **abducted, ER, elevated overhead**, open/close fingers **1–3 min** | **Reproduction of symptoms**; can't complete 3 min; relief on dropping arms | **Entire outlet** |
| **Cyriax release** | Examiner behind, elevates shoulder girdle passively **3 min** | Symptoms reproduced (unloading) | Unloading |
| **Halstead (TO)** | Downward traction on arm, **neck hyperextended + rotated to opposite side** | **Radial pulse disappears** | |
| **ULTT of Elvey (TO)** | Abduct 90°, elbow extended, **wrist dorsiflexed**, then **ear to contralateral shoulder** | Ipsilateral symptoms = **neurogenic TOS** | Plexus stretch |
| ✗ Wrong option: **Spurling (TO)** | Neck extension + lateral flexion + **axial compression** | **Radicular** pain | **Cervical radiculopathy** |

### Key radiology findings

| Test | Use / finding |
|---|---|
| **CXR + C-spine X-ray** | **Cervical rib**, prominent C7 TP, low shoulder girdle, **Pancoast** (or normal) |
| CT | Osseous lesions, rib/clavicle malunion |
| MRI | Soft-tissue anomalies |
| **EMG/NCV** | Historically equivocal (normal until permanent damage) · early: **↓ NCV in medial antebrachial cutaneous + median motor to APB** (C8–T1) |
| **Doppler US** | Subclavian vein obstruction/thrombosis — **95% sensitive, 92% specific** for venous TOS |
| CTA/MRA · arteriography | **Embolic disease / aneurysm** |
| **Venography** | Suspected **subclavian/axillary vein thrombosis** |

**Normal neuro exam + EMG/NCS** — does **not** exclude TOS (✓ cervical rib vignette)

![Bilateral cervical ribs](../../MCQ/attachments/q258-a-cervical-rib.jpg)

### Diagnosis – cervical rib

**Classic vignette** — **bilateral ring + small finger tingling**, worse **overhead**, normal neuro exam + EMG/NCS, cervical ribs on X-ray → **TOS**\
✗ Wrong options: C5–6 / C6–7 disc · cubital tunnel · radial tunnel

## 5 Management

```
Thoracic outlet syndrome
├─ Neurogenic → PT, activity modification, NSAIDs
│      └─ fails 6 mo / progressive atrophy → decompression
├─ Scalene contracture → US-guided scalene block (lidocaine / Botox)
└─ Vascular → by lesion (see table)
```

### Nonoperative

**First line** — avoid provocative activity (overhead, ± change job) · NSAIDs, muscle relaxants · **PT**: core/back + shoulder-girdle strengthening, **posture**, relaxation · TENS

**Worse results** — **obese** · **workers' compensation** · **double crush** (carpal/cubital tunnel)

**Anterior scalene block** — **US-guided lidocaine or botulinum toxin**; successful block → **14% higher** good surgical outcome

**Leffert (OB)** — reserve surgery for **intolerable** symptoms (complications significant)

### Operative criteria

| Indication | |
|---|---|
| **Failed nonop 6 months** | Decompression |
| **Progressive atrophy / worsening neuro deficit** | Decompression |
| Embolic events · stenosis with pain + insufficiency · **subclavian aneurysm** · thrombosis with critical ischaemia | Vascular intervention |

### Decompression procedures

| Procedure | Indication |
|---|---|
| **1st rib resection + anterior + middle scalenectomy + neurolysis** | **Most common**; **95% good outcomes**; combined transaxillary + supraclavicular |
| **Isolated scalenectomy** | **Upper plexus** symptoms · **no bony anomaly** · muscular/obese · **recurrence after 1st rib resection** |
| **Isolated pec minor tenotomy** | Symptoms reproduced at **retropectoralis minor** space |
| Cervical rib resection · band release · costoclavicular ligament resection · clavicle malunion ORIF | Per cause |

### Vascular intervention

| Lesion | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Acute embolism** | **IV heparin** ± embolectomy ± thrombectomy ± **tPA** + anticoagulation |
| Small-vessel embolism | **tPA + anticoagulation** |
| Large/proximal embolism | **Embolectomy + anticoagulation** |
| Mild stenosis | **Endovascular stent** |
| **Subclavian aneurysm** / severe stenosis or thrombosis + critical ischaemia | **Resection** ± repair / saphenous / arterial / synthetic graft |
| Chronic emboli + critical ischaemia | **Bypass** |

### Approaches

| | **Transaxillary** | **Supraclavicular** | **Posterior** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Use | **Most common**; **best 1st rib exposure** | **Upper plexus**, scalenes, 1st rib neck, vessels | **Recurrent TOS**, **prior neck surgery** |
| Best for | 1st rib, cervical rib, bands, **lower plexus (C7–T1)** neurolysis | **Isolated scalenectomy + arterial reconstruction** | Proximal plexus |
| Pros | **Cosmetic scar**, no NV retraction for rib | | |
| Cons | **Plexus injury risk** | Poor 1st rib view (needs plexus/vessel retraction) | Extensive dissection → shoulder dysfunction; **LTN, dorsal scapular, accessory nerve** risk |

## 6 Complications

**Pneumothorax** — **one of the most common** after 1st rib resection

## X vs Y

| | **Neurogenic TOS** | **Cervical radiculopathy** | **Cubital / carpal tunnel** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Distribution | **Non-radicular, wide (plexus)**, C8–T1 | **Radicular** | **Clear dermatomal/peripheral** distribution |
| Provocation | Overhead, Roos, Adson, Wright | **Spurling** | |
| EMG | Often normal early; MABC + APB motor ↓ | | |

| | **Gilliatt-Sumner (TOS)** | **CTS** |
|---|---|---|
| Thenar (APB) wasting | **Yes** (preferential) | Yes |
| Medial forearm sensation | **Abnormal** | Normal |
| Lesion | **Lower trunk C8–T1** | Median at wrist |

## How it's asked

**"35-y, bilateral ring/small tingling overhead, normal EMG, X-ray shown?"** → **thoracic outlet syndrome (cervical rib)**\
✗ Wrong options: C5–6/C6–7 disc · cubital tunnel · radial tunnel

**"Arms abducted, ER, overhead; open-close fingers 1–3 min — test?"** → **Roos test** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: Adson · Wright · Spurling

**"Gilliatt-Sumner hand in true neurogenic TOS?"** → **preferential APB atrophy, relative hypothenar sparing** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: symmetric interossei sparing thenar · claw from ulnar at humerotricipital groove · C6 thumb/index loss

**"Shoulder ER + max abduction above head 1 min, neck turned away — test and space?"** → **Wright test; retropectoralis minor space**\
✗ Wrong options: Adson = interscalene · costoclavicular

**"Most common complication of 1st rib resection?"** → **pneumothorax**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Total Elbow Arthroplasty

*Shoulder & Elbow · 56 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology & forms of elbow arthroplasty

**Trend** — **TEA for trauma** (elderly distal humerus fractures) = one of the **fastest-growing** indications; historically for **RA**

**Forms** — **TEA** · **hemi** (radiocapitellar, distal humeral) · **ulnohumeral distraction / interposition arthroplasty** · **olecranon fossa debridement** · radial head arthroplasty

### Implant design (linked, semiconstrained, unlinked) & survivorship

| | **Unconstrained (unlinked, resurfacing)** | **Semiconstrained (linked)** | **Constrained** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Example | **Ewald capitello-condylar** | **Coonrad-Morrey** | **Dee** |
| Hinge | None | **"Sloppy hinge": 7° varus-valgus + 7° axial rotation** | **Rigid hinge** |
| Needs | **Competent collaterals + soft-tissue envelope**, good bone, **precise alignment** | **collaterals not needed**; radial head not needed | — |
| Use | — | **Most commonly used** | **No longer used** |
| Results | No proven superiority | **Best results** of all designs | **Highest loosening** |
| Commonest failure | **Instability** (5–10% dislocation) | Instability (Orthobullets, TO) · bushing wear (OB) | **Aseptic loosening**, then **bushing wear** |

**Semiconstrained laxity** — ↓ stress at **bone–cement interface** → ↓ **aseptic loosening**, at the cost of **bushing wear**

**Constrained** — no coronal/rotational flexibility → **high shear stress → early loosening**; theoretically most stable

**Unconstrained suits** — humeroulnar disease with **intact collaterals + radiocapitellar joint**: OA, post-traumatic arthritis, acute intra-articular distal humerus fracture, distal humerus malunion, bony ankylosis

**Unconstrained relative contraindication** — **late-stage RA** (capsuloligamentous instability + bony erosion); RA → **linked**\
✗ Wrong options: unlinked: tendon injury → instability

![Semiconstrained (linked) TEA: humeral + ulnar implants, bushings, locking pin](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17110333-1.jpg)

### Design pearls

**Stems** — humeral + ulnar stems → better fixation, ↓ loosening

**Anterior humeral flange** — extracortical; resists **posteriorly directed + rotational** forces; designs without flange → **early humeral loosening**; improper flange placement → humeral loosening (TO)

**Radial head** — **not needed for stability** in linked designs; often debrided/resected in RA

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Imaging | Purpose |
|---|---|
| **AP + lateral elbow** | Bone stock, **medial + lateral columns intact**, **canal diameter** |
| **C-spine flexion–extension** | **RA** patients (pre-op) |
| CT | Bone stock / fracture |
| **True AP in full extension** (post-op) | **Bushing wear**: humeral yoke–ulnar component angle **> 7° = wear**, **> 10° = extensive/complete** (TO) · **> 10°** concerning (Orthobullets) |
| **Gruen zones** (4) | Zone 1 periarticular, zones 2–4 = stem thirds; **diffuse lucency ≥ 3 zones**, **focal ≤ 2 zones** |

**Semiconstrained laxity (TO)** — **7° total = 3.5° each direction**; **> 7° = partial wear**; **> 10° = complete wear**

![Bushing wear — varus-valgus tilt of ulnar component on AP](../../MCQ/attachments/q219883-illa-ap-elbow-bushing-wear.jpg)

### Cement mantle quality (Morrey, TO)

| Quality | Radiolucent zone | Cement beyond stem tip |
|---|---|---|
| Adequate | **< 1 mm** | Yes |
| Marginal | ≥ 2 mm | Yes |
| Marginal | < 2 mm | No |
| Inadequate | **≥ 2 mm** | **No** |

### Humeral bone loss grading

| Grade | Humeral bone stock |
|---|---|
| I | Articular surface damage only; **intact subchondral bone** |
| II | **Absent trochlea**; intact medial + lateral epicondyles |
| III | **Absent medial or lateral epicondyle** |
| IV | **Loss of both epicondyles** or resection **proximal to olecranon fossa** |

### Mayo (O'Driscoll & Morrey) periprosthetic fracture classification

| Type | Characteristics | Treatment |
|---|---|---|
| **I** (**commonest**, TO) | **Periarticular — humeral condyle or olecranon**; osteolysis around hinge + muscle distraction | Undisplaced → **immobilise / soft-tissue repair** (fibrous union OK) · displaced → **ORIF heavy nonabsorbable suture or TBW** |
| **II1** | Along stem | **ORIF, retain components ± strut allograft** |
| **II2** | Along stem, **loose implant, good bone stock** | **Revision long-stem ± strut allograft + impaction grafting** (± locking plate / cerclage) |
| **II3** | Along stem, **loose implant, severe bone loss** | **Revision + extensive allograft**; often **resection arthroplasty** |
| **III** | **Distal to prosthesis tip** | Well-fixed → **immobilise humerus, ORIF ulna** · loose → treat as **II2** |

**Basis** — modelled on **periprosthetic femoral** fracture classification; site + implant fixation + bone stock
