![Mayo periprosthetic elbow fracture types I–III](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3089-periprosthetic-fracture-mayo-o-39-driscoll-morrey-classifica.jpg)

## 5 Management

### Operative criteria – RA, elderly distal humerus fracture, failed interposition

| Indication | Criteria |
|---|---|
| **Rheumatoid arthritis** | **10–20%** of RA have elbow disease; **Larsen 3–5** (Orthobullets) · grade III–IV (OB) + pain, functional loss, instability; ideally **> 65 y**; synovectomy for less advanced disease |
| **Primary OA (advanced)** | **> 65 y**; **mid-arc pain** from ulnotrochlear cartilage loss |
| **Fracture** | **Physiologically elderly (> 70 y)**: acute complex **unreconstructable intra-articular distal humerus** fracture, missed fracture-dislocation, **poor bone** |
| Post-traumatic OA (advanced) · chronic instability | — |
| **Failed interposition arthroplasty** | End-stage arthritis + instability → **semiconstrained TEA** |

**Contraindications** — **absolute**: **active infection** (→ arthrodesis), **Charcot joint** · **relative**: poor neurological control (UE paralysis), **active patient < 65 y**, **olecranon osteotomy** · RA list (OB): active/**historical** infection, paralysis, won't follow instructions

**Olecranon osteotomy** — contraindication: preserve **extensor mechanism**; trans-olecranon approach **jeopardises ulnar component stability** → never for TEA

**Not contraindications** — central comminution · **ulnar nerve transposition** · radial head impaction · retrocapitellar comminution (radiocapitellar joint secondary)

```
Elderly unreconstructable distal humerus fracture
├─ Physiologically elderly, low demand, poor bone → TEA (semiconstrained)
│      └─ approach → direct posterior triceps-sparing
└─ not ORIF (unstable fixation), acute fusion or anterolateral approach
```

**Fracture TEA vs ORIF** — **faster recovery + more predictable function**; elderly accommodate limits, fewer complications\
✗ Wrong options: slower recovery

**Anterolateral approach** — brachialis/brachioradialis (radial nerve between) → **no access for TEA**

**Prior radial head resection in RA** — **semiconstrained > unconstrained** (both better in native elbows)

**Failed interposition, 60-yo sedentary** — **semiconstrained TEA**\
✗ Wrong options: unconstrained fails with ligament instability · revision interposition · Outerbridge-Kashiwagi · arthroscopic osteocapsular arthroplasty

### Survivorship & outcomes

| Indication | Outcome |
|---|---|
| **RA** | **Longest survivorship** of all indications (> fracture, flail elbow, OA, post-traumatic); **92.4% revision-free at 10 y**; **> 90% up to 15 y** |
| RA complications | **14%** (Orthobullets) · **~15%** (OB) — triceps avulsion, deep infection, periprosthetic fracture, aseptic loosening (✗ Wrong options: "< 5%") |
| **Primary OA** | **10-y survival 80–85%**; more active → **↑ aseptic loosening + stem fracture vs RA** |
| Overall | **> 80% at 10 y** |
| **Post-traumatic** | Functional ROM, satisfaction at 5 y; **complications 27–43%**, **re-operation 25%** |

**Longest survivorship combination** — **RA + semiconstrained**

**Linked TEA, OA vs RA** — OA **↑ aseptic loosening**; **instability equal** (linked)

### Surgical approach & triceps insufficiency

| Approach | Technique | Key fact |
|---|---|---|
| **Triceps-reflecting (Bryan-Morrey)** | Triceps + **anconeus reflected medial → lateral**; reattached to ulna with **nonabsorbable suture through bone tunnels** | **Triceps failure**; **↓ extension power up to 30%**; **delayed ROM** |
| Triceps tongue | Fascial tongue raised from olecranon proximally; release collaterals | Fractures or TEA |
| Triceps-splitting | Longitudinal split in continuity with forearm fascia; or **V-shaped turndown** (insertion intact) | Allows **extensor lengthening** |
| **Triceps-sparing (e.g. Alonso-Llames, para-olecranon)** | Mobilise medial + lateral triceps borders; insertion retained | **Best for acute fracture TEA**; **earlier ROM**, ↑ final ROM; **↓ ulnar visualisation** |
| **Triceps "on"** | Midline incision; release/protect **ulnar nerve**; release flexor-pronator + MCL, common extensors + LCL; elevate triceps off posterior humerus; dislocate ulnohumeral joint | **No triceps release → no insufficiency** |
| Trans-olecranon | Osteotomy, triceps stays on olecranon | **ORIF only**, never TEA |

**Triceps-off vs triceps-sparing** — triceps-off: **delayed ROM**, ↑ triceps failure · **no difference**: cement mantle, ulnar nerve palsy · sparing: ↑ final ROM

**Bryan-Morrey (1982)** — initial 49 patients no triceps failure; later series ↑ failure

**TO approaches** — splitting/reflecting keep triceps attached to periosteum + forearm fascia (± olecranon wafer); **triceps-preserving (bilateral tricipital)** = exposure by ulnohumeral dislocation, **useful in severe RA**; no consensus on best approach

**Post-op protection (triceps-reflecting)** — **restrict active extension** early; **6–8 wk** before **resisted extension**\
✗ Wrong options: active flexion · supination · passive motion

### Technique

**Positioning** — **supine** (arm over chest, assistant; avoid ETT) or **lateral decubitus** over bolster (no assistant, ↓ manipulation)

**Humerus** — resect **olecranon fossa**, **keep medial + lateral columns**; **bone wedge behind anterior flange**; orient to **posterior flat surface of distal humerus**

**Ulna** — resect **olecranon tip** + **coronoid tip** (prevents impingement on flange → **axial pistoning + loosening**); orient **perpendicular to dorsal flat olecranon**; centre of **greater sigmoid notch**, rotation from **posterior olecranon cortex** (TO)

**Alignment (TO)** — **implant–medullary canal parallelism** (Figgie); COR displacement limit **5 mm**; axis: **anterior humeral cortex** (AP), **roof of coronoid fossa** (depth), **condylar plane** (coronal rotation)

**Pistoning (TO)** — check coronoid, protruding cement, HO on trial motion

**Stability check (TO)** — joint line should separate **≤ 1–2 mm** on distraction at **90° flexion + pronation**; **UCL + extensor mass** contribute most

**Cement** — modern cementing; semiconstrained commonest

### Post-op care & lifelong lifting restrictions

**Immobilisation** — **all protocols acceptable** (immediate motion, or splint 60–90° for 2–5 d, 5–10 d, ≥ 10–14 d → active flexion + gravity-assisted extension); historically 4 wk; immediate motion safe

**Evidence** — **earlier ROM → fewer complications** · **prolonged immobilisation → higher implant survival** (Orthobullets); complications **lowest without immobilisation** in RA (OB)

**Lifting limit** — **5–10 lb** lifelong (Orthobullets, OB) · **≤ 5 lb** (OB) — read the options

**Avoid after TEA** — impact sport (golf, tennis) · immediate aggressive triceps strengthening · **immobilising wrist/hand 4 wk** (mobilise immediately) · avoiding pronation/supination (doesn't stress ulnohumeral implant)

**Patient selection** — > 65 y, low demand, able to obey restrictions ("none do"), **medical optimisation**

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Rate / fact |
|---|---|
| **Aseptic loosening** | **Radiographic 17%, clinical 6%** (Orthobullets) · clinically significant **7–15%** (TO) · **< 10%, usually > 10 y** (OB); **commonest failure of constrained** |
| **Infection** | **8%** (Orthobullets) · **3–8%** (TO) · **5–8%**, within first 5 y (OB); **commonest early failure** |
| **Instability** | **7–19%**; **commonest failure of semiconstrained** (Orthobullets, TO) and unconstrained |
| **Bushing wear** | Constrained + semiconstrained; isolated exchange **1–6%**, mean **7.9 y**; **commonest cause of revision / mechanical failure of semiconstrained** (OB) |
| **Periprosthetic fracture** | **5–30%** of primary TEA; **trauma** commonest cause |
| Component fracture | Humeral **0.65%** · **ulnar 1.2%** (ulna > humerus); usually **ulnar**, late |
| **Triceps insufficiency** | **2–8%**; complete disruption **< 2%** |
| **Ulnar neuropathy** | Paraesthesia **< 40%**, resolves **2–6 wk**; radial nerve less often |
| Wound healing | ↑ with **long-term steroids** |

### Complications – instability & component loosening (TO)

**Unconstrained, 3 months post-op (TO)** — **instability**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnar loosening · infection · fracture

**"True about TEA" (TO)** — **loosening most commonly humeral component** ✓\
✗ Wrong options: instability commonest in semiconstrained (it is commonest in **unconstrained**) · loosening commonest in unconstrained · periprosthetic fracture humerus > ulna

**Loosened component** — **humeral** (TO answer; historically commonest revision cause, now **< 5%** in semiconstrained) · **ulnar** with current flanged designs (another TO) — read the options

**Semiconstrained commonest failure** — **instability** (Orthobullets, TO) · **bushing wear** (OB) · humeral loosening historically (TO) — read the options

**Instability treatment (TO)** — collateral reconstruction usually fails → **revision to semiconstrained hinge**

**Constrained, commonest failure** — **aseptic loosening** > bushing wear > infection (8%); instability = unconstrained/semiconstrained; ulnar neuropathy < instability

### Infection

```
Infected TEA
├─ Acute (< 30 d) → serial I&D + antibiotic beads ± poly exchange
│      └─ S. epidermidis → implant removal + IV antibiotics
└─ Chronic → two-stage reimplantation (~3 months)
       └─ medically ill / poor bone stock → resection arthroplasty
```

**Acute window** — **< 30 days** (Orthobullets) · I&D + **polyethylene exchange within 6 wk** if components stable (OB) — read the options

**Organisms (TO)** — **S. aureus + S. epidermidis**; **S. epidermidis** = encapsulating, **biofilm** → persistent → **implant removal + IV antibiotics**

**Frail 78-yo RA, WBC 25,000, S. epidermidis** — **resection arthroplasty**\
✗ Wrong options: lifelong suppression (only indolent e.g. **C. acnes**) · I&D + poly exchange (not acute) · one-stage (**50%** success only) · two-stage at 3 wk (wait **~3 months**)

**Early failure, semiconstrained post-traumatic** — **infection**\
✗ Wrong options: loosening > 10 y · bushing wear = intermediate · component fracture = late · ulnar neuropathy = not implant failure

### Triceps insufficiency

**Bryan-Morrey, 4 wk, "pop" on resisted extension** — **triceps repair failure**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnar component fracture · axle disengagement · periprosthetic fracture (rare without major trauma, late)

**Can't extend at 6 wk after soft-tissue repair** — **Bryan-Morrey**\
✗ Wrong options: triceps-sparing · triceps-on · trans-olecranon · Alonso-Llames

**Triceps roles (TO)** — **sole overhead extension** + thick **soft-tissue cover** (↓ wound problems, infection); prevention = meticulous approach + repair

### Bone loss & salvage

**Causes** — multiple revisions, fractures, osteolysis

**≤ 8 cm distal humeral loss** — **longer prosthesis with extended anterior flange**; severe → **endoprosthesis (total humerus)**

**Salvage** — flail elbow, amputation, arthrodesis

## X vs Y

| | **Triceps-reflecting (Bryan-Morrey)** | **Triceps-sparing** |
|---|---|---|
| Triceps | Detached, reflected with anconeus, repaired | **Left on olecranon** |
| ROM start | **Delayed** | **Early** |
| Triceps failure | **Higher**; ↓ extension power up to 30% | Low |
| Exposure | Good | ↓ Ulnar visualisation |
| Best for | — | **Acute distal humerus fracture** |

| | **TEA for RA** | **TEA for primary OA** |
|---|---|---|
| Survivorship | **Longest** (92.4% at 10 y) | 80–85% at 10 y |
| Patient | Low demand | **More active → ↑ loosening, stem fracture** |
| Design | **Linked** (✗ Wrong options: unlinked) | Linked or unlinked |

## How it's asked

**"Unreconstructable distal humerus fracture, 78-yo — contraindication to TEA?"** → **olecranon osteotomy**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnar transposition · central comminution

**"85-yo nursing-home resident, comminuted distal humerus — treatment?"** → **TEA via direct posterior triceps-sparing approach**\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF · fusion · anterolateral

**"82-yo RA, distal humerus fracture — shortest recovery, best function?"** → **TEA**

**"RA, prior synovectomy + radial head excision, Larsen III?"** → **semiconstrained TEA**\
✗ Wrong options: unconstrained · synovectomy

**"Failed interposition arthroplasty, end-stage + instability?"** → **semiconstrained TEA**

**"TEA survival in RA?"** → **good at 15 years**

**"Unconstrained TEA — relative contraindication?"** → **late-stage RA**

**"Longest implant survivorship?"** → **RA + semiconstrained**

**"Constrained TEA — commonest failure?"** → **aseptic loosening**\
✗ Wrong options: bushing wear

**"Linked TEA, OA vs RA?"** → **OA ↑ aseptic loosening**

**"Most accurate pre-op counselling?"** → **semiconstrained = most favourable**\
✗ Wrong options: no extension loss with reflecting · RA complications < 5% · fracture TEA slower

**"Post-traumatic OA — true about designs?"** → **semiconstrained varus-valgus laxity ↓ bone–cement stress**\
✗ Wrong options: constrained best · semiconstrained needs collaterals

**"Bryan-Morrey, pop + can't extend at 4 wk?"** → **triceps repair failure**

**"Triceps-reflecting — restrict which motion?"** → **active extension**

**"Can't extend at 6 wk, soft-tissue repair failed — approach?"** → **Bryan-Morrey**

**"Immobilisation after triceps-sparing TEA?"** → **any protocol acceptable**

**"Triceps-off vs triceps-sparing?"** → **delayed ROM** with triceps-off

**"Lifelong instruction after TEA?"** → **limit load to ≤ 5 lb**

**"Frail RA, chronic S. epidermidis TEA infection?"** → **resection arthroplasty**

**"Semiconstrained TEA — commonest early failure?"** → **infection**

**"True about TEA?"** → **loosening most commonly humeral component** (TO)

**"Unconstrained TEA at 3 months — commonest complication?"** → **instability** (TO)

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Total Shoulder Arthroplasty

*Shoulder & Elbow · 57 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Principles

**Definition** — replace humeral head + **resurface glenoid**; **cemented all-polyethylene glenoid = standard of care**

**vs THA/TKA** — greater **ROM** · success depends on **soft tissues** (intact cuff, esp. subscapularis) · glenoid **less constrained** → **greater shear stress** → **mechanical loosening**

**Requirements** — **intact + functional rotator cuff** · adequate **glenoid bone stock + version**

**Outcomes** — **pain relief** = most predictable benefit (more than hemi) · preserved IR + ER · survival **93% at 10 y** (Orthobullets, OB) · ">90% at 10 y" (OB) · TSA/hemi for OA: good/excellent **95%** (Norris) · **worse for post-capsulorrhaphy arthropathy**

**Humeral fixation** — cemented or press-fit (porous-coated); both good longevity; humeral loosening **uncommon without infection**

### Humeral anatomy & implant design (version, stemless, platform stems)

| Parameter | Native value |
|---|---|
| **Humeral head retroversion** | Mean **~20°** (transepicondylar axis) (OB) · Boileau & Walch mean **17.9°** (−6.7 to 47.5°) · range **0–55°** depending on method (TO) |
| **Neck-shaft angle** (inclination) | **45° ± 5°** (range **30–50°**); arthritic ≈ 50° (flatter) (TO) |
| **Humeral head height** | Top of head **8–10 mm above top of GT** (TO, native) |
| **Lateral humeral offset (LHO)** | **Lateral base of coracoid → lateral margin of greater tuberosity** (TO) |
| Humeral offset | Centre of rotation → humeral canal axis; medial **4–14 mm**, AP **−2 to 10 mm** (TO) |
| Head thickness | **70% of radius of curvature** (OB) |
| Radius of curvature | Glenoid **22–28 mm** · head **23–28 mm** (TO) |
| **Glenoid version** | **0–3° retroversion** (OB) · **1.5° retroversion** (10.5° retro to 9.5° ante) (TO) · ~0°, <10° either way (TO) |
| Glenoid inclination | Superior **~4°** (4.2°; −7 to 20°) (TO) |

**LHO (TO)** — **↓ LHO** → ↓ lever arm of **deltoid + supraspinatus** → weak abduction · **↑ LHO** → **overstuffing** → ↓ motion, ↑ poly wear · reconstruct within **4 mm** of normal

**Retroversion axes (TO)** — proximal: articular surface plane / COR–articular centre / GT–articular centre · distal: **trochlear axis**, transepicondylar line, forearm

**Restoring COR** — may prolong glenoid fixation + ↓ poly wear (TO)

| Humeral implant | Key fact |
|---|---|
| **Stemless** | **Completely metaphyseal (biological) fixation** · **more bone preserving** · **no osteotomy needed for proximal humerus malunion** (not constrained by stem) · not for 3/4-part fractures (→ stemmed RSA in elderly) |
| **Stemmed** | Cemented or uncemented porous-coated |
| **Short stems** | ↑ **varus malalignment** (metaphyseal-diaphyseal mismatch) |
| Large-diameter, high-fill stems | ↑ **proximal stress shielding** |
| **Platform (convertible) stem** | Modular conversion TSA → rTSA without stem removal → **↓ operative time**, ↓ blood loss, preserves humeral bone, ↓ cost |

**Subscapularis failure** — **independent of implant design**

## 2 Mechanism

### Rocking-horse phenomenon & glenohumeral mismatch

**Rocking horse** — **eccentric glenoid loading** → lifting of opposite unloaded rim → **glenoid loosening** · causes: **superior migration of head (cuff deficiency)** · abnormal glenoid version (ant/post translation)

**Minimised by (TO)** — **glenoid radius of curvature larger than humeral head radius** (**2–4 mm**) → normal translation without rim loading\
✗ Wrong options: perfect congruence · larger head radius

**Mismatch (TO)** — recommended **<4 mm** (2–4 mm larger glenoid) · "mismatch <4 mm ↑ loosening" (another TO explanation) — read the options · matched within 2 mm: no ↑ loosening reported · **too much mismatch** → ↑ translation, ↑ poly wear, fracture

**Modern glenoids** — cemented all-poly, radius **2–6 mm larger** than head (TO)

| Glenoid conformity | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Conforming** | **More stable** · **rim stress** + radiolucencies |
| **Nonconforming** | ↑ **polyethylene wear** |
| Overall | Neither superior |

**Osteolysis** — **rare** in TSA: larger particle size → harder for macrophages to mount RANK–RANKL reaction (OB)

## 3 Presentation

### Indications & contraindications (cuff status, brachial plexus injury, RA, capsulorrhaphy arthropathy)

**Indications** — pain (esp. **night**) + ↓ ADL · **glenoid chondral wear to bone** (primary OA, inflammatory arthritis esp. **RA**) · posterior head subluxation · OA after primary/recurrent dislocation · complicated proximal humerus fracture not fixable (TO)

**TSA vs hemi** — TSA **preferred for OA + inflammatory arthritis** (better pain relief + forward elevation in RA) · **age <55: TSA > hemi** (outcomes, survival, satisfaction)

**AAOS CPG (end-stage GH OA)** — **TSA with all-polyethylene cemented glenoid** · (✗ Wrong options: humeral head resurfacing · CTA head · **metal-backed glenoid (↑ revision/loosening)**) · hemi + ream-and-run = option but TSA preferred

| Contraindication | Note |
|---|---|
| **Upper / preganglionic brachial plexus palsy** | **Absolute** (TO FNB) · root avulsion = flail arm, poor recovery |
| **Active infection** | "**Only absolute** contraindication" (other TO explanation) — read the options |
| **Irreparable rotator cuff tear** | → **hemi or rTSA**; **rocking horse** → glenoid loosening |
| **Rotator cuff arthropathy** (proximal migration on X-ray) | Glenoid resurfacing contraindicated |
| **Insufficient glenoid bone stock** | **Glenoid erosion to coracoid** → **favours hemiarthroplasty** |
| **Deltoid dysfunction** | e.g. can't abduct past **30°** |
| Relative (TO) | **Charcot**, severe plexopathy, **intractable instability**, prohibitive comorbidity, cuff + deltoid dysfunction |
| **Isolated supraspinatus deficiency** | **Relative** only (TO) |

| NOT a contraindication | Why |
|---|---|
| **Non-retracted isolated supraspinatus tear** / **2-cm full-thickness SS tear** | Reparable at TSA |
| Passive ER <10° | Release at surgery |
| **Eccentric posterior glenoid erosion / retroverted glenoid** | Eccentric reaming |
| **Poor glenoid bone stock** | Build up with graft (TO) |
| **Inflammatory arthritis** | **Indication** if bone stock adequate |
| Subchondral cysts | Normal end-stage OA finding |
| Age (young) | TSA favoured over hemi |

**Cuff tears at TSA** — full-thickness in **5–10%** of TSA patients (Orthobullets) · **<5%** in primary OA (OB) — read the options · **+ impingement signs → pre-op MRI**

**RA + good elevation (120°), no superior migration** — **TSA cemented all-poly**\
✗ Wrong options: rTSA · hemi · arthroscopic debridement = less advanced disease

| Hemiarthroplasty indications (TO) | Hemiarthroplasty contraindications (TO) |
|---|---|
| Rough humeral surface, **intact glenoid cartilage**, enough glenoid arc | Recent sepsis |
| **Insufficient bone for glenoid** | **Neuropathic joint** |
| **Fixed upward displacement** (CTA, severe RA) | Paralytic disorder |
| History of **remote infection** | Cuff + deltoid deficiency |
| **Heavy demands** (occupation, sport, LL paresis) | Non-cooperative patient |

### Post-capsulorrhaphy arthropathy

**Putti-Platt** — subscapularis + anterior capsule divided; lateral stump → anterior glenoid neck; medial stump → tuberosity (**pants-over-vest**) → **limits ER** → incongruent contact loading → **post-capsulorrhaphy arthropathy**\
✗ Wrong options: no coracoid transfer

**Magnuson-Stack** — middle subscapularis + piece of LT transferred **lateral to bicipital groove** (restrict ER) → **subscapularis lacerated** during biceps release at TSA → **↑ ER, anterior instability, + belly press**

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| View | Look for |
|---|---|
| **True AP (Grashey)** | Extent of arthritis · **superior migration** (= cuff deficiency) · Iannotti best-fit circle |
| **Axillary** | **Posterior glenoid wear** · head subluxation |
| **CT** | **Glenoid version + glenoid bone stock** |
| **MRI** | Rotator cuff (**Goutallier** fatty change) |

**Cuff-deficiency signs** — **acetabularization of acromion** → incompetent cuff → **rTSA** · disrupted **Shenton's line** of shoulder → cuff not intact

**Infraspinatus atrophy on MRI** — **independent risk factor for post-op superior cuff tear**

### Walch classification of glenoid wear

| Type | Description |
|---|---|
| **A1** | Concentric, centred head; minor central erosion |
| **A2** | Deeper central erosion; line joining rims transects head |
| B0 | Pre-OA **posterior subluxation** of head |
| **B1** | Posterior narrowing, osteophytes, sclerosis, **no bone loss** |
| **B2** | **Biconcave**, **posterior rim erosion**, retroverted · max erosion **posteroinferior (7–8 o'clock)** |
| B3 | **Monoconcave** posterior wear; head subluxation **>70%** or retroversion **>15°** |
| **C1** | **Retroversion >25°** regardless of erosion (dysplastic) |
| C2 | Biconcave, posterior bone loss, posterior translation |
| **D** | **Anteversion** or **anterior** head subluxation (<40%) |

![Walch classification of glenoid wear](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3075-walch-classification-of-glenoid-wear.jpg)

**Commonest OA pattern (TO)** — **posterior subluxation → posterior glenoid wear → ↑ retroversion**

**B2 vs A1** — **B2 ↑ radiographic loosening** (TO)

## 5 Management

### Implant choice

```
Glenohumeral arthritis
├─ Intact cuff + adequate glenoid → aTSA (cemented all-poly glenoid)
│      └─ retroversion >15° → augment or posterior bone graft
├─ Glenoid eroded to coracoid → hemiarthroplasty
├─ Irreparable cuff / CTA / proximal migration → rTSA (or hemi)
└─ Plexus palsy / active infection → no TSA
```

### Approach

**Deltopectoral** — **cephalic vein** = interval landmark · detach **subscapularis + capsule** from anterior humerus → **dislocate anteriorly** · tight shoulder: release **upper half** (Orthobullets) · **upper 1/3** (OB) of **pectoralis major** tendon

**Superior border of subscapularis (TO)** — **biceps tendon** (just medial to pec major insertion → follow up to **rotator interval**) · biceps ruptured/dislocated → **base of coracoid** marks medial interval

**Inferior border of subscapularis** — landmark for **axillary nerve** (anterior → posterior under GH joint → quadrangular space)

**Palpate cuff insertion** before head cut — ✓ protects cuff

### Subscapularis management & failure (peel, tenotomy, LTO; postoperative restrictions)

**Takedown options** — **peel** (off bone) · **tenotomy** (trans-tendon) · partial tenotomy · **lesser tuberosity osteotomy (LTO)**

**Which is best?** — **no technique superior**: no difference in strength, ROM/function, biomechanical strength or failure rates\
✗ Wrong options: LTO lower failure · peel more ROM · tenotomy higher failure

**LTO** — interest due to **bone-to-bone healing** (biomechanical strength); high union rate · **nonunion** → very poor outcomes, multiple revisions

**Most commonly ruptured muscle after TSA (TO)** — **subscapularis** · factors: **multiple previous operations**, **overstuffing**, **aggressive physio**, tendon length compromise (poor technique)

### Glenoid morphology & retroversion correction (eccentric reaming, bone graft, augments)

![Eccentric reaming of the high anterior side](../../MCQ/attachments/q212183-illa-eccentric-reaming.jpg)

**Why correct** — failure → glenoid malposition → ↓ contact area → ↑ contact pressure → **accelerated loosening**

### Operative criteria – glenoid retroversion

| Glenoid retroversion | Orthobullets / OB | TO |
|---|---|---|
| **<10–15°** (e.g. **9°, 11°, 13°**) | **Eccentric reaming of anterior (high) side** | **Up to 15°** → eccentric reaming |
| **>15°** (e.g. **19°, 21°**) | **Posterior bone graft** (Orthobullets) · **augmented glenoid or bone graft** (OB; augments ↓ complications) | **15–25° → glenoid augments** (reaming perforates anterior vault) |
| **>25°** | Allograft reconstruction of posterior defect | **Bone graft** |
| Insufficient bone (to coracoid) | No eccentric reaming → posterior bone graft or hemi | — |
| Cuff-deficient / acetabularised acromion | **rTSA** (reaming irrelevant) | — |

**15° threshold** — more reaming compromises **subchondral bone** + risks **glenoid vault penetration** · excessive **medialisation** weakens fixation + alters mechanics

**Eccentric reaming not for** — **humeral anteversion/retroversion** (address with head cut + stem) · humeral head collapse · **superior inclination 18°** (exceeds correctable)

**Glenoid deficiency** — build up with **iliac crest autograft** or **resected humeral head** · **never cement** to fill

**Axial CT: retroversion + bone loss, glenoid to be resurfaced** — **bone grafting of the glenoid** (posterior)\
✗ Wrong options: infection work-up · cuff repair · acromioplasty

### Component positioning

| Component | Target |
|---|---|
| **Glenoid version** | **Neutral to 10° retroversion** / "neutral or slight retroversion" (TO FNB) · **neutral** (5° ante to 10° retro acceptable) (TO) · **neutral to slight anteversion** (OB, another TO) · "recreate neutral" (Orthobullets) — read the options |
| Glenoid inclination | **Neutral**; superior/inferior inclination → highest failure; retroverted glenoid loosens more than anteverted (TO) |
| **Humeral stem** | **25–45° retroversion** (Orthobullets, TO) · **20–40°** (one TO explanation) |
| Glenoid retroverted | **↓ stem retroversion** → avoid **posterior dislocation** |
| Stem alignment | **avoid valgus** |
| **Head height (implant)** | Top of head **5–8 mm above GT** (Orthobullets, TO) |
| Head size | **avoid overstuffing** → ↑ joint reaction force + cuff tension |

**Accurate glenoid positioning (TO)** — **most critical** + most challenging step: limited bone stock, variable anatomy, few landmarks

### Glenoid component design

| Better | Worse | Note |
|---|---|---|
| **Convex (curved) back** | Flat back | Flat back ↑ loosening |
| **Pegged** | Keeled | Biomechanically superior; studies: no difference or ↑ loosening with keel |
| **All-polyethylene** | **Metal-backed** | Glenoid too small for metal + PE; metal-backed ↑ loosening/revision |
| **Cemented** (Orthobullets: ↓ loosening) | Uncemented | One TO states **uncemented better** — read the options |

**Central peg** — non-keeled designs: central peg usually **not cemented** → not a loosening risk factor

### Capsule & intra-operative fractures

**Anterior capsular contracture** (passive ER **<40°**) — **anterior release + Z-lengthening**

**Stretched posterior capsule** — **plication** (volume reduction)

**Intra-op GT fracture** (minimal displacement) — standard stem + **suture fixation** + cancellous autograft

**Intra-op shaft fracture** — remove → **long cemented stem + cerclage** · most common intra-op complication = **fracture**, humeral shaft **mid–distal diaphysis** (TO, Norris)

### Rehabilitation & postoperative restrictions

**Limiting factor early** — **protection of subscapularis repair** · restriction set by **strength of subscapularis repair**\
✗ Wrong options: supraspinatus · dislocation · loosening · pec release · capsule · bone quality

**Restrictions (≈6 wk)** — **no passive ER >30°** · **no active IR past plane of body** · **no pushing out of a chair** · passive/active-assisted only

**Allowed early** — pendulums · passive FE to 90° · **passive IR to chest** · active elbow flexion

**Progression** — ER isometrics → IR eccentric + isometric

### Postoperative stiffness & opioid use

**Stiff at 6 wk** (FE 90°, ER 0°), X-ray normal, sling still on — **start PT for ROM now**\
✗ Wrong options: more sling · aspiration · MRI · DVT duplex (DVT = swelling + pain)

| Postoperative narcotic use | Factor |
|---|---|
| **↓ use (protective)** | **Increasing age** |
| **↑ use** | **Pre-op narcotic use** · unemployment |
| No correlation | Gender, smoking, alcohol |

**Orthopaedics** — highest narcotic-prescribing surgical specialty

### Professionalism – impaired colleague

**Attending smells of alcohol before TSA** — **notify the department chairman** before case (chain of command) \
✗ Wrong options: medical board first · resident does case · discuss afterwards · refuse to participate

**Substance abuse** — **leading cause of medical board sanctions**; physicians recover better than general population

## 6 Complications

### Overview

| Complication (TO) | Rate |
|---|---|
| **Stiffness** | 66% |
| **Glenoid loosening** | **Commonest cause of failure (39%)** |
| Periprosthetic fracture (GT, shaft) | 11% |
| **Iatrogenic cuff tear** (neck cut, overstuffing) | 7.7% |
| **Humeral loosening** (infection, **RA, osteonecrosis**) | 6.5% |
| **Posterior instability** | **6%** |
| **Anterior instability** (subscapularis failure) | **5%** |
| Neurovascular (arcuate artery) | 4.8% |
| Infection | 4.6% |

**Overall complication rate** — **~15%** · order: glenoid loosening > instability > cuff tear > periprosthetic fracture > infection > modular dissociation > deltoid dysfunction (TO)

**Most common complication / failure** — **glenoid loosening** (TO, OB) · Orthobullets: newer evidence → **cuff failure may be more common** than glenoid loosening — read the options

| Most common (TO) | Hemiarthroplasty | **TSA** | Reverse TSA |
|---|---|---|---|
| Complication | **Progressive glenoid erosion** (fracture cases: **tuberosity displacement**) | **Glenoid loosening** | **Scapular notching** > instability/dislocation |
| Cause of revision | Glenoid erosion | **Glenoid loosening** | Glenoid component loosening |
| Nerve | — | **Axillary (neurapraxia)** | Axillary |

### Glenoid loosening – most common complication & risk factors

**Frequency** — most common cause of TSA failure: **~30% of primary OA revisions** (OB) · **39%** (TO) · reoperation **2.9% primary vs 28% revision** TSA

**Commonest complication at 5 y** — **loosening of glenoid component**\
✗ Wrong options: humeral loosening · acromial stress fracture · infection

| ✓ Risk factor | Not a risk factor |
|---|---|
| **Glenoid morphology**: **biconcave (Walch B2)**, **posterior glenoid wear**, excessive retroversion | **Age** (incl. >65) |
| **Dysfunctional/deficient rotator cuff** (rocking horse) | Inferior humeral head osteophytes |
| Insufficient glenoid bone stock | Subtle **anterior** subluxation (biconcave goes with **posterior**) |
| **Excessive reaming** removing subchondral bone | **Goutallier/cuff atrophy** (not correlated) |
| **Flat-back** glenoid | **Curved-back** (↓ loosening) · **pegged** component |
| Mismatch (see Mechanism) | Stemless humerus · diabetes (infection) · uncemented central peg |
| Soft-tissue instability · infection · aseptic osteolysis | ✗ Wrong options: osteonecrosis · RA · OA (as cause of glenoid loosening) |

**Radiolucent lines** — presence **does not correlate with symptoms** or clinical failure (3 + 7 y) · **progression does correlate** (50% by 3–4 y)

**Treatment** — glenoid removal (if bone stock) ± **bone graft** for later re-implantation · **convert to rTSA** (salvage) · glenoid morphology not fixable with aTSA → rTSA

### Subscapularis failure

**Commonest instability after TSA** — **anterior** (subscapularis + anterior capsule pull-off) (Orthobullets, TO) · TO complication list gives posterior 6% vs anterior 5% — read the options \
✗ Wrong options: "pull-off of rotator cuff" (= supraspinatus)

**Anterior dislocation after TSA** — **subscapularis rupture in all cases** (Wirth & Rockwood) · classic: **pushing up from chair** (fires subscapularis)

**Mechanism** — fall/catching self · **forced IR** or **excessive ER** (beyond normal **40–60°**) · forced ER withdrawal (hot pan) → LTO failure · late rupture with sport (golf, tennis)

| Sign of subscapularis insufficiency | Note |
|---|---|
| **↑ Passive ER** | e.g. 90° at side (abnormal at 72 y), 110° vs 80° |
| **Lift-off / IR lag** | Can't hold hand off back |
| **Belly-press** (abdominal compression) | Weak |
| **Bear hug** | **≥20% weaker** than other side |
| IR weakness · can't tuck shirt / reach back pocket | — |
| **Anterior instability with ER** | (✗ Wrong options: instability with IR = posterior) |
| **Axillary X-ray: anterior subluxation** | Head not centred |

![Anterior subluxation of humeral head after subscapularis failure](../../MCQ/attachments/q217830-a-subscap-tear-atsa.jpg)

**Confirm** — **ultrasound** · X-ray: displaced lesser tuberosity (failed LTO) · glenoid loosening/locked posterior dislocation **visible on X-ray** (normal X-ray → tendon rupture)

**Consequence** — loss of **anterior–posterior force couple** / concavity-compression → instability, early loosening

| Timing / patient | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Acute (<6 wk)**, young/active | **Early exploration + revision repair** (± augmentation) |
| **>6 wk** | Anterior capsular reconstruction · tendon transfer · **rTSA** |
| **Chronic / older** | **Revision to rTSA** (most reliable) |

**Subscapularis innervation** — **upper + lower subscapular nerves** ← **posterior cord**

| ✗ Plexus distractor | Branches |
|---|---|
| Lateral cord | **Lateral pectoral**, **musculocutaneous** |
| Medial cord | Medial pectoral, **medial brachial cutaneous (T1)**, MABC |
| Superior trunk | **Suprascapular**, nerve to subclavius |
| C5 root | **Dorsal scapular** (rhomboids, levator scapulae) |

### Rotator cuff failure after TSA (overstuffing, falls, sizing)

**Frequency** — **one of the most common complications**; common long-term secondary dysfunction · **common reason for conversion to rTSA**

| Cause | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Excessive bone removal at humeral neck osteotomy** (cut too distal / excessive retroversion) | **Iatrogenic** cut/detachment of cuff insertion (✗ Wrong options: 30° retroversion = normal · 45° inclination · deltoid retraction = axillary nerve) |
| **Overstuffing** (oversized head, ↑ LHO) | **Attritional supraspinatus + subscapularis** tears; ↓ motion |
| **Fall / trauma** | **Traumatic cuff tear** (SS 2/5, normal X-ray → MRI) |
| Undiagnosed pre-op cuff tear | Improper soft-tissue balancing |
| **Pre-op infraspinatus atrophy** | ↑ **superior cuff tear** |
| Degeneration, malposition | Late tear |

**Iannotti best-fit circle** — Grashey AP: 3 points = **lateral cortex below GT flare + lateral + medial ends of anatomic neck** · resurfaced head **outside circle = overstuffing** → **component sizing** error

![Iannotti best-fit circle for humeral head sizing](../../MCQ/attachments/q220135-illa-screenshot-2025-02-25-at-6-35-51-pm.jpg)

**Anterior + superior escape** — **SS + subscapularis** tears (Jobe + belly-press +; Hornblower −) ← **overstuffing**

**High-riding humerus after fall** — **cuff failure** → **convert to rTSA**

**Late pain at night, can't do overhead** — **positive empty can test** (SS tear) · repair if possible, else **rTSA**

**Catastrophic glenoid fracture** — humeral head **directly on glenoid bone** (normally gap = poly thickness; poly is radiolucent)

**Cuff tear → glenoid loosening** (cuff deficiency predisposes)

### Infection – C. acnes & work-up

| Risk factor | Not |
|---|---|
| **Male** (more sebaceous glands, more C. acnes; more TSA for post-traumatic/CTA) | Female |
| **Age <65** | Age >65 |
| **Reverse** TSA (vs anatomic) | Anatomic TSA |
| **Inflammatory** or post-traumatic arthritis | Primary OA |
| **Corticosteroid injection within 3 months** (e.g. 2 months) | Injection 4 months before |

**Rate** — C. acnes infection **1–2%** after primary TSA (Orthobullets) · **4.6%** (TO)

**C. acnes (P. acnes)** — **gram-positive, facultative, aerotolerant, anaerobic rod**; ferments lactose → propionic acid · **commonest cause of late/indolent infection + implant failure** · **>50% of chronic infections**; **S. epidermidis 2nd (15%)**

**C. acnes facts** — colonises **shoulder > hip/knee** · **men > women** bacterial burden · biofilm 18–90 h · presentation: early **pain + stiffness**, later swelling + redness · **usually no fever** (subtle)

**Cultures** — hold **14 days** (OB) · **10–14 days** in anaerobic bottles, mean detection **6 days** (Orthobullets TSA note) · **14–28 days** (Orthobullets PJI note) — read the options · 16S rRNA PCR · **sonicate** explanted implant

**Sampling** — **arthroscopic tissue culture 100% sensitive + specific** vs fluoroscopic aspiration **17% sensitive**, 100% specific · aspiration may be **normal** · imaging + in only 24%

**New pain after pneumonia / ↑ WBC + ESR** — **haematogenous** → **radiographs + aspiration with Gram stain + culture** · well-fixed implants → I&D may avoid full revision\
✗ Wrong options: wait 2 wk for repeat labs · PT/NSAIDs/stretching · MRI · emergent revision

| Timing | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Early (<6 wk)** | **Open I&D** |
| **Late (>6 wk)** / grossly infected (frozen >10 WBC/HPF, GPC in clusters) | **Remove components + antibiotic spacer** → **2-stage** reimplantation · IV antibiotics **6 wk** (penicillin G, ceftriaxone, clindamycin, vancomycin) then PO **2–6 mo** |

**Avoid in 2-stage stage 1** — bone grafting glenoid (won't heal with infection; do at stage 2) · resection arthroplasty (poor function) · head exchange/arthroscopic debridement (won't eradicate)

### Neurovascular injury during approach (musculocutaneous, axillary, radial, arcuate artery)

| Structure | At risk when | Deficit / fact |
|---|---|---|
| **Axillary nerve** (most common) | Inferior capsular release, inferior subscapularis, labrectomy; **excessive deltoid retraction** | Lateral shoulder numbness, weak abduction; **10–25 mm** below glenoid rim (↑ with abduction + ER); ≤3 mm from inferior capsule |
| **Musculocutaneous** (lateral cord) | **Retraction on conjoint tendon / strap muscles** (short head biceps + coracobrachialis) | **Weak forearm supination + elbow flexion** (biceps) |
| **Radial nerve** | **Latissimus dorsi tenotomy** for transfer (with rTSA) | — |
| **Arcuate artery** (terminal branch of **anterior humeral circumflex**, main GT supply) | **Elevating biceps tendon from groove** (runs lateral side of groove) | Bleeding |
| Anterior humeral circumflex vessels | Distal dissection at **inferior subscapularis** (peel) | — |
| Suprascapular nerve | ≤1 cm from glenoid rim | — |

**MCN distance from coracoid** — **~3 cm** (OB) · **2.0 ± 0.5 cm** (Apaydin; closest with arm **abducted + IR**) · **3.1 cm** (Boardman) — read the options · enters conjoint **5.8 cm** below coracoid · 0.5 cm from labrum at 90° abduction

**Nerve injury rate** — **1–4%** of shoulder arthroplasty · 58% of iatrogenic injuries fail to recover (Carofino)

**Axillary nerve + posterior humeral circumflex artery** — pass beneath GH joint through **quadrilateral space**

**Latissimus transfer** — routine with **Grammont (medialised)** rTSA for ER deficit (+ Hornblower) · ↓ use with **lateralised** designs

### Periprosthetic fracture – Wright & Cofield

| Type | Location | Intra-op | Post-op | |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | Near **stem tip, extends proximally** (tuberosities) | Span with standard (2–3 cortical diameters) or long stem; transosseous sutures for tuberosity | **Nonop** if minimally displaced · significant overlap = loose stem → **long-stem revision** | ![Wright and Cofield](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3075-wright-amp-cofield-classification.jpg) |
| **B** | **Centred at stem tip**, extends distally | Span with long stem; cement distal canal (✗ Wrong options: cement escape); strut allograft + cerclage | **Revise to long-stem** | |
| **C** | **Distal to stem tip** | Long stem, or near olecranon fossa → plate ± cerclage, strut | **ORIF**; plate overlaps stem by **2 cortical diameters** (avoid stress riser) | |

**Acceptable alignment** — **≤20° flexion/extension · ≤30° varus/valgus · ≤20° rotation**

**Humeral stem loosening** — more common in **RA + osteonecrosis** → **rule out infection** · uncommon with stemmed primary aTSA

## X vs Y

| | **Anatomic TSA** | **Reverse TSA** |
|---|---|---|
| Cuff | **Must be intact** | Deficient (CTA, pseudoparalysis, anterosuperior escape) |
| Design | Less constrained (shear → loosening) | Convex glenosphere + concave humerus; COR **inferior + medial** → longer deltoid lever |
| **ER at side + IR behind back** | **Better** (✓ reach behind back) | Worse |
| Return to activity | **Quicker** | Slower |
| Forward flexion / PROMs / revision / complications | **Similar** | Similar |
| Typical complication | Glenoid loosening, subscap failure | **Scapular notching**, instability, **acromial stress fracture (~4%)** (over-tensioning) |
| Dislocation | **Anterior** (subscapularis) | **Anterior**; mechanism **extension + IR + adduction** (rising from chair) (TO) · **hyperextension + ER** (another TO) — read the options |

| Post-op exam test | Muscle |
|---|---|
| **Belly-press, lift-off, bear hug, IR lag, ↑ passive ER** | **Subscapularis** |
| Jobe / empty can, drop arm / drop sign | Supraspinatus |
| ER lag sign | Infraspinatus |
| **Hornblower** | Teres minor |
| Yergason | Biceps |
| Loss of passive ER (complete) | **Locked posterior dislocation** |

| Late failure clue | Diagnosis |
|---|---|
| ↑ Passive ER, + belly-press, **anterior** subluxation, early post-op | **Subscapularis failure** |
| **High-riding** humerus, + empty can | **Supraspinatus / cuff failure** |
| Head outside Iannotti circle, attritional SS tear | **Overstuffing (sizing)** |
| Progressive glenoid lucency, biconcave pre-op, normal labs/cultures | **Aseptic glenoid loosening** |
| Pain + stiffness, humeral lucency, normal or ↑ labs | **C. acnes infection** |

## How it's asked

**"TSA absolute contraindication?"** → **upper brachial plexus palsy** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: SS deficiency · poor glenoid bone stock · retroverted glenoid

**"All-poly glenoid contraindicated in?"** → **irreparable rotator cuff tear**\
✗ Wrong options: RA · OA · osteoporosis · prior resurfacing

**"Putti-Platt 20 y ago, painful stiff shoulder — diagnosis?"** → **post-capsulorrhaphy arthropathy**

**"Contraindication to TSA?"** → **preganglionic brachial plexus injury**\
✗ Wrong options: ER <10° · posterior erosion · 2-cm SS tear · inflammatory arthritis

**"End-stage OA, AAOS CPG — next step?"** → **TSA with all-polyethylene cemented glenoid**\
✗ Wrong options: metal-backed · CTA head · resurfacing

**"Most appropriate aTSA candidate?"** → **72-y male, 9 months post TKA, debilitating pain, MRI shows intact rotator cuff**\
✗ Wrong options: plexus palsy · can't abduct >30° · proximal migration · erosion to coracoid

**"RA, FE 120°, no superior migration — most predictable?"** → **TSA with cemented all-polyethylene glenoid**

**"Favours hemi over TSA?"** → **glenoid wear to level of coracoid**\
✗ Wrong options: age · non-retracted SS tear · cysts

**"aTSA vs rTSA counselling?"** → **aTSA: increased ability to reach behind back**\
✗ Wrong options: equal ER · better FF with rTSA · worse PROMs

**"Primary OA, 7° retroversion — aTSA (Figure E) vs rTSA (Figure F)?"** → **procedure shown in Figure E (aTSA) provides better post-operative range of motion** (similar complications + outcomes)

**"Ideal glenoid component position?"** → **neutral to 10° retroversion** (TO)

**"Eccentrically ream the glenoid when?"** → **retroversion 15°** (TO) · **13°** (OB) · **9°** (OB)

**"Axial CT: retroversion + posterior bone loss, resurfacing planned?"** → **bone grafting of glenoid**

**"Walch B2 — maximum erosion?"** → **posteroinferior (7–8 o'clock), area D**

**"Glenoid augmentation needed for?"** → **retroversion 21°**\
✗ Wrong options: 11° · humeral retroversion · head collapse · acetabularization = rTSA

**"Cause of glenoid loosening?"** → **dysfunctional rotator cuff** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: osteonecrosis · RA · OA

**"Rocking-horse effect minimised by?"** → **glenoid radius larger than humeral head radius** (TO)

**"Recommended mismatch?"** → **<4 mm** (TO)

**"Commonest complication 5 y after TSA?"** → **glenoid component loosening** (TO)

**"Pre-op feature most linked to early glenoid loosening?"** → **biconcave glenoid / glenoid morphology**\
✗ Wrong options: osteophytes · age · Shenton's line · cuff atrophy · pegged/curved back · stemless · diabetes

**"Aseptic glenoid loosening 5 y post — true?"** → **pre-op posterior glenoid wear is a risk factor**\
✗ Wrong options: 2nd to infection · lucent lines correlate · central peg uncemented

**"Most likely mode of failure to warn about?"** → **glenoid loosening**\
✗ Wrong options: cuff tear · humeral loosening · fracture · infection

**"Most common muscle ruptured after TSA?"** → **subscapularis** (TO)

**"Landmark for superior subscapularis border?"** → **biceps tendon** (TO)

**"Early rehab restricted to protect?"** → **subscapularis tendon**\
✗ Wrong options: supraspinatus · dislocation · loosening

**"Greatest influence on early restrictions?"** → **strength of the subscapularis repair**

**"Can't lift hand off back, weak IR 8 mo after TSA?"** → **subscapularis insufficiency**\
✗ Wrong options: subscapular nerve palsy · biceps · brachial neuritis

**"Rose from chair at 3 wk → anterior dislocation — mechanism?"** → **subscapularis rupture**

**"Fall at 4 wk, ER 80° passive, subscap tear — other complaint?"** → **instability with external rotation**\
✗ Wrong options: with IR = posterior · lateral numbness = axillary

**"Fall at 4 wk, ER 90° at side, normal X-ray?"** → **rupture of subscapularis repair**\
✗ Wrong options: glenoid loosening · locked posterior dislocation

**"Repaired tendon's nerves arise from?"** → **posterior cord** (upper + lower subscapular)

**"Belly-press weak, ER 110°, displaced LT on X-ray?"** → **lesser tuberosity nonunion**

**"Prior Magnuson-Stack — failed repair after TSA gives?"** → **subscapularis, increased ER, anterior instability, positive belly-press**

**"Movement to avoid immediately after aTSA?"** → **passive ER to 60°**\
✗ Wrong options: IR to chest · FE 90° · pendulums · elbow flexion

**"Failed LTO after slip — exam?"** → **increased passive external rotation**\
✗ Wrong options: ER lag · drop arm · Hornblower · Yergason

**"Superior subscapularis technique long term?"** → **no technique is superior** / **no differences are observed between techniques**

**"Click, 80° ER, axillary X-ray anterior subluxation?"** → **subscapularis failure**\
✗ Wrong options: infection · SS tear (empty can 5/5) · axillary neurapraxia

**"Withdrew arm from hot pan, failed LTO — exam?"** → **positive bear hug test**\
✗ Wrong options: loss of passive ER = locked posterior dislocation

**"Golfer 3 y post aTSA, lift-off lag, off-centre head?"** → **subscapularis rupture**

**"FALSE about TSA?"** → **most common instability is pull-off of rotator cuff** (TO; it's subscapularis)

**"Manoeuvre risking cuff injury?"** → **excessive bone removal with humeral neck osteotomy**

**"Fall, SS 2/5, normal X-rays 2 y after TSA?"** → **rotator cuff tear**

**"Anterosuperior escape, + Jobe + belly-press — cause + risk factor?"** → **SS + subscapularis tear; overstuffing**\
✗ Wrong options: osteolysis (rare)

**"Pre-op infraspinatus atrophy → risk of?"** → **superior rotator cuff tear**\
✗ Wrong options: acromial stress fracture = rTSA

**"High-riding humerus after fall, 15 y post TSA?"** → **rotator cuff failure** → **rTSA**

**"24 mo post, night pain, can't go overhead — also present?"** → **positive empty can test**

**"Head larger than best-fit circle, attritional SS tear?"** → **component sizing** (overstuffing)

**"P. acnes after arthroplasty — incorrect?"** → **usually associated with fevers**

**"Pain after pneumonia, ↑ WBC/ESR, TSA 5 y?"** → **obtain radiographs + aspirate the shoulder**

**"Late pain post pneumonia, mild ↑ labs, normal X-ray?"** → **shoulder aspiration with Gram stain + culture**

**"Late infection with lucencies — organism?"** → **Propionibacterium acnes** (S. epidermidis 2nd)

**"Frozen >10 WBC/HPF, GPC in clusters — treatment?"** → **remove components + antibiotic spacer**\
✗ Wrong options: graft glenoid now · resection · head exchange

**"Draining sinus 3 mo post aTSA — risk factor?"** → **corticosteroid injection 2 months before surgery**\
✗ Wrong options: anatomic · female · >65 · OA

**"Retraction on strap muscles — weakness?"** → **forearm supination** (MCN)

**"rTSA + latissimus transfer — nerve at risk at tenotomy?"** → **radial**

**"Arcuate artery injured during?"** → **elevating biceps tendon from groove**

**"Inferior border of subscapularis protects?"** → **axillary nerve**

**"Conjoint tendon over-retraction injures?"** → **musculocutaneous nerve from lateral cord**

**"Stem + glenoid position in TSA?"** → **stem 25–45° retroversion + glenoid neutral or slight retroversion** (TO)

**"Lateral humeral offset = GT to?"** → **lateral base of coracoid process** (TO)

**"Average humeral head version?"** → **20 degrees retroversion**\
✗ Wrong options: 40°/60° retroversion · anteversion

**"Stemless vs stemmed — true?"** → **stemless less likely to need osteotomy for malunion**\
✗ Wrong options: fractures · ↓ subscap failure · stemmed more metaphyseal

**"Retained platform stem at revision to rTSA?"** → **lower overall operative time**

**"Stiff at 6 wk in sling, X-ray normal?"** → **PT for range-of-motion exercises**

**"Factor with ↓ post-op narcotic use?"** → **age**\
✗ Wrong options: prior narcotics ↑

**"Attending smells of alcohol before TSA?"** → **notify the department chairman**

<div style="page-break-after: always;"></div>

# Traumatic Anterior Shoulder Instability (TUBS)

*Shoulder & Elbow · 58 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**TUBS** — **T**raumatic **U**nilateral dislocation, **B**ankart lesion, requires **S**urgery

**Frequency** — one of the most common shoulder injuries; **1.7%/year** general population; shoulder = most commonly dislocated major joint

**High-risk groups** — **military**, **contact/collision athletes** (rugby, American football commonest)

**Hyperlaxity** — global hyperlaxity (Ehlers-Danlos, collagen disorders) → OR **2.68** for anterior instability · **3× recurrence** · fewer capsulolabral lesions · more often atraumatic instability

### Anatomy – static & dynamic restraints

**Static** — bony anatomy, capsule, GH ligaments, **labrum (+50% glenoid depth)**

**Dynamic** — rotator cuff, long head of biceps

**Concavity-compression** — cuff compresses head into glenoid concavity; **lost with anterior glenoid rim loss** → ↓ effective depth\
✗ Wrong options: adhesion-cohesion · negative intra-articular pressure: both preserved in closed capsule

| Ligament | Taut / restrains in | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| **SGHL** | **Arm at side (0° abduction)** | **Inferior** |
| **MGHL** | **45° abduction + ER** | Anterior (and posterior, OB) |
| **Anterior band IGHL** | **90° abduction + ER** | **Anterior – main anterior restraint** |
| Posterior band IGHL | 90° abduction | Posterior |

**Main anterior stabiliser (TO)** — **IGHL**\
✗ Wrong options: MGHL · SGHL · anterior capsule · subscapularis

**Main stabiliser above 45° abduction (TO)** — **IGHL**

**AIGHL glenoid attachment** — usually to **labrum**; less commonly direct to glenoid neck

### Pathoanatomy – Bankart lesion, IGHL & mechanism (abduction–external rotation)

**Bankart lesion** — avulsion of **anteroinferior labrum + anterior band IGHL** from anteroinferior glenoid; labrum separated from the **bony glenoid**; "essential lesion" of traumatic anterior dislocation (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: cartilage–labral junction only

| Bankart frequency | Source |
|---|---|
| **80–90%** of TUBS | Orthobullets |
| **85%** (Rowe) · **87%** (Hintermann) · **97%** (Taylor 63/67) · 73.5% (Wolf) | OB studies |

**Most common surgical finding** — **anteroinferior labral tear**\
✗ Wrong options: anterosuperior · posterior · Hill-Sachs

**Hintermann (212 dislocators)** — Bankart 87% · ventral capsule insufficiency 79% · **Hill-Sachs 68%** · GHL insufficiency 55% · complete cuff tear 14% · posterior labral tear 12% · SLAP 7%

| Lesion | Definition | Key point |
|---|---|---|
| **Bankart** | Labrum + IGHL off anteroinferior rim; **medial periosteal hinge undisrupted** (TO) | Most common |
| **Perthes (TO)** | Labrum **avulsed (not torn)**, attached by **intact but medially stripped periosteum** | May sit in **normal position** (neutral-arm MRI) → **hard to see arthroscopically**; stabiliser function lost → **recurrent dislocation** |
| **ALPSA** | "**Medialised Bankart**": labroligamentous complex **displaced medially (+ inferiorly)** on scapular neck, intact medial hinge, **fluid underneath**; anterior labrum absent on rim (TO) | **Heals medially** on glenoid neck · ↑ arthroscopic failure · common in **recurrent instability treated nonop** |
| **GLAD** | **Partial** anteroinferior labral tear + **adjacent glenoid articular cartilage defect** | ↑ arthroscopic failure · debridement relieves pain (Neviaser) |
| **HAGL** | IGHL avulsed from **humeral** insertion | See HAGL block |
| **Bony Bankart** | Fracture of anteroinferior glenoid | Up to **49%** of recurrent dislocators |
| **Kim lesion (TO)** | Concealed tear at **posterior** glenoid chondrolabral junction + concealed deep labral detachment | **Posterior** instability; visible chondrolabral junction intact; probing → **labral fluctuance**; ± glenoid hypoplasia |

![Labral lesion spectrum – Bankart, GLAD, Perthes, ALPSA, bony Bankart](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17121441-1.jpg)

**Association (TO)** — Bankart, Perthes, HAGL, ALPSA, GLAD → all **recurrent anterior** instability · Kim → **posterior**

**97% rule** — **97%** of recurrent instability have **Bankart or ALPSA**

**Mechanism** — anteriorly directed force with arm **abducted + externally rotated** · posterior instability = **adduction + internal rotation**

**Bankart vs position** — AIGHL/anteroinferior labrum taut in **ER at 90° abduction**\
✗ Wrong options: 45° = MGHL

**Associated lesion in young dislocator** — **Hill-Sachs**\
✗ Wrong options: full-thickness cuff tear = elderly · reverse Hill-Sachs = posterior · coracoid fracture = rare · GT fracture = less common than Hill-Sachs

**"Clunk" in 22-y basketballer** — Bankart + **Hill-Sachs**

### Combined anterior & posterior labral tears

**Pan-labral tear** — **270–360°** labral tear (anterior + inferior + posterior); anterior and/or posterior instability

**Exam** — positive **Jerk + Kim** (posterior) **+ apprehension + relocation** (anterior) → **270° tear** → anterior + posterior labral repair (arthroscopic)

**✗ Wrong options (images)** — bony Bankart / anterior Bankart (anterior repair only) · subscapularis tear (cuff repair) · Kim lesion (posterior only)

**270° repair** — **15% failure** (Mazzocca)

**Military** — high incidence of posterior and combined front-to-back tears → **unrecognised pan-labral tear** = cause of recurrence

### HAGL lesion

**HAGL** — **humeral avulsion of the (inferior) glenohumeral ligament**, anterior band IGHL, **without subscapularis tear**; term coined by **Wolf (1995)**

**RHAGL** — reverse HAGL = posterior band IGHL → **posterior** instability

**Frequency** — **7–9%** of unstable shoulders (Wolf 9.3%: Bankart 73.5%, capsular laxity 17.2%) · Taylor **1/67** · Rhee 2% — **rare**

**Patient** — **slightly older** than Bankart (Orthobullets) · **older + more violent trauma** (OB) · female collegiate athletes · hyper-abduction + ER

**MRI** — best on **coronal** sequences: **J-shaped IGHL**, contrast **extravasating down humeral neck**; bony HAGL variant; "floating" AIGHL = HAGL + capsulolabral detachment

**Associations** — always with other lesions (Bankart, LHB tear, cuff tear); more severe anterior pain

**Significance** — "other end of the IGHL" → **missed HAGL = cause of failed Bankart repair** → higher recurrence if unrepaired

**Treatment** — **open repair = classic / gold standard** · arthroscopic possible but technically hard (**70° scope**, accessory portals)\
✗ Wrong options: arthroscopic contraindicated classically

## 2 Mechanism

### Hill-Sachs lesion

**Hill-Sachs** — **compression/impaction fracture of posterosuperior (posterolateral) humeral head** against **anteroinferior glenoid rim**; chondral impaction injury

**Frequency** — **80–100%** of traumatic dislocations · **25%** of traumatic subluxations · 68% (Hintermann) · 73% on X-ray (Edwards)

**Most common associated intra-articular finding** with Bankart\
✗ Wrong options: cuff tear · SLAP · posterior labral tear · Buford

**Arthroscopy** — indentation on posterior head **beside the bare area**\
✗ Wrong options: bare area = normal cartilage-free zone

**Significance** — clinically irrelevant **unless it engages**; **end-range (ABER) instability**, not mid-range (TO)

**Glenoid bone loss (TO)** — from anteroinferior rim fracture or **erosion** by humeral head; **6.8% loss per dislocation** (average)

**Reverse Hill-Sachs** — anteromedial head; **posterior** dislocation; lesser tuberosity fracture = posterior dislocation

## 3 Presentation

**History** — traumatic event; ask **formal reduction vs spontaneous**; instability feeling; pain from subluxation/excess translation

**Apprehension** — supine, **90° abduction + 90° ER** → apprehension; positive in **mid-range abduction = glenoid bone loss**

**Relocation** — posterior force on humeral head → ↓ apprehension

**Load and shift** — grades 0–III (table below); **sulcus sign** with arm at side

### Diagnostic criteria – hyperlaxity

| Criterion | Threshold |
|---|---|
| **Beighton score** | **>4** |
| **Hyper-ER at side** | **>85°** |
| **Hyperabduction (Gagey)** | **>105°** |
| Load–shift | **>2+ in ≥2 planes** |

**ISIS hyperlaxity** — anterior (ER >85°) or inferior (Gagey) laxity = 1 point

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality / view | Use |
|---|---|
| **Trauma series: true AP (Grashey) + scapular Y + axillary** | Required; Grashey = beam ~45° off AP, end-on GH joint; **Y = humeral head over Y intersection** (scapular spine + coracoid) |
| **Axillary** | **Best for detecting dislocation**; bone loss visible here = already significant; Velpeau if can't abduct |
| **West Point** | **Glenoid bone loss / bony Bankart** |
| **Stryker notch** | **Hill-Sachs** (supine, hand on head, **10° cephalic**); poor for Bankart |
| AP in ER | Hill-Sachs (ISIS item) |
| Bernageau profile | Glenoid profile, "cliff sign"; **less accurate than 3D imaging** |
| **CT ± 3D** | Bony injury, **% glenoid bone loss**; **3D CT = gold standard** |
| CT arthrogram | **MRI contraindicated** (pacemaker, cochlear implant); poorer soft tissue |
| **MRI** | **Best for labral tear**; validated for bone loss |
| **MR arthrogram** | **Study of choice for soft tissue**: sens **86–91%**, spec **86–96%** (MRI 44–100% / 66–95%) |

### MRI positioning – ABER view

**ABER** — **abduction + ER** → **humerus in coronal plane, glenoid in axial plane**

**Shows** — **anteroinferior labrum**, **anterior band IGHL**, articular surface of supraspinatus, posterosuperior labral peel-back (sens 73%, spec 100%)

### Glenoid bone loss – assessment & critical thresholds

| Method | Fact |
|---|---|
| **3D CT en face** | **Gold standard**; **highest interrater reliability**; predictive error **similar to MRI** (Rerko) |
| **Best-fit circle (TO)** | Circle fitted to **posterior + inferior** glenoid ≈ original glenoid; or **contralateral glenoid** reference |
| Bare-spot method | Bare spot = glenoid centre (Burkhart); GBL = (B − A) / 2B (A = bare spot→anterior edge, B = →posterior edge) |
| MRI | Tends to **overestimate** GBL (✗ Wrong options: underestimates); less bone–soft tissue contrast; cuts must be en face |
| MR arthrogram | Not a substitute for CT (poorer bony definition) |
| X-ray (Bernageau, West Point) | Significantly less accurate |

**Worked example (OB)** — bare spot 9 mm anterior, 12 mm posterior → (12 − 9)/24 = **12.5%** + non-engaging HS → **Bankart alone**

**Worked example (OB)** — 6.9 mm lost of 28.3 mm → **24.4%** = critical → **coracoid transfer**

**Inverted pear** — glenoid morphology with ↑ bone loss (**>25%**, TO/OB); **89% failure** after arthroscopic repair

**Glenoid shape** — normally pear-shaped (narrow superior); anteroinferior loss → **inverted pear**

| Threshold | Meaning | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **>20–25%** | **Critical**; soft tissue alone fails (>2/3 failure) → bony procedure | Orthobullets, most OB |
| **>25–30%** | Osseous augmentation needed | OB (bone loss explanations) |
| **20–30%** | "Glenoid bone deficiency" → Latarjet; **>60% recurrence** with Bankart alone | OB |
| **15–25%** | Biomechanically unstable | OB |
| **21%** of glenoid length | Instability + ↓ ER after Bankart (Itoi) | OB |
| **17.3%** | Critical value (Shin: 43.9% vs 3.7% recurrence) | OB |
| **>13.5%** | **Subcritical** – critical may be this low; open Bankart ↑ recurrence | Orthobullets, OB |
| **<15%** | Soft-tissue stabilisation alone works | OB, TO |

**Bone-loss conflict** — critical **20–25%** (Orthobullets) · **25–30%** (some OB) · **13.5–17.3%** subcritical (recent studies) — read the options

**Strongest predictor of failure after arthroscopic Bankart** — **glenoid bone loss**\
✗ Wrong options: age at first dislocation · age at surgery · >3 anchors · on-track HS

**Most predictive intra-op finding** — **anterior glenoid deficiency 35%**\
✗ Wrong options: non-engaging HS · loose body · bursitis · 10% PASTA

**Mechanism lost with rim loss** — **concavity-compression**

### Hill-Sachs lesion & glenoid track (on/off-track)

**Bipolar concept** — glenoid bone loss + Hill-Sachs together; bipolar > unipolar engagement risk

**Glenoid track (TO)** — area of posterior humeral articular surface in contact with glenoid along **posterior end-range (ABD + ER)**; width = medial margin of track → medial margin of cuff footprint

| Formula | Value |
|---|---|
| **GT = 0.83D − d** | D = inferior glenoid diameter, d = anterior bone loss width (Orthobullets, TO, most OB) |
| GT = **0.84(D) − ABL** | OB (glenoid-track formula question) |
| 84% vs 83% (TO) | **84% cadaveric** · **83% live at 90° abduction** |
| **HSI = HS + BB** | Hill-Sachs width + **bony bridge** to cuff insertion |
| **HSI > GT** | **Off-track = engaging** → recurrence |
| **HSI < GT** | **On-track = non-engaging** |

**Coefficient conflict** — **0.83** (Orthobullets, TO, most OB) · **0.84** (one OB; "HS >84% of glenoid width = off-track") — read the options

![Glenoid track vs Hill-Sachs interval – on-track (A), off-track from glenoid loss (B) or large Hill-Sachs (C)](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3050-tracklesions.jpg)

**Medial extension** — **the more medial the HS extends, the more likely off-track** ✓

**✗ Glenoid-track traps** — on-track engages · unipolar > bipolar risk · **GT only by CT** (MRI also usable) · HSI < GT engages

**Dynamic arthroscopic exam (TO)** — assess engagement **after the Bankart repair**

**Tyre & pothole** — glenoid track = tyre, HSI = pothole; off-track = tyre falls in (big HS or big glenoid loss)

**Off-track predictive value** — 75% vs 8% recurrence (Shaha); **superior to >20% bone-loss cut-off**

| Worked example | Calculation | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **Kabaddi (TO)**: D 24, width 20, HSI 18 | d = 4 → **16.6%**; GT = 19.92 − 4 = **15.92** | **Off-track + subcritical, elite contact → Latarjet** |
| Water polo (OB): D 29, d 2, HSI 19 | GBL **6.9%**; GT = 24.07 − 2 = **22.07** | **On-track → isolated arthroscopic Bankart** |
| TO: 25% GBL, GT 18, HSI 21.3 | Off-track + critical | **Latarjet** |

### Instability Severity Index Score (ISIS – Balg & Boileau)

| Factor | Parameter | Points |
|---|---|---|
| **Age at surgery** | **<20 y** | **2** |
| **Sport level** | **Competitive** | **2** |
| **Sport type** | **Contact or forced overhead** | **1** |
| **Hyperlaxity** | Anterior or inferior | **1** |
| **Hill-Sachs on AP in ER** | Visible | **2** |
| **Glenoid contour on AP** | **Loss of contour** | **2** |
| **Total** | | **10** |

| Score | Recurrence after arthroscopic Bankart | Advice |
|---|---|---|
| **≤6** | **~10%** acceptable (3 points → 5%) | Arthroscopic stabilisation |
| **>6** | **~70%** | **Open / Latarjet** |

**Latarjet trigger** — ISIS **>6** (Orthobullets summary, OB, TO) · ISIS **>4–6** (Orthobullets technique) — read the options

**ISIS worked (OB)** — 18 ♀ competitive tennis (forced overhead), HS + glenoid contour loss = **9** (>70%) · competitive + contact + HS on ER AP, age 21, normal laxity (ER 80°, negative Gagey) = **5**

### Anteroposterior translation (load & shift)

| Grade | Translation |
|---|---|
| 0 | Normal |
| 1+ | **Up to glenoid rim** |
| 2+ | **Over rim, spontaneously reduces** |
| 3+ | Over rim, **does not reduce** |

### Sulcus sign

| Grade | Acromiohumeral interval |
|---|---|
| 1 | **<1 cm** |
| 2 | **1–2 cm** |
| 3 | **>2 cm** |

### Normal variants mistaken for pathology (Buford complex)

| Variant (TO) | Location | Feature | Prevalence |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Sublabral foramen (hole)** | **1–3 o'clock** (anterosuperior) | Unattached anterosuperior labrum | **11%** (TO) · ~12% (Orthobullets) |
| **Sublabral recess** | **12 o'clock** (biceps anchor) | Doesn't extend to 1–3 o'clock; **mimics SLAP** | — |
| **Buford complex** | **1–3 o'clock** | **Absent anterosuperior labrum + thick cord-like MGHL** | **~1.5%** |

![Sublabral foramen, sublabral recess, Buford complex](../../MCQ/attachments/TO17121231-1.png)

**Buford (TO)** — normal variant; **not associated with recurrent dislocation**; do not repair (→ loss of ER)

## 5 Management

```
First-time traumatic anterior dislocation
├─ Reduce under sedation / IA lidocaine → traction-countertraction (commonest)
│      └─ fails 5–10% → blocks: biceps tendon, capsule, fracture fragments
├─ Low-risk → sling ≤1 wk + PT (cuff + periscapular)
├─ Age >40 + persistent weakness → MRI shoulder (cuff tear)
├─ In-season athlete, no bone loss → rehab, RTP in-season → surgery end of season
└─ Young (<20–25) contact / military + Bankart on MRI → early arthroscopic Bankart
```

```
Recurrent instability – glenoid side (3D CT)
├─ GBL <13.5–15% + on-track → arthroscopic Bankart (≥3–4 anchors)
├─ GBL <15% + off-track → Bankart + remplissage
├─ Subcritical 13.5–25% → contact athlete / high ISIS → Latarjet
│      └─ non-contact, off-track → Bankart + remplissage
├─ GBL >20–25% / inverted pear → Latarjet (coracoid transfer)
└─ Failed Latarjet / massive loss → ICBG (Eden-Hybinette) or distal tibia allograft
```

```
Recurrent instability – humeral side (Hill-Sachs)
├─ On-track / non-engaging (<20%) → nothing extra
├─ Off-track / engaging 20–40% (>25%) → remplissage
└─ >40% → humeral head allograft / ICBG; hemiarthroplasty if >40–45% + arthritis
```

### Operative criteria

| Procedure | Indications |
|---|---|
| **Arthroscopic Bankart ± capsular plication** | **First-time** dislocation + MRI Bankart in athlete **<25 y** · high-demand athletes · **recurrent (>1) dislocation** after nonop · **GBL <20–25%** · on-track (± remplissage if off-track) |
| **Open Bankart ± capsular shift** | GBL <20–25% · **revision after failed arthroscopic Bankart** without GBL >20% · **HAGL** · acute glenoid fracture · **hyperlax needing formal capsular shift** · contact athlete returning to sport |
| **Latarjet / Bristow** | **GBL >20–25%** (inverted pear) · **subcritical >13.5%** in high-risk · **ISIS >6** · contact athletes / athletes of consequence (climbers, big-wave surfers) · Europe uses aggressively |
| **ICBG / distal tibia / distal clavicle graft** | GBL >20–25% · **revision of failed Latarjet** · **less graft resorption** than Latarjet |
| **Remplissage + Bankart** | **Off-track / engaging** HS (**>25–40%**) with **GBL <20–25%** · subcritical GBL (arthroscopic-alone recurrence ~20%) |
| **Hill-Sachs bone graft** | Engaging HS **>40%** |
| **Tendon transfer (conjoint / LHB)** | Capsulolabral deficiency; with labral repair if **GBL <15%** |

| Bipolar matrix (TO) | Hill-Sachs on-track | Hill-Sachs off-track |
|---|---|---|
| **GBL <15%** | **Bankart** | **Bankart + remplissage** |
| **GBL 15–25%** | **Latarjet** (Bankart + remplissage if lower demand) | **Latarjet** |
| **GBL >25%** | Latarjet or ICBG | Latarjet or ICBG |

| TO algorithm: GBL | Hill-Sachs | Treatment (TO) |
|---|---|---|
| **<10%** | None / non-engaging | **Bankart** |
| <10% | Engaging, head loss <30% | **Remplissage** |
| <10% | Engaging, head loss >30% | **Latarjet** |
| **10–25%** | None / non-engaging | **ISIS <6 → Bankart · >6 → Latarjet** |
| 10–25% | Engaging, head loss <20% | **Remplissage** |
| 10–25% | Engaging, head loss >20% | **Latarjet** |
| **25–40%** | Any | **Latarjet** + ICBG to humeral head if head loss >30% |
| **>40%** | Any | **ICBG / allograft** |

**TO Hill-Sachs size rule** — non-engaging (**usually <20%**) → nothing · engaging **20–40%** → remplissage · engaging **>40%** → bone graft (Eden-Hybinette)

**TO soft-tissue zone** — isolated Bankart if GBL **<15–20%** (stricter **13.5%** best-fit circle in high-demand athletes) + on-track

**TO subcritical (15–20%)** — **contact athlete (rugby, kabaddi, wrestling) → Latarjet even at 15%** (Bankart failure ~30%) · non-contact → Bankart + remplissage

**TO bone grafting triggers** — GBL >20–25% · **failed previous Bankart** · bipolar loss making an on-track HS off-track

**Wrestler, 30 dislocations (incl. in sleep), Bankart + 40% HS (TO)** → **Latarjet**\
✗ Wrong options: Bankart · remplissage · McLaughlin

### Recurrence risk – age, ISIS score & natural history

| Age at first dislocation | Recurrence with nonop | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **<20 y** | **~90%** (80–90% teens; >90%) | Orthobullets, OB |
| **14–18 y** | **~90%** | TO |
| **<25 y** | **60–94%**; likely to re-dislocate | OB, TO |
| <30 y | ↑ risk (significant) | OB |
| All comers | **33–67%** | OB |

**Most significant risk factor (TO, OB)** — **young age (<20)**; only consistent predictor regardless of immobilisation/exercise

**Age cut-off conflict** — **<20** (Orthobullets, ISIS, OB) · **<25** (TO, some OB) · **<30** (OB immobilisation question) · 21–30 (Kralinger) — read the options

| Recurrence risk factors (TO) | |
|---|---|
| **Modifiable** | **Manual occupation**, overhead occupation, immobilisation after dislocation, **collision sport**, time to RTS, psychosocial (pain, **fear of reinjury**) |
| **Non-modifiable** | **GT fracture**, **Hill-Sachs**, **bony Bankart**, **axillary nerve palsy**, **age**, **gender**, **hypermobility** |

**Orthobullets risk factors for re-dislocation** — **age <20 (highest)**, **male**, **contact sport**, **hyperlaxity**, **GBL >20–25%**, **GT fracture**

**Hill-Sachs & recurrence** — HS **not significantly associated** (TO) · HS = ISIS item / predictor after Bankart (OB) — read the options

**Dominance** — **non-dominant less likely to recur** (dominant limb used in high-risk activity; TO) · **no difference** dominant vs non-dominant (OB) — read the options

**Not top risk factors (OB)** — contralateral dislocation (seen in 25% of recurrent) · family history (34% recurrence risk) · patellar instability · dominance

**Natural history (Hovelius, 25 y nonop)** — 43% never re-dislocated · 15% re-dislocated then stabilised · 27% had surgery · immobilisation length no effect

**Recurrent after PT, dislocates in sleep** → **discuss surgical stabilisation**\
✗ Wrong options: steroid · NSAIDs · change therapist · 6 wk immobilisation

### First-time dislocation – nonoperative vs early arthroscopic Bankart

**First-time dislocators** — management controversial; **ASES: surgery for athletes 14–30 y at end of season** if **positive apprehension + bone loss**

**Reduction techniques** — **traction-countertraction (commonest)** · **Kocher**: arm at side in ER → forward flex → IR · **Hippocratic**: heel in axilla · **Stimson**: prone, weight hung from arm · scapular manipulation

**Immobilisation** — **no benefit >1 wk**; short (≤1 wk) = prolonged (≥3 wk) recurrence (41% vs 37%, Paterson) → **may stop sling at 1 wk**

**ER immobilisation** — early studies ↓ recurrence <40 y (labrum reduced to glenoid) → **subsequent studies refuted**; IR 24.7% vs ER 30.8% (Liavaag, NS)

**Immobilisation conflict** — no benefit / refuted (Orthobullets, OB) · **immobilisation decreases recurrence** (TO) — read the options

**Bracing** — **no evidence it ↓ recurrence**; limits ABD + ER in season

**Return to play (nonop)** — **faster than surgery**; 7–21 days (Orthobullets) · 2–3 wk (OB) · **3–4 wk** with full painless ROM + strength (OB) → can finish season

**Nonop vs surgery recurrence** — nonop **higher**: 88% vs 14% (Arciero) · 77% vs 11% (Bottoni); RTP without recurrence next season 40% vs 90% (Dickens)

**Delaying surgery** — further events → more labral damage, **more bone loss**, ↑ failure after Bankart; trend = **surgery after first dislocation**

**Bony vs soft-tissue (first-time)** — Latarjet **equal or lower** recurrence than Bankart

**Water polo, 3rd dislocation, GBL <7%, HSI 19 < GT 22.07** → **isolated arthroscopic Bankart**\
✗ Wrong options: remplissage → ↓ ER · Latarjet / DTA · humeral OATS

### Arthroscopic vs open Bankart repair

| | **Arthroscopic** | **Open** |
|---|---|---|
| Re-dislocation | **Similar** (RCTs) · slightly higher in some · 11% vs 8% (Harris) · 15.1% vs 7.7% (Rollick) | Similar |
| ROM | **Slightly better** (less pain, ER preserved) | More pain, ↓ ROM |
| Return to work/sport | Equal (RCT) | **Equal or slightly better** (some studies) |
| Arthritis | **No difference** | No difference |
| Approach | Arthroscopic, ≥3 (preferably 4) anchors | **Deltopectoral**; subscapularis **transverse split or tenotomy**; **inferior capsule shifted superiorly** |

**Arthroscopic advantages** — cosmesis, ↓ morbidity, ↓ ER loss, diagnostic confirmation

**Contraindications to arthroscopic (Stein)** — significant GBL · attenuated capsulolabral tissue · engaging HS · **HAGL** (open = gold standard)

**Isolated open Bankart + capsular shift** — **recurrent after arthroscopic repair, minimal GBL (6%), on-track, contact athlete** ✓

**✗ Open Bankart distractors** — 20% GBL rugby (→ Latarjet) · 5% GBL off-track (→ Bankart ± remplissage) · 45% HS (→ humeral graft) · 18% GBL wrestler (→ bony)

**Technique** — **fully mobilise labrum** before repair · **≥3 anchors** (<3 → failure) · "drive-through sign" pre-repair · knotless anchors

**Too many anchors** — **postage-stamp fracture** through anchor holes

**Recurrence after arthroscopic Bankart** — 13% (Ahmed) · 15.3% (Boileau) · 4% without bone defect vs **67%** with (Burkhart) · **89%** contact athletes with inverted pear

### Bony Bankart – arthroscopic fixation

**Small bony Bankart (<20%)** — **arthroscopic repair incorporating the fragment**; 95% RTS (Sugaya)\
✗ Wrong options: ORIF · iliac crest · coracoid transfer

**26-y accountant, 3rd dislocation, 10% bony defect, no prior surgery** → **arthroscopic bony Bankart repair**\
✗ Wrong options: ER immobilisation · remplissage · coracoid/iliac crest

**ORIF** — large **acute** glenoid rim fractures only\
✗ Wrong options: recurrent instability

**Bony Bankart + deltoid weakness after reduction** → **bony Bankart stabilisation** + observe axillary nerve (EMG changes in ~48%; surgery only if no recovery at **3–6 months**)

**Fixation** — open: screws or suture (linear / bridge)

**Imaging** — 3D CT > CT > MRI > X-ray for bone loss (MOON)

**Unaddressed bony Bankart** — commonest cause of failed soft-tissue Bankart\
✗ Wrong options: labral nonunion · HAGL · SLAP

### Remplissage

**Remplissage ("to fill")** — **infraspinatus + posterior capsule** sutured into Hill-Sachs (posterior capsulodesis + infraspinatus tenodesis) → **intra-articular HS becomes extra-articular** (TO) → can't engage rim in ER

**Goal (TO)** — ✓ extra-articular HS\
✗ Wrong options: more secure labrum · ↑ head diameter · ↑ glenoid track width

**Effect** — shortens HSI → **off-track → on-track**; ↑ rotation before engagement (**does not increase glenoid track**)

**Indications** — **off-track/engaging HS** + GBL **<20–25%** (<15% TO) · HS **>25%** (OB) / **25–40%** (Orthobullets) · chronic Bankart + engaging HS catching in **90/90** → **arthroscopic Bankart + remplissage**\
✗ Wrong options: remplissage alone · humeral graft alone

**Technique** — arthroscopic, percutaneous / posterolateral portal; **pass sutures before anterior repair, tie after**; knotted or knotless; **double-pulley**

**Post-op** — **avoid forward flexion + adduction** (tensions posterior capsule; abduction need not be avoided); brief ER immobiliser

**Complications** — **ER loss 5–15°**; recurrence **5% at 2 y**; IR loss <2°

**Outcomes** — vs **Latarjet (2 y): 1.4% vs 3.2% recurrence** despite greater bipolar loss · vs **Bankart alone: ↓ recurrence + revision** (MacDonald RCT) · Latarjet better ER

**Moderate HS + labral tear after 2nd dislocation** → **arthroscopic labral repair + remplissage**\
✗ Wrong options: PT · labral repair alone · hemiarthroplasty – HS not >45% · thermal capsulorrhaphy

**Connolly procedure** — **infraspinatus + GT bone block** into HS · analogous **Neer-McLaughlin** = subscapularis + LT into **reverse HS** (posterior)

**Humeral head allograft (OATS) (TO)** — massive deep HS **>40%** where remplissage would severely limit ER

**HS bone graft outcomes** — small cohorts; early arthritis **16% at 4 y**; Constant 78.5–86.8; options: allograft, arthroplasty, rotational osteotomy

### Latarjet/bone block – indications, mechanism & outcomes

**Latarjet** — transfer of **coracoid + conjoined tendon + CA ligament** to anteroinferior glenoid through **subscapularis split**; deltopectoral; open = arthroscopic outcomes (steep arthroscopic learning curve)\
✗ Wrong options: detached

| Triple effect | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| **Bony** | ↑ glenoid surface/width, **lengthens glenoid track / articular arc** |
| **Sling (TO)** | **Conjoint tendon** (short head biceps + coracobrachialis) over lower subscapularis → **dynamic stability to anteroinferior capsule in ABD + ER**; brace across lower third of joint |
| **Capsular** | **CA ligament stump** repairs capsule |

**Triple effect question** — **all three (bony + sling + CA ligament)** ✓

**Sling effect (TO)** — ✓ conjoint tendon dynamic stability\
✗ Wrong options: CA ligament transfer = capsular · bone surface = bony · LHB tenodesis = DAS

| | **Traditional Latarjet** | **Congruent arc** | **Bristow** |
