|---|---|---|---|
| Coracoid orientation | **Undersurface on glenoid**; lateral surface = new track | **Rotated 90°**; **inferior surface** = track, articulates with head | **Distal ~1 cm tip**, orientation unchanged |
| Track gain | Less (~10 mm) | **Most: ~15 mm** | Less |
| Note | **Less fracture-prone** at screw insertion | Better for **severe bone loss** | Less common; **MC nerve tethering** (TO) |

![Traditional (a–b, ~10 mm) vs congruent arc (c–d, ~15 mm) Latarjet](../../MCQ/attachments/q216803-illb-latarjet-types.jpg)

**Graft position** — flush, **no lateral overhang** (→ OA) · not **≥1 cm medial** or >1.5 cm migration (→ redislocation) · intra- vs extra-articular placement (intra-articular OA concern unproven)

**Outcomes** — recurrence **0–8%** (<10%) · good–excellent **>90%** · RTS same level **75–90%** · RTS any level 80–100%

**Return-to-sport conflict** — same level **75–90%** (OB) · 72.6% (Hurley) · 56–95% (Glogovac) · 49% contact athletes (Privitera) — read the options

**Latarjet vs Bankart (bone loss)** — 4.9% vs 67% recurrence (Burkhart); Latarjet 2.7% (Rollick) with ↑ complications

| Clinical scenario (OB) | Answer |
|---|---|
| **Inverted pear + recurrent instability, failed arthroscopic Bankart** | **Coracoid autograft (Latarjet)** |
| GBL **>20%**, rugby, 5 dislocations | **Open coracoid transfer** |
| GBL **30%** (best-fit circle), no prior surgery | **Open coracoid transfer** (✗ Wrong options: capsular shift = MDI · ORIF) |
| GBL **30%**, Beighton 2, HS | **Bony glenoid augmentation** (Latarjet / distal tibia) |
| GBL **33%**, lacrosse | Latarjet – **conjoint tendon sling** |
| Failed arthroscopic Bankart, **20% GBL** | **Latarjet – recurrence <10%** |
| **23-y pro rugby, small HS, 15% GBL** | **Latarjet** (✗ Wrong options: 33-y recreational with 10% → Bankart ± remplissage · linebacker large HS 6% → remplissage / humeral allograft · swimmer circumferential tear → repair) |
| ≥10 dislocations, **24.4% GBL**, HS 15.4 mm | **Coracoid transfer** |
| Pole vaulter, off-track HS, significant GBL | **Coracoid transfer** (✗ Wrong options: remplissage + plication without labral repair · latissimus transfer with competent subscap) |
| Infantryman, **25% GBL**, off-track, failed repair | **Coracoid transfer** (✗ Wrong options: re-repair with ≥3 anchors · arthroplasty) |
| Chronic anterior dislocation + inverted pear | **Open reduction + glenoid augmentation** (if HS <20%) |
| Failed Bankart + **postage-stamp fracture** | **Bone block** |
| Failed arthroscopic Bankart, GBL >20–25% on CT | **Open capsulorrhaphy + bone block** (✗ Wrong options: arthrodesis) |

**Chronic dislocation (Sahajpal)** — glenoid defect **>20–30% → bony augmentation**; humeral defect engaging or **20–40% → address**; **>40% → hemiarthroplasty**

**Postage-stamp fractures** — anterior rim fractures through anchor holes; male **≤25 y**, sport, **≥3 anchors**, **knot-tying anchors**, anchor-site osteolysis → **bone block**

**Recurrence predictors after Bankart (OB)** — age <20, Hill-Sachs, glenoid lesion, hyperlaxity, off-track\
✗ Wrong options: dominant side · SLAP · >5 preop dislocations · on-track

**Distal tibia allograft (DTA)** — **true osteochondral graft**; **matches humeral head radius of curvature** + large articular surface (TO); functions mainly by **↑ glenoid track**; revision of failed Latarjet\
✗ Wrong options: sling – differs from Latarjet

**Iliac crest (Eden-Hybinette) (TO)** — autograft for large defects where **coracoid volume inadequate**; 89% healing at 1.4 y; screws or buttons; risks: hardware failure, subscapularis repair failure

**Revising a failed Latarjet** — hardware removal + ICBG or DTA; electrocautery/nerve check to protect MC nerve (scarred, overlaps axillary nerve)

**Dynamic anterior stabilisation (DAS, TO)** — **LHB transferred to anterior glenoid through subscapularis split** → **sling effect**; for **chronic** anteroinferior instability with **poor labrum/GHL quality** and **limited bone loss**; also with SLAP, overhead athletes/throwers

**DAS technique (TO)** — LHB tenotomy, whipstitch · split subscap in middle · drill **3 o'clock**, 1.5–2.0 cm deep · tenodesis screw · add labral repair (bumper effect)

**DAS contraindications (TO)** — **significant bone defects** · LHB lesions · subscapularis lesion · prior LHB tenotomy/tenodesis; fills gap between soft-tissue and bone-block procedures\
✗ Wrong options: alternative to Latarjet with bone loss

**Conjoint tendon / LHB transfer (Orthobullets)** — conjoint detached from coracoid tip (CA ligament preserved) or LHB; socket in anterior glenoid + suspensory fixation through subscap split; low recurrence with labral repair if **GBL <15%**; biceps possibly better; no ROM loss

**Latarjet in epileptics** — contraindicated with **uncontrolled seizures**

### Seizure-related dislocation

**Epileptic, >10 dislocations, uncontrolled seizures, GBL >20–25%** → **closed reduction + neurology referral**\
✗ Wrong options: no reduction attempt · reduction alone · stabilisation now · fusion

**Rule** — **exhaust medical (seizure) management before surgery**; high recurrence even with bony augmentation

**Pathology** — **large bony defects** (HS + bony Bankart) + degenerative change; muscle contraction + trauma; anterior = posterior frequency

**Surgery once controlled** — **bony procedure** (Latarjet / bone graft); soft tissue alone fails

### Failed stabilisation with subscapularis deficiency – tendon transfer

**Signs** — **belly-press** weakness; post-open-repair subscap failure → **positive lift-off + excessive ER**

**Irreparable** — retraction + **atrophy** on MRI

**29-y, failed arthroscopic + open Bankart, irreparable subscap** → **tendon transfer (latissimus dorsi or pectoralis major)**\
✗ Wrong options: PT · Latarjet · Magnuson-Stack · RTSA – age

**Latissimus vs pec major** — latissimus **better replicates subscap line of pull / IR moment arm**, better scores; pec major historically to lesser tuberosity; pec major not after failed subscap repair post-arthroplasty

### Historical & thermal procedures (Putti-Platt, capsulorrhaphy)

| Procedure | Technique | Problem |
|---|---|---|
| **Putti-Platt** | **Lateral advancement of subscapularis + medial advancement of capsule** (imbrication) | **Loss of ER**, **arthrosis** with ↓ ROM (TO) |
| **Magnuson-Stack** | Subscapularis advanced lateral to bicipital groove (± to GT) | ↓ ER, posterior glenoid loading |
| **Boyd-Sisk** | Biceps transferred laterally + posteriorly | **High recurrence** |
| Bristow | Coracoid tip to anterior rim | **MC nerve injury** commoner (TO) |

**Putti-Platt (TO)** — ✓ arthrosis with compromised ROM · (✗ Wrong options: redislocation never reported · rotates HS by ↑ retroversion · MC nerve injury common (infrequent)) · Putti-Platt + Bristow → **IR contractures**

**Post-capsulorrhaphy arthropathy** — 1970s–80s open repair → pain, stiffness, **lack of ER**, **posterior glenoid wear + retroversion**, IR contracture

**Thermal capsulorrhaphy (TO)** — used in **shoulder instability**; monopolar RF, introduced **1996**\
✗ Wrong options: adhesive capsulitis · dural tear · stiff elbow

**Thermal mechanism (TO)** — **65–80°C** shortens capsule; collagen denaturation, ↓ mechanical properties, cell necrosis → fibroblast repair; stretches if loaded early; **grid/stripe** technique aids healing; ↓ capsular proprioceptors

**Thermal outcomes (TO)** — volume ↓ **26–36%** (vs plication **19%**); best in mild, unidirectional anterior instability **with** suture repair; **isolated → unacceptably high failure**

**Thermal complications (TO)** — **failure (commonest)**, **chondrolysis**, axillary nerve injury, capsular attenuation; now contraindicated

## 6 Complications

### Recurrence

**Commonest cause** — **unrecognised glenoid bone loss** with soft-tissue-only repair (80% of failures) · missed off-track HS · **<3–4 anchors** · missed HAGL · missed pan-labral tear (military) · seizures

**Anchor conflict** — **<4 anchors** = risk (Orthobullets) · **<3 anchors** = risk (OB) — read the options

**Age at surgery** — not related to failure (one OB) · independent predictor (Ahmed, OB) — read the options

### Bankart repair complications

**Stiffness** — **loss of ER** (overtightening; worse with remplissage) → treat **Z-lengthening of subscapularis**; overtightening → **post-capsulorrhaphy arthropathy** (older patients)

**Subscapularis failure (open)** — positive lift-off, excessive ER

**Hardware** — anchor pull-out; postage-stamp fracture

**Chondrolysis** — **thermal capsulorrhaphy** or **intra-articular pain pumps** (both now contraindicated)

### Neurovascular injury – axillary nerve & artery (dislocation, capsular shift)

**Most common nerve after dislocation** — **axillary** (course around surgical neck, close to joint); typically **neurapraxia** (stretch)\
✗ Wrong options: neurotmesis = penetrating/laceration · brachial plexus · MC

| Axillary nerve injury rate | Source |
|---|---|
| **Up to 5%** (transient neurapraxia) | Orthobullets, OB (older cohort) |
| **42%** clinical + EMG (Visser) | OB |
| **~48%** EMG changes | OB |
| Any nerve injury **21–65%**; neuro deficit 13.5% (Robinson); 11.6% (Atef) | OB |

**Rate conflict** — **≤5%** clinical (Orthobullets) vs **42–48%** EMG (OB) — read the options

**Axillary nerve** — **C5–6** (upper trunk, posterior cord) → deltoid + teres minor; weakness esp. **30–100° abduction**; ↓ sensation **lateral proximal arm**

**Timeline** — neurodiagnostics after first **2–3 wk** (14–21 d if no recovery) · persistent weakness + sensory loss at **12 wk → EMG/NCS** · surgery (neurolysis, graft, transfer) if **no recovery 3–6 mo**\
✗ Wrong options: observe more · repeat MRI · explore · triceps branch transfer before EDS

**High-grade injury risk (Avis)** — proximal humerus fracture, multiple nerves, neuropathic pain, **delayed reduction**

**Surgical anatomy** — axillary nerve anterior to subscapularis → drapes **inferolateral border** → **quadrilateral space**; **12 mm** (Orthobullets) · **12–16 mm** (OB) · 1–1.5 cm (Kang) from inferior glenoid / infraglenoid tubercle

**Arthroscopic risk zone** — **6 o'clock, inferior to infraglenoid tubercle** (also 4:30) during capsular shift/plication; plication **≤1 cm from rim safe**, >1 cm risk; anteroinferior portals riskiest\
✗ Wrong options: spinoglenoid/suprascapular notch = suprascapular n. · 9 o'clock posterior · 12 o'clock biceps

**Protective position** — **abduction + external rotation** ↑ nerve–capsule distance\
✗ Wrong options: adduction/IR – beach chair adduction + IR = risk

**Open capsular shift** — injury during **dissection of subscapularis off inferior capsule** / retractor on inferomedial subscap belly → **can't abduct, elbow flexion + supination intact = axillary (C5–6)**\
✗ Wrong options: MC C5–7 · lower subscapular nerve

**Axillary artery** — **1–2 cm** from inferior glenoid · closest at **≥90° ER: 4–5 mm** (4.1 mm Stone) · ≥2 cm from coracoid · **greatest risk at inferior glenoid with arm in ER**\
✗ Wrong options: thoracoacromial – at coracoid · MC – medial · radial – triangular interval · ulnar

### Latarjet complications – musculocutaneous nerve, graft resorption & overhang arthritis

**Complication rate** — up to **25%** (Orthobullets) · 15–30% (Gupta) · 30% (Griesser); higher than Bankart

| Complication | Fact |
|---|---|
| **MC nerve injury** | **Most common nerve** (Orthobullets, most OB); **instrumentation / aggressive medial retraction around conjoint tendon**, coracoid harvest; pierces coracobrachialis ~**5.6 cm** distal to coracoid tip |
| **Axillary nerve injury** | **During graft fixation**; 12 mm from infraglenoid tubercle; 2nd commonest |
| **Graft lysis/resorption** | Up to **90%** some resorption **within 6 months**; **superior** graft; late pain + apprehension at ~9 mo |
| **Lateral overhang** | **GH OA** (up to **38%**); 20% new arthritis (Mizuno) |
| **Stiffness** | **ER loss** (✗ Wrong options: IR · elbow flexion) |
| Nonunion, hardware (screw pull-out) | |
| Axillary artery | 4–5 mm at 90° ER |
| Subscapularis rupture | **Low** – split, not detached |

**MC nerve** — **terminal branch of lateral cord**, **C5–7**; → coracobrachialis, biceps, brachialis; injury → **lateral forearm numbness (LACN)** + **↓ elbow flexion/supination**

**Latarjet nerve conflict** — **MC = most common** (Orthobullets, most OB) · **axillary = most common** (one OB; neuromonitoring: all clinical deficits axillary) — read the options

**Resorption conflict** — common, **up to 90%** (Orthobullets, OB) · "uncommon overall", mostly first 6 mo (another OB) — read the options

**Nerve injury management** — mostly **traction/contusion neurapraxia** → **observe 3–6 wk**; delayed EMG if persistent; all resolved by 6 mo (neuromonitoring)

![Axillary and musculocutaneous nerves relative to coracoid tip](../../MCQ/attachments/q214994-illa-axillaryandmusculocutaneousnerve.jpg)

**After Latarjet** — MC + axillary nerves move medially, **MC overlaps axillary** → revision risk; axillary artery position unchanged

**✗ Nerve distractors** — lateral winging (spinal accessory) · medial winging (long thoracic) · IR weakness (subscapular) · thumb extension weakness (PIN/radial) · median, ulnar, radial (paths far)

**Sling post-Latarjet** — protects **graft healing**\
✗ Wrong options: subscap detached

### Older patients – rotator cuff tear after dislocation

**Rule** — young → recurrence · **old → rotator cuff tear**

| Age | Cuff tear after dislocation |
|---|---|
| **>40 y** | **30%** (Orthobullets, OB) · "30% at 30 y" (one OB) |
| **>60 y** | **80%** |

**Tendons** — "**SIT**": **supraspinatus** (commonest), infraspinatus, teres minor; recurrent instability in elderly → **subscapularis** tears\
✗ Wrong options: subscap – bear hug

**Cuff tear vs axillary palsy in elderly** — cuff tear **far more common**; **deltoid firing + full passive ROM + intact sensation** → cuff tear → **suprascapular nerve** muscles (SS/IS)\
✗ Wrong options: dorsal scapular · lateral pectoral · MC · axillary

**Persistent pain/weakness ≥2 wk after dislocation (>40 y)** → **MRI shoulder**\
✗ Wrong options: EMG · C-spine MRI · steroid injection – harms later repair · PT first · CT glenoid

**Test for commonest pathology** — **drop arm test** (supraspinatus)\
✗ Wrong options: O'Brien – SLAP · apprehension – young/bone loss · bear hug – subscap · Spurling – C-spine

**MR arthrogram** — **most sensitive (and specific) for full- + partial-thickness cuff tears**; MRI ≈ US

**Treatment** — surgical repair → better pain + satisfaction; full-thickness tears don't heal nonop; **GT fracture** with dislocation >50 y (↑ recurrence)

### Terrible triad of the shoulder

**Shoulder terrible triad (TO)** — **anterior dislocation + rotator cuff tear + brachial plexus (mostly axillary) nerve injury**; cuff usually **complete** tear

**EXCEPT (TO)** — **proximal humeral fracture**

### Late complications – chondrolysis, osteonecrosis & dislocation arthropathy

**Osteonecrosis (skeletally immature)** — rare; after fracture-dislocation or stabilisation → **observation** (revascularises)\
✗ Wrong options: arthroplasty · grafting · capsular release

**Chondrolysis** — progressive pain + stiffness ~1 y after Bankart with **heat (RF) probe** → joint space loss\
✗ Wrong options: frozen shoulder · subscap failure

**Dislocation arthropathy** — **age at first dislocation** (>25 y, Hovelius) + high-energy primary dislocation → severe OA; "**goat's beard**" inferior osteophyte; ↓ ER\
✗ Wrong options: current age · dominance · diabetes · sex

**After Bristow-Latarjet** — mild arthropathy 35%, severe 14%; first dislocation >23 y = risk

## X vs Y

| | **Bankart** | **Perthes** | **ALPSA** | **GLAD** | **HAGL** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Labrum | Off rim | Off rim, may look **in place** | **Medially displaced**, healed on neck | Partial tear | Intact on glenoid |
| Periosteum | Hinge intact | **Stripped, intact** | Sleeve intact medially | — | — |
| Cartilage | — | — | — | **Defect** | — |
| Failure point | Glenoid | Glenoid | Glenoid neck | Chondrolabral | **Humerus** |
| Best repair | Arthroscopic | Arthroscopic | Arthroscopic (↑ failure) | Arthroscopic | **Open** |

| | **Young dislocator (<25)** | **Older dislocator (>40)** |
|---|---|---|
| Main lesion | **Bankart** + Hill-Sachs | **Rotator cuff tear** (30% >40, 80% >60) |
| Main problem | **Recurrence** | Weakness, pseudoparalysis |
| Next test | MRI/MRA, 3D CT for bone | **MRI shoulder** |
| Nerve | Axillary neurapraxia | Axillary / plexus (less common than cuff) |

| | **Remplissage + Bankart** | **Latarjet** |
|---|---|---|
| Fixes | **Humeral side** (HSI ↓) | **Glenoid side** (GT ↑) |
| GBL | <15–25% | >20–25% or subcritical contact |
| Approach | Arthroscopic | Open (or arthroscopic) |
| ROM | **ER loss 5–15°** | ER loss; better ER than remplissage |
| Key risk | Stiffness | **MC nerve**, resorption, overhang OA |

| | **Latarjet** | **Distal tibia allograft** | **Iliac crest (Eden-Hybinette)** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Graft | Coracoid + conjoint + CA ligament | Osteochondral allograft | Tricortical autograft |
| Mechanism | **Triple effect** | **↑ glenoid track only** | Bony |
| Curvature | — | **Matches humeral head** | — |
| Use | Primary bony option | Massive loss, failed Latarjet | Large defect, coracoid inadequate, failed Latarjet |

| | **MC nerve (Latarjet)** | **Axillary nerve** |
|---|---|---|
| Cord / roots | Lateral cord, **C5–7** | Posterior cord, **C5–6** |
| When injured | Retraction / instrumentation **around conjoint tendon** | **Graft fixation**; capsular shift at 6 o'clock; dislocation |
| Deficit | **Lateral forearm numbness**, ↓ elbow flexion + **supination** | ↓ abduction (deltoid), lateral arm numbness |

| | **Anterior instability** | **Posterior instability** |
|---|---|---|
| Position | **ABD + ER** | **ADD + IR** |
| Labral lesion | Bankart, ALPSA, Perthes, GLAD | **Kim lesion**, posterior labral tear |
| Humeral lesion | Hill-Sachs (posterosuperior) | **Reverse Hill-Sachs**, LT fracture |
| Ligament avulsion | HAGL | RHAGL |
| Humeral fill | Remplissage / Connolly (infraspinatus + GT) | **McLaughlin** (subscap + LT) |

## How it's asked

**"Perthes lesion is?"** → **associated with recurrent dislocation** (TO)

**"Anteroinferior labral detachment with medially stripped intact periosteum?"** → **Perthes** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: Bankart · GLAD · ALPSA

**"Main anterior stabiliser of shoulder?"** → **IGHL** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: MGHL · SGHL · anterior capsule

**"Main stabiliser against anterior dislocation above 45° abduction?"** → **IGHL** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: subscapularis

**"MRI 2 months after dislocation – labrum displaced medially with periosteal sleeve?"** → **ALPSA** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: Bankart · SLAP · HAGL

**"Most common finding at surgery for traumatic anterior instability?"** → **anteroinferior labral tear**\
✗ Wrong options: Hill-Sachs

**"17-y footballer, MRI after injury – arthroscopic finding?"** → **Bankart lesion**\
✗ Wrong options: cuff tear · SLAP · glenoid fracture · HAGL

**"Labral tear + Hill-Sachs on MRA – position at injury?"** → **abduction + external rotation**

**"25-y basketballer, first dislocation – MRI shows?"** → **anteroinferior labral tear**\
✗ Wrong options: supraspinatus tear = >40 y

**"Typical separation on MRI after anteroinferior dislocation?"** → **anteroinferior labrum from bony glenoid**\
✗ Wrong options: from cartilage · capsule from humerus

**"Bony HAGL – when is this ligament most stressed?"** → **anterior force at 90° abduction + ER**\
✗ Wrong options: 0° inferior = SGHL · 45° = MGHL

**"Structure torn, name, position of greatest contribution?"** → **anteroinferior labrum, Bankart, ER at 90° abduction**

✗ Wrong options: dominant arm · overhead athlete · age <25 · biceps extension

**"Isolated labral repair would fail for which image?"** → **anterior glenoid fracture / bone loss**

**"Factor most linked to failure after arthroscopic Bankart, >10 dislocations?"** → **glenoid bone loss**\
✗ Wrong options: age at first dislocation · age at surgery

**"Why 3D CT for bone loss?"** → **highest inter-rater reliability**\
✗ Wrong options: lowest predictive error · MRI underestimates

**"Most significant risk factor for recurrence after first dislocation?"** → **young age** (TO)\
✗ Wrong options: bony Bankart · manual occupation · GT fracture

**"Highest risk for recurrent instability?"** → **age <20 at dislocation**\
✗ Wrong options: contralateral · dominance · family history · patellar instability

**"3 recurrences despite PT, last in sleep?"** → **discuss surgical stabilisation**

**"Competitive contact footballer, HS on ER AP, normal laxity – ISIS?"** → **5 points**

**"48-y first dislocation, 1 wk in sling, full strength?"** → **stop immobilisation now – no added recurrence risk**\
✗ Wrong options: ER/IR/neutral 2 more weeks

**"Arthroscopic vs open Bankart?"** → **similar dislocation rates**\

**"Isolated open Bankart most appropriate?"** → **recurrent after arthroscopic repair, 6% GBL, on-track**

**"Accountant, 3rd dislocation, 10% glenoid defect on 3D CT?"** → **arthroscopic bony Bankart repair**

**"25% glenoid loss, GT 18 mm, HSI 21.3 mm?"** → **Latarjet** (TO)

**"Wrestler, 30 dislocations, 40% posterolateral head loss?"** → **Latarjet** (TO)\

**"Incorrect about DAS?"** → **suitable arthroscopic alternative to Latarjet with bone loss** (TO)

**"Latarjet sling effect?"** → **conjoint tendon dynamic stability to anteroinferior capsule in ABER** (TO)

**"Latarjet stabilises by?"** → **bony support + conjoint sling + CA ligament capsular repair (1, 2 and 5)**

**"Ideal patient for open reduction + glenoid augmentation?"** → **chronic anterior dislocation + inverted-pear glenoid**\

# Triceps Rupture

*Shoulder & Elbow · 59 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Incidence** — rare · **0.8%** of tendon ruptures

**Demographics** — **♂ 2:1** · age **30–50** · competitive **weightlifting** (bench press), bodybuilding, **football**

### Risk factors & diagnosis (flake sign, MRI)

| Risk factor | Yes / No |
|---|---|
| **Anabolic steroids** | ✓ (established link) |
| **Local steroid injection** | ✓ |
| **Fluoroquinolones** | ✓ |
| **Renal osteodystrophy** / renal disease, **hyperparathyroidism** | ✓ |
| OI, RA, type I DM, Marfan | ✓ |
| Chronic olecranon bursitis, prior triceps surgery | ✓ |
| **Creatine supplements** | **not associated** |

### Anatomy – lateral expansion & anconeus

| Head | Origin |
|---|---|
| **Long** | **Infraglenoid tuberosity** |
| **Lateral** | Posterior humerus between teres minor insertion and **above spiral groove**, lateral border, lateral IM septum |
| **Medial** | Posterior humerus **distal to spiral groove**, medial humerus, medial IM septum |

**Triceps** — pennate · **only muscle of posterior arm compartment** · **radial nerve (C6–C8)** · primary elbow extensor

**Insertion footprint** — **1.1 cm distal to olecranon tip** · width **1.9–4.2 cm** · triceps tendon proper (all 3 heads → olecranon) + **lateral triceps expansion** (posterior ulnar crest · **ECU fascia + anconeus deep fascia** · antebrachial fascia)

**Anconeus** — **secondary elbow extensor**; terminal **15° extension** + supination · **compensates for torn triceps** → resisted extension may persist

**Anconeus vs distractors** — brachioradialis, brachialis = flexion · LCL = varus stabiliser · pronator teres = pronation

![Axial MRI: E = anconeus, A = brachioradialis, B = LCL, C = brachialis, D = pronator teres](../../MCQ/attachments/q220062-a-screenshot-2025-01-28-at-8-24-57-pm.jpg)

## 2 Mechanism

**Mechanism** — **forceful eccentric contraction** (bench press, muscle-ups) · **FOOSH** · direct blow

**Site** — most often **osseous insertion** (medial or lateral head) · less often muscle belly / MTJ

## 3 Presentation

**History** — **painful pop**

**Exam** — posterior elbow pain, swelling, ecchymosis · **palpable defect** (not always)

**Resisted extension** — inability to extend against resistance **not always present**: **intact lateral expansion** or **anconeus** compensation · complete tear → can't extend **against gravity** (OB)

**Modified Thompson squeeze** — **prone**, elbow at table edge, forearm hanging → squeeze triceps → **no extension** = complete rupture of **triceps proper + lateral expansion**

**Missed** — **10/23** missed initially (van Riet) · **nearly 50%** misdiagnosed (Yeh)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

**X-ray (AP + lateral)** — **"flake sign"** on lateral = **pathognomonic** of triceps avulsion (olecranon tip fleck)

![Flake sign: avulsed fleck at olecranon tip](../../MCQ/attachments/q217442-a-flake-of-bone-typical-sign-on-lateral-x-ray-image-.jpg)

**MRI** — location + severity; confirms, classifies, guides treatment · **partial** = small fluid-filled defect in distal tendon · **complete** = **large fluid-filled gap**, paratricipital oedema · use when too painful to examine or partial suspected

![MRI: distal triceps tear](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3071-picture2.jpg)

**✗ Flake-sign distractors** — distal biceps, brachialis, LUCL, MUCL tears · elbow arthritis · olecranon bursitis · anconeus avulsion (= **lateral epicondyle** fracture)

### Descriptive classification

| Feature | Options |
|---|---|
| Degree | Complete · partial · intact |
| Location | Muscle belly · MTJ · tendinous insertion · avulsion |
| Lateral expansion | Intact · torn |

**Formal system** — none

## 5 Management

```
Triceps rupture
├─ Partial, extends vs gravity / low-demand, poor health → splint 30° flexion 4 wk
├─ Acute complete → primary repair (best within 3 wk)
├─ Partial >50% + significant weakness → primary repair
└─ Chronic / delayed → reconstruction ± tendon graft
```

### Operative criteria

| Indication | Treatment |
|---|---|
| **Acute complete tear** (active, medically fit) | **Primary repair** |
| **Partial >50% + significant weakness** | **Primary repair** |
| Partial, extends against gravity | Splint |
| Low demand, poor health | Splint |
| Late (>3 wk) | Reconstruction, tendon graft |

**Approach** — posterolateral

| Tear site | Technique |
|---|---|
| MTJ | **V-Y advancement** ± plantaris augmentation |
| Tendinous insertion | **Bunnell / Krackow** non-absorbable whipstitch → **transosseous tunnels**, periosteal flap, or **suture anchors** |
| Bony avulsion | Tension band · screw + washer |

**Tunnels vs anchors** — **no difference** in biomechanical strength or function · **transosseous: ↑ re-rupture + complications**

**Post-op** — 30–45° flexion **2 wk** · active ROM at **4 wk** · no weightlifting **4–6 mo**

**Outcome** — early diagnosis + repair → excellent; anatomic footprint repair

## 6 Complications

**General** — elbow stiffness/weakness · **ulnar nerve injury** · repair failure

**Repair-specific** — olecranon bursitis · flexion contracture · re-rupture

## X vs Y

| | **Partial tear** | **Complete tear** |
|---|---|---|
| Extension vs gravity | Preserved | Lost (unless anconeus / lateral expansion compensate) |
| Thompson squeeze | Extension present | **No extension** |
| MRI | **Small fluid-filled defect** | **Large fluid-filled gap**, paratricipital oedema |
| Treatment | Splint 30° × 4 wk · repair if **>50% + weak** | **Primary repair** |

## How it's asked

**"Triceps rupture associated with all EXCEPT?"** → **creatine supplements**\
✗ Wrong options: anabolic steroids · local steroid · renal osteodystrophy · fluoroquinolones

**"40 y weightlifter, pop on bench press, lateral X-ray fleck — MRI shows?"** → **triceps tendon tear**\
✗ Wrong options: distal biceps · brachialis · LUCL · MUCL

**"44 y, FOOSH rollerblading, posterior pain, olecranon flake?"** → **triceps rupture**\
✗ Wrong options: arthritis · bursitis · LCL · anconeus avulsion

**"Muscle-ups, positive Thompson squeeze — structure assisting resisted extension?"** → **anconeus (E)**

# Valgus Extension Overload (Pitcher's Elbow)

*Shoulder & Elbow · 60 / 60*

## 1 Basics

### Epidemiology

**Definition** — **posteromedial elbow pain** from repetitive microtrauma in **throwing athletes**

**Prevalence** — up to **65%** of elite baseball players with symptomatic elbows

**Who** — **dominant arm** of throwers (pitchers, quarterbacks) · also swimmers, volleyball, gymnasts, racquet sports, golfers

**Location** — **posteromedial olecranon** (✓ label D) · posterior trochlea · olecranon fossa

**Association** — **cubital tunnel syndrome ~25%**

## 2 Mechanism

**Forces of pitching** — **medial tension (MCL)** · **lateral radiocapitellar compression** · **posterior extension overload** · **shear on medial olecranon tip + fossa**

**Phases (OB)** — **valgus stress in acceleration** (esp. with **UCL insufficiency**) + **extension impaction in deceleration**

**Medial laxity** — any cause → **compensatory ↑ stress posteromedially** → osteophytes / loose bodies

**Sequence** — repetitive impaction of olecranon into fossa → **chondromalacia/cartilage injury** → **osteophytes (posteromedial olecranon + humerus)** → **loose bodies** (fragmentation) · **capitellar osteochondral lesions** · **UCL attenuation** with repetitive strain

![Valgus extension overload: medial tension, radiocapitellar compression, olecranon fossa shearing](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3080-mechanism.jpg)

## 3 Presentation

### Diagnosis – posteromedial olecranon osteophytes, examination & throwing phase

**Pain** — **posteromedial**, at **full extension**; **deceleration / follow-through** (✓; after ball release) · rarely acceleration\
✗ Wrong options: wind-up, early/late cocking

**Function** — **loss of terminal extension** (osteophytes block olecranon seating) · ↓ accuracy · pain after ~25 pitches

**Exam** — **tender posteromedial olecranon** · **flexion contracture** (e.g. 10°) · **crepitus** (loose bodies, synovitis) · **pain with forced extension**

**Provocative tests** — **arm bar test** (sustained extension) · **bounce test** (repeated terminal extension)

**UCL assessment** — check competency (failure → valgus instability); moving valgus stress may be painful **without instability** in VEO

![Phases of pitching: VEO pain in deceleration/follow-through](../../Orthobullets/attachments/ob3080-phases-of-pitch.jpg)

## 4 Diagnosis & Classification

### Key radiology findings

| Modality | Finding |
|---|---|
| **X-ray AP, lateral, oblique** | **Posteromedial olecranon osteophyte** (AP key; medial olecranon) · loose bodies (capitellar fragmentation) · **MCL calcification** · **humeral hypertrophy** (↓ room in fossa) |
| **CT** | **Best osseous detail**; 3D for planning |
| **MRI** | Not needed for diagnosis · **associated MCL tear** (partial/complete) · capitellar OCD |

**Diagnosis** — **clinical** (posteromedial pain ↑ with extension) + imaging of posteromedial osteophyte

## 5 Management

```
Valgus extension overload
├─ First episode → NSAIDs + active rest 10–14 d → graduated throwing
├─ Repeat episode → active rest 4–6 wk → lengthened return
├─ Fails nonop / ↓ performance → arthroscopic osteophyte + loose body excision
│      └─ ulnar nerve symptoms / subluxing → ± decompression / transposition
└─ MCL insufficiency → not olecranon debridement alone (address UCL)
```

### Nonoperative

**First line** — NSAIDs, **stop throwing**, correct mechanics, ± steroid injection (**paucity of data**) · also **mid-season** or **end of career**

**PT** — **flexor-pronator strengthening** (✓ next step) · shoulder, forearm + kinetic chain

| Patient | Active rest | Then |
|---|---|---|
| **First-time symptomatic** | **10–14 days** | Graduated throwing |
| **Repeat symptomatic** | **4–6 weeks** | Lengthened gradual return |

### Operative criteria

| Factor | Rule |
|---|---|
| **Indication** | **Persistent symptoms after failed (extended) nonop** · diminished performance |
| **Relative contraindication** | **MCL insufficiency** (for olecranon debridement alone) |
| **Ulnar nerve release / transposition** | **Significant ulnar symptoms** · nerve **subluxes** over medial epicondyle (transposition for symptomatic unstable nerve) |

### Arthroscopic osteophyte resection

**Preferred** — arthroscopic (✓) or open **cheilectomy**; generally **excellent results** if no over-resection

**Position** — lateral decubitus, prone or supine (**supine + articulating holder** if combined with **MCL reconstruction**)

**Steps** — mark ulnar nerve course · diagnostic arthroscopy, remove loose bodies · **posterolateral viewing portal** · **direct posterior portal** (through triceps) · clear fibrous tissue (RF + shaver) · free fractured osteophyte (switch portals → remove via posterolateral) · **contour to native margin only** · **arthroscopic valgus stress test** (medial gapping = incompetent MCL)

**Also** — chondromalacia debridement/drilling · lateral meniscoid lesion / posterolateral plica

### Excessive olecranon resection – unmasking UCL insufficiency

**Rule** — remove **osteophyte only, not native olecranon** → else **loss of bony restraint → ↑ MCL strain + valgus angulation**

**Threshold** — **≥9 mm** resection → **UCL rupture** under valgus (cadaveric; Orthobullets, OB) · **>3 mm** / past native margin → ↑ MCL stress + valgus instability (Orthobullets complications) — read the options

**Result** — **valgus elbow instability** (✓) · **MCL damaged**\
✗ Wrong options: LUCL · RCL · annular · arcuate

**Presentation** — after debridement: **medial pain (late cocking)**, laxity + pain on valgus stress, **positive milking manoeuvre**, **negative arm bar**, new UCL tear on MRI

**✗ Failure-cause distractors** — accelerated return-to-throw · lateral instability (rare) · posterior bundle (dominant UCL structure = **anterior band of anterior bundle**) · anatomic variant · ulnar neuritis · capitellar OCD · olecranon stress fracture

## 6 Complications

| Complication | Prevention |
|---|---|
| **Valgus instability** | Avoid over-resection (see thresholds) |
| **Ulnar nerve injury** | Mark nerve before medial portals · **"nick and spread"** · limit/remove **shaver suction** in posteromedial gutter |

## X vs Y

| | **VEO** | **UCL injury** | **Medial epicondylitis** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pain | **Posteromedial** | **Medial** | Medial, **5–10 mm distal to epicondyle** |
| Phase | **Deceleration / follow-through** | **Acceleration** (late cocking) | |
| Test | **Arm bar, bounce**; crepitus | **Valgus stress at 20–30°**, **milking manoeuvre**, moving valgus stress | **Resisted flexion/pronation** |
| X-ray | **Posteromedial osteophyte** | No fracture, **no posteromedial osteophyte** | No VEO osteophytes |
| Contracture | **Yes** | | Unlikely |

| Differential | Key feature |
|---|---|
| **Acute UCL rupture** (pop, ↓ velocity, MRA full-thickness) | **UCL reconstruction** in competitive thrower (✗ Wrong options: isolated ulnar transposition · osteophyte excision without osteophyte) |
| Partial UCL tear, non-thrower / low-risk sport | Nonop: activity modification + PT |
| **MCL sprain** (recreational thrower) | Medial pain **only when throwing**, ↓ speed + distance, no instability, normal X-ray |
| **PLRI** | Lateral; **pivot shift, chair rise**; LUCL; dislocation or lateral epicondylitis debridement |
| **Capitellar OCD** | Lateral pain, swelling, **catching/locking** |
| Ulnar nerve subluxation | Palpable in flexion; neuritis to hand |
| Pronator syndrome | **Volar forearm** pain on resisted pronation; not throwing-related |
| Medial epicondyle avulsion | **Skeletally immature** |
| Olecranon stress fracture | **Posterior** pain + swelling; lateral X-ray/MRI |
| ✗ Wrong option: varus extension overload | Distractor — **valgus** EO (not varus) mimics MCL injury |

## How it's asked

**"Typical location of osteophytes in VEO?"** → **posteromedial olecranon (D)**

**"Pitcher, posteromedial pain after ball release, normal milking — diagnosis + next step?"** → **VEO; PT with flexor-pronator strengthening**\
✗ Wrong options: UCL reconstruction · PRP · LUCL reconstruction · extension splint

**"Pitcher, 10° flexion contracture, posteromedial tenderness, no instability?"** → **valgus extension overload**\
✗ Wrong options: medial epicondylitis · olecranon stress fracture · trochlear OCD · epicondyle avulsion

**"Exam findings in VEO?"** → **posteromedial pain, crepitus, flexion contracture**\
✗ Wrong options: pivot shift · distal epicondyle tenderness · lateral locking

**"Throwing phase most painful in VEO?"** → **follow-through / deceleration**\
✗ Wrong options: acceleration · late cocking

**"Excessive normal olecranon resection → on return to play?"** → **valgus elbow instability**

**"Valgus instability 9 mo after osteophyte removal — structure?"** → **medial collateral ligament**\
✗ Wrong options: LUCL · annular

**"Medial pain in late cocking + valgus laxity after olecranon debridement?"** → **excessive olecranon resection**

**"New UCL tear after VEO arthroscopy + accelerated rehab — cause?"** → **excessive olecranon resection**\
✗ Wrong options: accelerated protocol · posterior bundle

**"Pitcher, pop, full-thickness UCL tear on MRA, wants to return?"** → **UCL reconstruction**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnar transposition · osteophyte excision

**"Recreational thrower, medial pain only throwing, ↓ distance, normal X-ray?"** → **MCL sprain**\
✗ Wrong options: ulnar neuritis · pronator syndrome · medial epicondylitis · varus extension overload
